(1) **A4 POS** hid the whole settlement block (Received in · Balance due · Previous / Closing balance) because it was gated off for every discount-ladder receipt; `printsSettlement()` in [`A4InvoicePreview`](src/shared/components/print/A4InvoicePreview.tsx) now lets POS through — **Online Invoice keeps it off (owner's call)** — and the paginator reserves its height for a fully-on-credit bill too, not only when tenders exist. (2) **The slip printed at sale time never carried `balanceDue`** — `PosScreen` now sends the unpaid part; an overpaid bill (settled by its RV-…) prints the one **Payment adjusted (RV-…)** row the reprint shows, instead of no payment rows at all. (3) **The "Party balance" toggle did nothing on POS** — nothing read the running account. New [`billSettlementPrint.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billSettlementPrint.ts) is the one reader of received rows + balance due + party balance, moved out of `voucherReceiptSample` and now also used by [`InvoiceReceipt`](src/shared/components/print/InvoiceReceipt.tsx); the running account is read only when either paper's toggle is on and never for the walk-in ledger (it pools every walk-in). `/api/pos/sale` returns `partyBalance { before, after }` on the same terms, and the 80mm preview + bridge `printReceiptsBlock` print **Previous balance / Closing balance** under Balance due (so an 80mm Sale gets them too). (4) **Bug: a POS reprint read only `settlementJson` tenders**, so an overpaid bill (money on the linked RV-…) or a credit bill paid later printed as fully due — it now counts live links as "Payment adjusted", same as Sale. `partyBalanceAroundVoucher` takes a list of ids: the bill plus live vouchers linked to it on the **same POS shift** count as one visit, so an overpayment's RV-… does not make "Previous balance" show the old dues as already paid. Office bills have no shift, so Sale is unchanged. Bridge tests 14 pass. **Counters need a release** for the slip changes.
- **2026-09-27 — The POS Rcv dialog shows the party's balance.** Owner: *"display party balance in rcv voucher in pos"*. Picking a party in [`PosReceiptDialog`](src/modules/pos/components/PosReceiptDialog.tsx) now shows **Due ₹…** (amber) / **Advance ₹…** (green) / **No balance** under the name, plus a live **After this receipt: …** as the tenders are typed — informational only, never blocks the save. Plain words on purpose (owner chose Due/Advance over Dr/Cr); overdue deliberately left out. New **thin** route [`GET /api/pos/party-balance/[accountId]`](src/app/api/pos/party-balance/[accountId]/route.ts) returns `{ due }` — GL + master opening, no date ceiling, the same read as `closingForAccounts` (POS customer panel, sale credit gate), signed on the Dr side whatever the ledger's nature. Not the account-snapshot, for the same reason as the credit-limit card: that endpoint resolves tiers, loyalty, turnover and tickets to show one number. Read fresh on every pick, never cached; a request counter drops a slow reply for an earlier party.

- **2026-09-27 — Customer sign-in codes can no longer be guessed by replaying the cookie.** [`/api/portal/otp/verify`](src/app/api/portal/otp/verify/route.ts) kept its 5-wrong-tries count only inside the signed `portal_otp` cookie, which the browser holds: a script could resend the first cookie (0 tries) and guess the 6-digit code as fast as the server answered, then request a new code and carry on — a right guess is a ticket that signs in as that customer (orders, invoices, wallet, repairs). **Fix, shared:** the server-side count the staff reset got earlier today moved into [`checkOtpCode`](src/shared/lib/auth/otpChallenge.ts) itself, so every caller has it and none can forget it. Each caller names its own budget (`"portal-otp-code"`, `"staff-reset-code"`); the key is `<scope>:<channel>:<identity>` out of the signed cookie; 5 wrong in 15 min (`OTP_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, [`loginRateLimit`](src/shared/lib/auth/loginRateLimit.ts), in memory per instance) gives a new `"blocked"` answer — **429 even for the right code**, so it is no oracle; a right code clears the count. A wrong code now carries `triesLeft`, the smaller of the cookie's budget and the server's, so "N tries left" stays true across a re-sent code; at 0 the customer is told to *wait a few minutes, then request a new one* (a new code is refused until the window slides). New `loginTriesLeft()`; `isLoginBlocked` is built on it. Password sign-in keeps its own counter. The staff reset route lost its hand-written copy; its messages are unchanged. Verified on the dev server with forged cookies for made-up `@example.invalid` addresses (no SMS / email sent): one 0-tries cookie replayed → 4, 3, 2, 1 tries left, then 429, and 429 for the right code; a fresh code for the blocked address → 429; 2 wrong + right → 200 with a ticket and the count cleared; staff route the same (5 × 400, then 429). tsc + ESLint clean. **Trade-off, accepted:** a stranger can hold a customer's OTP sign-in off for 15 min by typing wrong codes — no worse than the send throttle already allows.
- **2026-09-27 — The customer display's UPI QR shows the amount typed, not the one before it.** Owner: *"pos qr code creates 3 of 32, 67 of 670"* — the DQ11 stayed on the part-typed amount and moved only when another digit was typed. **Cause:** [`sendToDq11Once`](hardware-bridge/qr.js) settled as soon as it *asked* the COM port to close, so the next queued picture opened the port while Windows still held it — *Access denied*, picture lost. A cashier pausing over 250 ms mid-amount queues two pictures (₹3, then ₹32), so the second always hit it. **Fix, all in [`qr.js`](hardware-bridge/qr.js):** settle only after the close callback (2 s cap); **latest wins** — while one picture goes out (~3.3 s at 921,600 baud) only the newest waiting one is kept; every POS screen goes out **a second time** when nothing newer waits, so the last picture lands even if the device turns out to lag one frame; and an idle tick still sending when a POS screen arrives no longer restarts the slideshow over it (`idleRun`), nor does a thank-you that a newer screen replaced. [`usePosCustomerDisplay`](src/modules/pos/hooks/usePosCustomerDisplay.ts) waits 1 s (was 250 ms) before sending the pay QR, so a half-typed ₹3 QR is rarer — a cashier pausing longer still sends one, replaced a few seconds later. Verified with a fake COM port that refuses a still-open port: old code ends on ₹3 / ₹67 and loses the cart under the slideshow; new code ends on ₹32 / ₹670 / cart. **Not yet seen on a real DQ11; ships with the next counter release.** Same commit: the checkout's Cash / return / payment-line boxes took no paise (*"32."* lost its dot through `Number()`); they are now [`TenderAmountInput`](src/modules/pos/components/TenderSplitInput.tsx), which keeps the typed text and still takes the ✓ / "/" / F10 fills while focused (why not `NumericInput`, which holds its draft until blur).
- **2026-09-27 — An outage no longer turns a good save into "Sync error".** Counter 93 showed *Sync error (2)* after its internet dropped: both rows were one attendance "last seen" tick (`attendance_days` 50), and each had failed ten times with *Can't reach database server* between 12:49 and 14:37. The office never refused them — [`markOutboxFailure`](src/shared/lib/sync/outbox.ts) counted an outage toward `MAX_PUSH_ATTEMPTS` like a refusal, so about two hours offline dead-lettered the row, and a dead letter is never retried, so the badge stayed red after the line came back. On a `v_live` till that is exactly when rows queue, so offline sales were exposed the same way. **Fix:** one classifier, [`isConnectionFailure`](src/shared/lib/sync/connectionError.ts) (moved out of `prismaClient.ts`, which uses it for the outage switch; it also now catches the pg adapter's P1008 "Operation has timed out" and the pool's checkout timeout). [`push.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) tags a failure `unreachable`; the flusher records it with `markOutboxUnreachable` — reason kept, no attempt spent, no backoff (a backing-off row is skipped by `getPendingOutbox`, which would let later rows overtake it). On start the flusher runs `requeueUnreachableDeadLetters` once: outage dead letters go back in the queue, except one with a later capture of the same record, which is retired as superseded so an older image cannot land after a newer one. Real refusals (constraint, released counter, never-push model) still count and still dead-letter at ten. Verified on a scratch cluster with Counter 93's rows and against real Prisma P1001 errors; **ships with the next counter release** — until then a stuck till is cleared by hand (`UPDATE _outbox SET dead_lettered_at = NULL, attempts = 0, next_attempt_at = NULL WHERE id IN (…)`). **Follow-up 2026-09-28:** 1.8.4 on Counter 93 still showed *Sync error (2)* — the sweep was a one-shot at flusher start, and it hit the replica while the app was booting: `[Outbox] Failed to re-queue outage dead letters: Error: timeout expired`, the same 5 s connect timeout the LISTEN client logs at every boot and recovers from 5 s later. The sweep now runs at the top of `flushOnce` until it succeeds once (`strandedSwept`), and returns null — not zero — when the replica did not answer, so a missed sweep is retried instead of counted as done.
- **2026-09-26 — The ₹0.01 "Difference in opening balances" solved; a voucher can no longer be saved a paisa out.** Owner: *"it should never happen"*. Openings were fine: the row appeared from 17 Sep because **SV-930802-26** (₹1,600 cash sale) posted Dr 1,600.00 / Cr 1,600.01. The server's total re-derived from the lines was 1,600.01 against a ₹1,600 payment row, and both checks in the way allowed a paisa — `tenderCoverageComplaint` (`≤ 0.01`) and the GL builder (`> 0.01` refused, a float sum). **Fix, three layers:** (1) [`tenderPaisaDrift`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/postInvVoucher.ts) — a one-paisa gap between payment and total goes on the voucher's own `roundOff` in both the create and edit routes, so invoice = payment = ledger; (2) `buildInvVoucherGlLines` rounds each leg to 2dp and puts a leftover paisa on Round Off (POS reaches here too); (3) [`assertGlBalanced`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/transactionEngine.ts) — `engineGlArm`, `engineCreateVoucher` and the trading edit's in-place rewrite refuse any line set not level **to the paisa**. `isBalanced` now judges the stored (rounded) amounts in whole paise ([`glGapPaise`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts)), so every route using it got stricter. SV-930802-26 re-saved on live: roundOff −0.01, total 1,600.00, Dr Round Off 0.01; Trial Balance and Balance Sheet level for 16 Sep, 17 Sep and today.
- **2026-09-26 — Every setting has one home: `src/shared/lib/constants/`.** Owner, asking where the inactivity logout was set: *"i want all constant together so i find easily"* — only the ones worth finding, not every capitalised list. `src/utils/constants.ts` (the sidebar, Quick Actions, POS rail, badge colours) is split into `constants/nav.ts`, `quickActions.ts` and `badges.ts`, and the tunable numbers left the logic files for `session.ts` (login 6 h, idle sign-out 45 min, PIN and OTP clocks), `hr.ts` (duty hours, grace, day shares, pay days, leave year), `paging.ts`, `inventory.ts` (Reorder ladder, FSN), plus `DEFAULT_DUE_DAYS` in `accounting.ts` and `SCREEN_HEARTBEAT_MS` in `polling.ts`. `constants/index.ts` opens with a one-line-per-file map. `fmt()` moved to `format/moneyInr.ts`; `auth/sessionAge.ts` is gone. No value changed. Numbers that stored data depends on (`BLOCK_SIZE`, `SECRET_BUCKET_DAYS`, nav surface keys) stay with their code on purpose. **Found, not fixed:** `pnpm check:nav-icons` already fails on `dev` — `Store`, `BadgeIndianRupee` and `Activity` each carry two concepts since the HR / POS commits; and `PIN_LENGTH` was never read (6 is hard-coded in `counterPin.ts` and the sign-in pad), so it was dropped rather than moved.
- **2026-09-25 — The super admin is on the staff lists again.** Owner, finding the Staff Directory one short: *"admin@networkservice.in is in db also and remove restriction to show it"*, and *everywhere*. The `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL` account's `User` row is a real person here, so `excludingSuperAdmin()` is gone and Staff Directory (list + profile read/save), Attendance, Payroll candidates and Commission all list every `User`. Payroll still posts nothing for anyone without a salary structure; it lists them with the reason.
- **2026-09-25 — Voucher numbers go counter-wise on every write mode; the post-Vyapar books renumbered; the Recycle Bin empties in one go.** Owner: *"implement counter wise voucher number for live/v_live too, backfill after vyapar import … upto 10th sept back fill with vyapar series/format, from 11th start ours"*.
  - **Numbering rule, reversed on purpose.** From 2026-09-20 the office numbered every relayed `v_live` sale as counter 1, so counter 91's 78 POS bills read `POS-1…`. Now the **counter the bill is raised at** picks the run on every mode: [`numberingCounter`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts) → the relay's `x-abeer-counter-number` header, else this PC's own setup, else 1 — the same resolution that stamps `sourceCounterId` ([`sourceCounterNumber`](src/shared/lib/counter/sourceCounter.ts)). Supersedes the 2026-09-07 "one common series for anything saved at the office" note below.
  - **Two writers, one row.** The office now writes a till's `VoucherSeriesCounter` row for its relayed sales, and the till writes it while local. Push no longer lets an older till image wind the cloud back: [`mergeSeriesCounter`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) keeps the higher `nextSeq` within a generation and drops a replay the office has reset or rolled into a new year. Worst case is a gap, never a reissued number. Residual: a till that falls back to local in the seconds before it has pulled the office's latest increment can still repeat that one number.
  - **A voucher born POSTED now takes a number.** `engineGlArm`'s create-posted branch only ever used a caller-supplied number, so the shift-close variance Journal, the takeover variance and gift-card issue/breakage vouchers posted unnumbered. It now allocates through `allocateSpineNumber`, like the attach branch.
  - **Renumbering — [`pnpm vyapar:renumber`](scripts/vyapar/renumber-own-vouchers.ts).** Vyapar's vouchers are the import batch (created before 11 Sep 21:00), proved against the backup's highest Sale / Payment-In / Sale Return numbers (2097 / 322 / 57) and by format; they are never touched. Ours dated ≤ 10 Sep continue Vyapar's series (Receipt `RV-10793-26` → `323`) or, for kinds Vyapar never numbered (Purchase, Payment, Journal), carry none. Ours from 11 Sep are renumbered counter-wise from `startFrom` in date → created → id order, and each counter row points past its run. Rewrites `voucherNo`, the number inside `description`, and `VoucherAllocation.reference`; bumps `updatedAt` so tills pull it. Refuses to apply while the Recycle Bin holds anything. Plan-only by default; `--apply --rollback` rehearses every write. Rehearsed on `abeersuite_dev` (152 vouchers) and applied on a throwaway copy. **Not yet run on live** — needs a backup, an empty bin, quiet synced tills. `VoucherAllocation` syncs append-only, so the few edited allocation references reach a till's replica only on a rebuild.
  - **Shift numbers closed up — [`pnpm pos:renumber-shifts`](scripts/renumber-shifts.ts).** A shift's number is its `pos_shifts.id`; deleted shifts left live on ids 1, 2, 7, 9–12, 14–18 with the sequence at 22. The script renumbers every shift 1..n in opening order (ids go negative first, then final; `ON UPDATE CASCADE` carries cash drops and both handover ends), re-points `UniversalVoucher.sourceShiftId`, rewrites "Shift #N" in voucher and GL-line text in one pass per value, bumps `updatedAt` so tills pull it (their hourly reconcile drops the vanished ids), and sets the sequence to n + 1 — only on a kept run, since a sequence ignores ROLLBACK. Refuses while any shift is not CLOSED. Shared start-up with the voucher renumber now lives in [`scripts/vyapar/liveTarget.ts`](scripts/vyapar/liveTarget.ts). A shift deleted later leaves a gap again; a durable serial would need its own `shiftNo` column.
  - **Empty bin.** `DELETE /api/audit` (super admin, body `{ confirm: "EMPTY" }`) removes every snapshot and releases trailing numbers repeatedly until none frees; the Recycle Bin header has an **Empty bin** button armed by typing EMPTY. `seriesKindOf` moved into [`releaseSeriesNumber.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/releaseSeriesNumber.ts) for both routes.

- **2026-09-23 — Targets & Commission, wired end to end: heads, per-person overrides, payslip working.** Owner, over two days: *"earnings, commission, target, etc. need a page to set/edit. user need to see it to get motivated"* → *"in set commission page is like coupon page here we create different commission or target head its not user specific"* → *"user kind applicable"* → *"user wise override %, per commission head. user see only applicable to him and globally on"* → *"complete wiring, update related reports payslip etc."* **Two tables** (`prisma/schema/hr.prisma`): **`CommissionHead`** (`commission_heads`) — the catalogue: name, kind (`PERCENT` a share of every sale · `ABOVE_TARGET` a share above a target · `SLABS` direct match — the slab the month's sales land in sets the rate on the whole amount · `BONUS` a flat sum at a target · `MANUAL` a count of things the app cannot see — new customers, social work — typed in by HR as **`CommissionEntry`** rows (`commission_entries`: who, head, date, count, note; `POST /api/hr/commission/entries`, `DELETE /entries/[id]`) with a unit, a monthly target count, pay per unit and/or a bonus at the target; owner, 2026-09-23: *"those are not possible to track automatically but we will manually create entry for those"*), the kind's own figures, `active` (globally on), `appliesTo` (UserRole labels; empty = everyone), sort order — and **`CommissionOverride`** (`commission_overrides`, unique per person+head): the person's own `rateBp` / `bonusPaise` in place of the head's, or `off`. No dating: payroll freezes what a period earned, so editing a head later never rewrites a paid month. **One resolution**, [`commissionServer.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/commissionServer.ts) `appliedHeadsFor`: a person is judged by every head that is on, reaches their role label, and is not off for them — with their override applied; **one arithmetic**, [`commissionScheme.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/commissionScheme.ts) `computeCommission` (rules paid together; every target scaled to the share of the month a run covers). **Whose sale**: the voucher's `createdById`; posted `SALE`/`POS`/`ONLINE_INVOICE` less `SALE_RETURN`. **Three surfaces quote it**: the board at `/office/hr/commission` ([`CommissionBoard`](src/modules/hr/components/CommissionBoard.tsx): **Set commission** = [`CommissionHeadsTab`](src/modules/hr/components/CommissionHeadsTab.tsx), See heads / Create head like the coupons page with search, kind filter, Active switch, Applies-to chips from the current staff's roles, a full-width editor that reads the head back as a sentence with a worked example; **Per person** = [`CommissionMonthTab`](src/modules/hr/components/CommissionMonthTab.tsx), a leaderboard whose rows open into the heads that reach the person with an own-rate / own-bonus box saved on blur, an on/off switch, and on a manual head the month's count against its target with Add entry and the entries listed); plus a **Give commission** button on this page and on Payroll opening [`GiveCommissionDialog`](src/modules/hr/components/GiveCommissionDialog.tsx) — one person, every head that reaches them, override / on-off / entries in one modal (owner: "the place is too inside"), the **My target** card on My Info ([`MyTargetCard`](src/modules/hr/components/MyTargetCard.tsx): bar to the biggest target with ticks for the rest, "₹X to go — about ₹Y a day for N days — and ₹Z is yours", earned so far, a ✓ / ○ line per head), and **payroll**: `commissionForPeriod` ([`commission.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/commission.ts)) hands back the total plus the working **head by head, month by month**, which the run freezes onto `PayrollRunLine.commissionPaise` + a "Commission" earning **and the new `commissionJson`**; the **salary slip** prints it as a "Commission · sold ₹…" table (head, terms, target, earned) under the pay heads, A4 and thermal, behind new print-template toggles `showCommission` / `showCommissionThermal` (Print Templates → Salary slip), and the template preview's sample slip carries one. APIs: `GET /api/hr/commission?month` (heads, roles, rows), `POST /api/hr/commission/heads`, `PATCH|DELETE /api/hr/commission/heads/[id]` (a PATCH re-checks the merged terms), `PUT /api/hr/commission/overrides` (an override that says nothing is deleted, not kept), `/api/hr/me` grew `commission`. Sidebar link `hr:commission` (Goal icon; no seed); writes take `hr:staff:edit`. Migration `20260923030000_commission_schemes` (folded three times while unreleased — its name is now a misnomer, kept because the folder is what `_prisma_migrations` will record): the two tables, `payroll_run_lines.commissionJson`, the two template columns. Applied to the local copy; **live gets it on the next deploy**.
- **2026-09-22 — Employee Activity, a card a day.** Owner: *"let select date range, or one day/date, every day one card, in the card day activity report, like auto granted hours, late penalty etc."* The day-by-day table on [`AttendanceBreakup`](src/modules/hr/components/AttendanceBreakup.tsx) is gone; in its place a range bar and one [`DayActivityCard`](src/modules/hr/components/DayActivityCard.tsx) per day, newest first. **The range is the screen's, not the pane's** — the same from/to drives the cards and the P / A / L beside each name, so the two can never be counting different days. Presets moved to [`datePresets.ts`](src/shared/lib/format/datePresets.ts) in shared (this week, this month, last month and the rest); accountancy's `presetRange` now delegates to it and keeps only its own financial year, so the arithmetic is written once. **The card shows the working, never its own opinion of it**: times active, duty owed, worked, away, late by, over/short, the log of every stretch, and the sum said in words. **Granted hours** are named where they happen — weekly-off and holiday duty on the day's own card at their own rate; the netted overtime in the range strip, because netting is a period's arithmetic and belongs to no single day. **The late penalty is the rule, quoted**: the API now runs `lateDutyCutMinutes` — the very function payroll calls — per day and ships the minutes plus whether that day fell inside the free allowance, counted oldest-first over exactly the days `attendanceEffectFor` judges (measured, not a holiday, not a weekly off) so the card and the payslip never disagree about which late day was the free one. `HoursPayload` gained `rules` and `effect`; `AttendanceEffect` moved to [`payRules.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/payRules.ts) so a screen can name its shape without importing a server-only module. **Money stays on the payslip** — a rupee figure here would need the basic and the pay period, and a second one is the one people argue with. **The card carries everything the day knows**, so nothing lives only in a tooltip: the duty window it was judged against (`10:00-19:00`, which moves through the year), duty owed, worked, times active, away, late by, **left early**, over/short, the leave kind when one was granted, the office's note, the log, and the sum in words. `earlyMinutes` in [`dutyTime.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/dutyTime.ts) is the mirror of `lateMinutes` and exists for the same reason — a short day and an early finish are different failings, and "6h of 9h" cannot say which happened. The payload also carries the person's own `idleLimitMinutes`, because it is the answer to "why was my away time not paid". **Seeing it needed data nobody had**: the cards stay empty until `AttendanceSpan` rows exist, and only a till writes those. [`demo-attendance.ts`](prisma/seeds/demo-attendance.ts) (`pnpm seed:demo-attendance --user <id> --from --to`, with `--dry` and `--undo`) writes a believable month for one person — ordinary days, late ones, an early finish, absences, an office override with a note, and duty on a day the shop was shut — touching `AttendanceDay` and `AttendanceSpan` and nothing else. It **refuses to run off localhost**, because these rows are indistinguishable from recorded ones and on live they would pay or dock somebody. Run for Rakesh Biswas over Sept 2026 on `abeersuite_dev`. **Two lies the real data exposed, both fixed**: a shut day was showing "left early 6h 29m" and "7h 44m short" against a duty nobody owed, and then grading itself ABSENT — the rules take such a day out of the netting entirely, so the card now says so and does not grade it. **Caught by driving it**: an untracked day was wearing an "absent" chip, because the clock's grade for nought measured minutes is ABSENT — arithmetically true, and a lie on screen. Only a mark somebody actually made shows now.

- **2026-09-22 — Employee Activity: a page of its own.** Owner: *"need a tab that has list pan like staff directory. and in main area log, attendance breakup"*, then *"keep the Attendance page same as before it will be use for manual override. the currant page is with list is also good name it employee activity, mount to employee name in attendance page and new nav in sidebar with role page tick."* So the two jobs are split across two screens instead of crowding one. **Attendance** (`/office/hr/attendance`) is untouched: the whole month for everybody, and the only place a day is marked by hand — clicking a name there now walks to the new page instead of opening a pop-up. **Employee Activity** (`/office/hr/activity`, [`AttendanceExplorer`](src/modules/hr/components/AttendanceExplorer.tsx)) is the Staff Directory shape: people on the left with this month's P / A / L, and the person's month filling the right — day by day, every stretch they were active under it. It reads only. **The table was not rewritten**: `HoursBreakupDialog` had it, so it moved out whole into [`AttendanceBreakup`](src/modules/hr/components/AttendanceBreakup.tsx) and the dialog — with nothing left to hold — went. **No new read**: the left list is counted from the register payload the page already fetches (AGENTS §7). **The gate** is the sidebar tick `ui:sidebar:hr:activity`, minted automatically from the nav entry in `constants.ts` (no seed — see AGENTS §5) and ticked per role on the Role page, the same way every other HR page is gated.

- **2026-09-22 — Commission: the room, not the rule.** Owner: *"make room for commission in hr module, commission calculation will be in commission page where verity of commission may be created. that later part."* So HR calculates **nothing** — it does not know who earns commission, what a target is or which bill counts. **The seam** is [`commission.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/commission.ts), deliberately the whole contract between the two: `commissionForPeriod(db, userIds, from, to)` is what payroll asks, and today it returns an empty map, because with no schemes on file that is the only honest answer — a guess here posts money into the books. When the Commission page lands, **only that function's body changes**. **Why nothing downstream needs editing**: a commission is not a new kind of money, it is an EARNING pay head (`commissionLine`), so it joins the proportioned heads and the pay-rule heads in `buildPayrollCandidates` and the totals cannot tell the three apart — `grossPaise`, `netPaise`, the payslip, the print template and the journal already carry it. `PayrollRunLine.commissionPaise` (folded into migration `20260923010000_attendance_spans`, additive, defaults 0) is a denormalised copy **already inside** those totals, never an amount to add on, so a sheet can name the commission part without parsing the JSON — the same reason `grossPaise` sits beside `linesJson`. **On screen**: a Commission column on both payroll sheets, reading — for everybody, so the sheet does not change shape the day it fills. **Still open**: the Commission page itself, and with it who is on which scheme — the owner's call was to keep that link out of HR entirely.

- **2026-09-22 — Attendance spans: the day can be taken apart.** Owner asked *"how many time he/she was active, how the day is calculated, hour breakup user wise day wise."* None of it was answerable, and not for want of a screen: `AttendanceDay.workedMinutes` is a running TOTAL, and a total cannot be taken apart again — 8h 10m says nothing about whether it was one stretch or five, when they were, or which till. **The table** is `AttendanceSpan` ([`hr-attendance.prisma`](prisma/schema/hr-attendance.prisma), migration `20260923010000_attendance_spans`, purely additive): one row per ACTIVE STRETCH, with `startedAt` / `endedAt` / `minutes` / `counterId` / `endedBy` (`OPEN` · `IDLE` · `SIGNED_OUT` · `SHIFT_CLOSED`). **The rule is not a new one** — it is `creditableMinutes` said out loud in [`attendanceSpans.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/attendanceSpans.ts): each sign of life extends the open stretch, a gap beyond the person's idle limit closes it at its last sign of life and opens a fresh one. The idle gap is stored nowhere, because it is not time; the breakup reads it as the difference between two spans. **The day row is untouched** and still what the register, the grading and payroll read — the spans are the ledger behind that balance and only ever explain it, which is why a missed span cannot break anything that works today. Written second, errors swallowed, in all three stamping paths ([`attendanceStamp.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/attendanceStamp.ts)). **The screen** is [`HoursBreakupDialog`](src/modules/hr/components/HoursBreakupDialog.tsx), opened by clicking a name in the register: a line per day carrying **Times** (the active count), duty, worked, away, late and over/short, with the month totalled at the foot, and any line opening the stretches it was added up from plus the sum said out loud. An office override shows the clock's own answer beside it — an override that hides what it overrode is how a dispute becomes unanswerable. **No back-fill**, deliberately: a span invented from an old `firstInAt`/`lastOutAt` would read exactly like a real one, so days before this say *"not tracked in detail"* rather than showing a zero that looks like an absence. Read side: [`attendanceBreakup.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceBreakup.ts) (pure arithmetic), [`attendanceHoursServer.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceHoursServer.ts), `GET /api/hr/attendance/hours?userId=&from=&to=`. Still open: printing the sheet through the print template, and `TRACK_OFF_COUNTER` is still false so only tills are measured.

- **2026-09-22 — the salary slip prints, and a holiday can belong to one branch.** Owner asked whether HR & People was complete; the audit found the module built end to end with two loose ends, both closed here. **(1) The printable slip.** The Payslips tab said *"a printable slip comes later"* and no `PAYSLIP` kind existed, against AGENTS.md §2. Now `PAYSLIP` is a kind in the print-template registry (`HR_DOCUMENT_KINDS`, group *HR & People* on the Print Templates page and its gallery, A4 by default, signature on, no tax / bank / party-balance rows), rendered by [`PayslipBody`](src/shared/components/print/PayslipBody.tsx) — person, period, days paid, earnings beside deductions, net in words — inside the shared letterhead chrome. The page is [`/receipt/payslip/[lineId]`](src/app/receipt/payslip/[lineId]/page.tsx): one POSTED `PayrollRunLine`, gated exactly like the payslips API (the person themselves or `hr:staff:edit`), every figure the frozen line. Browser print only — the hardware bridge has no salary-slip middle, so no silent thermal path is offered. The printer button on a Payslips row (staff profile and My Info alike) opens it. The template row is created on first save (the settings API upserts) and the personal snapshot carries it. **(2) Branch holidays.** `Holiday.branchId` was stored and never read, and the form could not set it. One rule now, `holidayApplies` in [`attendanceCalendar.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceCalendar.ts): a holiday with no branch is everybody's, one with a branch belongs to the people holding a seat there (`UserPosition → Position.branchId`, read through [`staffBranchesServer.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/staffBranchesServer.ts)). Applied in the register (per person), My Info, the payroll rules (holiday-rate minutes) and the counter's own next-opening line; the login screen's shop-wide answer keeps to org-wide holidays only. The Calendar page shows a branch picker when the shop has more than one branch and a *For* column always. Verified by tsc + lint and the public calendar route; the signed-in screens still need the owner's browser pass.
- **2026-09-22 — The day is accounted, on the card and on the slip.** Owner: *"the time has an allotment % — normal day @1, overtime @1.5, late penalty real late × x = accounted penalty time … then we sum to get the day total; I need everything in detail"*, then *"also show it in the payslip print."* **The arithmetic** is one pure function, `accountDay` in [`attendanceBreakup.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceBreakup.ts): from the figures payroll already produced it puts a rate beside each line — normal duty ×1, overtime ×`overtimeRate`, the late cut under the rule in force, short of duty ×`shortHoursRate` (a half day's already-withheld half excluded, an absent day not charged twice), or on a shut day the whole of it at the weekly-off / holiday rate — and sums them to the day's total. Nothing is re-measured, so the card and the payslip cannot disagree. **The card** ([`DayActivityCard`](src/modules/hr/components/DayActivityCard.tsx)) shows the table under "How the day is accounted", a nought line kept visible in grey so a free late day is seen being spent; the summary strip adds *Accounted, before netting* beside the netted Overtime payable / Hours owed. **The slip**: the month is frozen on the payroll line as `PayrollRunLine.attendanceJson` (`PayslipAttendanceData`, built by `freezeAttendanceForPayslip` at generation from the same `loadHoursBreakup` read the activity screen makes — one source for card, pay heads and slip). Frozen, not recomputed at print, because a slip must say the same thing in ten years and a day edited later must not change one already handed out; a line from before this carries NULL and prints without the section. [`PayslipBody`](src/shared/components/print/PayslipBody.tsx) prints it under the pay heads as *Attendance & hours* — SUMMARY lines, not days (owner: *"30 days is big to fit … write summary style, x days late, penalty ×1"*): duty worked, overtime, late (charged minutes × rate, free days named), short of duty, half day, absent, weekly off / holiday worked — each a count of days, the minutes × rate, and the accounted result, adding up to the month row, then one line on how the netting reached the Overtime / Hours owed heads. The day-by-day cards moved to the person's own **My Info** page (*My activity*, the month picked above, `AttendanceBreakup` fixed to the signed-in id), and `GET /api/hr/attendance/hours` is now gated like the payslips route — the person themselves or `hr:staff:edit` — where before any signed-in user could read anyone's days. Customisable through the Salary Slip print template: `showAttendance` / `showAttendanceThermal` (on by default), a *Salary slip* fieldset on the Print Templates page, the preview fed a sample week. Migration `20260923020000_payslip_attendance`, additive — a NEW file, because `20260923010000` was already applied locally and an applied migration is frozen. **Still open**: the browser pass on the template preview and a real posted slip needs the migration on the local copy first (the agent cannot run `db:migrate`).

- **2026-09-22 — Pay Rules: the clock now reaches the payslip.** Owner asked for *"a tab to define payment structure, like over time hourly cost 1.5x, weekend attendance price, late joining penalty etc."*, then *"make it 1.5 instead of 150"*, then a time-based punishment — *"reduce hour of the day by late minute x penalty, 5 minute late x 2"* — *"not flat %, its late dependent"*, and finally *"absent without granted leave penalty, minimum leave application time before the leave day"*. **The screen** is [`PayRulesCard`](src/modules/hr/components/PayRulesCard.tsx) at `/office/hr/pay-rules`, a new HR sidebar link (`ui:sidebar:hr:payRules`, no seed needed) gated by the existing `hr:attendance:edit`: five groups — Overtime, Weekly off & holidays, Absence & leave notice, Late arrival, and how a charged late day is punished. **Multiples, never rupees** — a basic is per person and lives on the staff record, so one rule fits every salary and a revision changes nothing here; every box carries a sentence underneath that rewrites itself as the number changes, and each box saves itself 600ms after the typing stops (the whole rule set every time, so a half-saved set is impossible). **The rules** live in [`payRules.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/payRules.ts) — shape, defaults (`1.5` overtime, `2` weekly off and holiday, `1` day for an unasked absence, 15 minutes' grace, 2 free late days, `3` days of leave notice) and the two pieces of arithmetic — stored as one JSON blob on `OrgDefaults.hrPayRulesJson`, migration `20260922234500_hr_pay_rules`, read defensively through `parsePayRules` so a row saved before a rule existed answers with the code default rather than putting `undefined` on a payslip. **The late penalty is a choice of five, never a sum**: nothing, days of basic off the payslip, the lateness counted N times over, N% of the day's duty for every M minutes late (a part slab counts whole — rounding down would make the first minutes of every slab free), or a flat N% of the day. Money OR time, because both for one late day is two punishments for one offence. **Time is the lighter hand and that is the point**: it does not touch the payslip, it shortens the duty the day is credited with, so the shortfall joins his own short hours, his overtime pays it back first, and only what is still owed at month end meets `shortHoursRate`. **The payroll wiring** is the real change. [`payRulesApply.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/payRulesApply.ts) counts what one person's clock did over the period — surplus netted against shortfall exactly as [`monthlyOvertime.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/monthlyOvertime.ts) does it (so the counter's own card and his payslip are arrived at the same way), minutes on a weekly off or a listed holiday kept out of the netting and paid at their own rate, late days charged oldest-first after the free ones, and absences with no approved leave counted — and turns it into ordinary `SalaryLine`s (**Overtime**, **Weekly off duty**, **Holiday duty** earnings; **Hours owed**, **Late penalty**, **Absence penalty** deductions), worked off his **Basic** head alone, falling back to all earnings when no head is named Basic. Only days the **till** wrote are judged on their minutes; a day the office marked by hand keeps its mark, here as everywhere. **`nonPayableDays()` is finally connected**: `prorate()` takes a `dayFactors` map, so an absence or an unpaid leave day is no longer paid — the seam written in phase 3 and left dangling since 2026-09-20 (*"not yet wired into payrollProrate.ts"*). `paidDays` is now fractional when a half day falls in the period. **The leave notice reads the rule too** — `LEAVE_NOTICE_DAYS` becomes the fallback, the API takes it from `loadPayRules()` and the Leave dialog from `getPayRules()`, closing the *"when that card becomes savable"* note left in `hrAttendanceSchema.ts` hours earlier. `lateMinutes()` gained a grace parameter for the same reason. **Not browser-verified past the screen**: the running dev server holds a Prisma client generated before the column existed, so every save answers 500 until it is restarted; `tsc`, `eslint` and the migration ran clean and the column is in the local copy. **Nothing has been run against live payroll** — the next generated run is the first that will carry these heads, and it should be previewed against a known month before it is posted.

- **2026-09-22 — the quota is now wired: warned when asking, shown when deciding, cut when paying.** The table from the entry below stopped being a number nobody read. **Asking** — the Request leave box shows *"Casual: 8 of 12 left this year (4 taken, 2 awaiting a decision)"* for whoever the leave is for (HR filing for someone else reads THAT person's balance, not their own), and going past the quota shows an amber line saying by how much and that those days will be unpaid. It **does not block** and the Save stays live, which is the owner's call. **Deciding** — approving fetches the person's balance for that moment and puts it in the confirmation: *"Casual: 10 of 12 used — this takes it to 14, so 2 days will be unpaid."* Fetched on the click rather than held for every row, and a failed fetch never gates the decision. **Paying** — [`unpaidLeaveDaysFor`](src/modules/hr/lib/leaveBalance.ts) is now the single place that answers "is this leave day paid?", for two reasons: the kind is not a paid one, or the day is **past that kind's yearly quota**. Which days are over is settled **by date** — the leave year's approved days of that kind laid out in order, everything after the quota is the part over it — so a person keeps the twelve he spent and only the thirteenth onwards is cut. [`nonPayableDays`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendancePayable.ts) dropped its own `kind: "UNPAID"` query and reads that helper, so payroll and the two screens cannot disagree about which day was the thirteenth. **My Info** grows a balance strip above the leave list, in the leave year of the month being looked at — paging back to last year shows last year's balance — carried on the page's one existing read rather than a second call. **Behaviour change worth knowing**: before today only an `UNPAID` request cut pay; from now an over-quota casual or sick day does too. **Two known wrinkles, not fixed here**: a weekly off or shop holiday falling inside a leave range still counts as a leave day (approval writes `LEAVE` over it, `days` counts calendar days), so it spends quota and can be cut — the fix belongs with `applyLeaveDecision`, and changing it in one place only would make the board and the quota disagree. And there is still no half-day leave. **Not browser-verified**: the dev session signed out when the Prisma client regenerated and the agent may not sign in; `tsc` and `eslint` clean.

- **2026-09-22 — leave types become master data, with a quota you can set.** Owner: *"how many leave each type? where to set?"*. The answer was **no limit on any of them, and nowhere** — the four kinds were a hardcoded list ([`hrAttendanceSchema.ts`](src/shared/lib/schemas/hrAttendanceSchema.ts)), nothing counted them, and Casual differed from Earned only by the word printed in the Kind column. New table [`LeaveType`](prisma/schema/hr-attendance.prisma) (migration `20260922230000_leave_types`): `code` — what `LeaveRequest.kind` already stores, so every filed request stays valid and the code is deliberately **not** editable — plus name, paid, `yearlyQuota`, `noticeDays`, and `carryForward` / `carryCap` sitting unused until the carry-forward policy is settled. Seeded **Casual 12, Sick 12, Earned no limit, Unpaid no limit** by [`ensure-leave-types.ts`](prisma/seeds/ensure-leave-types.ts), which **creates a missing type and never touches one that is there** — the quota is the owner's number from the moment he first changes it, and a reseed that reasserted 12 would quietly undo him. **Where to set it**: a new [`LeaveTypesCard`](src/modules/hr/components/LeaveTypesCard.tsx) on **Calendar & Hours**, beside the shop hours and the holiday list — the three standing calendar rules read together, under the one grant (`hr:attendance:edit`) that already owns them. An empty days box means no limit. **The balance is computed, never stored** ([`leaveBalance.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/leaveBalance.ts)): approved days inside the leave year, pending shown separately so a person can see his own outstanding ask, a request straddling 31 March split at the boundary, and the leave year running **1 April → 31 March** like the books. Read by [`GET /api/hr/leave/balance`](src/app/api/hr/leave/balance/route.ts) — own balance needs no grant, somebody else's needs `hr:leave:approve` — and the types by [`/api/hr/leave-types`](src/app/api/hr/leave-types/route.ts), default-open to read because the apply box has to be able to say what the quota is. **Owner's three calls, 2026-09-22**: going over a quota **warns and does not block** (HR still decides; the days past the quota are the ones payroll should treat as unpaid), **earned leave keeps no limit** until he settles the policy, and **carry-forward stays off** for every type. **Not yet wired**: the warning in the apply box, the balance on the Leave board and on My Info, and payroll treating over-quota days as unpaid — all four touch `LeaveBoard.tsx`, `api/hr/leave/route.ts`, `hrAttendanceSchema.ts` and `MyHrPage.tsx`, which a parallel session has open with uncommitted changes. **Not browser-verified**: the dev session signed out when the Prisma client regenerated, and the agent may not sign in; `tsc`, `eslint`, the migration and the seed all ran clean, and the four types are in the local copy.

- **2026-09-22 — "My Info": the staff member's own page, on one tick.** Owner: *"the hr module will have a page where user will see his own data, apply leave — user will have only one page permission not full hr module"*. Built on the gate fix in the entry below, so the tick `ui:sidebar:hr:me` opens this page **and nothing else in HR**. [`/office/hr/me`](src/app/(protected)/office/hr/me/page.tsx) → [`MyHrPage`](src/modules/hr/components/MyHrPage.tsx): four figures across the top (today's reading and hours worked, the month's days so far, extra time after the make-up, days lost), the person's **own month grid** day by day with the arrows stopping at the current month, **own leave** with a Request leave button, **own payslips**, and **the rules that apply to him** — duty hours, what counts as a full day, weekly off, joining date and this month's shop holidays. One read, [`GET /api/hr/me?month=YYYY-MM`](src/app/api/hr/me/route.ts), with **no permission tick and none missing**: it answers for the signed-in user only and there is deliberately no way to ask it about somebody else — the reasoning `/api/hr/my-day` and `/api/hr/activity` already set. It returns the register's own `RegisterPerson`, so the page reads a day through the very same [`readDay` / `summarise`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceCalendar.ts) the office register uses and the two surfaces cannot tell different stories about the same day; the cell colours moved to [`attendanceStyles.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceStyles.ts) so green means the same thing on both. Payslips reuse [`StaffPayslips`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffPayslips.tsx) against the route that already allows self, and the apply box is the Leave board's own dialog, now exported rather than copied. **A privacy hole closed on the way**: `GET /api/hr/leave` returned **every** request to anyone who could open the board, so a person ticked for one HR page could read a colleague's leave dates and reason — it now returns your own unless you hold `hr:leave:approve`. **No schema, no migration, no seed** — a `ui:sidebar:*` link mints its row on the first Departments / Role save. **Not browser-verified**: the page needs a sign-in the agent may not perform; `tsc` and `eslint` clean. Still open, and deliberately not built: **CL / EL balances** (no quota, no accrual, no carry-forward — every kind but Unpaid is paid without limit today), **half-day leave**, leave kinds as master data, the day count that charges a weekly off or holiday falling inside a leave range, a **target** section (no target or incentive exists anywhere in the schema), and any way for a counter cashier to reach this page from the till.

- **2026-09-22 — HR is ticked page by page, and the tick is now enforced at the door.** Owner, asking for one HR page where a person sees only their own data and applies for leave: *"user will have only one page permission not full hr module"*, and, when told that was not possible: *"in role page we suppose to have page wise tick like other module"* — which was right. The six ticks (`ui:sidebar:hr:staff` / `:attendance` / `:leave` / `:holidays` / `:payRules` / `:payroll`) already existed in the Role matrix and the sidebar already hid the untickeds. **The enforcement was module-wide**: every HR page and the area layout asked `hasModuleNavAccess(permissions, "hr")` — *"do you hold **any** HR link?"* — so one tick opened all six to anyone who typed the URL, payroll included. Fixed the way Customer Retention already does it: a **per-page `layout.tsx`** using the existing [`makePermissionLayout`](src/shared/lib/auth/makePermissionLayout.tsx) + [`sidebarChildPermissionId`](src/shared/lib/auth/sidebarModules.ts), one per HR page, each demanding its own tick. The area layout keeps the module check as the **outer door** ("do you belong in HR at all"), each page's layout is the **inner lock**, and the pages keep only their capability flags (`hr:staff:edit`, `hr:attendance:edit`, `hr:leave:approve`, `hr:payroll:run`) — the now-dead module redirect is gone from all eight of them. **The landing had to follow**: `/office/hr` redirected to Staff Directory, which would bounce anyone ticked only for Leave straight back out, so it now picks the first page the person actually holds, in sidebar order, and the module's `dashboardHref` points at `/office/hr` instead of a fixed page. **What changes for existing people:** nobody loses a page they were ticked for; a person loses URL access to HR pages they were never ticked for — which is the point. Admin bypass unaffected. **No schema, no migration, no seed** — the six tick rows are already minted by `sidebarPermissionSeeds()`. Same module-wide gap still stands in accountancy, BI, CRM, e-commerce and administration; deliberately untouched, as those are working screens and a separate call. `tsc` and `eslint` clean. **Not browser-verified** — the pages need a sign-in the agent may not perform. Next in this line: the self-service page itself (`/office/hr/me`, new tick `ui:sidebar:hr:me`) showing own day and hours, own attendance, own leave with apply, own payslips and own earnings, on `/api/hr/me/*` routes that never accept a userId — the shape `/api/hr/my-day` already set.

- **2026-09-22 — salary is for 26 days, and a day off worked is paid in hours, not in days.** Owner: *"the salary for 26 days. if work on weekend it gives extra for the day"*, then the divisor itself: *"salary is decided for 26days as per gvt. rule, now feb and January salary will be difference"*, and finally how the extra is priced: *"we have the hourly price from the (monthly salary/26)/stander office hour, we sum the hours and than calculate the payment. its not per day"*. Payroll used to divide a monthly head by the length of the calendar month, which made every full month pay the same. It now divides by the revision's own **`payDays`** — 26 by default, the government month, editable on the salary dialog because a contract may say otherwise — and pays for the **working days** in the period, a weekly off no longer being a paid day of its own (it is already inside the 26). On ₹16,000 with Sunday off that is ₹615.38 a day: January 2026 (27 working days) pays ₹16,615.38, February (24) pays ₹14,769.23, and a 26-working-day month lands back on exactly ₹16,000 — all three checked against the code. The extra for a day off is deliberately **not** a day: its minutes go into the month's overtime pot ([`monthlyOvertime.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/monthlyOvertime.ts)) as pure surplus — an off day owes no duty, so it can never be short, half or absent — the pot first pays back the hours fallen short on other days, and only what survives is money, at `(gross / payDays) / duty hours`. Four hours on a Sunday against five hours short elsewhere therefore pays **nothing**, which is the owner's *"extra day attendance not always extra payment"*. Schema: `StaffSalary.payDays` and `PayrollRunLine.payDays`, both defaulting to 26, migration `20260922200000_salary_pay_days`; existing revisions are backfilled to 26 and posted runs are frozen, so nothing already paid moves. UI: a **Paid days a month** box on the salary revision and appointment dialogs, showing the per-day and per-hour rate live ([`PayDaysField`](src/modules/hr/components/PayDaysField.tsx)); the payroll sheet and payslips now read "22 of 30 · ÷26". **Not yet wired to money:** the overtime hours are still only a figure on screen — payroll does not add them to a run, pending the owner's word.

- **2026-09-21 — Group Report: the Cash Book layout, with the account group as the unit.** Owner, with the Cash Book screen open: *"account group with similar view, in the list i need group"*, then *"name Group Report, list pan all groups. in main like cash book"*. New screen at `/office/accountancy/group-report` — [`GroupReportExplorer`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GroupReportExplorer.tsx), sitting under **Loans Book** in the Accountancy sidebar. **Left panel**: all 35 groups of the chart, indented the way they nest, searchable by group or parent name, each showing its rolled-up closing with a Dr/Cr suffix. **Right pane, nothing picked**: every group with Opening / Debit / Credit / Closing for the period, primary groups in bold and sub-groups indented under them, the count of ledgers beside each name. **A group picked**: its sub-groups as coloured header rows carrying their own totals, then its own ledgers — a ledger row drills into the existing register at `/office/accountancy/account-ledgers`. **No new API and no query-per-group**: `/api/accountancy/reports/trial-balance` already returns the group tree with `rolledUp*` figures, so one read feeds the whole screen, and the numbers agree with Trial Balance by construction — the same signing rule (amount signed against the group's own nature, the Dr/Cr label taken from the sign) is reused, so a contra ledger still reads correctly. **The all-groups Total row deliberately totals only Debit and Credit**: Opening and Closing there are Dr and Cr amounts belonging to different groups, and a single number would be meaningless — Trial Balance is the screen that splits them by side. A picked group does total its own Opening and Closing, since one group has one side. Follows the floor on the `ledger` topic without blanking the table, and Escape steps back from a group to the list. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap. Browser-verified on live data: 35 groups listed, Debit and Credit totals balancing at 11,492,572.97, Cash-in-Hand drilling to its six ledgers at 99,891.66 Dr and Loans (Liability) showing its three sub-groups with sub-totals, console clean.

- **2026-09-21 — the identity card looks the same on every surface, and now leaves the building as press artwork.** Owner, with three screenshots of the same card side by side: *"inconsistent design, some places the logo is not visible"*, then *"it should be same every where, and need a preview popup modal so we see quick big size, two print button no need, GIVE high quality image download so it can be print on pvc card. it need to supply painting press in image"*. **Two faults, one root each.** The printed card lost its blue strip because nothing in the app had ever set `print-color-adjust`, and a browser drops background colour when printing — so the white lettering on that strip printed white on white. And the wordmark, a single `#0133ff` on a transparent ground, sat straight on a `#1d4ed8 → #3b82f6` strip: invisible on screen, and only appearing in the print preview *because* the strip had gone white. Both are fixed at the root in [`StaffIdCard`](src/shared/components/print/StaffIdCard.tsx) — `print-color-adjust: exact` over the whole card, and the wordmark on a **white chip**, the same treatment [`login.module.css`](src/app/staff/login/login.module.css) already gives it over a dark panel. The brand name beside the wordmark is gone: the wordmark *is* the name, which is the rule the sign-in pages already state, and the tagline stays. **The tab** ([`StaffIdCardTab`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffIdCardTab.tsx)) drops to **one** Print button (it always opened the sheet *and* raised the print dialog, so “open the sheet” was never a second thing to want), and the card is now clickable — an **enlarge dialog** at 2.6× via `zoom`, held to one card's width so the component's own flex-wrap puts the back under the front and both are readable whole. Nothing is re-laid-out, so the big card cannot drift from the printed one. **Press artwork**: new [`GET /api/hr/staff/[userId]/id-card-image`](src/app/api/hr/staff/%5BuserId%5D/id-card-image/route.tsx) renders each side with Satori (`next/og`) exactly as a gift card's PNG already is — **2117 × 1370 px, 85.6 × 54 mm at 600 dpi with 2 mm bleed**, no border and no rounded corners, because the blank card is already cut and the blue must run past the trim. `dpi` and `bleed` are query knobs, clamped. One button saves both sides, and the file name carries the size, dpi and bleed so the press knows what it is holding. `next/og` ships only Geist **Regular**, so every bold line came out at normal weight — the name on an ID card no heavier than the tagline; the route now loads **Arimo 400 + 700** (metric-compatible with the Arial the screen card asks for), once per process, and draws with the built-in face if it cannot be fetched rather than failing to print. Satori keeps its own layout — the house rule — but every size goes through one `mm()` helper holding the stylesheet's own numbers. No schema, seed or permission change. **Browser-verified**: the wordmark reads on the blue strip, the dialog shows both sides whole, `print-color-adjust` computes as `exact` on the card and the strip, and both PNGs were rendered and eyeballed at the right pixel size with real bold. **Not verified by the agent — the actual print preview and a real card off the press need the owner's eyes.**

- **2026-09-21 — clearing a Personal field is an edit again.** Owner, on the HR staff profile: *"why not saving ?"*. The **Personal** tab ([`StaffPersonalForm`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffPersonalForm.tsx)) compared every blur against the record fetched when the tab opened, and that fetch is never refreshed after a save — so each field's “before” stayed frozen at page-load for the whole visit. Fill a box that was empty when the page opened, then change your mind and clear it: the empty box matched the stale original, Pattern D's rule 1 (*a tab-through is not an edit*, [`useFieldSave`](src/shared/hooks/useFieldSave.ts)) skipped the write, and the value stayed in the database with **no tick and no toast** to say the clear had been dropped. Only a page reload made clearing work. The PATCH already hands the whole record back, so the baseline now moves on with every save at no extra GET, tagged with the fetch it was saved over so the bank block's `reload` still wins. The box is also put in step with what was actually stored — a PAN comes back upper-cased — unless the user has already clicked back in, so a slow save cannot overwrite live typing; that also stops the next tab-through counting as an edit. The other Pattern D screen ([`StaffJobTab`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffJobTab.tsx)) never had this — it refreshes the parent through `onSaved: onChanged`. No schema, seed, API or permission change. **Browser-verified against the live record** (staff #7, every probe value removed again): type-then-clear lands both ways on an input and a textarea, a lower-case PAN stores upper-cased, tabbing through unchanged sends nothing, and a malformed PAN still reverts with its error.

- **2026-09-20 — the Recycle Bin now says which copy a row is, and deletes as far as that copy deserves.** Straight from the owner, reading his own bin: *"first give indication delete snapshot and edit snap shot. delete edit snap shot will restrict to restore before edit state. delete, delete snapshot will remove it completely all snapshot"*. Two very different things land in `AuditLog` and nothing on screen told them apart — the copy left when a voucher was **deleted** (its last surviving copy) and the pre-edit copy left when a voucher was **edited in place** and is still alive under the same number. Both said only "Restore", and two rows reading `RV-10795-26` were indistinguishable. Now each row carries a badge — **Deleted voucher** or **Pre-edit copy** — and the permanent delete reaches accordingly: destroying a pre-edit copy takes that one row and costs only the ability to go back to that older version, while destroying a deleted voucher's copy **deletes the voucher completely**, every copy of it in one act (`deleteMany` on entity type + `originalId`, which both the edit and the hard-delete writers stamp from the voucher's own id — the reference is not the identity, a draft carries a placeholder one). Only then is the number offered back, which is what makes the "last copy" rule of the release honest. The armed button and the resting tooltip both say which of the two is about to happen. New snapshots are stamped `action: "PRE_EDIT"` at both edit routes; everything written before today defaulted to `HARD_DELETED` whatever it was, so [`auditSnapshotKind`](src/shared/lib/accountancy/auditSnapshotKind.ts) falls back to the `"Edit (in-place) of …"` reason line the edit routes have always written — **the trail is read, never rewritten**, since tidying up evidence after the fact is exactly what an audit log must not do. Note this also clears a deleted voucher's already-restored history rows, which is what "completely" means. Super-admin-only as before; no schema, seed or permission change. **Not browser-verified by the agent** — the page needs a super-admin sign-in it may not perform.

- **2026-09-20 — a purged voucher gives its number back, when it was the last one.** Owner: *"in recycle page let delete the voucher if this is the last voucher in the voucher kind, release the voucher no for next fresh create. applicable to all kind"*. Numbering only ever moved forward — `nextRefFromSeries` post-increments the `(kind, counter)` row and nothing walked it back — so every recycle-bin delete left a permanent hole in the book. That is still right while the snapshot exists, because `auditRestore` puts the voucher back under its **original** number, which must stay reserved; the one moment the number is genuinely free is when the snapshot itself is destroyed. So [`releaseSeriesNumber`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/releaseSeriesNumber.ts) now runs inside `DELETE /api/audit/[id]`, in the **same transaction** that makes restore impossible. "Was it the last one?" is answered **by construction, not by parsing**: for each counter row of that series, rebuild the reference it *would* print at its current `nextSeq` using the very same `assembleReference` the allocator uses, and look for an exact string match. A tip match steps that row back one (mirrored onto the legacy `VoucherSeries.nextSeq` for counter 1, exactly as the allocator does); a mid-run number matches nothing and the series is left alone, so the book never renumbers itself behind anyone's back. **Two refusals keep it honest**, both found against the real bin: a number still printed on a living voucher is never released (an in-place edit parks its pre-edit copy in the bin while the voucher carries on under the same reference), and neither is one that any surviving `AuditLog` row still names — one reference commonly has several rows there (an edit each, then the delete), and while any of them lives a restore can put the voucher back under that number. Legacy `JOURNAL_ENTRY` rows carry no subtype and are mapped to the JOURNAL series rather than skipped. **All kinds**: a GL voucher type *is* its series kind, and inventory types route through the allocator's own `SERIES_KINDS` map (now exported from [`stock.ts`](src/modules/inventory/lib/stock.ts) instead of copied), which by construction excludes the legacy prefix kinds — POS_ESTIMATE, POS_ORDER and friends count their own surviving rows and already re-use a freed number by themselves. **All counters, on the owner's explicit call, duplicate-bill-number risk accepted:** the usual ownership rule (only the machine that numbers a row writes it) is knowingly relaxed, so an office purge that rolls back an offline till's tip can land on top of that till's queued increments and issue one bill number twice. The Recycle Bin toast says *"number released for re-use"* only when it happened, and the server log line carries it too. Super-admin-only as before; no schema, seed or permission change. **Not browser-verified** — exercising it means permanently destroying a real voucher on the live books.

- **2026-09-22 — the counter hand can now see his own day.** Owner: *"display the computed duty time on logout for those who logged in to the counter, it's counter wise so the counter user can assess his own work"*. Every figure was already being measured and shown only in the office register, so the one person who could not see the grade was the person being graded. On a counter sign-out — deliberate from the sidebar, or the idle auto-logout — the day is read while the session is still good, stashed in `sessionStorage`, and drawn on the login screen by [`TillCard`](src/app/staff/login/TillCard.tsx) (then `DutyCard`): hours worked, overtime, time away during duty, late, and the verdict (full / half / not counted, saying so when the office has overruled it). **On the login screen, not in a dialog** — the common way a till session ends is the idle logout, where nobody is standing there to read a dialog and the next person would click it away blind; this way the deliberate sign-out reads it on the way out and the idle one reads it on returning. Shown once, then taken. **Two new figures in the rule** ([`dutyTime.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/dutyTime.ts)): `overtimeMinutes` = worked above his own duty MINUTES (not after the duty-end clock, which would pay a latecomer for finishing late), and `shortMinutes`, its mirror — a day is over or short, never both. **The month nets** ([`monthlyOvertime.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/monthlyOvertime.ts)), owner: *"monthly overtime is after adjusting the full month — 3 days has less duty total 5h, user has to make up 5h by overtime; additional overtime of the month attracts extra payment"*. Surplus first pays back the shortfall; only the remainder is payable. A short day must not be docked twice, so the shortfall counted is only what payroll has **not** already withheld — an ABSENT day contributes nothing (paid at zero) and a HALF_DAY only what is beyond the half already taken by [`nonPayableDays()`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendancePayable.ts). **Nothing is paid out yet**: `payableMinutes` is a figure on a screen until payroll is wired to it deliberately. One new route, [`/api/hr/my-day`](src/app/api/hr/my-day/route.ts) — no permission tick and none missing: it answers for the signed-in user only, takes no `userId`, and off a counter returns `tracked: false` so an office user is never shown a confident zero. No schema change. **The card is not browser-verified**: the endpoint was driven signed-in on the dev server and correctly answered `tracked: false` off a counter, but the counter branch and the card itself need a real till. A gap left open on purpose: the **suggested next time to start duty** the owner asked for has a marked slot in the card and no rule behind it yet — the attendance session confirmed it was never asked to build one.

- **2026-09-22 — the shop opens with the sun, and the till says when to come back.** Owner asked for office hours that follow the season — *"give exact (calculate every day)"*, *"give winter and summer office hour"*, *"give global, our wise override"* — and for the sign-out screen to name the next office day and its opening, including when tomorrow is a weekend or a holiday. **The problem is real and local**: Guwahati's sunrise moves about 95 minutes between June and January while IST does not move at all, so a single fixed opening is wrong for half the year in each direction. **The curve, not slabs** ([`seasonalHours.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/seasonalHours.ts)): one opening per solstice on `OrgDefaults`, every other day interpolated on a cosine — flat near the solstices, steepest at the equinoxes, the way sunrise itself moves, so the time shifts about a minute every three days and nobody is made late by a rule that jumped overnight. **Exact to the minute** on the owner's call; rounding to fifteen would settle into four slabs a year and throw the daily calculation away. **The closing does not move** (also his call), so a winter day is genuinely shorter — 9h15m against 10h on a 10:30/11:15 pair. **One seam did the whole job**: `dutyHoursFor(start, end, opts?)` was already the single funnel the attendance API, the register, payroll, `dutyMinutesFor` and the staff editor all pass through, so widening it with `{ on, seasonal }` carried the season everywhere at once; calls that pass nothing behave exactly as before, and both anchors default to the old `10:30`, so **the migration is behaviour-neutral until somebody sets a winter time**. A person's own `StaffProfile.dutyStart` still wins outright — a fixed shift does not follow the sun. **Grading needed no new rule**: `dutyMinutesFor` reads the *span* of the hours, so the 75% / 40% lines shrink with the winter day by themselves and there is no second number to keep in step — but the register and `nonPayableDays()` now resolve **per day** rather than once per request, which is what stops a winter month reading as a fortnight of half days. **The sign-out answer is deliberately two answers**, because `/staff/login` has no session at all: the *personal* one fills the slot the duty-card session left in [`TillCard`](src/app/staff/login/TillCard.tsx) (then `DutyCard`) — his own weekly off and the holidays taken out, computed behind the auth in `/api/hr/my-day` — and the *shop-wide* one ([`NextOpening`](src/app/staff/login/NextOpening.tsx), new unauthenticated [`GET /api/office-hours/next`](src/app/api/office-hours/next/route.ts)) renders **nothing at all when the shop is open today**, so the two can never print the same advice twice. Both go through one builder ([`nextOpeningText.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/nextOpeningText.ts)) with a `shop` / `mine` voice, and the closure sentence anchors on the **first** closed day — anchoring on the named holiday said *"Closed Saturday for Durga Puja"* on a Thursday whose Friday was already a weekly off, true about Saturday and silent about tomorrow. **Holidays is now Calendar & Hours** and holds the globals ([`GlobalHoursCard`](src/modules/hr/components/GlobalHoursCard.tsx)) with a live preview of today's opening and both extremes — without it an exact-to-the-minute rule is a mystery until the day arrives. Four new `OrgDefaults` columns (`dutySummerStart`, `dutyWinterStart`, `dutyEnd`, `weeklyOff`), migration `20260922180000_seasonal_office_hours`; the table already replicates (registry no. 53), so tills get it offline. Left out of the seed's `update` **and** its `create` on purpose, so a reseed cannot reset the shop's opening. `DEFAULT_DUTY_START`/`END` and `parseHhMm`/`formatHhMm` moved to `seasonalHours.ts` and [`hhmm.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/hhmm.ts) to break an import cycle — both modules build constants at load time, so a cycle here crashes on import rather than misbehaving — and are re-exported from `dutyTime.ts`, so no import site changed. **The migration is committed but NOT applied anywhere** — `prisma migrate deploy` is blocked by this session's permission classifier — so the columns do not yet exist in any database and the screens will 500 until the owner runs it. **Not browser-verified** for the same reason.

- **2026-09-22 — duty time: the day is measured, not assumed.** Attendance said PRESENT and nothing else, so twenty minutes at the counter and a ten-hour shift were the same day and payroll paid both in full. Three findings drove it. **(1) The arrival was hanging off the wrong event.** The stamp ran only inside `authorizeStaffCredentials`, and sessions were JWT with no `maxAge` — Auth.js's 30 days, rolling — so a cashier who never signed out authenticated about once a month. On live, user 38 signed in on 19 Sep, worked the 20th and 21st, and the 21st was stored with an out time at 15:09 and no arrival at all. It was **not** a misconfigured till: `stampCounterPinLogin` is gated by the same `thisCounterId()` and that machine's PIN row updated fine. So opening a shift now stamps the arrival too — a daily event that reads its counter from the database and cannot be misconfigured into silence — and [`backfill-attendance-arrival.sql`](scripts/backfill-attendance-arrival.sql) repaired the one live row (watch the timezone: `openedAt` is `timestamp without time zone` holding UTC, so a bare `AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata'` converts the wrong way). **(2) A session now ends after 6 idle hours** ([`sessionAge.ts`](src/shared/lib/auth/sessionAge.ts), `updateAge: 0` so it measures from last use), which puts the PIN screen back every morning; a session that dies of idleness says so instead of bouncing the cashier to a blank login, and the deliberate sign-outs flag themselves so only the unexplained ones speak. **(3) Working time is now counted.** Owner's model: the idle stretch is not duty, and the day is the active spans added up. [`ActivityPulse`](src/shared/components/ActivityPulse.tsx) pings `/api/hr/activity` every 4 minutes **only when a real click, key or touch happened since the last one** — the condition is the whole design, because the app already re-reads data every 5 minutes on its own and a machine left switched on would otherwise report a full day to an empty shop. The server adds the gap since the last sign of life and **drops any gap longer than the idle limit**, so the idle time contributes nothing whether or not the auto-logout fired. Full / half / absent is a fraction of the person's **own** duty (75% / 40% of `StaffProfile.dutyMinutes`, default 9h read off the shop's real shifts of 8h50m–10h47m), so a part-timer who works their four hours has done a full day. Graded at **read** time, never frozen, so a cashier mid-shift is not stamped with a half day all afternoon — and [`nonPayableDays()`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendancePayable.ts) grades through the same `gradeDay`, because the screen and the salary must never disagree. **A manual office mark always wins and is never re-graded** — the owner's first requirement. Every number lives in [`dutyTime.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/dutyTime.ts). New columns `AttendanceDay.lastSeenAt` / `workedMinutes` and `StaffProfile.dutyMinutes`, migration `20260922100000_hr_duty_time` (applied cleanly to a local restore of live). **Not browser-verified** — the pulse, the idle sign-out and the grading have not been driven in a real signed-in session.

- **2026-09-20 — the staff identity card, at PAN-card size.** Owner asked for a printable staff ID, credit-card size, from the office *and* from the staff member's own profile. His call on the template: *“since its preview is available so no need print templet entry”* — so this is the one printable that is **not** a `VoucherPrintTemplate` kind (a deliberate exception to §2). What is on screen is what prints, because both surfaces render the same [`StaffIdCard`](src/shared/components/print/StaffIdCard.tsx): front and back at the true ID-1 size (85.6 × 54 mm, every dimension in mm so the printer cannot shrink it). Front — shop logo, brand and tagline, photo (initials when none is on file), name, designation, staff code, joined date, phone, blood group; back — emergency contact, address, “property of … if found return to …” and two signature lines. The shop's own identity comes from `COMPANY_DETAILS` through `getOrganisation()`, which is client-safe, so the preview needs no extra API. [`toStaffIdCardData`](src/modules/hr/lib/staffIdCard.ts) is the single place that decides what goes on the card — the preview (client, fed by the already default-open `GET /api/hr/staff/[userId]`) and the sheet (server, fed by Prisma) both call it, so they cannot drift. Wired as [`StaffIdCardTab`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffIdCardTab.tsx) into **both** screens: tab 10 **ID Card** on `/office/social/users/[id]` and the **identity** tab on the HR staff profile. Print opens [`/receipt/staff-id/[userId]`](src/app/receipt/staff-id/%5BuserId%5D/page.tsx) — both sides on A4 with a cut line, browser print only (a card is never a counter slip, so no bridge and no format picker). No schema change, no migration, no seed. **Not browser-verified yet** — the dev server needed a sign-in the agent may not perform; `tsc` passes.

- **2026-09-20 — bulk salary payment: one voucher, many people.** Paying three hundred staff meant three hundred Payment vouchers. A third tab, **Payroll → Pay** ([`PayrollPayTab`](src/modules/hr/components/PayrollPayTab.tsx)): tick who is being paid, choose the cash or bank it leaves from, and post. The list is read off each person's **salary ledger**, not off the payroll runs — that is the whole point, because the ledger has already netted any advance against the accrual, so somebody who took ₹5,000 in hand on the 10th shows ₹11,000 owed rather than ₹16,000; working from the run would pay them twice. People holding an advance (a debit balance) are listed separately with a note that the next run clears it by itself. [`POST /api/hr/payroll/pay`](src/app/api/hr/payroll/pay/route.ts) writes **ONE** Payment voucher — a debit line per person, one credit to the source — because a single withdrawal is a single entry in the bank book, while each salary ledger still settles individually. No salary expense is touched: that was taken when the run was posted; this only settles the liability. People are named by USER id and the route resolves each ledger itself, so a crafted request cannot debit some other account, and the source must be an ASSET ledger. Gated on the PAYMENT create + post permissions rather than `hr:payroll:run` — this is money leaving, so the authority that matters is the one to write and post a payment. Amounts default to the whole balance; typing less pays part, typing more leaves them holding an advance, which is allowed and is exactly what a debit balance means. Browser-verified read-only: the tab, the empty state, the payable API and the cash/bank picker against the real chart. Nothing was paid.

- **2026-09-20 — a payroll run can be cancelled, and staff can see their own slips.** Two pieces the module was missing. **Cancel**: the run already carried a `CANCELLED` status and `overlappingRun()` already honoured it, but nothing could set it — so a wrong run was stuck forever, still claiming its dates even after its journal was cancelled by hand in Accountancy. [`POST /api/hr/payroll/[id]/cancel`](src/app/api/hr/payroll/[id]/cancel/route.ts) voids the journal through the shared `cancelVoucherInTx` (the same path the vouchers screen and the POS shift routes use — balances reversed, voucher kept and struck through) and stamps the run `CANCELLED` in the same transaction, so a run can never claim “posted” about a voucher that is not. `skipIfCancelled` covers a journal someone already cancelled by hand. **Cancelling frees the days**, which is the whole point: the period can be generated again.
