A draft is still deleted, not cancelled — it never reached the books. Gated by `hr:payroll:run` plus the JOURNAL cancel gate; a reason is required and kept. **Salary slips**: owner, asked whether the payslip should print — *"display in users own profile so they can see. print will be later."* So [`StaffPayslips`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffPayslips.tsx) is a reading surface on the person's own profile page (tab 9, `selfOrAdmin`), not a document: each posted period with days paid, gross, deductions and net, expanding on click to the pay heads. [`GET /api/hr/staff/[userId]/payslips`](src/app/api/hr/staff/[userId]/payslips/route.ts) is **the one HR read that is not default-open** — what a person is paid is their business and HR's, so it refuses anyone who is neither. Only POSTED runs appear (a draft has not happened, a cancelled one has been taken back out), and every figure is the run's own frozen line, never recomputed. Browser-verified. A printable payslip through the print template is still to come, as are bulk payment and statutory deductions.

- **2026-09-20 — HR & People, phase 3: attendance, holidays, leave.** The last agreed slot, built beside the payroll session (which owns payroll, the staff profile and the HR nav from here on). Owner's rule from 2026-09-18: attendance is **auto from the counter sign-in plus an office register**. So a day is written by two hands — the till's first sign-in of the business day writes PRESENT with the in time ([`attendanceStamp.ts`](src/shared/lib/hr/attendanceStamp.ts), called from both authorizers; the shift close stamps the out time) and HR clicks a cell on the [register](src/modules/hr/components/AttendanceRegister.tsx) (P → ½ → A → L → clear, optimistic with rollback). A day with no row is **read off the calendar**, never invented: holiday, then approved leave, then `StaffProfile.weeklyOff`, else unmarked ([`attendanceCalendar.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendanceCalendar.ts)). Tables `AttendanceDay` (unique userId+date), `Holiday`, `LeaveRequest` in [`prisma/schema/hr-attendance.prisma`](prisma/schema/hr-attendance.prisma), migration `20260920220000_hr_attendance_leave`; routes under [`/api/hr/attendance`](src/app/api/hr/attendance/route.ts), `/api/hr/holidays`, `/api/hr/leave`; pages Attendance / Leave / Holidays under HR & People. Leave: anyone files their own, `hr:leave:approve` decides or files for others; **approval writes LEAVE onto the days that have no mark yet** (a till-recorded PRESENT is a fact and stays) and taking it back removes exactly those days ([`leaveServer.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/leaveServer.ts)). `hr:attendance:edit` gates the register and the holiday list. **The payroll seam** is [`nonPayableDays()`](src/modules/hr/lib/attendancePayable.ts) — ISO date → 0 (ABSENT, or LEAVE under an approved UNPAID request) or 0.5 (HALF_DAY); a day absent from the map is paid, because the structure is monthly and weekly offs, holidays and paid leave are paid days. Not yet wired into `payrollProrate.ts` — owner: *"calendar days are fine for now"*; the payroll session owns that loop. Holidays ride the personal snapshot (config); attendance and leave rows do not (transactions).

- **2026-09-20 — salary expense is one ledger for the whole shop, not one per person.** Owner, pointing at the Job tab: *"exp account is common to all, then what the exp account doing in the hr staff page — it should be the liability account of the person"*. Right on both counts, and the two sides answer different questions. The **liability** is per person and always will be: “how much do we owe *this* person, right now” must be visible and settleable one by one, which is what their own account under *Salary Payable* is for. The **expense** is a P&L question — “what did labour cost the business” — and three hundred staff must not become three hundred lines in it. So `StaffProfile.expenseAccountId` is gone (migration `20260920200000_payroll_one_expense_ledger`), and the Job tab shows **their own salary ledger**, read-only, with its balance read as “owed to them” or “advance held” — a fact about the person, not a setting. The Dr side now lives on `PayrollRun.expenseAccountId`: resolved once by `ensureSalaryExpenseAccount()` (find-or-create *Salary & Wages* under Indirect Expenses, same reasoning as the Salary Payable group — Vercel runs migrations, not seeds) and frozen with the run, so changing the org's ledger later cannot rewrite a posted journal. The journal is now **one debit and many credits**, not a debit per expense account. `PayrollRunLine.expenseAccountId` went with it. Owner's instruction on the four legacy per-person expense ledgers (Aka / Naba / Rakesh / Prakash Salary, ₹1.70 lakh between them, booked `Dr <Name> Salary / Cr Bank` on each payday — cash basis, no liability ever recorded): *"ignore anything existing, create what we need, we will manually edit / adjust / delete"*. Nothing was migrated or touched. **Still true and worth remembering: April–August salary is already booked and already paid that way, so the first real run must start 1 Sep 2026, not the 1 Apr the Generate tab suggests by default.** Browser-verified: the expense picker is gone from the Job tab and the salary ledger line reads in its place.

- **2026-09-20 — Payroll becomes a page, and a period stops being a month.** Owner: *"the page where i see per head what is supposed to be the total from last payroll to till day"*, *"right click expand breakup pay head etc."*, *"one generated goes to another tab where we select history list"*. Three consequences. **(1) A run now pays a DATE RANGE**, not a calendar month: `PayrollRun.periodMonth` (unique) becomes `periodFrom` / `periodTo`, and because a range cannot be made unique by the database the “no day paid twice” rule moves into `overlappingRun()`. The Generate tab opens on the day after the last run through to today — for the very first run, which has no predecessor, the earliest joining date. Migration `20260920180000_hr_payroll_period_range`. **(2) Amounts are proportioned by days** ([`payrollProrate.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/payrollProrate.ts)): a monthly head is worth `amount ÷ days-in-THAT-month` for each day the person was employed inside the period. Dividing by the real length of each month, not a flat 30, is what makes a whole month come back to exactly the structure's figure in February as much as January; fractions are carried at full precision and rounded once per head at the end. It walks day by day because that is the only version that is obviously correct — a revision landing mid-period, a joiner on the 12th and a leaver on the 20th all fall out of the same loop. Owner chose this over “full month regardless”, and it needs no attendance, only dates. Verified on live data: 1 Apr–20 Sep 2026 on ₹16,000/month gives ₹90,666.67 over 173 days — five whole months plus 20/30 of September. **(3) The UI is a page with two tabs**, [`Generate`](src/modules/hr/components/PayrollGenerateTab.tsx) and History, and generating switches to History and opens the new draft; the old `NewPayrollRunDialog` is deleted. A row **expands on click** to its pay heads, on both the Generate tab and the stored run. Right-click was asked for and deliberately **not** used: across this app it belongs to `LinkContextMenu`'s “Open in new window”, and click-to-expand is already the house idiom (`VoucherRegisterTable`). Browser-verified read-only — the period, the per-head figures, the breakup and the tab switch; nothing was generated or posted.

- **2026-09-20 — HR stops sending you to Administration.** Owner, pointing at the three link-out buttons on the staff profile: *"dont redirect to admin module for seat, login and permission"*, *"role and permission supposed to already exist, we just use them here"*, and — on where the rest belongs — initial personal / bank details in HR, *"create joining later with pay structure"*, *"we will appoint to a position where"*. The split settled: **Administration DEFINES** roles, permissions and positions; **HR GIVES** them to a person. The profile is now self-contained — tabs **Job · Access · Personal · Pay · Money**, no `ExternalLink` anywhere. **Access** ([`StaffAccessTab`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffAccessTab.tsx)) picks an existing role, the data scope and the manager, and sets a password for someone locked out — all through the same `PUT /api/users/[id]` the Admin → Users screen uses, never a second write path, and it says on the tab that roles are defined elsewhere. **Seats** ([`StaffSeatsCard`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffSeatsCard.tsx)) appoints to and vacates an existing position through `/api/positions/[id]/holders`. **Personal** embeds the very same [`StaffPersonalForm`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffPersonalForm.tsx) the person sees on their own profile page — HR puts the first copy on file at hiring, they keep it current afterwards. And **Appoint** ([`AppointStaffDialog`](src/modules/hr/components/AppointStaffDialog.tsx)) makes the joining one act: joining date, employment type, staff ID, seat and the **first salary structure** together, because a joining date on one tab and a salary on another let a person exist half-hired. It is a shortcut, not a gate — every field stays editable on its own tab. Three gates now, not one: `hr:staff:edit` for the HR record, `users:edit` for Access, `positions:edit` for Seats; holding only the first leaves those surfaces read-only instead of bouncing you to Administration. No new permission, no schema change — the HR detail payload simply carries `scope`, `managerId`, `manager` and `userRole`, which `STAFF_USER_SELECT` already read. Browser-verified read-only: all five tabs, the role picker listing the real roles, the seats list, and the Appoint dialog; nothing was saved against the live books.

- **2026-09-20 — HR & People, phase 2: the monthly payroll run.** Owner: *"i may not remember salary of all 300 staff — give a page in the module where we generate the payable"*, and *"we will create salary in bulk"*. The month's accrual stops being a journal typed by hand. **One run per month**, unique on `periodMonth`, so a month cannot be accrued twice. Generating it reads, for every person who had joined and had not left by the posting date, the salary revision in force on that date — and **freezes** the amounts onto the run, so a raise given in October can never rewrite what September posted. Anyone the run cannot pay is still written down with the reason (no structure, no ledger, net of zero) rather than silently dropped, and the sheet says how many. Posting turns the run into **one journal voucher** dated the month end: a credit per person to their own ledger under *Salary Payable* (each Money tab and payslip must stand on its own), and debits **grouped by expense ledger**, so the P&L reads one "Salary & Wages" figure instead of three hundred rows; the expense side is `StaffProfile.expenseAccountId`, falling back to the org's *Salary & Wages*. Shipped: `PayrollRun` + `PayrollRunLine` in [`prisma/schema/hr.prisma`](prisma/schema/hr.prisma), migration `20260920160000_hr_payroll_runs`; routes under [`src/app/api/hr/payroll`](src/app/api/hr/payroll/route.ts) (list, generate, month preview, draft PATCH / DELETE, and `[id]/post` which holds the JOURNAL post gate as well); [`payrollServer.ts`](src/modules/hr/lib/payrollServer.ts) owns the generation and the journal shape; the sidebar gains **HR & People → Payroll** ([list](src/modules/hr/components/PayrollRunList.tsx), [run workspace](src/modules/hr/components/PayrollRunWorkspace.tsx), [new-run dialog](src/modules/hr/components/NewPayrollRunDialog.tsx)); one new permission `hr:payroll:run` (view rides `ui:sidebar:hr:payroll`). **No pro-rata** — there is no attendance yet, so a month is a month; a mid-month joiner is either paid in full or held back by unticking them, and paid separately. Deductions reduce the net and nothing else: PF / ESI do not yet post to liability ledgers of their own. Browser-verified read-only: the list, the month preview (staff-wise, with the "no salary structure" rows named) and the totals; nothing was generated or posted against the live books.

- **2026-09-20 — HR & People, phase 1: the staff record.** The first HR surface. Owner's calls, 2026-09-18 → 20: *staff = User + HR rows* (no separate employee table — the person who signs in, holds a seat and opens a shift IS the person HR keeps a record of; a helper who never logs in still gets a User row); *accrual, one ledger per person* under a new **Salary Payable** group (LIABILITY, under Current Liabilities) — the ledger is opened the first time a profile is saved, so salary and advances can be paid *today* through an ordinary Payment voucher against it, and its balance always reads "owed to them" (Cr) or "advance outstanding" (Dr); *no extra restriction anywhere* — no office-only sync rule, no read gate on the HR APIs, the snapshot captures everything ("we will secure the app itself sooner"); and *staff already keep their own details on the existing profile page*, so HR builds no second personal form: the ID / bank fields joined that page as a **Personal & Bank** tab (self or HR may save it). Shipped: [`prisma/schema/hr.prisma`](prisma/schema/hr.prisma) — `StaffProfile` (1:1 on User; staff code, joined / left dates, employment type, weekly off, personal / KYC, `bankJson` in the party shape, `ledgerAccountId`, `expenseAccountId`) + `StaffSalary` (dated revisions, lines as JSON in paise, gross / net denormalised); migration `20260920120000_hr_staff_profiles`; sidebar section **HR & People** (`key: "hr"`, hue 335 beside Administration and Settings) with one child, **Staff Directory**; pages [`/office/hr/staff`](src/app/(protected)/office/hr/staff/page.tsx) and the profile workspace ([`StaffProfileWorkspace`](src/modules/hr/components/StaffProfileWorkspace.tsx): Job / Pay / Money — the Job tab is Pattern D's first real screen, every field saves on blur); routes under [`src/app/api/hr/staff`](src/app/api/hr/staff/route.ts) (list, detail, partial PATCH, salary POST / DELETE); one permission `hr:staff:edit` (view rides `ui:sidebar:hr:staff`); **Mark as left** sets `leftOn`, vacates the seats, revokes every session, and both sign-in paths refuse from that day ([`staffHasLeft`](src/shared/lib/hr/staffStatus.ts)) — `/api/counter-pin/status` answers `left` and the till drops the face card. DRY: `USER_SELECT` and `PositionChip` moved out of Admin → Users into [`staffSelect.ts`](src/shared/lib/users/staffSelect.ts) / [`PositionChip.tsx`](src/shared/components/PositionChip.tsx) so both lists describe a person the same way. Sync: `StaffProfile` is pinned to `watermark` in `sync-tables.json` — its only cascade parent is User, and a child is pulled only when the parent row moves, which an edit to `leftOn` never does. Not built (later phases): attendance (auto from counter sign-in + office register), holidays, leave, payroll run, payslip print, statutory PF / ESI / PT, document scans.

- **2026-09-19 — A near miss auto-match refuses is now pointed at, never taken.** Owner, right after the rule above went in: *"if that kind of guess found we can hint that we need to find in another date but not to crate without verify"*. So the pair auto-match now declines — same money, same side, a **different day** with no reference in common — is shown on the open bank line as an amber chip: `possible: UV-3519 · 10 Sept 2026 · 28 days away — check`. **Clicking it ticks both rows and stops there.** Nothing is written; the Match button simply lights up as `Match 1 ↔1` and the person presses it, or unticks and walks away — which is the whole point, since the reason the match is refused is that the amount alone proves nothing. Three decisions, all deliberate: it hints on the **bank side only** (both sides would advertise every pair twice, and the bank is the truth), it looks across the **whole period on screen rather than the auto-match window** (the window governs what may be *created*, and the case worth pointing at is exactly the one outside it — a 7-day window would have hidden the owner's own ₹100 at 28 days), and a line that already has a **same-date suggestion gets no hint**, because that amber bracket is the better answer and two amber things on one row is noise. Several candidates → the **nearest by date** is named and `(+N more)` says the choice exists rather than hiding it. **The hover carries what the entry actually is** — `UV-3519 — CONTRA · CONTRA 2507 · swipe mechine` — because a pair days apart is almost never on the same page of the table, so without it the only way to judge the suggestion would be to go hunting for the other row. The Auto-match message gained a tail too: *"… 2 have the same amount on another date — shown in amber, check before matching."* **Pure screen work**: both sides are already loaded, so there is no API, no schema and no extra read — the hints are a `useMemo` over `data`, built from the unfiltered payload so switching to Matched does not blind it. Verified in the running app on the live SBI ledger: two hints on that ledger (the ₹100 at 28 days, and a ₹32,000 at 3 days), the chip and its hover read correctly, and the click lights `Match 1 ↔ 1` without saving. **One bug caught only by driving the UI**: the two new props were added to both row components' type but not to their destructuring, which `tsc` passed and the browser met as `hint is not defined` — the Next dev overlay, not the console (see AGENTS.md's standing note about lint and types passing while React breaks). `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean.

- **2026-09-19 — Auto-match stops guessing across days, and a stretched pair says so on the row.** Owner, pointing at a ticked ₹100 on the reconciliation screen — *"why the 100 is tick who is its pair?"* — and then, once the pair was named: *"these are not in same date, so auto match is restricted"*. The pair was statement line 100 (13 Aug 2026, ₹100 Google Pay UPI credit, ref `283521452256`) against **UV-3519, Contra 2507 dated 10 Sep 2026**, narration *"swipe mechine"* — **28 days apart, with nothing in common but the figure**. [`planAutoMatch`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/bankReconciliation.ts) took it because the reference token was only ever a **tie-breaker between candidates**, never a requirement: a ₹100 debit sitting alone inside the window was accepted however far away it was, and the window had been run at 300 days at some point. **Measured on the live SBI ledger before changing anything**: of 97 auto matches, **96 were same-day**, **0 were cross-day with a shared reference**, and the single cross-day one was that mistake. The rule is now two doors instead of one — **same day** (amount + direction, as before), or **a different day inside the window but only when the two sides share a reference token** (UTR, UPI id, cheque number, IMPS ref), which is what makes a late-clearing cheque provable rather than guessed. A different day with nothing shared is refused outright, however lonely the candidate; the existing tie rule (equal reference score *and* equal distance → leave it for a person) is untouched, and manual many-to-many matching is untouched. One line does it — `.filter((c) => c.dist === 0 || c.ref === 1)` — and on the live data it keeps all 96 good matches and drops exactly the one bad one. **The window's meaning changed with it**, so the toolbar says what it now means: *"same day, or up to N days on a matching reference"*, with the full rule on hover — nobody should set 300 again and expect it to be safe. **A matched statement row now carries its gap**: `↔ UV-3519 · auto · 28 days apart` in amber, in both the merged and the split view, since a match by hand can still span anything and a stretched pair was previously invisible unless you opened both sides. (It also explains the missing bracket — the green bracket only draws over adjacent rows, so a match whose halves are a month apart never had one.) **Deliberately no backfill** (owner: *"no need back fill"*): a match already saved is not re-examined, since re-running Auto-match only clears *stale* matches — the ₹100 stays ticked until it is unmatched by hand with the ↺ icon. Verified in the running app on the live SBI ledger: the new toolbar wording renders, and the row the owner circled now shows *28 days apart*. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean. `planAutoMatch` has no other caller, so nothing else had to move.

- **2026-09-19 — A ledger can be made and altered from inside the voucher, on Tally's own keys.** Owner, looking at a Payment whose particulars box said *No accounts match SBI POS Rent*: *"let create / edit ledger (if possible same key board short cut tally use)"*. [`GlAccountCombobox`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GlAccountCombobox.tsx) — the one picker behind every GL voucher, the Journal, Trading and Manufacturing vouchers, the POS expenditure dialog and three settings screens — now opens the existing [`CreateLedgerDialog`](src/modules/accountancy/components/CreateLedgerDialog.tsx) and [`EditLedgerDialog`](src/modules/accountancy/components/EditLedgerDialog.tsx) in place, so nothing new had to be written to create a master. **Alt+C** makes one, named from whatever is typed (a new `defaultName` prop), and **Enter on a no-match** does the same — both mirroring `InvProductCombobox`, which has had exactly this affordance for products; a **+ New ledger: ‹text›** row sits at the top of the dropdown for the mouse. **Ctrl+Enter** alters the highlighted ledger, or the one already in the box. **That key was Save, and here it stops being Save — deliberately**: [`useFormKeyboardNav`](src/shared/hooks/useFormKeyboardNav.ts) binds Ctrl+Enter *and* Ctrl+A to save, where Tally uses Ctrl+A to accept and Ctrl+Enter to alter a master. The combobox therefore stops the event **only when there is genuinely a ledger to alter**; with an empty box it falls through and Save still happens, and Ctrl+A saves from anywhere as before. **The full Account row is handed to the edit dialog, not a partial one** — `PATCH /ledgers/[id]` posts the particulars of every kind the chosen group does not match as `null`, so a ledger edited from a picker that only knew its name and balance would have had its bank / loan / card / BNPL / wallet / gateway block quietly wiped. `/api/accountancy/gl-accounts` already returns those columns (it spreads the whole row); the `GlAccount` type just never declared them, and now does. `onSaved` carries the new name back so the box re-labels itself at once. **A ledger made here shows up instantly** and survives a filtered caller: Payment's particulars list is expense-only, so a ledger created under another group would never arrive in it — the picker keeps its own `justCreated` list merged over the incoming one, same pattern as `InvProductCombobox`. Afterwards the shared [`useGlAccounts`](src/shared/hooks/useGlAccounts.ts) store is re-read once (an `onMastersChanged` prop overrides that for a caller holding its own list). **Two cost rules honoured.** Both dialogs used to fetch the group tree on every open; opening them from a voucher row would have meant that read per ledger, so `loadGroupOptions()` in [`groupOptions.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/groupOptions.ts) reads it **once per tab** with in-flight dedupe, and the three places that write a group call `invalidateGroupOptions()`. And the two dialogs are `next/dynamic` + mounted only while open, so the group picker, the particulars blocks and the opening-bills dialog behind them stay out of a till's first paint. `allowCreate` / `allowEdit` props default on, so every surface gets it. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean. **Not browser-verified** — another session holds :3000 and this session's preview pane is refused localhost, so the click-through (nested dialog inside the voucher dialog, focus returning to the row, Ctrl+Enter vs Save) is the owner's to run.

- **2026-09-19 — Reconciliation reads as one table, and the upload dialog is full width.** Owner: *"UI area is not enough to match, we can put both side statement in same table two different colour row"*. [`BankReconciliationPage`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BankReconciliationPage.tsx) gained a **Merged / Split** toggle (merged is the default, remembered in `localStorage` under `recon.view`). Merged puts statement lines and book legs in **one full-width table** sorted by date, then by a **signed delta** — money into the bank positive, so `deposit − withdrawal` on the bank side and `debit − credit` on the books side — which drops a line and the entry that explains it onto **consecutive rows carrying the same figure in the same column** (withdrawal over credit, deposit over debit). Each row is banded by colour *and* tagged `Bank` / `Books`, never colour alone; a matched pair still goes green. Selection stays two sets, so Match, the totals guard and Book-it are the same code as before; Split keeps the old two panes untouched. **A suggested match group** is any set of still-open rows sharing a date *and* an exact signed amount with at least one row from each side — direction has to agree, so a deposit never pairs with a payment of the same size. Those rows carry an amber bracket down the left and a **Select N** chip on the first one, which ticks the whole group so Match is one press away; `first`/`last` are read off the final row order, so a matched row sitting in the middle cannot leave a bracket hanging open. Side colours were strengthened to sky-100/violet-100 with 500-weight rails, and each row now picks **one** background (selected → matched → side) instead of stacking three `bg-*` utilities whose winner was down to Tailwind's emit order — the same latent bug fixed in the split rows. **Groups are bounded**: neighbouring rows that share a match `groupId` — or a suggestion — form a run, and a run of two or more carries the bracket (green for a match, amber for a suggestion) with a heavy rule closing it and opening the next; rows outside any group keep the hairline, so the table does not read as bars. A match whose halves are days apart is not adjacent, so it gets no bracket rather than a wrong one. **Several statement lines book as one voucher.** Owner: *"let create voucher, with multiple row (if ticked multiple row), row level description. because some time subscription fee and other charges deducted separately but related to same purpose"*. Tick the bank lines and the toolbar offers `Payment N` / `Receipt N` (by direction) and `Contra N`: the bank takes **one leg for the total** and every ticked line becomes its own particulars row carrying **its narration as that row's description** — `GlVoucherPage` already shows a row's note box when the row has a note, so all of them come up visible. The ledger column is left blank, the one thing to fill in. On save all the lines match to the new voucher's bank leg in one group, totals agreeing by construction. Mixed directions are refused outright rather than guessing which side the bank leg sits on. `bookIt` now takes a list; the per-row links pass a list of one. **A group is never split across pages**: the merged table pages block by block — a run whole, a loose row on its own — so a page runs a row or two short rather than cutting a group in half, and `PaginationBar` gained optional `pageCount` / `rangeFrom` / `rangeTo` for a parent whose pages are not uniform slices (every other caller is untouched). The merged view now opens on **All**, not Unmatched. Auto-match's `within N days` cap went 90 → **300**, in the Zod schema and the input together. Hover deepens the row's **own** colour (sky-200 / violet-200 / green-200) instead of washing it with `bg-muted`, which read as the bank tint and made a hovered Books row look like a Bank row. Only the chosen view renders, halving the rows in the DOM. The page title and its one-line description are gone — the breadcrumb already names the screen — and a **Summary** button beside Refresh folds the reconciliation-statement strip away (remembered under `recon.stats`), worth about 134px, four more rows. Separately, [`BankStatementImportDialog`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BankStatementImportDialog.tsx) was stuck narrow because `DialogContent`'s own `sm:max-w-lg` beats a plain `max-w-5xl` — now `w-[90vw]` with `max-w-none sm:max-w-[90vw]`, so the column mapper and its preview get real room.

- **2026-09-18 — Bank reconciliation is live: the bank's statement is uploaded, kept, matched, and booked from one screen.** Owner: *"we need to activate bank reconciliation, we will upload the statement"*, then the rules — *"every bank format to same, we need to match definitely as per the bank, we need to detect duplicate statement, duplicate entry etc. we have reconciled lock, we will work from the lock date"* — and *"study tally reconcile, we have to make it better, let us create voucher right from the reconcile screen"*. The placeholder at `/office/accountancy/reports/bank-reconciliation` is replaced by [`BankReconciliationPage`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BankReconciliationPage.tsx). **Three satellite tables, spine untouched** ([finance.prisma](prisma/schema/finance.prisma), migration [`20260918120000_bank_statement`](prisma/migrations/20260918120000_bank_statement/migration.sql), additive only): `BankStatementImport` (one upload: file hash, profile, account number + IFSC read off the statement, period, opening/closing), `BankStatementLine` (one normalised shape whatever the bank; `@@unique([accountId, fingerprint])`), `BankStatementMatch` (statement line ↔ `VoucherGlLine.id` as a **loose id**, like `VoucherAllocation`; one `groupId` per match action). **Every bank lands in one shape before matching sees it** — [`bankStatement.ts`](src/shared/lib/import/bankStatement.ts) is pure and runs in the browser and on the server: bank *profiles* recognised by header signature (`sbi-tsv` first, built on the owner's real download — SBI's "xls" is a tab-separated text file with seventeen particulars lines above the table) with a generic auto-guess + hand mapping for anything else, every Indian date and amount spelling, and the instrument number pulled out of the narration (UTR, UPI/IMPS ref, cheque no, INB id) because that is the one token both sides share. [`spreadsheetRead.ts`](src/shared/lib/import/spreadsheetRead.ts) tells text from real Excel by the first bytes, not the extension; `.xls/.xlsx` go through SheetJS (`xlsx` 0.20.3 from the SheetJS CDN tarball — npm's 0.18.5 carries an open advisory), loaded only on this page. **Trust nothing from the browser twice** ([`bankReconciliation.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/bankReconciliation.ts) `importStatement`): the account number / IFSC printed on the statement must agree with the ledger's `bankJson` (mismatch blocks; blank ledger particulars warn), the whole file's sha1 must be new, the running balance must walk line by line (a break means a missing or doubled line and blocks unless the user ticks *save anyway*), the statement's opening must continue from the last saved line (warns), and every row's fingerprint (`accountId|date|amounts|balance|narration|nth`) must be unseen — so a re-download of an overlapping period saves only what is new, and two genuinely identical entries on one day both survive. `dryRun: true` on the same POST is the preview. **The lock is the floor**: rows dated *before* `reconciledThrough` are refused on upload and untouchable in matching; rows *on* the lock date stay open (owner: *"after i lock some transaction may happen same day later"*). **The bank is the truth**: `POST /ledgers/[id]/reconcile` now refuses a date any statement line up to it is unmatched for (`assertLockAllowed`); unmatched *book* entries are allowed through — that is what a cheque issued and not presented is. **Matching**: auto-match pairs same direction (deposit ↔ DEBIT leg), exact amount, within N days (default 7), a shared reference token deciding between candidates, then the nearest date; an exact tie is left for a person (`planAutoMatch`, pure). Manual is many-to-many with both sides' signed totals gated equal (gateway lump ↔ several receipts, one receipt ↔ two transfers). A voucher edited (lines rewritten by `reapplyTradingVoucherInPlace`) or removed leaves a match with no live leg: the read reports it **stale**, auto-match clears it, nothing in the engine had to learn about statements. **Tally studied, and gone past**: Tally keeps only a bank date per entry and discards the imported statement; here the statement is stored (so "not in books" lines stay on screen until booked and a re-download cannot double anything), identity and continuity are checked before saving, matching is many-to-many, and the lock has the existing retreat. Its one good idea — *Create Voucher* from an unmatched bank line — is **Book it**: Payment / Receipt / Contra opens the real form in [`GlVoucherDialog`](src/shared/components/GlVoucherDialog.tsx) pre-filled through the existing `stashDuplicate` channel (bank leg, amount, statement date, narration, reference), and `GlVoucherPage` gained `onSaved(voucherId, posted)` so the screen matches the new voucher's bank leg to the line the moment it is posted. **Summary strip** is the reconciliation statement from everything up to `to`, not only the period: books balance ± not-yet-in-bank ± not-in-books = derived bank balance, against the statement's printed closing, with a *Lock upto <date>* button on the latest date every statement line is explained. The ledger report's edit-reversal collapse moved to [`collapseEditReversals.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/collapseEditReversals.ts) so both screens list the same rows. Writes reuse `accountancy:masters:edit` (the gate the watermark already had — no catalog change, no seed); reads need login like every report. `pnpm sync:tables` / `pnpm db:local-schema` regenerated (118 tables; lines and matches sync as `child`, imports as `append`) — **a schema release: the counter build ships before the d_office deploy (AGENTS.md §3)**. Verified: the SBI sample parses to 148 rows with zero continuity breaks and the profile, account number, IFSC, period and opening read off the header; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap; eslint clean. **Not yet applied anywhere**: `.env` points the dev server at live, and the migration reaches live only through the deploy, so the screen cannot be exercised in a browser until either the owner says to apply the (additive) migration to live by hand or the release lands. Not in this round: PDF statements, printing the reconciliation statement (would need a print template), the tick + bank date on the Bank Book register, post-dated cheque tracking.

- **2026-09-17 — A service is no longer a POS-only thing: it goes on every trade voucher, on both sides, through ONE ledger.** Owner: *"sale side has income group, we will use in purchase side will go to exp. its no more limited to pos only"*, then, on wiring: *"service looks like product in UI and INVOICE its GOOD, do the same for OTHER VOUCER too, it has own GST rate too. Exiting add account in office keep working. \*\*\* no exp ledger need use the same ledger on both side, it will auto adjust \*\*\*"*.
  - **The expense ledger was the obvious answer and the wrong one.** The plan on the table was a second `expenseAccountId` column on `PosService` — a migration, a counter release ahead of the Vercel deploy, a `schemaGuard` risk on every till. The owner cut it: one ledger, both sides. It is already true in the code — [`postInvVoucher`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/postInvVoucher.ts) takes a charge row's direction from the voucher type (`raw > 0 ? bodyType : counterType`), so the ledger is **credited on a sale and debited on a purchase** with nothing to configure. **No schema change, no migration, no counter release.** The trade-off, said once and accepted: the ledger then reads NET (sold minus bought), so a month of buying more than you sell leaves it with a debit balance on the P&L.
  - **The actual hole was on create, not on edit.** `PATCH /api/inventory/vouchers/[id]` already carried `taxAmount` on a charge row; **`POST` dropped it**, so an office voucher could never raise a taxed service at all. Both routes now take a `services[]` array and re-resolve the ledger and the GST rate **from the catalogue** — the browser sends an id and a qty × rate, never an account.
  - **One resolver, four callers.** [`resolveServiceCharges`](src/shared/lib/accountancy/serviceCharges.ts) is now the only place a service becomes a charge row: POS sale (its inline copy deleted), office create, office edit, and the counter's quotes. Same ledger, same unrounded net/tax, same printed label everywhere — `SERVICE_VOUCHER_TYPES` names the money vouchers that may carry one, and the stock-only movements (GRN, Challan, Rejection, Transfer, Physical Stock) are deliberately not among them.
  - **A saved service used to decay into a bare ledger amount.** `settlementJson` stored only account + amount + description, so re-opening a voucher lost which service it was, how many, and at what rate. It is a **JSON column**, so the round-trip fields (`serviceId`, `serviceName`, the operator's `note`, `quantity` × `rate` at `taxRate`) were added with **no migration**. Name and note are stored in their own right, not parsed back out of the printed label — a service named with a `·` in it would have come back cut in half.
  - **The inclusive price, handled where the two conventions meet.** A catalogue price is tax-INCLUSIVE (₹300 at 18% means the customer pays ₹300); the office voucher's PRICE column may be in either mode. [`serviceLine`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/tradingVoucherKinds.ts) converts to whichever is on, and keeps **six decimals** on the exclusive value — 254.24 grosses back to ₹300.0032, 254.237288 divides back exactly.
  - **On screen it is a line, not a footer row.** `LineState` gained `service`, so a service sits in the items table with its own qty, price, GST and amount and every column behaves as it does on a product; the ITEM cell names it instead of offering the item box, MRP and FREE go blank. **"+ Add Account" is untouched**, as asked.
  - **Two bugs found in the path and fixed.** (1) The edit route computed `chargesNet` from the REQUEST, so a PATCH that rewrote the items without mentioning charges dropped every stored charge out of the total while leaving it in `settlementJson` — it now reads the effective list, and the form always sends both arrays so a deleted row is really deleted. (2) The A4 rate-wise (HSN/SAC) table subtracted the GST from a charge's `amount`, which is already the net — a ₹254.24 service was filed as ₹208.46 taxable, and its tax appeared with no base under it because the print sample never carried `taxAmount`/`taxRate` at all.
  - **The counter's quotes learned services too**, closing the `TODO(pos-services)` that made `PosQuoteScreen` refuse to save an Estimate or Order carrying one. A services-only quote is now a real quote, and both it and a services-only office voucher skip the empty goods-line insert.
  - _Verified: `tsc` clean, `eslint` clean on all 19 touched files, `gen-sync-registry --check`, `gen-local-schema --check` (114 tables — nothing moved), `check-nav-icons`, `check-guard-lint` and all five `check-counter-mode` variants pass. **Not yet exercised in a browser** — a worktree's dev server cannot be started from here; the click-through belongs on `main`._

- **2026-09-17 — The bill-wise status left the party page and reached the Day Book and every voucher register.** Owner, with the two screens side by side: *"we need the bill wise status in the daybook and voucher reports too"*. The party's Transactions tab had shown `Settled` / `Partial ₹17,300.00` / `Due` since the bill-link work; nowhere else did, so the one place that answers *"has this bill been matched off?"* was the one place you had to already know which party to open. **The chip is now one component on one rule.** `settlementOf` and a new batched `loadSettlements` moved into [`billLink.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) beside `sumLinked`, and [`customerTransactions.ts`](src/modules/customer/lib/customerTransactions.ts) dropped its own copy — the party tab and the Day Book cannot disagree now because they call the same function. [`SettlementChip`](src/modules/accountancy/components/SettlementChip.tsx) is the shared cell, and [`VoucherRegisterTable`](src/modules/accountancy/components/VoucherRegisterTable.tsx) — the one table behind the Day Book, All Vouchers, the fifteen inventory registers, the six GL registers and Journal Entries — gained a `showSettlement` column headed **Bill-wise** that hosts the same link popup. It reads `|movement|`, so the account's normal side never has to be resolved: a ₹4,000 receivable and a ₹4,000 payable are equally "₹4,000 waiting to be matched". **Nothing is shown where there is no bill**: no party, not POSTED, or a kind in `NON_REGULAR_KINDS` (estimates, orders, challans, GRN — they post no money) reads as a plain dash rather than a green lie. **Two things had to be fixed on the way.** `InvVoucherList` was putting the **VoucherDetail** id in `sortId`/`rawId`, so a register row had no spine id and the popup would have opened the wrong voucher — rows now carry an explicit `universalVoucherId` instead of overloading `sortId`. And the Day Book's IndexedDB cache is only ever topped up by a *delta*, so a client holding rows from an older build would never learn the new field and the column read blank; `viewKey` now stamps a `ROW_SHAPE` version, which retires an older cache by itself. Cost: the Day Book, the GL register and the journal register already load every GL line for their own totals, so the party leg is free there and only `sumLinked` is added (two queries a page); the inventory register pays one extra indexed leg query. Measured on live: Day Book ~0.65 s for 50 rows, Receipts 0.7–0.9 s for 320, Sales ~3 s for 2,114 — the last two are the unpaged registers' own pre-existing cost.

- **2026-09-17 — The ledger code is retired; a ledger is known by its name alone.** Owner, pointing at the grey `5006` beside **Capital** on Masters → Ledgers: *"retire the code from ledger"*, and then, asked how far: drop the column. `Account.code` is gone from the schema, and a `DROP COLUMN` takes it off live — folded into [`20260917090000_product_opening_branch`](prisma/migrations/20260917090000_product_opening_branch/migration.sql) rather than filed separately, because that migration had not reached any database yet (AGENTS.md §3). **It was already a field nobody could fill:** neither the Create nor the Edit Ledger dialog ever carried it, so every ledger made the normal way — and every party ledger — has held `NULL` since the Ledgers screen replaced the old Chart of accounts dialog; only 19 of 113 snapshot ledgers had one. **Three screens stop showing it** (the Ledgers list, the Ledger Explorer's detail strip, and the Voucher Register's Reference cell on GL lines, which is now blank — an item line still shows its SKU), **two search boxes stop secretly matching on it** (the GL account combobox and the Outstandings `searchKey`, whose placeholders now say what they actually match), and **two dialogs lose a required Code input** — Chart of accounts and the Opening Balance page's new-ledger dialog, where Name took the freed grid slot. **It also fixes an ordering nobody chose:** `/api/accountancy/gl-accounts` sorted `code ASC`, and Postgres sorts NULLs last, so the code-less ledgers — the cash ledger among them — sank to the bottom of every GL dropdown; that is `name ASC` now, which every row can satisfy (the workaround note at `TradingVoucherPage.tsx:112` is retired with it). **Two things used the code as a real key and moved to the name:** `getWalkInAccountId` (the parked offline-sync collision resolver) now matches `ACCOUNT_LEDGERS.WALK_IN` with the identity columns pinned null, and the personal snapshot keys GL ledgers by name only — **`SNAPSHOT_VERSION` is 2**, so an older snapshot is refused loudly instead of dying mid-import on an unknown `code` argument. **The one thing lost is rename-safety in the seed:** `upsertLedger` used the code to recognise a seeded ledger an admin had renamed ("Bank Account" → their real bank) and skip it; matching by name alone means a full reseed re-creates the original beside the renamed one. That was already true of the other 60-odd code-less seeded ledgers, and reseeds are for reset and drift recovery, so the duplicate is visible when it happens. **Counters did not need to move first:** a column dropped upstream lands in `schemaGuard`'s `extra` bucket, which does not halt the worker, and `pull.js` takes its insert columns from the source rows — the stale local column simply stops being written and clears on the next reseed.

- **2026-09-17 — Opening stock now has a place it is lying in, and every existing opening was moved to Head Office.** Owner: *"backfill HO, and improve UI/code to let user select branch during import. (stock location only)"*. `InvProduct.openingStock` was a master column with no branch on it, so [`liveStock.ts`](src/modules/inventory/lib/liveStock.ts) had no choice but to fold the whole opening into **every** branch scope — the rule was `stock = openingStock + Σ(in − out)` with `branchId` narrowing only the ledger half. With one branch that is invisible; with two, a product that opened with 40 units reads 40 at Head Office **and** 40 at the second branch, and adding the branch reports together double-counts it. On live that is **644 of 1467 products and 12,865 units**. **New nullable `InvProduct.openingBranchId`** ([inventory.prisma](prisma/schema/inventory.prisma), FK to `Branch`, `ON DELETE SET NULL`, indexed) scopes the **quantity only** — it does **not** make the master branch-owned (there is still deliberately no `branchId` on `InvProduct`, one product row is shared by every branch) and it does **not** touch money: `openingValue`, `computeTradingStockAdjustment` and [`valuation.ts`](src/modules/inventory/lib/valuation.ts) stay org-level, unchanged. **This is not a revival of the per-row opening DATE field** killed on 2026-07-28 below — it is a location, and `openingStockDate` stays null. **`null` keeps its old meaning on purpose** (owner's call): an unassigned opening still folds into every branch, so a product the migration does not reach, or one created by hand without a location, reads exactly as it read before instead of silently dropping to zero — the very failure `liveStock.ts` exists to prevent. **Backfill rides in the migration, not a script**: [`20260917090000_product_opening_branch`](prisma/migrations/20260917090000_product_opening_branch/migration.sql) stamps `BR-HQ` on every product with an opening and no branch, guarded three ways (`openingBranchId IS NULL`, `openingStock IS NOT NULL`, and an `EXISTS` on the branch so a database without Head Office is skipped rather than failing an FK) — so `prisma migrate deploy` carries it to live on the Vercel build and each counter replica gets the same file, with no `tsx` script to run by hand. **The read rule changed in exactly one place**: `ProductStockRow` gained `openingBranchId` as a **required** field, which is what made all six branch-aware call sites fail to compile instead of quietly keeping the old answer (fsn, stock-health, stock-turnover, reorder, stock-summary, the item ledger); the products list uses `include` so it already carried the column. `loadLiveStockByBranch` now gives the opening to its own branch alone and adds that branch to the picker even when the ledger has never mentioned it, while `total` still counts it once — so the branch figures deliberately do **not** sum to the total when an opening is unassigned. **Import UI**: [`ItemImportPanel`](src/modules/settings/components/ItemImportPanel.tsx) step 3 grew a *Stock location for opening quantities* dropdown, shown **only when an Opening stock column is mapped**, defaulting to the working branch from the top-bar switcher (or the only branch when there is one) and never guessing between several. It is one value for the whole file, **not a CSV column** — a file is one stock-take at one place. Both [preview](src/app/api/settings/item-import/preview/route.ts) and [commit](src/app/api/settings/item-import/commit/route.ts) carry it; [`productImport.ts`](src/shared/lib/import/productImport.ts) validates the branch **once, up front** (a bad id fails the file rather than surfacing as a raw FK violation halfway through the write pass), stamps it on create only when there is an opening quantity, and on patch only when `openingStock` itself is being written — a rate-only patch is not a restatement of where anything is lying. **Three bugs found on the way and fixed here.** (1) The **combined sentinel** `__combined__` reaches `loadLiveStockMap` through `resolveActiveBranchId` ([products/route.ts:77](src/app/api/inventory/products/route.ts:77)); it was already matching no ledger rows, and with the new column it would also have excluded every branch-stamped opening — turning the whole catalogue to zero on the one screen asking to see everything. `scopeBranch` now normalises it to org-wide, which repairs the pre-existing ledger half too. (2) The **BI movement register** kept its own `!branchId` opening rule and never came through `liveStock.ts`, so a branch-stamped opening would never have appeared there **even at its own branch**; `openingAppliesTo` is now exported and shared, since that report folds the opening itself (its version is date-aware and also needs the prior period). (3) Clearing an opening quantity through [the product form](src/app/api/inventory/products/[id]/route.ts) or [the opening route](src/app/api/inventory/products/[id]/opening/route.ts) left the location behind — a stock location for no stock; both now clear it. `pnpm sync:tables` and `pnpm db:local-schema` regenerated (`resources/local-schema.sql` gains the column, index and FK; the sync registry is unchanged, since a plain nullable TEXT column rides the normal column set and needs no special-casing). `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean on all fourteen touched files, `seed:check-docs` in sync. **Not browser-verified** — another session's dev server holds :3000 and the office routes need the owner's sign-in. **Open, deliberately not done:** the product edit dialog has an opening-stock field but **no location picker**, so a product created by hand opens unassigned; the owner has not yet said whether to add it. A 13-agent branch-mechanism audit run alongside this work also flagged, and this commit does **not** touch: money reports carry no branch filter at all while quantity reports do (no warning on either screen); `requireWritableBranchId` and `resolveActiveBranch` have **zero callers**, so nothing rejects the sentinel on the WRITE path; three ecommerce/online-payment paths hard-code `"BR-HQ"`; and only 13 of 411 API routes consult the active branch at all.

- **2026-09-17 — The contra-signing rule reached the two Masters screens, and the Balance Sheet now says Dr or Cr in words.** A 20-agent audit of the previous commit (5ea02702) found it had left two screens behind. Both aggregate ledgers into group totals **independently of** `buildGroupTree`, so they kept the old per-account signing: [`groups/[id]/detail/route.ts`](src/app/api/accountancy/groups/[id]/detail/route.ts) (Masters → Groups) and [`account-groups/route.ts`](src/app/api/accountancy/account-groups/route.ts) (Masters → Ledgers tree). They had always been wrong; the Trial Balance was wrong the same way, so they matched. Fixing the reports stranded them — Sales **87,728.86** higher, Purchases **29,293.22**, Suspense **34.00** — and the Trial Balance group row **links straight to that page**, so one click gave two numbers, under a code comment still promising it was "guaranteed to agree with the BS / Trial Balance". The rule is now one exported function, [`groupSign`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/groupNature.ts) (pure module, no prisma), used by all three roll-ups. Verified live: Masters tree, group detail and Trial Balance now all read **2,200,152.07 / 1,810,535.73 / 227,608.20** for the three groups. **Second half — the Balance Sheet prints a side, not just a colour.** `AmountCells` rendered any negative as `fmt(Math.abs(n))` in `text-destructive`: no minus sign, no side, and **on a mono printout the red vanishes entirely**, so a contra read as an addition. It now carries the Trial Balance's own Dr/Cr suffix (`SidedCell`'s idiom), with the side taken from `node.type` so a Suspense A/c relocated to Liabilities — which rewrites `type` and flips every sign beneath it together — still reads right. This fixes all ~36 negative rows on the page, not just Cash Over. **What it immediately exposed, and is NOT a regression:** the Balance Sheet prints **Sundry Debtors 38,735.20 Cr** while the top bar calls the same figure **Receivables 38,735.20 (Dr)**. Checked before believing it — Sundry Debtors holds **no contra account at all** (2 DEBIT, 3,679 null, so `groupSign` is +1 for every one) and the ledgers genuinely net to **−38,735.20**, a credit, consistent with the largely-unlinked receipts recorded on 2026-09-16. So the Balance Sheet is right and the two surfaces disagree on the sign of a money figure. **Open, not touched here.** **Also open from the audit:** Ratio Analysis reads the same group totals, so its Sales drops 87,728.86, Purchases 29,293.22 and **Nett Profit 58,435.64** ([ratio-analysis/route.ts:63](src/app/api/accountancy/reports/ratio-analysis/route.ts:63) computes `grossProfit = (sales + directIncomes) - (purchases + directExpenses)`) — the correct net-of-returns figures, but the owner should agree the new profit before it goes near a bank or a return. P&L, Cash Book, Bank Book, Loans Book, Cash Flow, Fund Flow, Usable Fund and the top-bar pills were all confirmed **unchanged to the paisa**. Balance Sheet balances at **1,696,911.53** both sides with the plug at **0.00**; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean.

- **2026-09-17 — A Rs 17 till overage was putting Rs 34 on the Balance Sheet, and returns were being ADDED to sales.** Owner, on the mystery plug: *"opening balance mismatched in different date"*, then *"go find it"*. Hunted in the live DB with `psql` (read-only session) after the preview browser got signed out mid-chase. **It was never an opening balance and never stock** — my first reading, that the inventory valuation was Rs 34 above the books, was wrong and is corrected here. The row on the Balance Sheet called **"Difference in opening balances" is simply `assetBaseline - liabBaseline`** ([balance-sheet/page.tsx:256](src/app/(protected)/office/accountancy/reports/balance-sheet/page.tsx:256)) — a catch-all for ANY imbalance, whatever caused it. The name only describes the *usual* cause. **The actual cause: voucher 3725, a JOURNAL dated 16 Sep 2026** — Dr `Cash – Counter 21 naba – Head Office` 17.00, Cr `Cash Over` 17.00, an ordinary till overage. `Cash Over` is **CREDIT-natured but sits in the ASSET-typed `Suspense A/c` group**. `aggregateClosingAsOf` signs each balance against the ACCOUNT's own nature, so Cash Over was stored as **+17 meaning "17 credit"**, and `buildGroupTree`'s roll-up then added that +17 into the Asset side, where + means **debit**. Cash correctly went up 17; Cash Over pushed assets up 17 instead of down 17; **error = exactly 34**. Proved arithmetically before touching code: liabilities from openings + posted lines = 1,696,911.52 and matched the report **exactly**, while assets came to 850,902.15 against the report's 850,936.15 — and a query for "2 x net movement of every credit-natured ASSET account" returned **-34.00**. All 3,713 vouchers balance individually (checked), so nothing was mis-entered. **The same bug was costing far more on the P&L groups, silently:** `Sales Returns` (DEBIT-normal in CREDIT-natured Sales Accounts) and `Purchase Returns` (CREDIT-normal in DEBIT-natured Purchase Accounts) were being **added** to their groups — the Trial Balance read Sales **22,87,880.93** where net sales are **22,00,152.07**, and Purchases **18,39,828.95** against **18,10,535.73**. It never unbalanced anything because `netProfitLoss` sums raw Dr/Cr by group id and is correct, so only the group subtotals lied. **The repo had already solved this once and the fix never travelled**: `toPeriodOnlyTree`'s docblock names Sales Returns and this exact trap, but it only guards the P&L (and rebuilds from `debits`/`credits`, so it stays correct and untouched). **Fix:** [`buildGroupTree`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/groupTree.ts) re-signs each ledger against its **group's** nature once, at attach time, so every roll-up below is a plain sum of numbers that mean the same thing; `debits`/`credits` stay raw. The Trial Balance page follows: `ledgerSide` and `deriveSummary` now side by the group, not the account. **Ledger rows print identically** — a contra arrives negative and `SidedCell` flips the label itself, so Sales Returns still reads "Dr"; only the group subtotals change. The Dr-Cr difference is algebraically unchanged (adding +X to Dr equals subtracting X from Cr), so the Trial Balance's balance check is unaffected — but its Grand Total now nets returns instead of inflating both sides. Verified against live: asset side 850,936.15 -> **850,902.15**, BS plug 34.00 -> **0.00**, Sales and Purchases to the figures above. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean. **Not browser-verified** — the preview pane's session was revoked ("Your access was updated") and signing it back in is the owner's. **Open, deliberately not renamed here:** the plug row is called "Difference in opening balances" on both reports while catching any imbalance at all, which is what made a September journal entry look like an opening-balance problem. Worth renaming to something honest.

- **2026-09-17 — The credit limit card on a party's Profile now shows the AUTO limit, not just the manual one.** Owner: *"on profile tab credit limit card display auto too"*, then *"we need to see the current auto limit of the customer, manual is already available(editable)"*. The limit actually enforced anywhere is **max(manual, auto)** — [`resolveEffectiveCreditLimit`](src/shared/lib/accountancy/creditLimit.ts) — where auto is `pctOfSales%` of that customer's POSTED SALE/POS totals over the trailing `windowDays`, rounded and capped by the `CreditLimitConfig` singleton. Until now the **only** place that number appeared as money was the POS customer panel, via `/api/pos/account-snapshot/[accountId]`; the office profile knew nothing but `Account.creditLimit`. With live sitting at auto-ON, **1% of 365-day sales capped at ₹1,000** and 168 of 200 sampled contacts holding no manual limit at all, the card was telling the office *"Cash-only — credit sales are blocked at the register"* for customers who in fact had a working auto limit. **That sentence now reads off the effective figure**, so it stops lying, and it tracks the box as you type instead of lagging a save behind. New **thin** route `GET /api/accounts/[id]/credit-limit` returns `{ manual, auto, effective, salesInWindow, autoEnabled, windowDays, pctOfSales }` off the same three exported helpers POS and the sale-time guard use (`loadCreditLimitConfig` / `windowSalesTotal` / `computeAutoCreditLimit`) — one brain, no second formula. Owner asked whether the POS snapshot could simply be reused; it was **deliberately not**, because that endpoint resolves tiers, loyalty, outstandings, turnover and the last three tickets — roughly ten queries — to display two numbers, against §7's *fetch only what you need*. The shared unit is the helper, not the endpoint. Auto off → the sales aggregate is skipped entirely, so the route is one query. [`AccountCreditLimitCard`](src/modules/customer/components/AccountCreditLimitCard.tsx) fetches it on mount and again after a save, and renders **Manual / Auto / Effective** in a small boxed breakup with a footnote naming the rule (*30% of ₹X sales in the last 90 days … change it in Settings → Credit limit*). The box appears **only when auto is in play**; a fetch failure degrades the card to exactly its old manual-only self. Manual stays the one editable half — auto is read-only and belongs to Settings. Both Person and Company profile tabs share the card, so neither page changed. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean with an 8 GB heap (the two `.next/dev/types/validator.ts` complaints are Next's stale generated types). **Not browser-verified** — navigation to :3000 was denied in that session.

- **2026-09-17 — The Trial Balance now counts the stock in the godown, so it agrees with the Balance Sheet.** Owner, with the two reports side by side: *"balance sheet and trail balance not agree"*. On 31/03/2026 the Balance Sheet read **Current Assets 886,369.03** and the Trial Balance **27,179.61**, with a row called **"Difference in opening balances" 859,189.42 Dr** making up the gap. Both grand totals still came to 1,369,764.97, so no money was lost — the two reports were classifying the same rupees differently. **The plug WAS the stock, to the paisa.** Closing stock is never posted per voucher to a GL account (Tally-style, see `postInvVoucher.ts`), so `Stock-in-Hand` is a real group with **no ledger inside it** (`closingStock.postedToGl: 0`, confirmed live) and each report has to inject the valuation itself. [`balance-sheet/route.ts`](src/app/api/accountancy/reports/balance-sheet/route.ts) did; [`trial-balance/route.ts`](src/app/api/accountancy/reports/trial-balance/route.ts) did not, so the TB's Dr side was short by the whole value of the shop and its plug absorbed it — 510,575.55 Dr against 1,369,764.97 Cr, a slack of exactly 859,189.42. The Balance Sheet has **the same plug row** and it simply never fired. The clue that it was structural rather than a coincidence: the loans that paid for the stock are on the books, the stock they bought is not. **Fix, in one place rather than two:** new `sumGroupLedgers` / `injectLedger` in [`groupTree.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/groupTree.ts), and the Balance Sheet's hand-rolled walk-and-inject is deleted in favour of them — when those two drifted apart is exactly when the reports stopped agreeing. The Trial Balance values stock at **both** ends through the existing `computeTradingStockAdjustment` (opening as at the instant before `from`, closing as at `to`), so its Opening column balances. **The half that is easy to miss:** the TB has an Opening column the BS does not, so stock *movement* needs a home too — otherwise the period's consumption lands straight back in the plug. Owner chose the trading entry over renaming the row, so the page carries **"Opening less Closing Stock"**, the same trading-account figure the P&L already computes. **First attempt only balanced periods starting 1 April**; testing mid-year ranges caught it. Income and expense ledgers carry their *year-to-date* figure in the Opening column, so the trading row is measured from the financial-year start (`closingStock.atFyStart`) for both columns, and `deriveSummary` now returns `openingDiff` **and** `closingDiff` separately — they were one number on the reasoning that every JE balances, which stock breaks because it is a valuation, not a journal entry. **Verified read-only against live** through the signed-in preview browser across seven ranges — FY-aligned, a single mid-year day, a mid-year month, a window where stock *rose* so the row goes Cr (−14,297.46), and 2023→date — **all balance in both columns**, where four of them did not before. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean on all four touched files, `seed:check-docs` in sync. `BookPage` was checked first and is unaffected (it only ever renders Cash / Bank / Loan groups). **Still open, found on the way and NOT fixed:** on today's date the **Balance Sheet** carries a plug of its own of **₹34.00** — assets exceed liabilities by that much, meaning the live inventory valuation is ₹34 more than the books imply. That is a data question, not this code path, and the offending voucher has not been identified.

- **2026-09-16 — A bill-wise link made on one tab now reaches the tab beside it, instead of waiting for a reload.** Owner, after linking on **JSK Computer → Outstanding**: *"i did in outstanding tab but the transaction tab not updated"* — the statement still showed `Due` on payments the link had settled, and *"when we save/link within a second it got updated, same we need when we go to any tab/page"*. The data was never wrong: the server already returned `SETTLED` for `UV-3139`, `UV-2763`, `UV-2312`; only the screen was old. **The cause is the change channel's own rule** — a Supabase broadcast does not echo to the sender (`appChannel.ts`), and the axios publisher already maps `/api/accountancy/` to `ledger` ([`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts)), so **every other window was told and the acting one never was**. That is deliberate: the acting window "already knows, and updates through its own save path". The hole was that [`PartyOutstandingsPanel`](src/modules/accountancy/components/PartyOutstandingsPanel.tsx) had no save path back to the page it sits on — it could refresh itself and nothing else. It now takes `onChanged`, fired after a link save and after a receipt / payment, and the three customer pages ([all](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/all/page.tsx), [person](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/person/page.tsx), [companies](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/companies/page.tsx)) mark the statement stale on it. **A server-side** `publishChangeServer(["ledger"])` **was written and then withdrawn** — the client publisher already covers every other machine, so it would only have added a second broadcast per link. The same pages also stopped **throwing away** a real `ledger` signal that arrives while the statement is off screen: the listener used to be gated to `tab === "transactions"`, so a link made on another counter was silently dropped and the auto-load then skipped because rows were already in state. Now off-screen marks stale and the tab re-reads on the way in, **quietly**, over the rows already there — no fetch for a hidden tab (§7), no spinner where a table just was. The reverse direction is closed too: linking from the **Transactions** chip clears the Outstanding tab's party cache (`clearPartyCache`, now exported), which would otherwise remount onto a 60s-old entry showing the bill still open.

- **2026-09-18 — The bill-wise chip drops its chain where there is nothing to link, everywhere it appears.** Owner: *"remove the chain icon if nothing existing to link"* — a `Due` row on a party with no unplaced money on the other side still offered the chain, and the popup opened empty. The same rule `BillLinkStatus` already used on the party rows now decides the voucher chip: [`loadSettlements`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) returns **`canLink`** alongside `state`/`open`, worked out from `loadPartyUnallocated` for the parties of the page's unsettled rows only — a Dr row needs the party's open `cr`, a Cr row its open `dr`. Every screen that shows the chip gets it from the same place: Day Book, the GL / inventory / journal registers, orders-estimates and the party's own Transactions tab (that one computes it directly, over ALL history rather than the period on screen, because the popup offers the whole ledger). [`SettlementChip`](src/modules/accountancy/components/SettlementChip.tsx) needed no change — it already renders a plain chip when no `onLink` is passed. **Verified on live against the independent path**: for eight parties, summing `loadLinkableDocs` by side gave exactly the `canLink` the new rule produced (Hitesh Khura, dr 31,810 *and* cr 36,810 → clickable; Subhas da Photostate, dr 5,334 with nothing on the other side → 161 plain chips, none clickable).
  - **`loadPartyUnallocated` became one SQL aggregate** in the same change. It used to read **every GL line of every named party** into JS and add them up there; on a Day Book page of forty parties that cost ~10s. It is now a single grouped query — legs per (account, voucher), minus what is linked from either end while the other end is alive — which is the same rule, stated once, in the place the data already is. The `linked` CTE is bounded to the vouchers `legs` named; without that bound it aggregated the whole allocation table and was slower than the loop it replaced. Receivables, Payables, `/api/person`, `/api/companies` and the new chip all ride on it.
  - **Cost, measured on live:** a Day Book page went from ~1.0s to ~2.2s.
  - **Then the Day Book filter was re-pointed at the same rule (same day).** Owner: *"it should show unsettled only, those has chain icon"* — the tick was keeping every `Due` / `Partial` row, and most of them had no chain, so the list was mostly work nobody could do. It now keeps exactly the linkable rows. New [`loadLinkWorkParties`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) names the parties with open money on BOTH sides — **7 on live, out of 238 with open rows** — and the scan is narrowed to their vouchers in SQL before a settlement is measured, so the filter got FASTER as it got stricter: **~5.2s → ~2.4s**, and the whole FY's link work is 88 rows over two pages. The filter is also renamed **Unsettled only** (§ `settlement=open` is unchanged on the wire), because "Unpaid" read as though it were about bills alone while it covers every voucher with a party — a receipt or payment sitting on no bill included.
- **2026-09-16 — A party now says on its own whether anything is waiting to be linked, so the popup is opened only where there is work.** Owner: *"how we know that some thing pending to link, click status of every party to check/link is time wasting"* — every row on Receivables read `Overdue 138d` with a chain icon, and opening one answered *"Nothing open on the other side for this party"*. The question behind it was the one before: *"if a party ledger balance != outstanding bill means unsettled bill?"* **No.** A receipt is never a bill ([`loadOpenBills`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/outstandings.ts) sweeps SALE / POS / PURCHASE / CREDIT_NOTE / DEBIT_NOTE / OPENING only), so an **unlinked** receipt is fully in the ledger and wholly absent from the bill list. Sale 4,000 with an unlinked receipt 3,000 → ledger 1,000 Dr, outstanding 4,000. The gap is not an unsettled bill, it is **unplaced money**. New [`loadPartyUnallocated`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) is [`loadLinkableDocs`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) read for many parties at once — same party-leg-minus-linked rule, rolled up per account — giving `dr`, `cr` and **`toLink = min(dr, cr)`**, the part that can actually be matched. `dr − cr` is the ledger balance, so `toLink = 0` is exactly the party whose bill list already agrees with its ledger. **One aggregate for a whole report** (two queries when it was written; folded into a single grouped SQL read on 2026-09-18), not four per row: `loadLinkableDocs` per party would be 732 queries on a 183-party Receivables. It rides on `PartyOutstanding.toLink` and `LedgerOutstanding.toLink`, so no screen asks the server anything new — the bill-wise tab reads the party rows the same payload already carries. New [`ToLinkChip`](src/modules/accountancy/components/ToLinkChip.tsx) marks the party row; [`BillLinkStatus`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BillLinkStatus.tsx) now shows its chain icon **only** when the party has something on the other side, and stays clickable regardless because the popup is also the way to **Receive now**; [`PartyOutstandingsPanel`](src/modules/accountancy/components/PartyOutstandingsPanel.tsx) opens with one line — amber `₹X to link` or green *Fully linked*. Overdue and unlinked are kept apart on purpose: Prodip Kr Das is ₹11,920 overdue and reads **Fully linked**, because there is no money of his sitting anywhere. Measured on live the day it was written: **139 of 183** debtors carry link work worth **₹5,89,154** against ₹5,86,818 outstanding — Akbor Ali Ahemad has a ₹44,350 sale and receipts of ₹20,000 + ₹24,350 against it, none of them linked, so his ledger reads 0.00 while his bill reads 44,350. The book is very largely unlinked, and the marks say where. **Then the party lists too** — owner: *"in the party list give hint so i know, pending to link, it may be a Brocken chain or any icon"*. `ToLinkChip` grew a `compact` shape: a bare amber **`Unlink2`** — a chain with a piece missing — beside the balance chip on All Customers, Person and Companies, with the amount in the tooltip, because those rows already carry a name, a phone and a balance. The number rides on `/api/person` and `/api/companies` behind **`?withLinkHint=1`**, opt-in for the same reason `paginate` is: both routes also feed the till's party cache, `referenceStore` and every voucher picker, and none of those draw the mark — a plain call still comes back with no `toLink` key at all. `usePartyBrowseList` asks for it always, since its only two callers are the two browse lists.

- **2026-09-16 — The replica's own "the rows are in" is now a subscription of its own, and the party statement listens to it.** Owner's report, from Counter 94 (`R:Local · W:Office`): on **Customers → Prodip Kr Das → Transactions**, receipt 320 showed the red **Due** chip and sale 928 read **Partial ₹16,600**, while the bill-link popup opened on the same sale said **₹11,600 to place** and **"Nothing open on the other side"**. The ₹5,000 gap was receipt 320 exactly. **No arithmetic bug:** [`customerTransactions.ts`](src/modules/customer/lib/customerTransactions.ts) calls the very same `sumLinked` the popup's server uses, so the two cannot really disagree — the owner pressed Refresh and the screen came to 11,600 / Settled on its own. The books were right all along (₹11,600 + ₹320 open = his ₹11,920 Dr), and "nothing on the other side" was right too: both pending rows are **sales**, and a link is only legal between opposite sides, which is why the popup offers *Receive now* instead. What was wrong was that the screen never noticed its own replica had caught up. **Owner's rule, stated plainly:** *"Realtime message tells the sync engine to update replica. but we need a pure local subscription that fire only on replica update purely local"* — and *"keep existing for web and live"*. **The signal already carried everything needed and was being thrown away.** `sync-once.js:672` prints `__SYNC_APPLIED__ {rows, models}` after committing, `syncManager.ts:173` forwards it, `preload.ts:163` and [`syncApplied.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/syncApplied.ts) turn it into a window event — and `useRealtimeTopics`'s listener was `const onApplied = () => schedule()`, discarding `info.models` entirely. New [`onReplicaUpdate(fn, models?)`](src/shared/lib/realtime/syncApplied.ts) is that source on its own: the replica's commit and nothing else, never the Supabase nudge (which only ever means "engine, come and pull" and fires while the replica is still behind). New hook [`useReplicaUpdate`](src/shared/hooks/useReplicaUpdate.ts) wraps it for counter-only surfaces. `useRealtimeTopics` now **composes** the two — local half `onReplicaUpdate`, web/live half the Supabase channel exactly as before — and both feed one [`createSettledReader`](src/shared/lib/realtime/settledReader.ts), so a window carrying both sources still does one read per burst and one read on returning to it, not two. Behaviour for all 129 existing call sites is unchanged. **`models` filtering is built but off by default, deliberately**: `useRealtimeTopics.ts` had refused topic filtering on the grounds that a hand-kept map "silently narrows what refreshes as the schema grows, which is exactly how the worker's own topic map rotted" — that objection stands, so narrowing is opt-in, and the names are the generated `syncRegistry.generated.ts` model names rather than a second hand-made map. **Rolled out so far to the two screens that caused the report** — `customers/person` and `customers/companies`, which are later clones of `customers/all` **with its two subscription lines left out** (`all/page.tsx:175,263`). Their `loadTransactions` gained a `quiet` flag first: it used to set `txLoading`, which replaces the whole table with a spinner, so a background re-read would have blanked a statement the owner was reading — AGENTS.md §7, show cached data while refreshing. A quiet read also raises no toast, since a background failure is not something the user did. **The wider gap is measured and open: 177 office pages, 30 subscribed** (settings 28/0, bi 21/0, accountancy 62/19, inventory 15/4). There is no shared data layer to fix it in one place — no SWR or react-query, and `useApiResource` (which already splits `loading` from `refreshing` correctly) has only 4 callers, the rest hand-rolling `useEffect` + axios. An app-wide audit of the remaining screens is running; batches will be money-first, and forms/optimistic surfaces are to be left alone. `tsc --noEmit` clean at exit 0 with an 8 GB heap, eslint clean on all seven touched files. **Not browser-verified** — proving it needs two windows (Head Office + a counter), which is the owner's machine.

- **2026-09-16 — A bill's Status is now the way into its bill-wise links, on Receivables, Payables and a party's Outstanding tab.** Owner: *"in customer module if we click on status we can edit/update bill wise link, we want to do same in payable/receivables"*, then *"also same in customer profile outstanding tab"*. The customer's **Transactions** tab has always opened [`BillLinkDialog`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BillLinkDialog.tsx) from its Settled / Partial / Due chip — money looking for its bills. The three outstanding screens showed the same kind of Status (`Within 12d` / `Overdue 138d`) as **dead text**, so the bill could not look for its money: the only affordance on the row was the ₹ hand, and that **makes new money** — useless when the receipt already exists and is sitting unlinked as an advance. New [`BillLinkStatus`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BillLinkStatus.tsx) is that cell — same words, same ageing colour, plus a chain icon and a side-aware tooltip ("money already received" on a receivable, "already paid" on a payable, read off the bill's own `side`, so the component needs no telling which report it is on). It is **presentation only**: each screen still hosts the popup, because what happens after a save differs — [`OutstandingsReportScreen`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OutstandingsReportScreen.tsx) (Receivables **and** Payables, one screen) re-reads the whole report, [`PartyOutstandingsPanel`](src/modules/accountancy/components/PartyOutstandingsPanel.tsx) also drops that party's 60s cache; both clear the bill-link cache, so a bill the link has just settled leaves the list instead of sitting there stale. The report carries the party alongside the bill (`{accountId, name, voucherId}`) because it lists every party at once, where the profile tab already knows whose bills these are. **No API, schema, seed or permission change, and no extra reading cost** — every bill row already carried its `accountId`, `accountName` and spine id, opening-balance rows included, which is why those are linkable too; a pre-spine row has no id a link could point at and stays plain words. The ₹ hand, the party drill-down and the Party-wise tab are untouched. `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean; **not yet opened in the browser** — the preview pane is not signed in to the office, so the visual pass is the owner's.
