- **2026-09-16 — A party's opening balance now takes the Delete tick, not the Edit tick.** Owner: *"in customer module opening balance, credit limit, delete should be gated via department-role page tick"*, then, after seeing where each one actually stood: *"everything already exist, move opening balance to delete level permission gated, dont hide just disable, who can delete customer can edit customer opening balance too"*. **Two of the three were already done.** `customers:delete` has its own tick on the Role page (Contacts row → Delete), [`DeletePartyCard`](src/modules/customer/components/DeletePartyCard.tsx) hides itself without it, and both `DELETE /api/person/[id]` and `DELETE /api/companies/[id]` call `requireAnyPermission(CUSTOMERS_DELETE)` — so Delete needed nothing. Credit limit rides `customers:edit` and the owner left it there. **Opening balance was the real gap.** It sat inside the same `<fieldset disabled={!canEditCustomer}>` as the credit limit and the classification card in both [`PersonProfileTab`](src/modules/customer/components/PersonProfileTab.tsx) and [`CompanyProfileTab`](src/modules/customer/components/CompanyProfileTab.tsx), with `canEdit` passed as a **hardcoded `true`** — so the only gate on it was plain `customers:edit`, the same tick that lets a junior fix a phone number, and `PATCH /api/accounts/[id]` enforced exactly one `hasPermission(user, CUSTOMERS_EDIT)` across credit limit, account group and opening balance together. Rewriting what a party owed before the books started moves real money the same way removing the party does, so the two now ride one tick: the opening-balance card moved into its own `<fieldset disabled={!canDeleteCustomer}>` (the `canEditCustomer` cards split either side of it, their shared grey-out props lifted to one local `editGate` so they are not written twice), and the route grew a **second, field-scoped** check — `data.openingBalance !== undefined` requires `CUSTOMERS_DELETE` — placed after `validateRequest` because the payload has to be parsed before the field can be seen. **Greyed, never hidden**, per the owner: [`AccountOpeningBalanceCard`](src/modules/customer/components/AccountOpeningBalanceCard.tsx) used to swap its whole form for a "you don't have permission" line; it now always renders the amount, the Dr/Cr select and the Bill-wise button, lets the parent fieldset disable them, and appends one sentence to its own description instead — the same shape [`CustomerQualityTab`](src/modules/customer/components/CustomerQualityTab.tsx) already used for the no-discount flag. The figure therefore stays readable to anyone who can open the profile, which is the point. **No new permission and no migration** — nothing was added to `PERMISSION_CATALOG`, only the `customers:delete` description rewritten to say it now carries the opening balance too, so a reseed is cosmetic and the counters need no release before the deploy. **Two things found on the way and deliberately NOT changed**, both recorded here rather than fixed: `PUT /api/accountancy/accounts/[id]/opening-bills` — the **bill-wise** opening balance, and since 2026-09-16 the form all 64 live parties are held in — has only `requireAuth()` and no permission check at all, reachable from Accountancy → Opening Balance and Edit Ledger as well as from the card's own Bill-wise button (disabled here by the new fieldset, but the route stays open); and `PATCH /api/settings/credit-limit`, the auto-limit calculator singleton, is likewise `requireAuth()`-only by an explicit comment ("Settings pages are sidebar-gated; the write API stays open to any staff"). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean at exit 0 with an 8 GB heap, eslint clean on all four touched files. **Not browser-verified** — the dev server on :3000 answers the office routes only when signed in, and the negative case needs a role holding `customers:edit` without `customers:delete`, which is the owner's to click.

- **2026-09-16 — One EPBX extension may now sit on several counters, because one line can be wired to several phones.** Owner: *"counter has epbx ext, extension can be shared among counters using parallel connection, so allow one ext to multiple counter"*. This **reverses one rule** of the 2026-09-14 entry below: that change let one counter hold several extensions, but still refused to let one extension be held by several counters — on the reasoning that a call would otherwise pop up at both. On a parallel line it **rings** at both, so popping up at both is the correct behaviour, not a fault. The refusal lived in exactly one place, a hand-written loop in [`PATCH /api/pos/counters/[id]`](src/app/api/pos/counters/[id]/route.ts) that read every other counter's list and named the clashing extension; it is gone, and with it the last enforcement of uniqueness anywhere (the DB index went on 2026-09-14). Nothing is keyed on this field — the voucher prefix and the offline id-range come from `counterNumber`/branch — so a shared value costs nothing downstream: `useMyCounterExtension` still finds THIS PC's counter by `boundDeviceId`, and `GET /api/calls?ext=` simply answers the same call to each counter that holds the extension. Admin help text and the `pos.prisma` comment both stated the old rule and are corrected.
  - **The real work was the replica, not the API.** A counter PC provisioned before 2026-09-14 still carries `pos_counters_epbxExtension_key`, the old unique index — `resources/local-schema.sql` is a from-empty snapshot that can only ADD, so a re-apply never removed it, and the counter's `schema_hash` check sees nothing wrong. The 2026-09-14 entry called this residual *harmless, since two counters would need identical lists to trip it*. **Sharing makes identical lists the ordinary case**, so that dormant index would have started failing `pos_counters` writes on exactly the old tills, and a table that halts halts the whole till (`schemaGuard.js`). So the generator gained a **`RETIRED_INDEXES`** list ([`scripts/gen-local-schema.ts`](scripts/gen-local-schema.ts)) that emits a `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS` per entry into the generated SQL, which every counter re-applies on start — no hand work on any till. The list is deliberately keyed to when a removal starts to **matter**, not when the rule was dropped, and may only name objects this schema no longer creates.
  - **Ordering, for the release.** The counter build carrying the DROP must reach the tills **before** two counters are given the same extension — an old till that gets the shared value first still has the index. Verified: `local-schema.sql` regenerated (114 tables, 108 triggers) with the DROP present at the end of the snapshot.
- **2026-09-16 — A receipt now settles the bills it paid, and an edit stops destroying the tags that say a bill was paid in cash.** Owner reported the receivables engine over-stating what customers owe, with two causes confirmed against live. D Adhikary (account 7740) was the example: five POSTED receipts worth ₹1,375, **zero** `VoucherAllocation` rows, several of them same-day matches to a sale — so `getLedgerOutstanding` saw six open bills totalling ₹1,370 and the POS panel shouted "Overdue ₹1,370" at a customer whose ledger was **16.00 Cr**. The owner had already repaired that one party by hand through the bill-link popup on 15 Sep (every link stamped 12:54–20:04, one at a time); a sweep of the deployed books found the repair had stopped there — **413 of 483 posted settlements were unlinked, ₹27,00,512 across 177 parties**, dates running right up to 15 Sep, so the pile was still growing daily. Payments were the bigger half (156 vouchers, ₹19,84,331) — the payables side had the identical disease and nobody had looked.
  - **Cause 1: only one door in five ever wrote a link.** `postReceiptVoucher` has taken an `allocations` array since the bill-link feature shipped, and the POS sale's collect-on-the-go surplus ([`/api/pos/sale`](src/app/api/pos/sale/route.ts)) fills it. The two plain "party is settling up" doors never did — the counter's receipt panel ([`/api/pos/shifts/[id]/receipts`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/%5Bid%5D/receipts/route.ts)) and the office Receipt/Payment form ([`/api/accountancy/vouchers`](src/app/api/accountancy/vouchers/route.ts)). The online-payment and POS-order receipts are unlinked **on purpose** (they are advances until the invoice is raised), so the hole was exactly two doors wide. Both now link on save. **This is not decoration: since 253eca21 the credit limit is enforced on sales and reads the same figure, so a customer who paid every time could be refused a credit bill for money they had already handed over.**
  - **Linking runs AFTER the voucher's own transaction, never inside it — the decision the whole design turns on.** Every refusal `assertLinkPlan` can raise is a `BillLinkError` thrown in the caller's transaction: a deactivated party ledger, a party outside Sundry Debtors/Creditors, a bill someone settled a moment earlier. Hooked into the save, any of them would roll back **the receipt**, not the link — the cashier loses the entry with the cash already in the drawer. So [`autoLinkSettlement`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/autoLinkSettlement.ts) is a second transaction that runs once the voucher is committed and posted, and **never throws at its caller**: a refusal comes back as `skipped` and the voucher stays unlinked, which is what every one of them used to be anyway. Standing apart is also what lets it use the **full validated path** rather than the unchecked shortcut `postSettlementVoucher` must take inside its own tx, hold the same `pg_advisory_xact_lock(4711, accountId)` the manual popup holds, and take 30s on a party with a long history instead of the caller's 5s default.
  - **It invented no new planner.** [`planAutoLink`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/autoLinkPlan.ts) is `loadLinkableDocs` + `planLinks` and nothing else — the party's documents read off **their own GL legs**, and the house order (overdue first, then nearest due, smaller bill on a tie). Two things fall out of that choice for free: it is **side-agnostic**, so a PAYMENT against purchase bills runs the identical path as a RECEIPT against sales (the payables half of `postSettlementVoucher` had existed with zero callers since it was written); and it **sees bill-wise OPENING vouchers**, which the counter's older `getOpenSaleBillsForFifo` cannot — and every party on live was converted to bill-wise openings on 2026-09-16, so a counter receipt clearing a carried-forward balance would otherwise have allocated nothing. Auto settles only bill-shaped documents (`SALE / POS / PURCHASE / OPENING / CREDIT_NOTE / DEBIT_NOTE`); netting one settlement against another is a judgement about two payments and stays a person's job in the popup.
  - **Cause 2: an edit silently destroyed the tender tags, for good.** [`reapplyTradingVoucherInPlace`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/reapplyTradingVoucher.ts) deleted a voucher's GL lines and rewrote them copying five fields — `accountId, type, amount, description, seq` — while the builder had set `role`, `method`, `reference` **and** `branchId`. Nothing complained; the lines came back looking right with the tags blank. So any trading voucher edited even once lost its `role=TENDER` marks permanently, and **a tag has no ledger leg behind it, so nothing can ever rebuild one**. The damage is not where it first looks: the Receivables/Payables report was moved onto the party leg by 30fa4e4f and is safe, but `getOpenSaleBillsForFifo` still reads `total − role=TENDER − links` with no leg guard, so **edit a cash sale once and it reappears at the counter as fully open for its whole value** — and, being the FIFO list, the next over-payment gets allocated against that phantom. Reprints lost their "Paid" the same way ([`voucherReceiptSample`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/voucherReceiptSample.ts)), and `method`/`reference` took the UPI/cheque reference needed to match a bank statement with them.
  - **Fixed as one mapper, not three added fields.** `spec.glLines` and the engine's own lines are both already `EngineGlLine`, so the two inlined `.map`s that had drifted are now one exported [`glLineRow`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/transactionEngine.ts) — a field added there reaches every writer and no writer can quietly omit one. An edited voucher now comes out byte-for-byte like a freshly posted one. The two **financial** writers (`engineCreateVoucher`, the voucher PATCH) deliberately stay as they are: they carry `method`/`reference` already, and `role` is null on generic financial legs by design.
  - **The backlog: [`pnpm reconcile:bill-links`](src/scripts/reconcile-bill-links.ts), dry run by default.** It plans with **the very same `planAutoLink` the live doors call** — a dry run built on a parallel implementation would predict nothing — and prints per party what each settlement would clear, down to which bill and how much of its open room, with anything left over named as the advance it stays. `--fix` writes; `--receipts` / `--payments` / `--party=` / `--limit=` narrow it. In dry run it carries a `reserved` map so two receipts against one bill are not each shown clearing it in full; under `--fix` that map stays empty because each link is committed before the next voucher is planned. It also counts the settlements that name **no party at all** and points at `pnpm repair:bill-links`, which is the script that stamps the party on and has clearly never been run with `--fix` — 22 such vouchers, ₹2,19,765.
  - **`backfillTenderRoles` never existed.** The comment at `loadTenderLines` promised historical lines were tagged by it; the helper is nowhere in the repo and only the sentence survived. Both that comment and `getOpenSaleBillsForFifo`'s claim to "mirror `loadOpenBills`'s paid math exactly" (untrue since 30fa4e4f) now say what is actually the case.
  - **No migration and no sync regeneration:** `role`/`seq`/`method`/`reference` are already columns (`finance.prisma:190-197`) and already in the replica DDL; `gen-sync-registry --check` and `gen-local-schema --check` both pass with the change in place, because the generator reads the schema and is blind to code paths. `VoucherAllocation` already syncs (append on `createdAt`, capture trigger present).
  - **Before the first `--fix`, every till must be pushed and idle.** `VoucherAllocation` pushes upsert by primary key, but the table also carries `@@unique([targetType, targetId, sourceType, sourceId])` — a till holding an unpushed link for a pair this script also writes will dead-letter on that constraint, and one failing row stalls that till's whole 50-row drain, not just this table. There is also **no live/demo guard in any script in that folder**: `DIRECT_URL` resolves to live Supabase here, so `--fix` writes the real books on the first run with no prompt. The header says both, and `--fix` prints the host it is about to write to.
  - _Verified: `tsc` clean; dry run exercised on live for account 7740 (correctly reports all five receipts already linked, nothing to do) and across the whole book. **Nothing has been written to live** — the `--fix` run, and the decision on whether to move `getOpenSaleBillsForFifo` onto the party-leg reading, are the owner's._

- **2026-09-16 — The party box searches the whole customer list now, because every smaller pool it used to search was hiding people.** Owner: *"simplify pos party dropdown. its missing party"*, with two screenshots. On the office browser `6000456884` listed eight unrelated parties and not **Prodip Kr Das**, whom the Contacts page finds by that same number on that same machine. On the counter app `d adhika` listed six Adhikarys and not **D Adhikary**, whom the dev browser leads with. `git log v1.3.7..HEAD` shows no search-logic change between the packaged till and dev, so the two screens differed by **environment**, not version — and the two misses had two different causes with one shape: **the ranking never ran over the whole list.**
  - **The browser fault.** `searchParties` called `searchPartyRows` without `fallback`, which defaults to `true`, so when the grown cache matched nothing it padded the list to eight rows with **letter-pair guesses** — `6000456884` shares digit pairs with almost any phone. `usePartySearch` then read eight rows as "a full page" and **never asked the server**, on the reasoning that the server caps at eight too. It does, but over a different pool: the cache's best eight came from the regulars, the server's from everybody. So any customer this browser had not already looked up was unreachable from **every** party box in the app — and because the highlight resets to row 0, **Enter billed whichever stranger the guess put first**, with the green "only one match" ring around them when a single guess survived.
  - **The counter fault.** A replica-served till has no Dexie at all, so it asked `/api/person?lookup=`, which did `take: 8, orderBy createdAt desc` **before** it ranked. More than eight people match "adhika", so the eight *newest* were the only candidates and D Adhikary — an older row — never left Postgres. The 2026-09-11 comment above that code says "Rank before cutting"; the cut it describes was still happening, one step earlier, in SQL.
  - **Owner's design, and it removes both by construction:** *"load full list to memory every time, from dexi in web and replica in app… we can use dexi for both case so same code, in local dexi read replica."* One module now owns the list — [`partyCatalog.ts`](src/shared/lib/parties/partyCatalog.ts), rewritten from the grown cache it replaces — held in memory, backed by **its own Dexie database** (`abeer-parties`), filled from `GET /api/parties/catalog?sync=1&since=…` which on the web reads live and on a till reads that machine's own replica. Same code, same request, both places. Search is then `searchPartyRows` over the whole list: no request per keystroke, no debounce, no cache-versus-server ladder, no double ranking. ~3,500 parties, about a megabyte once, then deltas. **And the looser tiers finally mean something** — a typo, a run-together `medhidura`, or "nothing matched, here are the closest" is honest over a complete pool and was pure artefact over a partial one.
  - **Its own database, on purpose.** [`browser/localDb`](src/shared/lib/browser/localDb.ts) deliberately deletes its cache on a replica-served till (2026-09-07, below) and four other caches depend on that. This list wants the opposite — held **everywhere**, because holding it is what makes the search correct, not what makes it fast. A separate Dexie DB lets both rules stand without either module knowing about the other; `localDb` v5 drops the `parties`/`partyMeta` stores it no longer owns.
  - **Delta freshness was the hard half, and it was broken twice on the one field that matters.** `Phone` and `Email` had `createdAt` and no `updatedAt`, so `gen-sync-registry` filed them **`append`**: a till received *new* phones but never an edited or deleted one, and the hourly reconcile compares an append table **on keys alone**, so a corrected number stayed wrong at the counter forever. Independently, `contactWrite.ts` never touched the parent row, so a phone change did not move `Person.updatedAt` — which is exactly what the catalogue delta asks about, so the browser's copy missed it too. **The browser half is closed on this branch:** new `touchOwner` bumps the owning Person/Company inside the same transaction on every create / update / delete / verify path, which is what makes a contact change visible to the catalogue delta at all — and a **deletion** can be reported no other way, since the phone row is gone. **The replica half is written but HELD**, on branch `hold/contact-updated-at`: migration `20260916090000_contact_updated_at` adds `updatedAt` to `Phone` and `Email`, reclassifying them to `watermark` (numbers 59 and 29 unchanged, so no replica's stored state is orphaned), following `call_updated_at` exactly — **`CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` backfill and not `createdAt`**, so each till sorts past its stale append mark and re-drains once. Held deliberately (owner, 2026-09-16): once the schema carries the column, Prisma selects it and **every party screen errors until the migration is applied to live** — and applying it to live ahead of the tills halts their sync outright, so it can only travel with a counter release. Until it lands, a phone **edited or deleted** in the office still does not reach a counter replica; a newly **added** one does.
  - **Deleted, not replaced:** the grown cache, `/api/parties/changed` (the catalogue delta covers it), the entire `lookupAccounts` engine and the `lookup` / `phone` / `group` / `wide` / `full` params on `/api/person` — the `?q=` browse branch and `POST` stay for the Contacts and Companies pages — plus `partyFullCatalog.ts` and two orphan party pickers (`PartyCombobox.tsx`, `components/PartyPicker/`). `wide` had been a documented no-op since 2026-09-11 with four call sites still passing it.
  - **Two bugs fell out of the same change.** Browse-on-focus — clicking the empty POS box to see the group's list — asked `/api/person?lookup=` with an **empty query**, which scores nothing and was then filtered to the ranked set, so it returned `[]` every time and **has never once worked**; it now reads the held list. And the exact-name check before the create dialog searched only the eight ranked hits, so a party whose name you typed exactly could be the ninth and get duplicated; it now scans the whole list.
  - **Three defects found by re-reading the refactor, since none of it could be run** (both databases were unreachable from the dev machine and the app needs a login). **(1)** `usePartyBrowseList` fell back to held parties whenever the server returned **zero** rows — so a filter that genuinely matches nobody painted the cached list as though those were the results, and kept painting it. "No rows" is an answer; the placeholders now stand only until the page for *these* filters has actually landed (`landedKey === filterKey`). **(2)** `invalidateParties` — called the instant a party is created — joined any sync already in flight, which may have read the database **before** that party existed, so the one search guaranteed to happen next could miss them. It now waits the in-flight one out and forces a fresh read. **(3)** Every party picker asked for a delta **on mount**, and the app is full of them (a voucher form carries three), so moving between screens was a delta plus two `COUNT`s each time for a list realtime already keeps current. Mount-time reads are throttled to 30s; a change signal and a write pass `force` and are never delayed.
  - **Next step, when the held half ships.** `git cherry-pick hold/contact-updated-at`, then `pnpm prisma generate`. **Release order is not optional:** the counter build goes out **before** the Vercel deploy carrying the migration, because a column the server has and a replica lacks is `missing` to `schemaGuard.compare`, which halts **every table** on that till with exit 4. Note `20260914090000_counter_multiple_epbx_extensions` also still reads as pending on live, so that deploy applies two migrations.
  - _Owner to confirm on a real counter: edit a customer phone in the office, then find them at the till by the new number; and that the first sync log after upgrade says `strategy changed (append -> watermark)` once._

- **2026-09-16 — A counter bill can go on credit, and the office sale finally obeys the limit it was always shown.** Owner asked whether POS blocks credit even when the customer has a limit. It did, and the limit had nothing to do with it: **four** separate gates refused any unpaid amount before `checkCreditLimit` was ever consulted — the checkout dialog's `onCredit > 0.01` in `confirmBlocked`, two toasts in [`PosScreen`](src/modules/pos/components/PosScreen.tsx) ("needs at least one paid line", "must be paid in full"), and a 422 in [`/api/pos/sale`](src/app/api/pos/sale/route.ts) telling the cashier to go and use `/sale`. The credit-limit machinery at the counter was therefore **decoration**: `checkCreditLimit(tx, accountId, onCredit)` was called with an `onCredit` the route itself guaranteed to be 0, as its own comment admitted, and the limit's only real job was drawing "Avail. credit" on the customer panel. All four gates are gone. Whatever the counter does not collect — including collecting **nothing at all**, which the owner asked for explicitly — rides the customer's own ledger as an AR tender, and the **only** thing that can refuse a bill now is the effective limit, `max(manual, auto)`, weighed against the customer's effective balance. `payments` may be an empty array (the schema demanded `min(1)`), and the two stale "POS enforces exact pay, so this is always 0" comments on the AR-tender branch are now descriptions of something that happens.
  - **Half of it was already built and waiting.** The AR tender leg, the `arRemainder > 0.005` branch, [`outstandings.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/outstandings.ts)' POS-side receivables (whose comment already anticipated "credit-on-POS sales (partial payments, future feature)"), the ticket timeline's "paid in full / partly on credit / fully on credit", and the printed bill — [`InvoiceReceipt`](src/shared/components/print/InvoiceReceipt.tsx) already excludes the party's own ledger from the received rows and emits `balanceDue`, which the thermal template prints as **Balance due**. None of that needed touching. Shift cash needed nothing either: the till figure is re-summed from GL lines, and an AR tender never debits the till.
  - **Two holes opened by unlocking it, both closed.** The client guard treated a null limit as *unlimited* and returned "no breach" — harmless while nothing could go on credit, a green Confirm on a walk-in afterwards. It is deny-by-default now: no customer, no limit, no credit. And `checkCreditLimit` itself refuses an **anonymous ledger** (`personId` and `companyId` both null — the seeded walk-in cash customer, a counter ledger) before it even reads the limit, so a stray number typed onto that ledger cannot open a door to nameless credit. New code `NO_NAMED_CUSTOMER`, deliberately carrying no figures, because no limit can fix it.
  - **The office now runs the same check, from the same function.** Next-up item 13, closed. On an office sale the unpaid part is a tender row pointing at the party's own ledger — the form's read-only "Balance" row, which is why [`tenderCoverageComplaint`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/postInvVoucher.ts) can insist the rows add up — so that row **is** the credit being taken. New `checkSaleCreditLimit` reads it and hands it to the same `checkCreditLimit`; it sits in [`postVoucherInTx`](src/modules/inventory/lib/postVoucherInTx.ts) **before anything is written**, which covers both office callers at once (create-and-post, and posting a draft saved earlier) and leaves no trace when it refuses. `/[id]/post` was not carrying `partyAccountId` at all — its spine select now does. SALE only: POS checks itself, and ONLINE_INVOICE stays out so a webshop order is not refused at the invoicing step by a limit the shopper never saw.
  - **What the live books said before switching the office half on, and why the dialog exists.** ₹6,95,078 outstanding across 160 parties and 312 open bills; of 200 contacts sampled **one** had a manual limit (₹10,000), 31 were 0 and 168 blank; `CreditLimitConfig` is auto-ON but at **1% of 365-day sales capped at ₹1,000**. So DN Enterprise, ₹70,354 open, has an effective limit of at most ₹1,000 — strict enforcement would have refused their next credit bill, and most others, tomorrow morning. Owner's call: *"same DRY, but in office we may provide a quick modal to edit the limit"*. So the refusal carries its own figures — `CreditLimitDetail` (account, limit, manual limit, balance, on-credit, required) rides the `CreditLimitError` and both office routes return it as `creditLimit` on the 422 — and [`CreditLimitDialog`](src/modules/customer/components/CreditLimitDialog.tsx) shows outstanding / this sale / limit needed / limit now, suggests the next ₹100 above what is needed, saves through the existing `PATCH /api/accounts/[id]` (which enforces `customers:edit` itself) and then **re-runs the save that was refused** — the filled-in bill is never lost. `creditLimitDetailOf` in [`extractApiError.ts`](src/shared/lib/api/extractApiError.ts) is how a screen tells that refusal from any other. The counter deliberately gets no such dialog: a cashier is told to collect; deciding what a customer is good for is an office act.
  - **One fix fell out of the unlock.** The "enter cash or it'll all go on credit" guard existed only because the server demanded a payment row, and it also refused a bill a **gift card** had covered in full (payable 0, collected 0, nothing on credit). It no longer blocks; it labels the note "Gift card ₹x" and carries on.
  - **Not covered, stated plainly:** editing an already-posted sale (`PATCH /api/inventory/vouchers/[id]`) still does not check the limit. The balance already contains that bill's old AR, so the honest test is `balance − old + new`, and shipping `balance + new` there would wrongly refuse ordinary edits. Left for a deliberate pass.
  - Verified: `tsc` clean, eslint clean on every touched file. On the live dev server the counter was driven for real — customer with a ₹10,000 limit and ₹0 due, ₹30 in the cart, nothing collected: **Confirm is enabled** and reads *"₹30.00 goes on the customer's account — they owe it after this bill"*, where before it was dead. **No sale was posted**: the server is pointed at the live Supabase database, so the write-side smoke test (a real part-paid bill, an over-limit refusal, the office dialog) is the owner's to run, on demo or with their say-so.

- **2026-09-15 — DRY pass, first five batches: one dropped write fixed, four sets of drifted copies folded into `src/shared`.** Owner asked for the DRY scope under AGENTS.md §3 and then for all of it; the audit's headline was that the shared layer is fine and *adoption* is the gap, plus one place where a copy hid a bug. **(0) Quick-create product lost its sale price.** [`NewProductDialog`](src/modules/inventory/components/NewProductDialog.tsx) posted `pricePaise`, a name that exists only as a read-side alias in the lookup route; `invProductCreateSchema` has no such key and Zod strips unknown keys silently, so the create succeeded with `savedSalePricePaise` null and the pricing engine filled in something else. Proved on live (created, read back null, deleted), then the dialog sends `savedSalePricePaise` and reads the price back from the real columns. A typed `0` is now a real price (free item) in both dialogs, owner's call. The lenient schema was left alone on purpose — `.strict()` was offered and declined. **Open question surfaced:** neither dialog sets `effectiveSalePriceSource=SAVED`, so a saved price only becomes the *charged* price once Update & Print Labels flips the source; pre-existing design, not changed here. **(1)** The inventory settlement matrix (`SETTLES_BY` / `PENDING_TYPES` / `unsettledClause`) was byte-identical in the pending-stock and pending-vouchers reports — the definition of "still open stock", written twice. Now [`settlementMatrix.ts`](src/shared/lib/accountancy/settlementMatrix.ts) (not `settlement.ts`, which owns `settlementJson`). **(2) The three branch toasts in `http.ts` had never fired.** They keyed off `data.error === "forbidden_branch"` etc., but all 13 `BranchAccessError` catch sites sent `{ error: e.message }` — the sentence, never the code — and had drifted to 403 in three places, 400 in the rest. New [`branchAccessResponse()`](src/shared/lib/api/branchAccessResponse.ts) answers **additively**: `{ error: <sentence>, code: <machine code> }`, 403 for `forbidden_branch`, 400 otherwise; `http.ts` reads `data.code`; `handleApiError` gained the branch beside the offline and voucher-date ones. `error` stays human on purpose so the ~46 screens that read it by hand never toast a raw code (owner chose this over making `extractApiError` prefer `message`). Every catch site is one line now; `pos/counters`' admin fallback and `accountancy/vouchers`' deliberate swallow are untouched. **(3)** Small byte-identical copies folded: `ASSIGNED_POSITION_SELECT` ×4 → [`ticketSelects.ts`](src/shared/lib/api/ticketSelects.ts); `getWithRetry` ×3 → [`http/getWithRetry.ts`](src/shared/lib/http/getWithRetry.ts); `toCsv` ×2 → [`csvSerialize.ts`](src/shared/lib/import/csvSerialize.ts) beside `csvParse`; `FieldError` ×4 → [`FieldError.tsx`](src/shared/components/FieldError.tsx); the strict `parseId` ×3 → [`parseNumericId`](src/shared/lib/api/parseNumericId.ts) (kept separate from `parseIdParam`, which accepts 0 and fractions); two money `sanitize` copies → `parseDecimalInput`, whose regex was corrected to drop *every* dot after the first (it kept the third on a pasted `1.2.3.4`); `org-chart`'s `users.find` inside the node loop (O(n²)) reads `managerId` off the user row instead. The four `buildTree`s stay separate — four shapes, four sort rules. **(4)** `year-ending`'s local `fmtDate` → `toDateOnlyUtc` (its inputs are UTC-midnight FY dates, so `toISOString` was right and now says so); BI `fmtDate` delegates to `formatDateCompact`. **Declined by owner, recorded so they are not re-proposed:** one `round2` (the EPSILON copies stay), one rupees-in-words (`numberToWords.ts` stays; negatives still clamp to "Zero Rupees Only"), the `extractApiError` adoption sweep. **Landed the same day, six more commits:** (7) [`parseRupeesToPaise`](src/shared/lib/format/moneyInr.ts) replaces six local string→paise parsers; (8) one UPI id rule — the POS regex became the shared [`isValidVpa`](src/shared/lib/parties/partyBank.ts), trimming, blank still passing as an optional field; (9) [`deltaSync.ts`](src/shared/lib/api/deltaSync.ts) owns `MAX_DELTA_ROWS`, `deltaSlice`, `pageSlice` and the two response builders behind tickets / admin-tasks / deals / help / day-book — envelopes checked key-for-key against the dev server, and the three boards' unbounded snapshot branches deliberately left unbounded because their boards do not consume `hasMore`/`loadMore`; (10) one [`initials`](src/shared/lib/format/initials.ts) (the POS counters version, which was the correct one — the other eleven turned a doubled space into "JU") and three of the hand-rolled avatars folded into `UserAvatar`, the four with their own palettes left alone; (11) **no `window.confirm` left in `src/`** — 41 sites in 33 files now ask through `confirmAction` with a short title, the consequence as the body and a button that names the action; five sync handlers that only set state afterwards became async, and the branch delete was clicked through in the browser (styled dialog, cancel leaves the row). **Spike on the masters template** ([brands](src/app/(protected)/office/inventory/masters/brands/page.tsx)): `useApiResource` + `extractApiError` took 14 lines out and made the post-save refetch keep the rows on screen; `useTableSort` was *not* adopted — the page has no sortable column, so it would have been invented UI. Verdict: the hook fits a masters page cleanly; the remaining seven can follow the same shape when a page is next touched, one at a time, not as a sweep. Verified throughout: `tsc` clean after every batch, `check:guard-lint` green, eslint clean on every touched file, `check-doc-sync` green; both pending reports, org chart, all five delta endpoints, Year Ending and the brands page exercised on the dev server. The perf work on `dev` is still unreleased; owner chose to continue on top of it and cut a dev build to test both together.
- **2026-09-14 — A counter can now hold several EPBX extensions.** Owner: *"counter has epbx extension, allow multiple extension with comma"*. `PosCounter.epbxExtension` stays one string but may now be a comma-separated list of whole numbers (`"201,202"`). **Checked first that nothing else rides on it:** the schema comment claimed the extension was also the voucher prefix and the offline id-range number, and the owner confirmed it is not — both come from `counterNumber`/branch ([`blockStartFor`](src/shared/lib/counter/idRange.ts), [`claim-number`](src/app/api/pos/counters/[id]/claim-number/route.ts)). The comment was stale and is corrected; the field is phone-only. `normalizeExtension` becomes [`normalizeExtensions`](src/shared/lib/counter/idRange.ts) (splits on commas, every entry must be a whole number, de-duplicates, rejoins without spaces — a single number behaves exactly as before) alongside a `splitExtensions` reader used by every consumer. **The DB unique index goes** ([`20260914090000_counter_multiple_epbx_extensions`](prisma/migrations/20260914090000_counter_multiple_epbx_extensions/migration.sql)): a list cannot be indexed per entry. Overlap is still refused, now explicitly in `PATCH /api/pos/counters/[id]` — it reads the other counters' lists and names the clashing extension and the counter holding it. `GET /api/calls?ext=` accepts the list and matches `toExt IN (...) OR fromExt IN (...)`, so the follow-up popup pops for a call on any of the counter's phones; `useMyCounterExtension` passes the stored value straight through, unchanged. Admin form is a text field with comma hint, and the counter card lists each extension. Verified: `tsc` clean, `local-schema.sql` regenerated. **One residual:** an existing counter replica keeps the old unique index (replicas apply `local-schema.sql`, never migrations, and the generator emits no DROP) — harmless, since it constrains the whole string and two counters would need identical lists to trip it.
- **2026-09-14 — Shift Close reports unified into the Print Template engine (`SHIFT_CLOSE`).** Owner: *"shift close print in print tamplet"*. Per AGENTS.md Rule 2 (*"Every printable document must be customizable through the print template"*), the hardcoded 258-line HTML string popup previously used on shift close in POS ([`ShiftCloseDialog.tsx`](src/modules/pos/components/ShiftCloseDialog.tsx)) and the duplicate 255-line inline HTML print function on the shift audit page ([`shifts/[id]/page.tsx`](src/app/(protected)/office/pos/shifts/[id]/page.tsx)) have been retired and unified into the core VoucherPrintTemplate system under the `SHIFT_CLOSE` kind.
  - **Template Customization:** Configurable under `/office/settings/voucher-templates` ("POS & Register" group) with live preview in both A4 and 80mm Thermal, default paper format (`THERMAL_80MM`), custom headers, address, store contacts, disclaimer, footer line, and Cashier/Manager signatures.
  - **Dedicated Preview & Receipt Route:** [`/receipt/shift/[id]`](src/app/receipt/shift/[id]/page.tsx) powered by a new shared presentation component [`ShiftCloseBody.tsx`](src/shared/components/print/ShiftCloseBody.tsx) (dual A4 and 80mm thermal responsive layouts rendering complete reconciliation, cash flow, tenders, collections by activity, safe drops, linked vouchers, and notes), standard `ReceiptPreview`, and `PrintToolbar`.
  - **Reprint from Shift Audit:** Both `/office/pos/shifts/[id]` and the POS Shift Close dialog open the template-powered receipt route with `autoprint=1`.
  - **Hardware Bridge Support:** Added `printShiftCloseMiddle` to [`hardware-bridge/thermal.js`](hardware-bridge/thermal.js) for direct ESC/POS thermal printing over Electron IPC / network / USB, with full offline parity test coverage in [`hardware-bridge/thermal.test.js`](hardware-bridge/thermal.test.js) (12/12 passing).

- **2026-09-14 — A voucher line the office has thrown away can no longer come back from a counter.** The gap the entry below deliberately left open. `VoucherGlLine` and `VoucherInventoryLine` carry no `updatedAt` and no `createdAt` (registry `dates: []`), so [`push.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) has no timestamp to arbitrate them with and **every push of one is an unconditional upsert**. Meanwhile every office edit route rewrites a voucher's lines by `deleteMany` + `createMany` — the voucher PATCH, the journal-entry PATCH, the inventory PATCH, the opening-bill rewrite, `reapplyTradingVoucherInPlace`, the shift repost — minting **fresh ids** for the replacements. A counter still holding an unpushed `_outbox` row for one of the discarded ids re-creates it under a voucher that is still POSTED, which is exactly the set `recomputeAccountBalances` re-sums; that re-sum is authoritative and trusts the line table completely, so the inflated figure becomes `Account.balance` cloud-wide with no error and nothing reporting that the voucher's debits and credits have stopped agreeing. **The window is real but narrow** — a pushed outbox row is never re-sent and a pulled row never re-enters the outbox, so only rows still PENDING count; what widens it is that the drain is 50 rows at a time in `_outbox.id` order, so a counter that queued through an outage routinely puts a batch boundary mid-voucher (spine up, lines waiting), and a line that fails stops the whole run for hours of backoff — hours in which the office is looking at a voucher whose lines are missing, the very thing someone reaches in to correct. **Two obvious fixes were rejected, on evidence.** Refusing lines on a POSTED parent would reject every sale (a POS sale creates its spine DRAFT, attaches lines, *then* flips to POSTED, so lines push after the spine; handover-accept inserts the spine already POSTED). And **adding `updatedAt` would not have fixed it at all**: LWW can only drop a row when the cloud row EXISTS, and a resurrected line's row was deleted — `findUnique` answers null and the upsert takes its CREATE half regardless of any timestamp. That would have been a migration, with the counters-before-server release sequence attached, bought for nothing. **The rule that works is the id range.** A counter physically cannot mint an id below `CLOUD_RANGE_END` — [`prismaClient.ts`](src/shared/lib/prisma/prismaClient.ts) refuses *every* local write until the reservation is confirmed applied — so a cloud-range line hanging off the parent voucher can only have been written upstream, and its presence says the office has rewritten that line set. Incoming counter-range line + parent already carrying a cloud-range line → drop as superseded, warned and counted as success, the same shape as the closed-shift and un-post guards. A normal sale's parent never holds a cloud-range line, in one batch or five. The whole decision is one exported `lineReplayWasSuperseded`, memoised per parent per run (one indexed query per voucher), so the verification script drives the same code the till runs rather than a re-spelling of it. **The coupling is now a fourth [`check-guard-lint`](scripts/check-guard-lint.ts) rule:** a new file that deletes voucher lines must announce itself in `LINE_RECYCLER_ALLOWLIST`, because a new recycler is what would invalidate the id-range proxy. Verified: `tsc` clean, guard-lint passes and was proved to trip on a scratch recycler, and [`verify-line-resurrection.ts`](src/scripts/verify-line-resurrection.ts) — 13/13 against the live DB inside a transaction that always rolls back, covering single-batch, split-batch, handover-accept, resurrection, cloud-range incoming and non-recycled tables, for both line models. **Spotted from here, fixed in the entry above** (`7dae6c38`, landed in parallel): the same review flagged that pass 3's `deleteByKeys(local, entry, diff.onlyLocal)` had no exclusion for rows still pending in `_outbox` — a counter's freshly-created unpushed line is `onlyLocal` by definition. That guard now lives inside `deleteByKeys` itself. Worth recording that the sketch written here first got the join **wrong** — it assumed `_outbox.row_pk` and `keyExpression` produce the same text, and they do not (`chr(31)` vs the four-character ``), which is why the guard joins on `row_key`. **One residual, deliberately left:** the guard is in the delete path only, so the `rowHashDiff` → `pullByKeys(force=true)` refresh can still overwrite a locally-edited-but-unpushed row with the source's older copy. It self-heals (the outbox payload still pushes, the next pull brings it back) and it cannot reach `VoucherSeriesCounter`, which is `watermark` and so never row-hashed — but a **dead-lettered** row loses its local edit the same way a dead-lettered delete does, which is the trade-off that entry already states and accepts.

- **2026-09-14 — The Year Ending page no longer runs a seed with deletes on every open; a local-write counter could not load it at all.** `GET /api/accountancy/voucher-series` called [`seedVoucherSeries`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/seedVoucherSeries.ts) on every request, and that function ended with two `deleteMany` calls retiring the long-dead `GL_POSTING` series. The replica guard in [`prismaClient.ts`](src/shared/lib/prisma/prismaClient.ts) refuses `delete`/`deleteMany` before it looks at `where`, so on a `WRITE_MODE=local` till — where `prisma` resolves to the replica — a delete that would have matched nothing turned the page into "Failed to load year-ending settings". Same shape as the 2026-09-05 sale-save bug. The quieter half: even without the deletes, the GET wrote on every visit (BooksConfig upsert, `createMany`, 24 label `update`s, two `updateMany`s), and because `VoucherSeries` carries `updatedAt`, each label update changed the row, so the capture trigger shipped all 24 series rows back to Supabase from a till every time the page opened. **Now** the function is split: `ensureVoucherSeries` is create-or-relabel only and is what the route runs — and only where the data is authored, `if (!shouldWriteLocal())`, since a local-write counter's replica is a mirror that gets the rows by pull; `seedVoucherSeries` keeps its name for the two seed callers (`bootstrap-seed`, `codeOwnedCatalog`) and is `ensure` plus the one-time fixups (`JE→JV`, `PV→PMT`, `GL_POSTING` retirement). Migration-style work runs from the seed scripts only. Nothing is lost on live: `GL_POSTING` was retired in `2d6f1d1b` (June) and the page has been opened many times since. _Proven by reading and by the existing `check:counter-mode` variants (the guard refuses a delete regardless of `where`; `prisma` is the replica under `WRITE_MODE=local`); no counter database here to click through._
- **2026-09-14 — The office's "Reset Counter" could zero an offline till's number slip and make it re-print invoice numbers.** The second of the two bugs the entry below left open, now closed. [`voucher-series/[kind]/reset`](src/app/api/accountancy/voucher-series/[kind]/reset/route.ts) ran `updateMany({ kind }, { nextSeq: 0 })` across **every** counter's `VoucherSeriesCounter` row — a row that has exactly one writer by design (the machine numbering on it), because two writers under last-write-wins re-issue numbers. Traced end to end, every link real: an offline till keeps billing and its increments queue in `_outbox`; the office resets, so the cloud's copy of the till's row is `0` with a **newer** `updatedAt`; on reconnect [`push.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) sees "cloud newer", **drops the till's row image and counts it as pushed**; the next pull's monotonic guard writes the zero onto the replica (`localAuthority` from the fix below holds only *deletes*, not updates); the till's next bill takes number 1 again. **Now the reset is an intent, not a write to other machines' rows:** `VoucherSeries.resetGen` (office-owned) is bumped, only counter 1's row is zeroed (the office IS counter 1, and the legacy `nextSeq` the Year Ending page reads goes with it), and [`nextRefFromSeries`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts) compares its **own** row's `resetGen` against the series' the next time it numbers — behind → zero `nextSeq` and catch up, folded into the same single write as the FY rollover it already did. An offline till loses nothing and restarts on its next bill; a fresh row is born at the current generation. Chosen over "reset counter 1 only" because that would have made the button silently do nothing for tills with no way for the office to restart them. **Push gets a second lock:** the registry's `localAuthority` flag now reaches the TS side ([`gen-sync-registry.ts`](scripts/gen-sync-registry.ts) emits it), and `push.ts` never drops a row of such a table as "superseded" — it warns and pushes the local image over the newer cloud one, because for this table the drop is a lost increment, never a benign skip. **Schema:** two `Int @default(0)` columns, folded into the still-unapplied [`20260913120000_call_updated_at`](prisma/migrations/20260913120000_call_updated_at/migration.sql) per the one-migration-per-release rule (`migrate status` against live shows it pending; origin/main is still v1.3.4). Default 0 on both sides means no row resets on upgrade. Regenerated `local-schema.sql` and the sync registry. **Counter release before the Vercel deploy**, as for every column added to a synced table. Dialog copy on Year Ending now says the office restarts now and each counter on its next bill. **No harness covers `push.ts` or the allocator; manual repro:** (1) till 24 online, post two POS sales → `(POS,24).nextSeq = 2`; (2) pull its network, post one more → local `3`, `_outbox` pending; (3) office: Reset Counter on POS — before: cloud `(POS,24)` → 0; after: that row untouched, `VoucherSeries.resetGen` +1, `(POS,1)` → 0; (4) reconnect, let it push + pull — before: replica row 0, next bill re-uses #1; after: push lands `3`, the queued sale keeps its number, and the till's next sale numbers **1** with its `resetGen` caught up. Verified: tsc clean, `sync:tables --check`, `db:local-schema --check`, `check:guard-lint`, `check-doc-sync --strict` all green; **real-counter test pending.** Still open from the entry below: `GET /api/accountancy/voucher-series` runs `seedVoucherSeries`, whose `deleteMany` the replica guard refuses on a `WRITE_MODE=local` till.
- **2026-09-14 — The hourly reconcile could delete a counter's own number slip and restart its invoice numbers from 1.** Found on the same voucher-numbering review, traced in code, not yet seen live. Pass 3 of [`sync-once.js`](sync-engine/sync-once.js) reads "present on the replica, absent in Supabase" as *deleted upstream* and calls `deleteByKeys` — and nothing on that path ever looked at `_outbox`. But a till is also an **origin**: a row it just wrote sits pending in the outbox and is absent upstream for the opposite reason. The sync engine is a separate process from the app's flusher, so it can reach Supabase while the push is still behind — a freshly set-up counter on patchy internet is the obvious case. 92 of 114 tables reach that delete; for most the row returns once pushed, but `VoucherSeriesCounter` is one row per `(kind, counterNumber)` and [`nextRefFromSeries`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts) silently re-creates a missing row at `nextSeq: 0` — the till prints numbers already on customers' bills. **Now:** `deleteByKeys` ([`pull.js`](sync-engine/lib/pull.js)) guards itself, so no caller can forget: keys with a *pending* `_outbox` row are held ([`outboxGuard.js`](sync-engine/lib/outboxGuard.js) — dead-lettered rows are deliberately not, or a refused row would keep its table re-verifying against Supabase hourly forever), and a table flagged `localAuthority` in the registry `overrides` (only `VoucherSeriesCounter`) loses nothing, pushed or not — the second lock for the one table where the loss means duplicate bills. The guard joins on `_outbox.row_key`, **not** `row_pk`: `keyExpression` joins compound keys with the four-character literal `\x1f` (`standard_conforming_strings` is on) while the trigger uses `chr(31)`, so `row_pk` would silently never match — the separator is now one exported constant, `KEY_SEPARATOR`. Proven both ways on a throwaway cluster: the old function deleted the pending row (`removed=1, rows left=0`); `pnpm test:reconcile` check 4 now covers it. Two adjacent bugs found and **left open**: an office series reset (`voucher-series/[kind]/reset`) zeroes every counter's row, so an offline till's queued increment is dropped by `push.ts` as "success" and the zeroed row is pulled down — same symptom, separate cause; and `GET /api/accountancy/voucher-series` runs `seedVoucherSeries`, whose `deleteMany` the replica guard refuses on a `WRITE_MODE=local` till.

- **2026-09-15 — An update now unpacks itself BEFORE the counter goes down; the restart is two folder renames.** Owner: *"vyapar download+install in 3 min with web downloader/installer, ours 15min"*, then the part that decided the work — *"one day is bearable but cant waste time on every update"*. So the fresh install was left alone and the whole target became the per-release downtime. **Measured, against the real 1.3.5 → 1.3.6 pair**, because three guesses in a row had been wrong: the differential download is **7.5 MB of 162.5 MB (4.6% reused blocks)** and happens with the app running, so it was never the cost; the installer's payload unpacks to **672.7 MB across 14,840 files** and took **272 s on a dev machine**, all of it with the app dead. **Compression cannot touch it.** `differentialPackage: true` makes electron-builder pin the payload to `normal`/1 MB dict/non-solid and ignore the `compression` field outright (app-builder-lib's `differentialUpdateInfoBuilder`: *"do not allow to change compression level"*) — `maximum` and `store` were built and both produced a byte-identical **169,736,659**-byte payload, so `"compression": "maximum"` in package.json has never done anything. The work had to move, not shrink.
  - **[`electron/stagedUpdate.ts`](electron/stagedUpdate.ts) (new)** unpacks the downloaded installer into `.AbeerSuite-staged-<version>` **beside** the program folder — a sibling, so the later rename stays on one volume — the moment `update-downloaded` fires, at below-normal priority so the till keeps billing. [`updateManager.ts`](electron/updateManager.ts)'s safe-moment loop holds a *quiet* update back while that runs (a deadline still overrules it), and `install()` then calls `beginSwap()` instead of `quitAndInstall`, turning `autoInstallOnAppQuit` off so the installer cannot run over the swap.
  - **[`resources/swap-update.ps1`](resources/swap-update.ps1) (new)** is the only part that runs with the counter down: wait for the pid, wait for anything still holding files under the program folder (`pg_ctl stop -m fast` returns before PostgreSQL has closed its files), carry `Uninstall AbeerSuite.exe` across (NSIS writes it, so the payload has no copy), rename old out, rename new in, start the app, fix `DisplayVersion`, delete the old folder. Every failure path rolls back or leaves the old folder untouched and **still starts the app** — a counter on last week's version is a nuisance, a counter that will not start is a shop that cannot bill.
  - **Two faults found by testing rather than reasoning.** `cmd /c start /belownormal /wait` **returns exit 0 when the command it ran failed** — it would have reported a broken unpack as a good one — so priority is now set with `os.setPriority` on a directly-spawned child. And the bundled `7za.exe` (the reduced standalone build) has **no NSIS support**: asking it for `$PLUGINSDIR/app-64.7z` matched nothing and exited **0 with an empty folder**. It does, however, find the 7z signature inside the installer and read the exe *as* that archive (`Type = 7z`, `Physical Size = 169,736,659`) — so one `x` on the installer yields the whole tree, no intermediate 170 MB file, half the disk needed.
  - **Refuses towards the old path, never towards a broken till:** no `7za.exe` in resources, under 1.5 GB free, no write permission beside the program folder (a Program Files install without elevation), or an unpacked folder missing `AbeerSuite.exe`/`app.asar`/`standalone` — each abandons staging and lets electron-updater install the ordinary way. `7za.exe` is fetched at build time by [`scripts/fetch-7za.ts`](scripts/fetch-7za.ts) through electron-builder's own pinned toolset (so version and SHA-256 stay in one place, theirs) and is gitignored like `resources/pgsql`.
  - **Verified: the swap and its rollback**, in a sandbox — new version in place, uninstaller carried across, marker and old folder removed; and with an incomplete staged folder, the install directory untouched and still on the old version. Also verified: single-pass extraction of the real 1.3.6 installer (14,840 files, exit 0, valid tree), that a deliberate failure now reports non-zero, `tsc` and `eslint` clean, `swap-update.ps1` parses. **Not verified — needs the owner's machine:** a real staged update end to end on a counter. The packaged exe cannot be run from here, and this path only exists in a packaged build.
  - **Not done, deliberately:** shrinking the payload. `AbeerSuite.exe` 224.6 MB + `standalone` 206.1 MB + `pgsql` 93.3 MB is ~90% of it and none of it is trimmable; the three shipped `node_modules` trees were checked for duplication and share **0.5 MB**. `"compression": "maximum"` is left in package.json as dead config — removing it changes nothing at runtime and belongs with some other commit.

- **2026-09-14 — Posting does not un-happen: a counter can no longer push a voucher back to DRAFT, and a reopened shift can no longer be re-closed from an offline till.** Came out of a review of the voucher-numbering change. [`push.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) settles a counter's replayed write by **last-write-wins on `updatedAt`**, which is the wrong rule for a lifecycle state: a till that still believes a voucher is a draft, writes to that row after the office has posted it, produces a DRAFT image that is honestly *later*, so LWW waved it through and the cloud's POSTED row was overwritten. No clock skew needed — the owner's machines are internet-synced, and the hazard does not depend on them. The capture trigger makes it worse than it looks: it ships `to_jsonb(NEW)`, the **whole row**, so even an edit that only touches a narration re-asserts whatever `status` the replica happens to hold. Since numbering moved to post time the cost became permanent — the DRAFT image carries `voucherNo: null`, blanking an allocated number out of a GST series that never re-issues it. **The guard is keyed on `binding`, not on status**, and that distinction is the whole point: there IS a legitimate counter-side POSTED→DRAFT writer — the "Mark Draft" toggle on the POS Sales Register ([status/route.ts](src/app/api/inventory/vouchers/[id]/status/route.ts)), which refuses anything whose binding is not `OPTIONAL` — so a blanket status check would have silently eaten every estimate a cashier un-finalised. The rule is therefore *incoming DRAFT + cloud `REGULAR` + cloud POSTED (or CANCELLED, whatever the binding) → drop as superseded*, warned and counted as success rather than dead-lettered, exactly as the closed-shift guard already does; the cloud row rides the `findUnique` the LWW branch was already making, hoisted above its short-circuit because that short-circuit is precisely the case being guarded. **And a regression from the day before, found in the same pass:** `repostParkedVoucherInTx` deletes the parked voucher's old GL lines and `discardParkedDraftInTx` deletes the draft, both inside the shift close route's transaction on the mode-routed client — and a counter's replica [refuses every delete](src/shared/lib/prisma/prismaClient.ts). A shift reopened while online and re-closed after live went down threw from inside the transaction and **the cashier could not close the till**. It now refuses up front with a readable message: a reopen is an office-assisted correction, and finishing it needs the same connection. The coupling that keeps the push guard honest — that an un-post never reaches the outbox, because reopen writes through `prismaAuthoritative` — is now a third [`check-guard-lint`](scripts/check-guard-lint.ts) rule rather than a comment. **That entry left one gap open** — the unarbitrated GL-line push. It is closed by the entry above.

- **2026-09-13 — A draft takes no voucher number any more; the series advances when it posts, and Save & Post is one request.** Owner: *"if its saved as draft it has no SL"*, and *"our office voucher are saved as draft then converted to post it takes extra time so i want it post instantly if it really posted"*. [`engineCreateVoucher`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/transactionEngine.ts) called `nextRefFromSeries` even with `post:false`, so **every draft burned a number at save time** and an abandoned one left a permanent hole. **Now** the number is taken at three post-time seams: at the tail of `enginePostSpine` (last, so the series row's lock is held for as little of the caller's 30-second transaction as possible), in `engineGlArm`'s ATTACH branch — which now **reads the spine first**, so a voucher that already carries a number keeps it, which is what makes a parked shift-close voucher's repost land back under the same number — and in the inventory post route's non-GL flip, which GRN / Challan / Rejection / Physical Stock / orders reach POSTED through without ever touching the GL arm. Two things travel with the allocation, both read off the **spine** rather than the machine doing the posting: its own **date**, so a March-dated draft posted in April does not roll the series into the new FY (it cannot un-roll one — a series is a single running counter); and the **counter it was raised on** (`sourceCounterId`), so the counter segment of the reference names the till that billed it, not whichever PC posted it. The number also stopped being interpolated into `description` at create time — `enginePostSpine` never rewrote that string, so a deferred number would have frozen `"JOURNAL null: …"` into the Day Book. `CreatedVoucher.reference` is `string | null` now; registers, the optional/linkable lists and both print paths fall back to `UV-<id>` through a new shared [`voucherDisplayNo`](src/shared/lib/accountancy/uvNo.ts), the Day Book can be **searched** by that same `UV-<id>`, and `seedVoucherSeries` counts only numbered rows so a reseed cannot push `nextSeq` past numbers that were never issued. `POST /api/accountancy/vouchers` grew a `post` flag gated on the per-kind **post** permission, so the GL form and the refunds payout do it in one request instead of create-then-post. **Deliberately left:** the Journal Voucher dialog numbers a CONTRA from the JV- series and the spine has nowhere to remember that, so those still number at create — removing it needs a `seriesKind` column, which **cannot reach the live database before a counter build carries it** ([schemaGuard](sync-engine/lib/schemaGuard.js) halts a till whose replica lacks a column the source has, and a Vercel deploy runs migrations). The partial unique index on `(type, voucherNo)` is **not** shipped: opening bills all share `DEFAULT_OPENING_REF`, so it would fail to create on a real shop — see the plan file for the other four blockers.

- **2026-09-13 — One writer for `Account.balance`, and the sign-flip it was hiding.** Planning the voucher-numbering change turned up a live bug on the way past: the journal-entry lifecycle routes read a ledger's nature from bare `normalBalance` and then asserted it non-null — `nbById.get(id)!` in [cancel](src/app/api/accountancy/journal-entries/[id]/cancel/route.ts), post, restore and hard-delete. An account whose `normalBalance` is null takes its nature from its **group**, and `null` fed into [`balanceDelta`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts) fails `=== "DEBIT"` and falls through to the CREDIT branch — so the delta was applied on the **wrong side** instead of being skipped. Cancelling such a journal voucher did not undo its balance; it moved it the other way. **How exposed:** 3,733 of 3,818 ledgers on the live books carry a null `normalBalance` — not an exotic party-ledger case, it is 98% of the chart, payment gateways and expense heads included, and 197 GL lines sit on JOURNAL vouchers today. It had never actually fired (`recompute:caches` reports no drift, and zero null-nature lines sit on a CANCELLED voucher), so nothing needs repairing — the first JV cancel would have been the first victim. The GL voucher **edit** route had the same bare read on both legs and therefore the milder twin: `if (!nb) continue` fired on the reverse *and* the apply, so the old delta stayed and the new one never landed. [`auditRestore.reapplyBalance`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/auditRestore.ts) recreated a restored voucher's lines and never put those legs' balance back. **Now:** every delta goes through one `applyLineBalances(tx, lines, "apply" | "reverse")` in [balance.ts](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts) — group fallback, and `round2` at the money boundary where the engine already rounds, so an unrounded request float can no longer move the cache by a hair the lines cannot account for. Cancel, restore and the GL edit gained the `retreatReconciliation` they were missing (taking a voucher out of the books dirties a certified window exactly as much as putting one in; the edit retreats at the earlier of the old and new dates). And [`check-guard-lint`](scripts/check-guard-lint.ts) grew a second rule: a hand-rolled `account.update(… balance: { increment | decrement })` outside `balance.ts` / `transactionEngine.ts` now fails `pnpm precommit`. It matches the **model**, not the text, so `GiftCard.balance` is not a false positive, and it cannot see absolute recompute-from-truth writes — which is correct, those are not deltas. `openingBills.ts` is allowlisted deliberately: it pre-aggregates one signed delta per account and treats a null-group, null-nature ledger as CREDIT where the shared writer skips it, so converting it is a behaviour change and waits for that call. **Next:** this was Part 0 of the voucher-numbering plan — a draft still burns a number at save time, and taking the number at post is Part 1.

- **2026-09-13 — Calls staff had already logged kept coming back on the counter’s follow-up icon: the edit could never reach the replica.** Owner: *"we are facing a problem even after adding deal or note it come back."* "Handled" is derived, not a flag — [`/api/calls`](src/app/api/calls/route.ts) treats `note IS NULL AND ticketId IS NULL` as unlogged — and that rule was never the problem. A till READS its replica and RELAYS writes to the office, so the PATCH that marks a call handled lands only in Supabase; and `Call` had no `updatedAt`, so the generator filed it as `append` on `createdAt`. A PATCH never moves `createdAt`, so **the edit had no way down**, and the hourly reconcile could not see it either (an `append` table is compared on keys alone, and the keys matched). Permanent divergence, not slow. `7b859f55` added `updatedAt` + index to the model on 2026-09-13 but shipped no migration and reached no tag, so no till ever had it. **Now:** the column ships as [`20260913120000_call_updated_at`](prisma/migrations/20260913120000_call_updated_at/migration.sql) — idempotent, because `0_init` already declares it and live got it by hand-run `db push`; the backfill is `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, never `createdAt`, so every row sorts past each replica’s stale `append` mark and the table re-drains once, bringing the missing notes with it. **The engine now notices a cursor that has changed meaning:** `_sync_state` records `cursor_column` beside the watermark, [`state.cursorFor`](sync-engine/lib/state.js) drops the cursor when the strategy or column moves (a NULL `cursor_column` is *not* a change — that would re-read all 111 tables out of Supabase on the first cycle after this ships), and that one cycle passes `force` through [`pullWatermark`](sync-engine/lib/pull.js) to `upsert`. The force matters because live was migrated *before* the counters will be upgraded: the two sides’ `updatedAt` backfills are stamped at different moments and are not comparable, and the monotonic guard would otherwise discard the whole re-drain in silence. **Client side,** [`CallFollowUpPopup`](src/modules/pos/components/CallFollowUpPopup.tsx)’s "I just saved this" set now persists (it was a plain ref, emptied by every reload and by crossing between the office and POS layouts) and **expires** after `CALL_POPUP_HANDLED_TTL_MS` — past that the call returns *with* a line saying it was logged and has not arrived, because hiding for ever buries a save that never landed and returning unannounced invites a second ticket for the same call. The icon reads `50+` instead of a flat `50`. **Order is not optional:** counter build first, `pnpm db:migrate` second — `schemaGuard` halts a till on a column the source has and the replica lacks, stopping every table on it; [`docs/migrations.md`](docs/migrations.md) and the release skill now both say so. **Open:** office PCs with no counter bound still fall back to every till’s backlog, so their icon never empties. Verified: tsc + eslint clean, `cursorFor` unit-checked on all five cases; **real-counter test pending** — nothing proves it until a till runs the new build.

- **2026-09-13 — A shift reopen now PARKS its closing vouchers instead of cancelling them, so re-closing stops dirtying the register.** Owner, during close testing: *"we edit shift reopen cancelling the shift closing contra/journals re closing create new it make report dirty during test period."* Every reopen burned two fresh voucher numbers and left two dead `CANCELLED` rows behind — five test closes, ten dead vouchers. The reading that fixed it is the owner's: a wrong close is not *"this voucher happened and was undone"*, it is *"these figures were wrong"*, and these two vouchers are raised by the system for itself, never printed, never handed to anyone. **Now:** [`parkVoucherInTx.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/parkVoucherInTx.ts) adds the third lifecycle move next to cancel and restore — `POSTED → DRAFT` (balances taken back out, reconciliation retreated, row + voucher number + GL lines kept), `DRAFT → POSTED` in place via the engine's existing `existingSpineId` ATTACH path, and a discard that removes a parked DRAFT with **no AuditLog** and refuses anything that is not a DRAFT. [Reopen](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/reopen/route.ts) parks both and now *keeps* `closingContraVoucherId` / `varianceJournalEntryId` on the shift; [close](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/close/route.ts) reposts those same two rows with the corrected figures, so one shift close means one Contra number and at most one Journal number however many times it is reopened. A parked row the corrected close no longer needs — cash now matches, or nothing left to sweep — is discarded: it never posted, so there is no ledger effect, no print and nothing for an audit snapshot to protect. The owner's own boundary on that liberality: *"human generated and system generated are not same"* — and it stops at DRAFT, since a POSTED system voucher (a POS sale is system-generated too) still goes through the ordinary hard-delete route with its full snapshot. [Shift delete](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/delete/route.ts) discards parked drafts and cancels posted ones as before. Cancel and restore were rewritten onto one shared `applyLineBalances(tx, lines, "apply" | "reverse")` in [`balance.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/balance.ts), so the two directions can no longer drift apart. **Open:** the voucher-number gap questions this came out of — numbers are allocated at create even for a draft (`engineCreateVoucher` calls `nextRefFromSeries` with `post: false`), a recycle-bin **purge** now gives the number back when it was the last one issued (2026-09-20, above) though a mid-run gap still stands, and an editable number field to refill such gaps is still only discussed, not built.

- **2026-09-13 — A voucher now carries its shift, and the two POS side tables are gone. Schema changes ship as migrations again.** Owner's screenshot of the POS Payment/Receipt tab: three receipts listed, two of them showing `—` for a reference because their vouchers had been deleted. The rows were not the vouchers — they were `pos_receipts` rows, and the tab was built from that table with the voucher looked up afterwards only to print its number, so a deleted voucher left its row behind forever. Owner's reading of it, which is the fix: *"end of the day all are normal vouchers, just belongs to a shift, that also belongs to a counter"*. `PosReceipt` and `PosExpenditure` each held a copy of the voucher (amount, account, note, creator, timestamp) whose only non-duplicate column was `shiftId`, and POS **sales** had no shift link at all — [`shiftMath.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/shiftMath.ts) attributed them by a `createdAt` window and stayed right only because the GL filter happened to be restricted to that counter's own ledgers. **Now:** `UniversalVoucher.sourceShiftId` (plain `Int?`, no FK, indexed — same reasoning as `sourceCounterId`: provenance must outlive the shift row), stamped in the two places every voucher already passes through ([`transactionEngine.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/transactionEngine.ts) `engineCreateSpine` + the create-posted branch) via [`shiftIdForCounter`](src/shared/lib/counter/sourceShift.ts) — the shift standing at the source counter, `AWAITING_HANDOVER` included, skipped entirely when there is no counter so the office pays nothing for it. The POS routes pass their shift explicitly, since the URL already names it. Readers rewritten to the voucher: the Payment/Receipt tab ([`cash-movements`](src/app/api/pos/cash-movements/route.ts)), the shift tender tally (linked, no window), and one shared [`listShiftMovements`](src/modules/pos/lib/shiftMath.ts) behind the shift detail page, the close report and the delete guard, so the three cannot disagree. The refund path's advance lookup moves off matching the note text `"Advance against <ref>"` onto a real `ADVANCE_FOR` `VoucherLink` to the order's spine. **Consequence, which was the whole point:** a deleted voucher disappears from every one of these at once — there is no second row to forget. **Migrations are back** (`prisma/migrations/`, [`docs/migrations.md`](docs/migrations.md)) because this change has to *carry data*, which `db push` cannot: `0_init` is a baseline of the 2026-09-12 schema that every existing database marks applied once (`pnpm db:migrate:baseline`), then the enum value, then add-column → backfill (exact for receipts and expenditures, counter+window for historic sales) → drop tables, as one transaction. `pnpm db:migrate` deploys. Regenerated: sync registry (114 models, numbers 64/68 retired) and `local-schema.sql`. **Not yet applied to any database — the owner runs the baseline and the deploy.** Verified: tsc + eslint clean; **browser: pending.**

- **2026-09-13 — Shift close now reads the till's book balance; the hand-written copy of the Cash Book is gone.** Owner, on the Close Shift dialog: "Expected in drawer" showed ₹0 on a shift whose Cash Book tab showed ₹3.00 from two receipts. The close math was a *second* derivation of the drawer — it picked voucher types one at a time (POS tender, then RECEIPT, then SALE_RETURN) inside a `sourceCreatedAt` window — and it had been wrong twice in one day: `postSettlementVoucher` ([`postReceiptVoucher.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/postReceiptVoucher.ts)) stamped every RECEIPT's `postedAt`/`sourceCreatedAt` to the business date (midnight), so a receipt always fell *before* the shift's open time and was dropped; cash refunds were never subtracted at all; the counter board missed refunds even after they were added. Owner's model, now the code's: **opening float = the till's book balance at open** (carry-over + the vault top-up Contra — exactly what [`shifts/route.ts`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/route.ts) POST already stored), so **expected in drawer = till book balance now − cash dropped to safe** (drops are physically out but stay on the ledger until the closing Contra). Sales, receipts, refunds, expenditures, a manual entry — all land on the till ledger, so nothing is re-derived and no timestamp is involved. [`shiftMath.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/shiftMath.ts) shrinks to `effectiveBalancePaise` (the same `effectiveAccountBalance` open uses, so open and close read the ledger identically) + `computeExpectedCash(tillBookBalance, drops)`; `sumShiftCashSalesPaise` / `sumShiftCashRefundsPaise` deleted. Close and handover-accept read the till on `prismaAuthoritative` (they post the variance JE; a counter's replica can lag a cycle); the detail GET and the counter board read the replica for the live figure. The informational "Payments collected this shift" tally keeps its POS-tender window but reads receipts through the shift's own `PosReceipt` links (every counter receipt flow writes one; `pos_receipts` is replicated) instead of a time window. UI: the breakdown box is Opening float · Till movement this shift (signed) · Dropped to safe · Expected, with "see the Cash Book tab" underneath; ActiveShiftBar shows "Till ±₹x"; shift page / counter board say "Till movement". The `postedAt` fix stays (`sourceCreatedAt` is now honest for Day-Book ordering). API field rename: `cashSalesPaise` → `tillMovementPaise` (+ `tillBookBalancePaise`); `refundsPaise` gone. No schema change. Parity harness asserts on the till balance delta + the linked tally. Verified: tsc + eslint clean; **browser: see verification note below once done.**
- **2026-09-12 — A wrongly closed POS shift can now be reopened and closed again, or deleted when empty.** Owner's ask on the Shift Reports page: a closed shift was untouchable, so a wrong cash count stood forever. Two new routes, both gated on the existing `pos:shift:force-close` tick (or admin) and both asking for a reason. [`POST /api/pos/shifts/[id]/reopen`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/reopen/route.ts) cancels the closing Contra and the variance Journal — through the new shared [`cancelVoucherInTx`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/cancelVoucherInTx.ts), which the voucher cancel route now also uses, so the balance reversal has one home — clears every closing figure and puts the shift back to OPEN; the manager then re-closes through the ordinary `ShiftCloseDialog` in its force-close form, so no second close path exists. [`POST /api/pos/shifts/[id]/delete`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/delete/route.ts) removes a shift only when it carries no receipts, drops, expenditures or handover, cancelling its opening Contra too; a shift with sales is refused with the advice to reopen instead. **Both refuse when a later shift has already run on that counter** ([`shiftManage.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/shiftManage.ts)) — the next shift started from this one's sweep and carry-over — and when the shift ended by handover. Both write on `prismaAuthoritative`: a counter replica's push drops any `pos_shifts` write against a shift the DB says is CLOSED (the 2026-09-02 guard), which would have swallowed the reopen. Screen: the shift detail page ([`shifts/[id]/page.tsx`](src/app/(protected)/office/pos/shifts/[id]/page.tsx)) grows "Reopen & re-close", "Close shift" (for an open shift) and "Delete shift" for holders of the tick. No schema, seed or permission change. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean on every touched file; **not yet clicked through in a browser** (worktree).
  **Follow-up 2026-09-13 — reopen tolerates a hand-cancelled voucher, and a re-close can be dated.** Owner hit "Voucher is already cancelled" on Shift #1: its ₹14,330 variance Journal had been cancelled from the vouchers screen, and reopen's `cancelVoucherInTx` refused to cancel it twice, rolling the whole reopen back. `cancelVoucherInTx` now takes `{ skipIfCancelled }`; reopen and delete pass it, so an already-cancelled voucher is simply unlinked (it is the state reopen wanted anyway). The voucher cancel route keeps the strict check. Second ask: the corrected close must land on the **original day**, not today.
