[`PATCH /api/pos/shifts/[id]/close`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/close/route.ts) accepts an optional ISO `closedAt` — manager-only (the same force-close / admin tick as reopen), never before `openedAt`, never in the future — used for the shift's `closedAt` **and** as the GL date of the closing Contra and variance Journal (`businessTodayIso(at)` now takes an instant, so the day is the shop's calendar day of that moment). `ShiftCloseDialog` grows an editable "Closed on" date-time (`TallyDateTimeInput`) only when the caller passes `initialClosedAt`; the shift detail page remembers the close moment before reopen and passes it back, so the default is the original time and the owner edits it if that too was wrong. The counter's ordinary close is untouched. Same page: the "Expected / Declared" card printed declared first — label now reads "Declared / Expected".
- **2026-09-12 — A delete on the office now leaves the counter in about a second, not within the hour.** Owner's screenshot, both screens side by side: every coupon deleted on the web office (0 there), all eight still listed in the desktop app on Counter 94 (`R:Local`). Not a delete bug and not coupon-specific: a counter replica pulls by watermark, and a deleted row has no `updatedAt` left to move, so no pull can ever report it. The only thing that removes it is the worker's reconcile pass — at most once an hour per table, 12 tables a cycle — so a delete surfaced on a till anywhere from minutes to well over an hour later. **Fix, the permanent one (owner's choice over a topic→table patch in the worker):** the office server announces what it deleted. `wrapLive` in [`prismaClient.ts`](src/shared/lib/prisma/prismaClient.ts) calls `noteDeleted(model)` after every successful `delete` / `deleteMany` that reached Supabase — the one choke point, since `createLocal()` refuses deletes on a counter — and new [`deleteAnnounce.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/deleteAnnounce.ts) coalesces for 300 ms and publishes `{ topics: [], at, deleted: ["Coupon"] }` through `publishChangeServer` (now takes an extras argument), handing the pending publish to Next's `after()` so a Vercel function is not frozen before it goes out. Browser tabs ignore a signal with no topics, so no screen refetches twice. Worker side: [`signal.js`](sync-engine/lib/signal.js) collects `deleted` across the settle window, [`index.js`](sync-engine/index.js) holds the names in `pendingVerify` until the next non-relay cycle and passes `--verify`, and pass 3 in [`sync-once.js`](sync-engine/sync-once.js) reconciles those tables first, outside the hourly schedule and the budget, together with their registry children and every `ON DELETE CASCADE` referencer (`reconcile.referencingCascades`, sharing one parameterised query with `referencingNullers`) — the source removed those rows itself, the replica deletes the parent with FK actions suspended. Verified: stubbed listener test (three payloads in one burst → one call with `["Coupon","InvBrand"]`, junk entries dropped), and the FK lookup run against Counter 94's real replica config (`Coupon` → cascade `CouponProductRule`, set-null `LoyaltyMilestone`). Still a hint: raw SQL and seeds announce nothing, the hourly pass stays the safety net. **The running counter needs the next build before this is live on it.**
- **2026-09-11 — The customer box now finds people the way a cashier remembers them.** Owner, on the till: *"suppose i remember customers part of address, and part of name, i found the customer with part of mobile no"* — a report of failure, not a feature request. Typing `rosy fancy 9954` returned **nothing**: the search required every token to match and only ever looked at name / phones / ledger aliases, so the locality fragment killed the row, and the mobile alone was what found the customer. The row had been **printing** that address the whole time. Three faults, all fixed. **(1) Nothing was ranked.** The server ordered by `createdAt desc` and the cache imitated it with `id desc`, so results came back by how recently someone *registered*: typing `hri` led with Tas*hri*f, Dhritrish and Bhrigu while **Hridoy Medhi** sat at row 2 and **Hriday Das** at row 6. **(2) Companies were unreachable.** `take: 8` persons + `take: 8` companies, concatenated persons-first, then `.slice(0, 8)` — once eight people matched, no company could appear at all. Both sides now take a full page and the ranked merge chooses between them. **(3) One rigid tier.** Now: name, every phone, every email, **address**, aliases and linked company names, all in the first pass — `address` is a plain COLUMN, so unlike phones it costs no join, and the `wide` flag that used to gate it bought nothing while costing the commonest lookup there is. The matcher is the product box's, lifted to [`search/textScore.ts`](src/shared/lib/search/textScore.ts) and shared: [`partySearch.ts`](src/shared/lib/parties/partySearch.ts) assembles party text, `searchProducts` does the ranking, and **/api/person, the grown cache and the counter all now run the same function** — the old contract was two hand-written matchers kept in step by comment, always one edit from breaking. So `medhidura` splits into a name and a locality **across two different fields** and lands on one row, for the same reason `papera4` works. Address punctuation becomes word breaks ("Abhayapuri,Duramari") or "dura" would rank as debris mid-word. Phone tokens are normalised before matching: phones are stored as bare digits, so `+91 8486-308647` — the form that comes off WhatsApp — had been searched as a *name* and matched nothing; consecutive number-ish tokens are joined first, since a number is dictated with spaces in it. **On the counter the whole customer list is now held in memory** (`partyFullCatalog.ts`, new `GET /api/parties/catalog?sync=1`, warmed at app start beside the ledger list). **⚠ Corrected 2026-09-16: that half never ran a single time.** It gated on `servedByReplica() && localDb() !== null`, and `localDb()` returns null *exactly* when `servedByReplica()` is true — the 2026-09-07 entry below had made it delete the Dexie database on a replica-served till three days earlier. So `fullList` was permanently null, `/api/parties/catalog` had no caller, and `PartiesWarmup` was a no-op on every page. See the 2026-09-16 entry at the top, which makes the whole-list search real and extends it to the web. That reverses the "GROWN, NOT SYNCED" argument at the top of [`partyCatalog.ts`](src/shared/lib/parties/partyCatalog.ts) — deliberately, and only there: `Person`, `Company`, `Phone`, `Email`, `Account`, `AccountAlias` and `PersonCompany` are all in the replica, so reading all 3,543 of them is a local Postgres query and no Supabase bill, and holding them buys the tiered match that no per-query SQL endpoint can offer. The web and unbridged machines keep the grown cache untouched; that file's header now says so. **Row colour is confidence** — normal where a word really matched, amber where only a typo or letter-pair closeness did — and a **sole surviving row is ringed green**, because that is the moment the cashier greets by name. With several rows left the second line leads with whatever *separates* them, so the next question ("which area?" / "last four digits?") is obvious without reading the list. Measured on the real 3,543 parties: `hri` now leads with Hriday/Hridoy/Hrishikesh/Hritik; `medhidura` → **1 row**; `8486-308647` → 1 row; and typing `medhi dura 848` one character at a time falls 8 → 1 and never jumps back up. The dead `/api/pos/people/search` route — a second, divergent party search with no caller anywhere — is gone.

- **2026-09-10 — The update splash can be pushed aside now, not dismissed.** Owner: the updater "should download silently… the gap should display a modal like this with minimize button". The download already was silent and the quiet/urgent/schema tiers stay exactly as they were — the only change is the window that fills the gap between `quitAndInstall` and the new version's first screen ([`scripts/update-splash/AbeerSuiteUpdateSplash.cs`](scripts/update-splash/AbeerSuiteUpdateSplash.cs)). It now has a **minimise button** and sits on the **taskbar**, so a cashier who needs the screen back during a long unpack can get it and still return to the window. Close stays impossible: `ControlBox` has to be on for minimise to be drawn at all, which draws the X with it, so `CS_NOCLOSE` in `CreateParams` takes the X, Alt+F4 and the system-menu Close back out. It still closes on its own when the sentinel disappears or the 15-minute deadline passes.

- **2026-09-09 — A customer's own Outstanding tab, and a bill settled without leaving the customer.** Owner: "add outstanding tab to customer module and let settle". Receivables already answered “who owes us”; what was missing was the same answer from inside the party the user has open. New [`PartyOutstandingsPanel`](src/modules/accountancy/components/PartyOutstandingsPanel.tsx) is that tab — ageing figures, the party's open bills (date, ref linked to the source voucher, its kind, due date, total, paid, outstanding, status) and a settle button on each row. It sits on all three customer screens, for people and companies alike: [`customers/all`](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/all/page.tsx), [`customers/person`](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/person/page.tsx), [`customers/companies`](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/companies/page.tsx). **It reads one ledger, not the book.** `getLedgerOutstanding` survived the drill-down page's deletion with no HTTP route left, so a lean one is back — `GET /api/accountancy/outstandings/[accountId]?asOf=` — rather than pulling Receivables down and filtering it in the browser. **A party screen has to ask the bill which way its money runs**, where a group report already knows: that helper reads BOTH directions, so a party who is our customer *and* our supplier has both sides in one list. [`OutstandingBill`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/outstandings.ts) therefore gained **`side` (`1` they owe us, `-1` we owe them)**, taken from the party's own leg — the same leg the 05:40 fix made the report trust — with the voucher type as the fallback for a row with no readable leg. The button follows it: **Receive** opens a receipt, **Pay** a payment, as a dialog over the customer profile, so the user never loses the party they opened. For the same reason the summary is **split by side** — one row of ageing cards per side that has money, “Total Receivable” and “Total Payable” — and the bill table has **no footer total**: adding money owed to us to money owed by us is not a total of anything. **Cost:** the tab reads only when opened, holds the answer for 60s so switching tabs costs nothing, refreshes quietly on the `ledger` topic, and drops its cache the moment a bill is settled. **Shared, not copied:** the period labels and colours and the status wording moved to [`lib/ageing.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/ageing.ts), and the compact `05 Sep 26` date to `formatDateCompact` in [formatDate.ts](src/shared/lib/format/formatDate.ts) — [`OutstandingsReportScreen`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OutstandingsReportScreen.tsx) reads both from there, so the `TODO(config)` on the 30/60/90 boundaries has one home. The tab shows today's position; the “As on” box stays on the reports. `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean; **not yet opened in the browser** — the work was done in a worktree, so the visual pass waits for the merge.

- **2026-09-09 — The reason behind the reason: one rule, enforced in one route and not the other.** Owner: _"dont warry about data because we will clean before production deploy, need to think about the reason why problem come and fix so that problem never happen"_. The silent GL skip below was the mechanism; the cause is older and simpler. **`POST /api/inventory/vouchers` checked that the tenders sum to `totalAmount`** and refused with 400 otherwise — the invariant that keeps a voucher's money side equal to its goods side. **`PATCH …/[id]` never did**, and its settlement merge is `tenders !== undefined ? tenders : existingSettlement.tenders`, so an edit that changes the lines while saying nothing about tenders keeps the OLD money rows. Goods to ₹211,885.84, money left at ₹76,319. The GL builder was the only thing that noticed, and its objection was thrown away. A rule written once and copied nowhere is a rule half the code does not have. **[`tenderCoverageComplaint(tenders, total)`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/postInvVoucher.ts)** is now that rule, in one place, returning the sentence to show the user or null: POST calls it instead of its inline copy, PATCH calls it on the **effective** total and tenders (given, else stored) before the transaction opens. A voucher with no payment rows at all is still left to the GL builder, which knows which types legitimately have none. **Proved, not believed** — `pnpm verify:bill-links` is now **53 checks**: the rule itself (short, exact, a paisa of rounding, no rows), an in-place edit whose payment rows no longer cover the total refused with `GlBuildError`, and the ledger that voucher already had left byte-for-byte as it was. **And a detector, `pnpm check:voucher-ledger`** ([script](src/scripts/check-voucher-ledger.ts)): read-only, sweeps every posted trading voucher and compares its invoice against its ledger's money legs, flagging any posted with no ledger entry at all and marking the ones edited after posting — the run that would have caught POS-26-27-0039 on day one instead of never. It says plainly that a service CHARGE row makes the ledger legitimately exceed `totalAmount`, so a gap is read rather than assumed. Defence in depth, four layers: the shared rule at the door, the builder's `GlSkipReason` behind it, the harness proving both, and the sweep for anything a seed or an import writes behind the app's back.

- **2026-09-09 — The voucher whose invoice and ledger disagreed: an edit was allowed to keep the old GL.** Chasing the ₹135,566.84 ghost above to its cause. `POS-26-27-0039` carries 31 lines worth ₹211,885.84 and a GL of ₹78,159.31 with no party leg. The **audit log** told the story: at 2026-08-15 16:55 the owner edited it in place, and the pre-edit snapshot shows 13 lines, total ₹76,319, tender cash ₹76,319 — balanced, with a GL **byte-identical to today's**. The edit raised the header and left the tender alone, so [`buildInvVoucherGlLines`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/postInvVoucher.ts) built Dr ₹78,159.31 against Cr ₹213,725.91, hit its own balance check and returned `skip` — and [`reapplyTradingVoucherInPlace`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/reapplyTradingVoucher.ts) rewrites the ledger only `if (spec.glLines.length > 0)`. The lines and the header changed, the ledger silently kept the pre-edit figures, and the edit committed. **The root cause is that an empty `glLines` says nothing about why**: a GRN that never posts to GL and a sale that does not balance look identical to the caller, so carrying on is the only thing it can do. The builder now says which — `GlSkipReason` **"none"** (type does not post, zero-value voucher) or **"refused"** (unbalanced, no tender row, no body ledger) — carried on the spec as `glSkipReason` + `glNotes`. The edit path throws on "refused" (and on any voucher that had GL lines and would come out with none), inside the route's transaction so nothing is written; **`enginePost` throws too**, because the same silence at create would post an invoice with no ledger entry at all. `GlBuildError` carries it the way `BillLinkError` does and both inventory-voucher routes answer **422** with the message in the user's own words: _"the payment rows come to ₹78,159.31 but the voucher comes to ₹213,726.15"_. **`GET /api/audit/[id]`** is new — the list route returns no snapshot, so this evidence was unreadable; it returns one recycle-bin snapshot in full, read-only, on the same per-kind key as restore. A sweep of all **316 posted inventory vouchers** found only three whose money side does not match the invoice: this one, `SV-26-27-0169` (not a fault — ₹3,500 of goods plus a ₹400 service charge, ₹3,900 collected; `totalAmount` excludes charges) and `PV-26-27-0011` (₹1.45 of rounding drift across header, lines, tender and leg). **The three vouchers are left as they are** — `POS-26-27-0039`'s ₹135,566.84 is an invoice body with no accounting behind it, and whether those goods were really sold on credit is the owner's to say, not a report's to invent. Verified live: a no-op edit of that voucher is now refused 422 and its GL, lines, header and description are untouched afterwards.

- **2026-09-09 — The counter no longer opens on a spinner: it opens on its own sign-in screen.** Owner, comparing us to VS Code, Discord, Slack, Postman, Obsidian, Notion and GitHub Desktop: *“to avoid server start can we switch to IPC”*. The comparison is fair — none of those boot a local server; they load a file off disk into a client-side SPA and reach Node over IPC. **We cannot, yet:** `loadApp()` navigates the window to `http://127.0.0.1:786`, ~107 of 260 pages are server-rendered, `src/proxy.ts` and NextAuth gate every route, and the same tree deploys to Vercel. So the server stays — but the *waiting* did not have to. [`resources/shell/login.html`](resources/shell/login.html) is now loaded by `loadFile()` in the first frame, and the ten-odd seconds Postgres and Next take are spent typing into a real form instead of watching a spinner. Plain HTML by necessity: it runs before any server exists. It carries the shop logo inlined as a data URI, a greeting and clock, the existing `sendBootProgress` messages on a progress bar, rotating counter tips **written from the real key handlers in `usePosBilling.ts`** (F2 customer box, `/` scan box, F6 new bill, arrows + `+`/`-` on a cart line, Ctrl+Enter to put the rest on cash), and a feature panel built from the module registry in `src/utils/constants.ts` — dropped below 1080px, because a cramped card beats no card on a 1366 counter screen. The background is a photograph through a fallback chain (owner's own picture → the copy main kept → the configured URLs → an inlined SVG floor), so it is never blank for a frame; the owner corrected an early assumption that counters boot offline — they have internet, and cache covers the gaps. Everything editable sits in one `CONFIG` block at the top of the file. **The sign-in itself deliberately does not happen in main.** `/staff/login` also reconciles this PC's counter binding, resets a released counter and enforces `auth:login:outside-counter`; signing in behind its back would skip all three. So main parks what was typed, navigates, and the real page collects it through `takeShellCredentials` and submits it itself — the shell buys the typing time and nothing more. A cashier who never signed out is sent straight in (`hasStaffSession()`), never shown a login screen for a session they already hold. `warmRoutes` now fires the moment the server answers rather than waiting on `did-finish-load`: the shell is already up, so those seconds *are* the idle ones. The old `splashHtml()` is gone. _Shipped as `1.3.1-pre.1` to the test release repo only, to be measured against the office build before it is kept._

- **2026-09-09 — Receivables was reporting money nobody owed, and the popup is out of the settlement path.** Owner opened the link popup on a bill and got "₹ 0.00 to place · This voucher has nothing against this party" — and the popup was right. `SV-26-27-0045` is a **cash sale**: its GL is Dr Cash 200 / Dr Discount 15.05 against Sales + tax, with **no party leg at all**. The report listed it as ₹200 receivable because it worked out what was unpaid as *invoice total − legs tagged `role=TENDER`*, and that trusts a tag — this voucher's cash leg was never tagged (tagging arrived later, by backfill), so ₹200 of money already taken read as ₹0 of money taken. **[`loadOpenBills`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/outstandings.ts) now reads the party's own leg on the bill**, less what has been linked against it — the same rule [`billLink.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) uses, so the report and the popup cannot disagree again: a tag can be forgotten, a leg cannot, and if the party owes nothing there is nothing on their ledger to read. The leg's **direction** also decides which report a bill belongs in. One query for the whole report (`accountId × spineId`), not one per row; spine-less legacy rows keep the tender reading, having nothing better. **On demo data Receivables goes ₹205,752.61 → ₹41,038.75 over 43 → 30 bills.** Thirteen bills had no party leg at all (₹149,713.86), of which ₹135,566.84 is one POS voucher whose stored `totalAmount` says ₹211,885.84 while its own GL balances at ₹78,159.31 with no AR leg — and whose customer's ledger closes at **₹41,496.95 Cr**, an advance, with no entry for that voucher anywhere. `SV-26-27-0279` was printing its whole ₹22,401.25 when ₹15,000 of it went to petty cash and a bank on the day; it now reads paid ₹15,000, open ₹7,401.25, exactly its legs. Payables also moved ₹197,737.95 → ₹166,886.50, but **not because of this change**: `PV-26-27-0010` was settled in full by `PMT-26-27-0015` while the work was going on, and the old reading drops a fully-linked bill just as the new one does. Its books are consistent — invoice ₹30,850, party leg ₹30,850. _The two vouchers whose stored total disagrees with their own GL are left alone: that is a data question for the owner, not something a report should paper over._ **And the popup left the reports entirely** — "we don't need the modal, open voucher directly because voucher already covered everything". True: the receipt / payment form carries the open-bills panel that ticks this bill, lists every other open item of the party and settles from credits they already hold. So the row's button opens that voucher as a dialog over the report, with `settleKind` on each page naming the side (a receivable is received, a payable is paid) instead of re-deriving it, and `BillLinkDialog` keeps only its real job — linking a voucher that has *just* been posted — losing the `settleDirectly` / `onSettle` props the reports had needed.

- **2026-09-09 — Settling a bill from Receivables / Payables no longer costs a popup or the page.** Owner, on a party with nothing open on the other side: "no need to open the modal if nothing to link, open voucher modal directly instead" — then "open receipt or payment vouchers modal variant so we stay in the page". Two small props carry it. [`BillLinkDialog`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BillLinkDialog.tsx) takes **`settleDirectly`**: once the open-bill list is back, if the party has nothing on the other side and the bill still has money on it, the popup does not sit there with a single live button — it calls `openSettlingVoucher()` itself (one-shot, guarded by a ref). It also takes **`onSettle`**, which decides *where* that voucher opens: given, the caller hosts the form; left out, the old `router.push` to `/vouchers/{receipts,payments}/new?partyAccountId=..&amount=..&billId=..` stands, so the Sale / Purchase and GL voucher screens are untouched — a form must not throw the user off the voucher just posted. [`OutstandingsReportScreen`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OutstandingsReportScreen.tsx) passes both and renders [`GlVoucherDialog`](src/shared/components/GlVoucherDialog.tsx) over itself, so the receipt / payment opens **on top of the report** — the URL never leaves `/office/accountancy/reports/payables`. To feed the form inside a dialog, which has no route of its own to read, `GlVoucherPage` gained **`settleBill` (`{partyAccountId, amount, billId}`)** and its settle-a-bill effect now reads that in preference to the URL — the same three values, one code path. Closing the dialog either way (saved, cancelled, Esc) clears the bill-link cache and re-reads the report. **Found while testing:** the popup's own **Link** button had never worked — `POST /allocations` has taken `links: [{ voucherId, amount }]` since the bill-wise rewrite (2026-09-08), but `buildPlan` still sent `universalVoucherId`, so every press came back Zod's "Invalid input: expected number, received undefined" and no link was written; `settleFromPicks` already mapped it correctly, and the popup now does the same. Verified in the browser on Payables: the link button on Ashlesh Enterprises' PV-26-27-0015 opens the Payment Voucher dialog straight away with the party, ₹ 21,106 and the bill ticked; JSK Computer, which *has* an open payment to link, still gets the popup, and its "Pay now" opens the same dialog instead of navigating; `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean.

- **2026-09-09 — Receivables and Payables became one screen, searchable, filterable by period, and the drill-down page went away.** Owner asked for a search box, then for it to cover *every* identifier, then for the ageing cards to act as filters, then why Payables had no tabs, and finally why a separate page existed at all when the same report can just filter itself. **One screen now serves both** — [`OutstandingsReportScreen`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OutstandingsReportScreen.tsx) — and each page says only which side of the book it reads; Payables thereby gained the Party-wise / Bill-wise tabs and the bill-wise settle link it never had. **Search matches identifiers, not just names:** `getOutstandings` builds a lower-cased `searchKey` per party (name + GSTIN + every phone number, plus company reg / tax id) and per bill row (that key plus the voucher no), from three small selects run **only for the parties that actually have an open bill** — a creditors group holds thousands of settled ledgers. Typing then filters what is already on screen: no refetch, no extra DB read. **The four ageing cards are the period switches** — click one to see that period alone, click it again or the Total Outstanding card to see all; the cards keep reporting the whole book (they are the buttons, so their figures must not move), while the row under each table adds up only what is shown, and Party-wise moves its Total column to the chosen period so the eye and the footer agree. A `TODO(config)` marks the 30/60/90 boundaries — still in `bucketFor()` — for the settings page the owner wants. **The Ledger Outstandings page is deleted** (page + `/api/accountancy/reports/ledger-outstandings` + its breadcrumb): clicking a party now narrows the report in place, held by **account id** so two look-alike names cannot be confused, with a chip naming the party and Esc to clear. What that page had over the report came along — the Ref column is a link to the source document via `referenceHref`. `getLedgerOutstanding` stays in the lib; the POS account-snapshot route reads it. **The link popup stopped building vouchers.** "Receive / Pay now" no longer opens a form inside the dialog; it hands the bill to the real screen — `/vouchers/receipts/new?partyAccountId=..&amount=..&billId=..` — and `GlVoucherPage` reads those three off the URL once on mount, opening with the party in the first particulars row, the amount typed and that bill already ticked in its open-bills panel. The date, the cash or bank account and any part payment stay the user's, on the screen that owns them. `POST /api/accountancy/allocations/settle` had no other caller and is removed; `postSettlementVoucher` stays, since POS still posts receipts through its wrapper. Screens also lost a chunk of vertical space — shorter period cards, smaller heading, tighter rows — after "reduce blank spaces to save vertical space".

- **2026-09-09 — Auto-Link got a priority, a promise, and a way to be saved twice safely.** Owner compared our Open Bills panel against Vyapar's "Link Payment to Txns" and asked for bill-wise settlement proper: auto-link by priority, manual choices always winning, and operations safe against duplicate clicks — "no popup exiting UI is ok… just add what missing". **Priority** is one comparator in [`billLinkPlan`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLinkPlan.ts), in the owner's words "kill overdue, then nearest due date, for same due date lowest amount": sorting by due date puts the longest overdue first and walks forward into the not-yet-due, and a tie goes to the SMALLER bill because that closes more bills with the same money. Bill date drops out of the rule entirely. **Manual wins** — `planLinks` now builds *around* the ticks already made: their money leaves the budget first and they are never lowered, so Auto can only fill the remainder (it used to rebuild the whole map and throw hand-made ticks away). A tick that no longer fits is clamped and said out loud rather than counted on screen and dropped at save. **Reset** clears every tick, auto and manual alike; saved links are untouched, because a tick is only a proposal. **Saving is declarative**: each line states what the pair should TOTAL, not what to add, so a replay lands on the same figure — the real hazard was never the double click (already refused for having no room) but the *partial retry*, where a committed plan whose response was lost got re-added to any bill with room left. A party-scoped `pg_advisory_xact_lock(4711, accountId)` closes the matching race, since the unique key stops a second ROW for a pair but not a doubled amount on the one row; deliberately **not** an upsert, because that key includes `sourceType` and a legacy `PAYMENT`-sourced row would be invisible to it, giving two rows, a bill netting to zero, and a disappearance from Receivables. Validation moved from per-link to `assertLinkPlan` — a 200-line plan was ~1,400 queries inside one transaction. Four integrity holes closed alongside: **editing a linked voucher** is now refused with "unlink first" (shrinking a fully-linked ₹10,000 receipt to ₹3,000 left it at −₹7,000 open, so it vanished from the list while the bill it settled still counted the money and dropped out of Receivables), as is moving a settled voucher to another party; **opening bills reach the reports** ([`outstandings`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/outstandings.ts) built bills from SALE/POS/PURCHASE/notes only, so a converted opening balance linked fine and was simply missing from Receivables — its worth comes from the party leg, not the debit legs, whose other side is Suspense); **settlements print on both sides** (the receipt sample read target-side rows only, so a purchase settled against a sale printed as fully unpaid to a supplier); and **POS clears the same bill the office does**, sharing the comparator instead of sorting by bill date. One `dueDateOf` replaced four copies that stepped a UTC-midnight date with local-time `setDate()`. Panel gained Auto, Reset, a scrolling list and a **Linked** column — its old "Settled" was `face − open`, which also swallowed cash taken at the counter and so read differently from the same column in the popup. `pnpm opening:to-bills` and a **"Convert all to bills"** button finally run the backfill that had no caller anywhere; both skip a party already broken up by hand and both ask twice, because converting moves the balance the POS credit check reads. `pnpm verify:bill-links` is now **44 checks**, including the same plan saved twice, the refused edits, and an opening bill in the outstanding report — the check whose absence let that gap live.
- **2026-09-09 — Screen Size: a slider, because not everyone has the same eyes.** Owner: keep Automatic, but let each person set their own size. [`ScreenSizeForm`](src/modules/settings/components/ScreenSizeForm.tsx) now offers a native range input 50–150% in 5% steps (the same bounds main clamps to in `displayPreference.ts`, so nothing the slider offers can be refused), with − / + nudge buttons, a large live percentage, and "Back to Automatic". The slider applies on release, not on every pixel of the drag — applying mid-drag jitters the very window being dragged in. The seven quick steps from main stay as shortcuts. No Electron change: `display:set` already accepted any factor in range. Native `<input type="range">` with `accent-primary`, not a new slider component — nothing else in the app needed one.
- **2026-09-09 — Screen Size: hidden in the web version, granted like its siblings.** Owner's call, reversing the R1-only decision below: the page is treated "normally like the Thermal Printer page" — `ui:sidebar:settings:screen-size` is now granted to R1, R2, R3 and pooled into `DEP-OP` / `DEP-SALES`, exactly as `thermal-printer` and `qr-display` are, in both the snapshot and Supabase (one-off idempotent script, run once and deleted; `sync-permissions` refuses on this PC because it is a counter). The one difference: the link is meaningless in a browser (it drives the Electron window), so `SettingsNavItem` gained `desktopOnly`, and [`Sidebar.tsx`](src/shared/components/Sidebar.tsx) hides such links unless `isElectronEnvironment()` — decided in a `useEffect`, never inline, so the server HTML and the first client paint agree. Role Matrix chip and department pool need no code: both derive from `SETTINGS_NAV`. Verified in the browser: Thermal Printer and QR Display listed, Screen Size absent. Holders must sign in again to pick up the grant (permissions ride the JWT; no session revoke was run).
- **2026-09-08 — The app now sizes itself to the counter's screen.** Owner: "the app best fit in 80% zoom, because it is developed in big screen but counter screen is smaller. so it invite scroll bar often." Two separate causes, and only one of them was about zoom. **(1) The window was bigger than the screen.** `createShellWindow` opened at a fixed 1366 x 860 (and child report windows at 1280 x 820) without ever asking the display how big it was, so a counter with a 1366 x 768 screen got a window ~130px taller than the screen it sat on — the bottom of every page under the taskbar, and scrollbars no amount of zoom would have removed. New `fitToScreen()` clamps both to `screen.getPrimaryDisplay().workArea`, brings `minWidth`/`minHeight` down with it (a floor above the screen would undo the fit at show time), and maximises the main window when the design size did not fit. **(2) 80% is now the app's own default**, instead of the owner pressing Ctrl+− on each counter. New [`displayPreference.ts`](electron/displayPreference.ts) resolves a zoom from the screen's **width only** — `round(width / 1700)` to the nearest 0.05, clamped 0.7–1.0, where 1700 is the design width **derived from the owner's own measurement** (1366 / 0.8 ≈ 1700). That gives 1366 → **80%**, 1536 → 90%, 1680 and up → 100%, 1280 → 75%. Height is deliberately out of the formula: a table scrolling vertically is how a table is meant to behave, it is the sideways scrollbar that reads as broken, and folding height in dropped a normal 1366 x 768 counter to 75%. **Why zoom and not smaller CSS:** the screens write their small text as fixed pixels — `text-[10px]` / `text-[11px]`, **918 places** — so a CSS-only shrink pulls the boxes in while that text stays put and spills out of them. Zoom scales text, icons, padding and borders in exact proportion, which is precisely the result the owner had already verified by hand. The choice is saved per PC in `display.json` in `userData`, the same shape as `autostart.json`, and re-applied on every `did-finish-load` (Electron keeps zoom per origin, and one boot crosses a `data:` splash and http://localhost). **A real application menu replaces Electron's default one** for one reason: the built-in Zoom In / Zoom Out change the zoom without telling the app, so a size set with the keyboard was forgotten on restart; the replacement routes them through `stepZoom`, which saves, and Ctrl+0 means "back to Automatic". Edit (undo/cut/copy/paste) is carried over deliberately — on Windows those accelerators come from the menu, and dropping it would kill Ctrl+C in every input box. **New page: Settings → Screen Size** ([`screen-size`](src/app/(protected)/office/settings/screen-size/page.tsx) + [`ScreenSizeForm`](src/modules/settings/components/ScreenSizeForm.tsx)) — Automatic (showing what it resolved to *on this PC*) plus fixed steps 70–125%, applied live so a busy page can be watched while choosing. It is added to `COUNTER_SETTINGS_KEEP` — without that, counter mode hides the page and the counter staff it exists for could never reach it. **Correction, same day — the claim that this needed "no permission, catalog or seed change" was wrong.** It was true only of the *business* permission (the entry reuses `settings:manage`). Adding the entry to `SETTINGS_NAV` **auto-created a permission**: [`sidebarModules.ts`](src/shared/lib/auth/sidebarModules.ts) derives one `ui:sidebar:<module>:<link>` id per nav link and [`codeOwnedCatalog.ts`](prisma/seeds/lib/codeOwnedCatalog.ts) folds those into the catalog, so `ui:sidebar:settings:screen-size` existed in code and in no database — and the Role page would have shown its chip while **silently dropping the grant on save** (`dropped.unseeded`). Two more defects from the same commit: `check:nav-icons` failed (`Monitor → POS, Screen Size`; the entry now uses `MonitorCog`), which had **`pnpm precommit` red**; and the `GROUP_SLUGS` edit in [`SettingsNav.tsx`](src/modules/admin/components/SettingsNav.tsx) was a **no-op, because that component is imported nowhere** — reachability is decided only by the `ui:sidebar:*` grant, since `Sidebar.tsx` ignores `item.permission` and the settings layout has no guard. **Fixed with a new reusable applier, [`sync-permissions.ts`](prisma/seeds/sync-permissions.ts)** (`pnpm seed:sync-permissions`), rather than a full seed, whose R1 top-up would have added ~109 grants to R1 and resurrected anything deliberately removed in the Role Matrix. It imports `PERMISSION_CATALOG` so it embeds no permission list of its own — the flaw that got the old `apply-permissions` deleted — creates only what the DB lacks, **never deletes** (a DB row absent from the catalog is reported and left alone), and bumps `tokensValidFrom` unless `--no-revoke`, because permissions ride the JWT. Owner's call, after a first pass that granted all three: **R1 only** — so `--revoke-from` was added as the symmetric twin of `--roles` (it removes a `RolePermission`, never the `Permission` itself, and is scoped to the one id and the named roles). Counter staff never see the page and do not need to: Automatic gives them the right size, and Ctrl+ + / Ctrl+ − still works and still sticks. Applied to Supabase and verified — R1 went 177 → **178**, exactly +1, which is the proof no R1 top-up ran. Snapshot matches the database (`rolePermissions` 380; R1 178, R2 165, R3 37). `departmentPermissions` deliberately untouched — sidebar pooling is module-level, so `DEP-OP`/`DEP-SALES` already unlock the whole settings module. `Permission` and `RolePermission` are both in `sync-tables.json`, so the counters receive the row and the grants by sync; nothing to run on a counter, and `assertNotCounterReplica` refuses if anyone tries. **One pre-existing drift found on the way and deliberately left alone:** R3 holds `auth:login:outside-counter` in the snapshot but not in the live database — someone took it off the Counter Operator since the 6 Sept capture, which is a sensible tightening. It is flagged rather than fixed because a full `pnpm prisma db seed` would push it back on, re-enabling off-counter login for counter operators. Owner to settle by capture or by re-ticking. In a browser the page says so and points at the browser's own zoom. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.json` (`--max-old-space-size=8192`) and `electron/tsconfig.json`, ESLint clean on every touched file. **Owner to confirm on a real counter:** whether Automatic picks the right level there — that is the one thing that cannot be checked from here.
- **2026-09-08 — Receipt / Payment form made counter-friendly; optional payment instrument.** Owner: "we are manually adding next ledger, an unwanted balance matching card in the middle, the UI is not industry standard, need payment instrument (optional)." In [`GlVoucherPage`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GlVoucherPage.tsx), **Payment and Receipt only** (Contra / Credit Note / Debit Note keep the Dr·Cr layout): the Dr/Cr prefixes, the derived top amount and the "Dr · Cr · Balanced/Incomplete" card are gone — one **Total received / Total paid** line under the table instead (the money side always equals the particulars, so there was nothing to match). **Tally-style rows:** a blank entry row is always waiting at the bottom (no Add Row); blank rows are ignored for totals, validation and save; picking a ledger jumps to its amount, Enter on the amount jumps to the next row's ledger, Enter on the empty last row jumps to Narration. The **note box shows only for the row in hand** (or one that already has a note) and Tab skips it, so the list stays short. **Payment instrument (optional) on each particulars row** — owner's second pass: "remove instrument from Received in because it's not one" (one party pays by UPI, the next by cheque) — a **By** dropdown (Cash / UPI / Card / Cheque / Bank transfer / Other) + a **Ref no.** box per row, stored on that party line's existing `VoucherGlLine.method` + `.reference` columns (the POS tender columns; **no schema change**), accepted by `POST /api/accountancy/vouchers` and the `[id]` PATCH, reloaded in edit mode and by Duplicate, and part of the edit dirty-check. Labels live in one place, `PAYMENT_INSTRUMENTS` in [`constants/accounting.ts`](src/shared/lib/constants/accounting.ts). **Print:** `buildGlSample` now sends only the money-side line(s) for Payment / Receipt, with the rows' instruments gathered onto it — "`Cash – Counter 24 · Cheque (IN955468), UPI (4532…)`" — so the receipt's "By:" line reads how the money came instead of listing every GL leg including the party; templates untouched. **Last-used Cash/Bank ledger is remembered per kind** (`localStorage` `glvoucher:lastTop:<kind>`, applied on the first accounts load in create mode, kept after each save) so a routine receipt is party + amount. Header (`VoucherTopBar`) and footer (`VoucherActionBar`) unchanged by request. **Later the same day:** the By / Ref no. columns are **hidden behind `SHOW_INSTRUMENT_COLUMNS = false`** (owner: the row note carries the instrument details for now; everything behind them stays wired), entry boxes sit white on the grey page with fainter hints, and a party's **open bills show beside the form the moment the party is picked** — new [`OpenBillsPanel`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OpenBillsPanel.tsx), read-only, from the same `/allocations/open` API the popup uses (`side=1` for a Receipt, `-1` for a Payment), one request in flight per party and a 30s cache cleared after a post or a link; then made a **tick-list** (owner: "give check box for billwise"): with an amount already typed, ticks fill bills WITHIN it (full, then a `*`-marked part of the next; a further tick is refused once placed); with the amount empty, each tick ADDS the bill to the row and unticking takes it out; typing below the placed total drops ticks last-first. Each bill is a **Bill | Total | Settled | Open** line, and the ledger money not linked to any bill (opening balance, unlinked receipts, advances) sits under the bills as an **On account** row (not tickable — an untouched remainder already lands there), so bills + on account = the **Ledger balance** shown beneath; that is why the ledger figure and the bill-wise open can differ. The ticks are handed to `BillLinkDialog` (new `initialPlan` prop) after posting, so the popup opens ticked the same way. The link itself still happens there (only two posted vouchers can link), so the on-save popup is otherwise unchanged. Verified in the browser on `/vouchers/receipts/new`: Enter-flow, draft save (201), thermal print showing "By: Cash – Counter 24 · Cheque (IN955468)", edit-page prefill of the instrument, remembered ledger on a fresh form, note box appearing only for the focused row; `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean.
- **2026-09-08 — Bill-wise link did not work, and one idea fixed the whole class.** Owner asked whether it worked. It did not: the entire `VoucherAllocation` table held **one row ever** — ₹836, `RV-26-27-0016` — and **both its ends were dangling**, pointing at a bill and a receipt that had been hard-deleted, so that ₹836 was still being netted off a bill by a receipt that no longer existed. Ten posted Receipts and 24 posted Payments had **no link at all**, against 311 posted bills; `partyAccountId` was **null on all 34** of them, because [`GlVoucherPage`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GlVoucherPage.tsx) read that field only when duplicating and never sent it on save. The one button that promised the feature, `TradingVoucherPage`'s **Link Payment**, was a `toast.info("coming soon")`; a finished 219-line `BillAllocationDrawer` sat with **zero importers** and types that no longer matched the API; and `/allocations/open` returned `VoucherDetail.id` where every reader nets by **spine id**, so posting its answer back would have linked the wrong document.

  **The idea.** Stop asking what kind of voucher something is and read **the party's own leg in the ledger**: `side = sign(Σ debits − Σ credits on that party ledger)`, `open = |leg| − linked`. New [`billLink.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLink.ts) owns it, with the pure planning half in [`billLinkPlan.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLinkPlan.ts) so the popup can import it without pulling Prisma into the client bundle. Every one of the owner's four asks then needs **no special case**: a link is legal when both vouchers touch the same party ledger with **opposite non-zero signs**, which gives sale↔receipt, sale↔credit-note, **sale↔purchase** for a party who is both customer and supplier, and **journal↔either**, while correctly refusing sale↔sale and anything with a Contra (no party leg → side 0 → never offered).

  **`sumLinked` replaced `sumReceiptAllocations` + `sumVoucherAllocations`** — byte-identical bodies — and changed two things about them. It counts a link from **either** side, without which a purchase settling a sale never came down; and it ignores a link whose **other end is not alive**, which is what makes a cancel or a delete **reopen the counterpart by itself**, with no repair job. That read-time rule is the only one that survives a Recycle-Bin restore, which re-inserts with a **fresh id** — a link keyed on the old id could never be mended. Hard-delete additionally takes its links with it and records them in the AuditLog snapshot (`billLinks`), but correctness does not depend on that running. Cancel deliberately leaves them: the read filter already reopens the bill, and deleting on cancel would be lossy if un-cancel ever lands.

  **The popup** ([`BillLinkDialog.tsx`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BillLinkDialog.tsx), replacing the orphaned drawer): tick the bills, with an editable amount per row for a part payment, **or** press **Auto**, which clears **oldest first** — the same order the POS surplus already uses. Both go through one `planLinks`, so the save path never knows which was used, and anything left over stays as the party's advance (with symmetric netting an unlinked receipt **is** the advance, so `ON_ACCOUNT` / `ADVANCE` / `NEW_REF` need no row — `AGAINST_REF` is the only method written now). Mounted on the Sale/Purchase voucher (replacing the stub), the office Receipt/Payment voucher, the Receivables bill rows, and Ledger-Outstandings rows — which is where a dual-role party's two sides both show. `GET /allocations/open` now returns **one list keyed by spine id** plus `self`, so it tells the caller how much of the open voucher is free to place and narrows the list to the side it may legally settle; the caller reasons about no signs at all.

  **Also fixed on the way.** `POST /allocations` had no over-allocation, same-party, self-link or aliveness check, and normalised only `targetType` — a client could send `sourceType:"PAYMENT"` and the row became **permanently invisible** to every reader. It now takes `{accountId, voucherId, links[]}` in one transaction through `assertLinkable`, normalises orientation so the receivable side is always the target, and is idempotent; `DELETE` gained the same permission gate (it was `requireAuth()` alone). Both are gated on `cancel` for **both** kinds via the existing voucher matrix, so **no new permission** and no seed/catalog churn. `GlVoucherPage` now stamps `partyAccountId` (top account for a credit/debit note; the single party row for a receipt/payment — several parties leaves it null, and nothing is lost because the link rules read the GL legs). Ledger-Outstandings' credit/debit-note rows pointed at `/office/accountancy/journal-entries/<id>`, **a route that does not exist**, so every one 404'd — replaced with the shared `referenceHref`, which knows both id spaces. And `ledgerStatusFilterUv` moved to [`ledgerStatus.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/ledgerStatus.ts) (re-exported from `aggregateLedger`, so no importer changed) because reaching it dragged `balance.ts` → `releaseGuard` → `server-only` in, which made anything importing it unusable from a plain `tsx` script.

  **Schema.** `VoucherAllocation` gained `@@unique([targetType, targetId, sourceType, sourceId])` — one row per pair, so re-linking tops the existing row up instead of leaving two rows telling half the story each. `resources/local-schema.sql` regenerated (`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, so an older replica cannot trip on it); the sync registry is unchanged, since an index does not affect strategy. **`prisma db push` is still owed on live.**

  **Follow-up the same day — "what do I link against when there is nothing?"** Two more asks, and one of them needed no code. **A sale return already reopens room**: it credits the party, so it is a payable-side document with a sign like any other, and the engine never consults `refReturns` — proved by five new checks (settle a sale in full, issue a ₹1200 return, see it offered against the next sale, link it, ₹1800 left to collect, earlier sale untouched). The other ask did need building: when a bill has nothing to link against, **create the settling voucher in the popup**. `postReceiptVoucher` only ever wrote receipts, so it is now the RECEIPT-only wrapper around **`postSettlementVoucher`**, which does both directions — the legs simply swap sides, and every other rule is shared. New `POST /api/accountancy/allocations/settle` creates AND links in **one transaction**, so a settlement voucher can never be left floating because the link failed; the full `assertLinkable` check runs here (unlike the POS path, which trusts its own FIFO planner for speed) because a human chose the bill and the amount. **The kind is never asked** — a receivable takes a RECEIPT, a payable takes a PAYMENT — and the route refuses to "settle" a RECEIPT / PAYMENT / CONTRA, since money that already moved needs linking, not more money. **A permission bug shipped in the commit above and is fixed here:** the allocation routes used `userCanVoucher`, which returns **false** for kinds with no matrix row — so linking any of the 29 POS bills would have been forbidden for everyone. `userCanVoucherKind` is the pass-through variant those routes wanted all along. `verify:bill-links` is now **24 checks, all passing**.

  **Follow-up 2 — every opening balance can now be a list of bills.** `Account.openingBalance` is a number on the master with **no GL lines behind it**; reports fold it in at display time. Since Receivables, Payables and linking all read GL legs, **₹5 lakh across 89 party ledgers was invisible to all three** and could not be settled against. New [`openingBills.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/openingBills.ts) lets that one number become the bills it really was — each a real `OPENING` voucher with a party leg, so it ages, shows as outstanding and links, **with no new engine code**: `billLink` reads the party's leg and never asks what kind of voucher made it. **No double-counting**, because the two layers coexist per account and never both: `aggregateLedger.loadMasterOpenings` folds in only accounts whose scalar is not null, so converting one (scalar nulled, vouchers posted) is automatically correct and **not one report had to change**. Bill number and date are the shop's own record of the old books; the date drives ageing and defaults to 1 April of `indianFyStartYear()`, the same helper the voucher numbering uses. `PUT /api/accountancy/accounts/[id]/opening-bills` saves a breakup; `POST /api/accountancy/opening-balance/to-bills` (dry-run by default, one transaction per account so one bad party cannot undo eighty good ones) converts every party at once into a single bill each. The shared [`OpeningBillsDialog`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OpeningBillsDialog.tsx) is mounted on the Opening Balance master page. **A settled opening bill cannot be dropped or reduced below what is already against it** — that would un-settle a banked receipt.

  **Three things the live database taught us on the way.** (1) **The counter-leg cannot go to "Difference in Opening Balances":** `bootstrap-seed.ts` says outright that no such ledger may exist — it is a synthetic the Balance Sheet computes as Σ(Dr openings) − Σ(Cr openings) — which also means [`reconcileOpeningSuspense`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/openingBalance.ts) **has never done anything**, since it looks that ledger up and returns early. Openings post both legs instead, the second to the ledger under the seeded **Suspense A/c** group, so each voucher balances itself; the synthetic line shrinks by exactly what Suspense gains and the sheet still balances. (2) That resolver is by group, not name, because the ledger is not seeded — and it prefers the ledger *named* after the group, because on these books **`Cash Over` (₹26,149.01, normal balance CREDIT) is filed under `Suspense A/c`, an ASSET group**, while its sibling `Cash Short` sits correctly under Indirect Expenses. Till overage is being reported as an asset instead of income; **not moved — that shifts money between the P&L and the Balance Sheet, so it is the owner's call.** (3) Converting moves `Account.balance`, which `checkCreditLimit` reads, so `pnpm opening:preview` measures it first: on live, **zero accounts would newly exceed a set credit limit**, and the 48 debtors that would newly show a due already cannot buy on credit (deny-by-default). **Nothing has been converted yet.** `verify:bill-links` is now **30 checks, all passing**.

  **Verified** by new `pnpm verify:bill-links` — 19 checks against the live database inside one rolled-back transaction: purchase settles a sale and both sides net; cancel reopens the bill and un-cancel re-applies it; hard-delete reopens it and it then links to a **different** voucher; a self-link nets nothing; the four guards refuse; Auto is oldest-first (400 + 400 + 200) and a tick fills only what was ticked. All 19 pass, and the database is provably untouched afterwards (allocations still 1, vouchers still 406). The existing `parity-harness` cannot run in this checkout at all — `server-only` is neither installed nor declared, a pre-existing gap unrelated to this work — so its allocation case was rewritten to a real pair (it had asserted on a **self-link**, which the symmetric rule must now ignore) but could not be executed. **`pnpm repair:bill-links` reports what live needs and is not yet applied:** 17 vouchers can take an unambiguous party stamp, 0 ambiguous, and 1 orphan link to sweep. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean (`--max-old-space-size=8192`), ESLint clean on every touched file. **Owner to confirm in the UI:** the office pages need a staff sign-in, so the click-through on Receivables / Ledger-Outstandings / a Receipt voucher is yours.

- **2026-09-08 — A replica whose schema is behind reported every row as drifted, and would have crashed the repair.** Live `--deep` on Counter 94 after `sourceCounterId` landed on the office: all 405 `UniversalVoucher` and all 35 `Ticket` rows read as different, because that counter still runs the previous build and its replica has no such column. One schema fact, reported as 440 data faults. Worse, `--repair` would have attempted it: `upsert` builds its column list from the SOURCE row, so it would name a column the replica lacks and fail on every batch. **Fix.** `reconcile.missingColumns` compares the two content fingerprints' column lists (the local fingerprint already returns its own, so this costs no extra query). [`verify-replica.js`](sync-engine/verify-replica.js) now prints the missing column names instead of thousands of ids and skips comparison and repair for that table; pass 3 skips the content repair and logs `schema behind`. The tolerated direction is unchanged — a column the REPLICA kept and the source dropped still must never read as a difference. schemaGuard should halt the worker long before either path; this is the second lock on the same door. Covered by a third section in [`test-reconcile.js`](sync-engine/test-reconcile.js) — 15 checks, all passing. **Live state of Counter 94 after the sweep:** 111 tables exact including contents; `Ticket` and `UniversalVoucher` schema-behind (clears when this counter takes the next build); `LoyaltyLedger` / `LoyaltyLot` each short two rows, which the hourly key reconcile repairs on its own; `UserSurfaceSeen` 7 rows drifted — which the child content-hash fix repairs, but only once this counter runs a build containing it. **The running counter still has the OLD engine: none of these fixes are live until the next release.**
- **2026-09-08 — An admin task moved on one machine never told any other machine.** Owner's report, with both screens side by side: three tasks dragged into Open on the desktop app (Counter 94, `R:Local · W:Office`) stayed in In Progress / On Hold on the web, comment counts included — "change in local not going to web". Not a write problem and not the replica: `/api/admin-tasks` announced the **tasks** topic alone, while every board built on [`useTicketsDelta`](src/shared/hooks/useTicketsDelta.ts) listens on **tickets**, and so do the sidebar badges ([`useNavCounts`](src/shared/hooks/useNavCounts.ts) counts `prisma.ticket`). An admin task IS a Ticket row — same model, admin pipeline — so both surfaces were deaf to the one write that moves them. The till looked live only because a counter also wakes its screens on the sync worker's `sync:applied`, which is not filtered by topic; the web has no such fallback and sat until someone pressed Refresh. [`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts) now adds a second rule, `/^\/api\/admin-tasks/ → ["tickets"]`, unioned with the existing `tasks` rule (every rule is tested and the results merged), so the Work Board and the badges hear it while `RecurringTaskList` keeps reacting to `tasks`. `/api/admin-task-recurrences` deliberately does not match the new rule. No subscriber changed. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean (`--max-old-space-size=8192`), ESLint clean, `check-doc-sync` clean.
- **2026-09-08 — A replica row can differ from the office with the SAME key and the SAME `updatedAt`, and nothing was able to see it.** Owner's report: Settings → Customer Tools showed two Trash tombstones on Counter 94 that the web showed none of. Not the Dexie cache — that page was never a cached path. **Root cause, proved on the live pair.** `Person 8282` was converted to `Company 17` on 2026-07-27 and synced correctly (`convertedAt` still identical on both sides). Later the office deleted `Company 17`, and PostgreSQL's `ON DELETE SET NULL` nulled `Person.convertedToCompanyId` **inside the database** — so Prisma's application-level `@updatedAt` never ran and the timestamp did not move. Here the same delete is applied under `SET LOCAL session_replication_role = replica` ([db.js](sync-engine/lib/db.js)), which suspends FK actions on purpose so rows can land out of dependency order, so we never made the matching change and kept a dangling `17`. Neither healer could notice: the watermark pull is `(updatedAt, pk) > cursor` and `updatedAt` never moved, and the reconcile fingerprint hashes **primary keys only**, which matched on both sides. `last_verify_at` was populated with an empty `last_error` the whole time. The schema has **95** `ON DELETE SET NULL` constraints, all single-column; `ON DELETE CASCADE` (73) needs nothing, since it leaves a KEY difference the existing hash already catches. **Fix 1 — the cause.** `repairReferencing` in [sync-once.js](sync-engine/sync-once.js): after pass 3's `deleteByKeys` removes parent keys, every table referencing that parent through a SET NULL / SET DEFAULT foreign key has its affected rows re-pulled from the source. The source is the authority — we copy its answer instead of re-implementing FK semantics against 95 constraints. Every delete reaches a replica through that one function, so one hook covers the class, and it costs nothing on a cycle with no deletes. **Fix 2 — the false all-clear.** `verify-replica.js` used the same key-only hash, so the tool that answers "is the replica right?" would have called this table clean. New `--deep` hashes `to_jsonb(row)` for every strategy (`reconcile.contentFingerprint` / `rowHashDiff`), `--repair` re-pulls what disagrees, and a plain run now says out loud that it compared keys only. **Not scheduled** — it reads every row on both sides, which is the egress this project exists to avoid. Also fixed a crash: `--keys` called `reconcile.missingLocally`, which does not exist, so it threw on the first differing table; it uses `keyDiff` now and reports both directions. **What the first full `--deep` found** (116 tables, 20,748 rows, 23 s): 113 exact including contents; `LoyaltyLedger` and `LoyaltyLot` each short two rows (a key difference the hourly pass repairs itself); and **`UserSurfaceSeen` seven rows diverged in content** — a `seenAt` two days stale. **Fix 3 — the second cause.** `child` tables now hash row CONTENTS, not keys, on exactly the argument `full` tables already win on: a table with no watermark of its own has nothing but the fingerprint to detect a change. A child row edited in place without touching its parent was invisible to both the pull and the key hash — the mirror of the 2026-09-03 child-INSERT bug. Two consequences: the reconcile's two fingerprints can no longer run in parallel (a content hash has the replica hash only the columns the SOURCE still has, so the source's list must come back first), and pass 3 now also runs `rowHashDiff` and re-pulls what it finds — without that, a content mismatch with matching keys would repair nothing, still differ, and never be verified: an hourly loop making no progress. Cost is a seq scan per child table per verify interval; watch `InvStockLedger`, `VoucherGlLine`, `VoucherInventoryLine`, `TicketOrderLine` as volume grows. **Fix 4, and the one review would never have caught.** `upsert` refuses to overwrite a row whose incoming `updatedAt` is not strictly newer — correct for out-of-order pulls, exactly wrong for a repair, because this entire class of divergence has IDENTICAL `updatedAt` on both sides. The first version of Fix 1 therefore fetched the row, reported success and wrote nothing. `pullByKeys` now forces the write and defaults to it, since every caller is reconciliation, which has already proved the source is the truth; `cacheColumns` stay excluded. **Tested.** New [`sync-engine/test-reconcile.js`](sync-engine/test-reconcile.js) reproduces both faults against a throwaway pair of databases and asserts a key hash calls each clean while the new machinery finds and repairs it — 12 checks, all passing. It is what caught Fix 4. `sync-once.js` now guards `main()` with `require.main === module` so the file can be required without starting a cycle. **Old note kept for the record:** **That last one is a SECOND cause of the same blind spot, and Fix 1 does not cover it:** a `child` table has no watermark of its own (it is pulled when its parent's `updatedAt` moves), so a child row EDITED IN PLACE without touching its parent is invisible to both the pull and the key hash — the mirror of the 2026-09-03 child-INSERT bug. That decision was taken — see Fix 3. `Person 8282` repaired via `--deep --repair`; Person now matches exactly. Gates: `node --check` on all three files (ESLint ignores `sync-engine/`), plus the live before/after run above. **Also noticed, not chased:** `InvProductCode` is in the sync registry but does not exist on the replica, and `_sync_status` reports `1 stale local column(s): CustomerSocialProfile.avatarUrl`.
- **2026-09-08 — Quick Actions from an open customer profile: party-facing vouchers open with that party in place.** [`activePartyStore`](src/store/activePartyStore.ts) holds the profile open right now: the Persons and Companies pages set it when a detail loads (kind / id / name / phone / accountId — the `PickedParty` shape) and clear it on close / unmount; everywhere else it is null and vouchers open blank. `ButtonBar` reads it at click time: **Receipt / Payment** open in the dialog with the party in the first particulars row and the cursor in its amount box (`GlVoucherPage` gained `initialParty`; Received In keeps the last-used cash/bank ledger); **Credit / Debit Note** open with the party locked in the top picker; **Sale / Purchase / returns / Estimate / orders** still navigate to their full page, with the party handed over through a one-mount sessionStorage note (`stashPreloadParty` / `consumePreloadParty`, the ⋮ → Duplicate pattern) that `TradingVoucherPage` consumes into Credit mode + `setParty`. Nothing personal goes into a URL. The party is captured at click time, so closing the profile mid-entry does not blank the form. Right-click "Open in new window" and the "All Create Actions" grid's window.open for trading vouchers cannot carry the note (new window, no shared sessionStorage) — expected. Contra / Journal are untouched. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean, ESLint clean. _Not browser-verified (pane sits at the staff login; no credentials entered by the agent). Owner to try: open a person → Receipt +, expect the party in row 1; → Sale +, expect Credit mode with the party locked._
- **2026-09-08 — Quick Actions: the six GL vouchers open OVER the office; right-click still gives a new window.** A left-click on Payment / Receipt / Contra / Credit Note / Debit Note / Journal in the Quick Actions bar (and in the "All Create Actions" grid) used to navigate the tab away to `/vouchers/…/new`. Now [`GlVoucherDialog`](src/shared/components/GlVoucherDialog.tsx) hosts the SAME form component in a 94vh × 96vw dialog — `GL_DIALOG_KIND_BY_ACTION` maps the action key to a kind; anything not listed (Sale, Purchase, GRN, POS, inventory) keeps navigating. `GlVoucherPage` and `JournalVoucherPage` take an optional `onClose`: when set, Back / Cancel / Esc / after-save call it instead of `router.back()` / `window.close()`. The dialog disables Radix's own Esc and outside-click close and shows no X, so the form's "discard unsaved changes?" prompt is the only way out of a dirty form; the form's Esc handler now ignores the dialog it lives in (checks for an open dialog that does NOT contain the form's root) rather than any open dialog. Modifier clicks (Ctrl/Shift/middle) on the bar keep the browser's own new-tab behaviour; the right-click "Open in new window" is untouched. The Journal rows also wait for one character before listing (`minChars={1}`), like Payment / Receipt / CN / DN; Contra rows list only cash & bank ledgers and keep opening on focus. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean, ESLint clean. _Not browser-verified: the Browser pane refused localhost navigation this session; the office and voucher routes respond and the dev log is error-free. Owner to try one click on Receipt in the bar._
- **2026-09-08 — Voucher ledger dropdown: the freeze on focus was the render, not the DB.** Clicking the Receipt / Payment particulars box blocked the main thread for **1.8 s** (measured with a `longtask` observer) because [`GlAccountCombobox`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GlAccountCombobox.tsx) drew all **3,646** ledgers (21,739 DOM nodes) the moment the input focused; the account list itself was fetched once on mount, not on focus. Fixes: (1) the list is capped at **50 rows** with a "type more to narrow down" footer, filtered under `useMemo`, and the query is **debounced 150 ms**; (2) a `minChars` prop — particulars rows on Payment / Receipt pass `1` so nothing opens on a bare focus, the top Cash/Bank picker keeps `0`; (3) the dropdown is gated on the input actually having focus. (4) The `includeParties=true` account list now lives once per tab in [`useGlAccounts`](src/shared/hooks/useGlAccounts.ts) — an in-memory Zustand store with in-flight dedupe, re-read on the realtime **ledger** topic so balances stay honest — used by `GlVoucherPage` and `JournalVoucherPage`; **deliberately no Dexie tier** (owner: no major web changes), so the web still refreshes on every voucher open but paints from memory at once. On a **replica-served counter** [`GlAccountsWarmup`](src/shared/components/GlAccountsWarmup.tsx) in the root layout loads the list at app start (`servedByReplica()` is now exported from `localDb.ts` for it), so the first voucher of the day opens ready. After: focus → no long task, no dropdown; "a" → 50 rows in ~60 ms. Other `gl-accounts` consumers (chart of accounts, opening balance, trading vouchers, settings) untouched.
- **2026-09-07 — The Dexie browser cache is retired on a counter served by its own replica.** Every module in [`localDb.ts`](src/shared/lib/browser/localDb.ts) — products, masters, parties, ticket boards, Day Book — is a DELTA cache: it keeps rows and next asks only for what changed since a remembered `computedAt`. Since the same day's `parseSince` change, a replica-served counter **ignores that stamp and answers in full anyway** (the replica is on the same PC, so a full read costs nothing). So on that machine the whole tier saved nothing and only wrote the stamps that caused the staleness bug in the entry below. It is now off there: `localDb()` returns `null`, and all twenty call sites across the five consumers already treat `null` as "no cache" and fetch in full — **no consumer file changed**. **The gate.** `useCounterMode` writes the exact mirror of `shouldReadLocal()` — `isCounter && (mode === "LOCAL" || (replicaReady && preference !== "live"))` — to `localStorage` on every status answer; `localDb()` reads it once and **freezes it for the life of the document**. Frozen because callers memoise on top of it (`partyCatalog` caches an is-open boolean and an in-memory row map; one `syncProducts()` calls `localDb()` three times), so a value that changed mid-page would hand out a real database early and `null` later. An answer that arrives while a page is open therefore applies to the NEXT load. **On the change into replica-served** the tables are `clear()`ed — not `close()`d and not `Dexie.delete`d: callers hold the handle across an await and use it without a try/catch, so closing under them throws `DatabaseClosedError` mid-sync, and deleting an open database waits on every tab and window. At the top of a later load, where nothing holds a handle, the database is deleted outright. Clearing also makes the `READ_MODE=live` revert safe: rows written against the replica's clock can never come back to life. **Accepted:** on a counter's first-ever launch the cache runs for the first ~10 s until the poll answers (exactly today's behaviour), and if the replica later goes not-ready the cache stays off until the next load — the right direction, since reviving replica-era stamps is the bug itself. **Rejected:** a server-injected `<html data-*>` attribute (`src/app/layout.tsx` has no `dynamic` export; it would cost static rendering on the public store pages), `window.electronAPI` (Electron-ness is not replica-served — a till on `READ_MODE=live` reads Supabase and wants the cache), `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` (one build ships to Vercel and the counters, and it cannot see `replicaReady`), a cookie (same timing, and invites an SSR branch), and middleware (does not exist here; edge runtime cannot import `counterMode.ts`). Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean, ESLint clean on both touched files. **Owner to confirm on a counter:** after two launches, `localStorage["abeer.served-by-replica"]` is `"1"`, `indexedDB.databases()` shows no `abeer-replica`, and POS search / party picker / a tickets board / the Day Book all still load.
- **2026-09-07 — On a replica-reading counter, "changed since" is ignored and screens wait for the replica to say "collect now".** Owner's report: a help ticket moved on the desktop app (Counter 94, `R:Local · W:Live`) showed on the web at once but never on the desktop itself — not after leaving and reopening the page — while the Refresh button fixed it and web changes appeared on both. Cause: every delta cache remembers the server's `computedAt` and next asks for `updatedAt > since`. On a counter that query runs on the replica, where rows land seconds after Supabase stamped them. The board re-read straight after its own PATCH, got the old row from the replica, stamped its Dexie view with the replica's clock — later than the row's real `updatedAt` — and every delta after that (the worker's `sync:applied`, page reopen) asked for changes after a stamp the row could never pass. **Owner's rule:** on the web the screen owns the Supabase message; in local mode the sync engine owns it, and the screen may act only on the replica's own message. **Three changes.** (1) Server: one gate, [`parseSince`](src/shared/lib/prisma/counterMode.ts) — `null` whenever `shouldReadLocal()`, so all eleven `since` routes (tickets / deals / help / admin-tasks, day-book incl. `refsSince`, nav counts, parties/changed, masters sync, products lookup, and `isUnchangedSince` behind cash-position / usable-fund) answer in full on a replica-served read; a full read of local Postgres costs nothing, and an early read can no longer hide a row. `useTicketsDelta` and `useDayBook` now accept a page answer to a `since` request (it was silently dropped before). (2) Own save: [`useTicketsDelta.afterOwnWrite`](src/shared/hooks/useTicketsDelta.ts) — on a till reading its replica with plain live writes it reads nothing until `sync:applied` (6 s fallback for a worker whose socket is down); everywhere else (web, `v_live` where the response is held until the replica has the rows — `setWritesHeldUntilApplied` in `appChannel.ts`, fed by `useCounterMode`) it is the old forced reload. Help, Deals and Administration boards use it for their move/save/dialog paths; Refresh buttons keep `reload`. (3) Nav badges listened to the raw Supabase signal directly and recounted on the replica before the rows landed; `useRealtimeTopics`'s effect body is now the exported `subscribeRealtimeTopics` primitive and `useNavCounts` goes through it. **Deferred (owner):** the call follow-up popup on the desktop — the popup filters by this PC's counter extension, and `Call` syncs insert-only (no `updatedAt`), so a call handled elsewhere never updates on a counter. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` clean (`--max-old-space-size=8192`), ESLint clean on every touched file. **Owner to confirm on Counter 94:** drag a help ticket on the desktop — no snap-back, count updates within seconds; reopen the page — still right; web change — desktop follows. **Follow-up 2026-09-08 — the blink.** Owner reported the card now stays, then blinks back to the old column and returns. One read is not `afterOwnWrite`'s to hold: a sync cycle ALREADY RUNNING when the save landed reports `sync:applied` for other tables, and the re-read that fires answers from a replica that still lacks this row. So the save's own answer is now PINNED — every board's PATCH already returns the flattened row — and any copy read back with an older `updatedAt` is not believed, in the Dexie paint, the delta merge, a full page and `loadMore` alike. The pin clears itself the moment a read answers with that stamp or newer, and by time (60 s), so a row deleted or filtered away elsewhere can never stick to a board. `afterOwnWrite(saved?)` narrows at runtime, because `HelpQuickForm`/`DealFormDialog` call their `onSaved` with an id rather than a row.
- **2026-09-07 — `v_live` relays EVERY write, and the title bar says so.** Counter 94 (a full build in counter mode, `R:Local · W:v_live`) failed to hard-delete a converted-customer tombstone from Customer Tools Trash with the generic "Failed to hard-delete tombstone"; the app log had Prisma `P2025` on `prisma.person.delete()`. Cause: [`relayPolicy.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/relayPolicy.ts) forwarded only `COUNTER_API_ALLOWLIST` segments ("the relay boundary is the build boundary"), so `customer-tools` ran on the till itself — the lookup and reference counts read the replica (row present), the delete went to Supabase directly (row long gone: the pull has no delete feed, so an office hard-delete never removes the replica copy). **Owner rule:** in `v_live` a till never writes Supabase itself while the office is reachable; the local replica is written only when live is confirmed down (offline mode). The allowlist check is gone — every non-GET `/api/*` relays, minus the never-relay list and the 4 MB cap; the office authenticates each call from the till\x27s cookie and applies its own permissions, so nothing opens that the office did not already gate. `check-counter-mode` now asserts a `customer-tools` DELETE and an `accountancy` POST relay. The shell word for `v_live` writes is **Office** ([`statusLabels.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/statusLabels.ts)) — it used to print "Live", which read as "not v_live"; the badge tooltip says saves go to the head office. `.env` / `.env.local` comments list `v_live | live | local`. **Closed 2026-09-28:** the Trash itself is gone — a convert or merge deletes the old row outright, and the delete reaches the counter through `noteDeleted` like every other office delete.
$1: a till relays its writes to the office in one round trip, and holds the response until its own replica has the rows.** Owner measured that saving a voucher on the Vercel office is much faster than on a counter, and the code says why: `/api/pos/sale` makes ~35 sequential Prisma calls before and inside its transaction plus two per cart line ([`stockMovement.ts`](src/modules/inventory/lib/stockMovement.ts)); across the WAN that is `RTT × (35 + 2 × lines)`, beside Supabase it is milliseconds. New [`writeRelay.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/writeRelay.ts) (the mechanics) and [`relayPolicy.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/relayPolicy.ts) (the pure boundary rules, so `check-counter-mode` can assert them without a Next build), called from [`proxy.ts`](src/proxy.ts) — whose matcher now includes `/api`; that branch is a plain `NextResponse.next()` on every build but a relaying counter, never resolves a session, and loads the relay lazily so `pg` stays out of every other build's proxy. **Honest cost of that widening:** on Vercel every API call now passes through the proxy function first (one extra invocation and hop, no DB read), and a self-hosted `next start` tees every non-GET API body for the proxy; a static matcher cannot be made conditional per build, and the Vercel numbers should be watched after the deploy — forwards every non-GET `/api/*` call to `WRITE_API_URL` from the counter's OWN server. The browser still posts to 127.0.0.1, so no CORS, no cookie problem, no UI change. Below the relay `v_live` IS `live`: `shouldWriteLocal()` is unchanged, `localWriteIsFallback()` became `!== "local"` so the offline policies still apply, and the outbox, flusher, id ranges and every write handler are untouched. Trial on one till; counters keep shipping as they are.
