  - **The duplicate-bill rule.** The local fallback runs only when the request provably never reached the office — DNS, refused, TLS, a 2 s connect timeout — decided on Node's `http(s).request` `secureConnect` event, with one fresh connection per relay so a stale keep-alive socket can never muddy it. Anything after that point (30 s response timeout, a reset mid-flight) answers `502 relay_uncertain` with "may already be saved — check before entering it again", because a second local attempt would mint a second bill under a new number that `check:voucher-duplicates` cannot see. An HTTP error from the office is the app's own answer and passes straight through — a `403` means the office refused, so it is never run locally. A 15 s breaker stops an office outage costing every bill the connect timeout.
  - **Same cookie, same secret, same NAME.** Auth.js keys the session token on secret AND cookie name (`salt = cookieName` in `@auth/core/jwt`), so the base plan's "rename `authjs.session-token` → `__Secure-…`" could never have decrypted at the office. [`auth.ts`](src/auth.ts) now pins `authjs.session-token` everywhere, `secure` by protocol, and the relay forwards the Cookie header verbatim, chunks included. Cost: everyone signs in once more after this deploy, office included. `counter.env` now ships `WRITE_API_URL` and `AUTH_SECRET` — [`counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts)'s header is rewritten, the per-machine secret stays the behaviour when `.env` carries none, and `ensureAuthSecret` treats a placeholder as absent; `generate-counter-env.ts` refuses a `v_live` build without both or with a placeholder/short secret; `verify-package` lists `WRITE_API_URL` as public (the origin is already compiled in as `COMPANY_DETAILS.sites.office`).
  - **Read-your-write (owner's gap).** A relayed row lands in Supabase while screens re-read the replica — under `live` today they see it only after Realtime wakes the worker and a full 113-table cycle. So on an office 2xx the relay `NOTIFY`s the replica (`sync_pull_now`, `{ id, models }`); the worker ([`index.js`](sync-engine/index.js) + new [`localSignal.js`](sync-engine/lib/localSignal.js)) runs `sync-once.js --only A,B,C --reason relay` at once (list form of `--only`, schema check trusted, reconcile pass skipped), answers on `sync_relay_done`, and the relay releases the response — bounded at 3 s, timing out to today's behaviour. Models come from `topicsForApiPath` plus a new `TOPIC_MODELS` map in [`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts); unlike the worker's deleted map it can only degrade softly (a missing model is pulled by the Realtime cycle a moment later), so it lists only what a screen shows right after its own save.
  - **~~One common series for anything saved at the office (owner, 2026-09-07).~~ Replaced 2026-09-25 — counter-wise on every mode, see the top entry.** The POS sale, order, shift-receipt (Rcv) and shift-expenditure (Exp) routes no longer pass the shift's counter into `invNextRef` / `postReceiptVoucher` / `engineCreateVoucher`; `resolveCounterNumber()` numbers a counter PC's own write under its counter and an office write — a relayed sale included — as counter 1. Side effect accepted: a sale saved from an office browser numbers as counter 1 too. A till still numbers as counter N while offline, so one machine runs two series until `local` is dropped — where this is heading if `v_live` wins the trial. Two office-side races that the shared prefix newly exposes: POS orders/estimates still number by `count(+1)` on the legacy path ([`stock.ts`](src/modules/inventory/lib/stock.ts)), now serialised per prefix with a transaction-scoped advisory lock; the financial-year rollover of counter 1's series row is **not** atomic under two concurrent relayed saves on 1 April (pre-existing, once-a-year race — left open, noted here).
  - **Adversarial audit before commit (12 coverage agents, 3 review lenses) — fixed:** the two shift routes above, which the plan's "three G11 sites" had missed; `AUTH_SECRET` was being baked into **every** desktop build whenever `.env` held one — `generate-counter-env.ts` now withholds it unless `WRITE_MODE=v_live`, and requires an https `WRITE_API_URL` (http only for localhost); the never-relay list could be bypassed with a percent-encoded path (`/api/settings/%72estore`) that the office would decode — `decideRelay` now matches the decoded, dot-normalised path (`canonicalPath`, refusing anything that will not decode or is double-encoded); an office `401` on a relayed write bounced the cashier to the login page in a loop — a **bare** 401 (no `code`: the office could not decrypt the cookie) now means "relay misconfigured": logged, relay paused 60 s, request runs here (nothing was written, `requireAuth` precedes all work), while a `SESSION_REVOKED` 401 still passes through so the cashier is signed out as designed; a gateway `502`–`504` with no JSON body (Vercel cutting off a function that may already have committed) is answered with the same "may already be saved" sentence as a lost response on our own leg, as the base plan required, instead of a raw HTML page no toast can read; Vercel's `strict-transport-security` was relayed to the till's http origin — stripped, with `alt-svc`; widening the proxy matcher to `/api` makes Next tee every API body with a **silent** 10 MB cap — `experimental.proxyClientMaxBodySize: "50mb"` in [`next.config.ts`](next.config.ts). **Worker:** after every relayed write the browser's own change signal starts a full safety-net cycle, so the next targeted pull would routinely sit behind it and the 3 s hold expire — a targeted pull now steps a running safety-net cycle aside (killed; per-table transactions, nothing half-written) and the interrupted cycle resumes afterwards protected from a second pre-emption, so a busy till cannot starve it; `--only` now brings a model's whole subtree (TicketOrderLine under TicketOrder under Ticket), `sync_relay_done` goes out in slices of 100 ids (pg_notify's 8000-byte cap), and a worker halted on schema drift answers targeted pulls at once instead of spawning a doomed child per bill. **Pre-existing gap that `v_live` made visible:** `PosReceipt`, `PosExpenditure` and `PosCashDrop` are `child` tables pulled only when their `PosShift` row moved, and nothing that added them touched the shift — so a receipt reached another machine (or, relayed, the till's own replica) only on the hourly reconcile. New [`touchPosShift`](src/modules/pos/lib/touchShift.ts) bumps the shift in the same transaction from the sale (surplus receipt), Rcv, Exp and cash-drop routes. **Same day, from the owner's dev log:** the outbox flusher started on `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL` alone and knocked on a non-existent local PostgreSQL every 5 s (with an empty error line, because Node's `AggregateError` for "every address refused" has no message). [`instrumentation.ts`](src/instrumentation.ts) now starts it only when `COUNTER_PC=true` is set as well — electron/main.ts sets both together, only after the replica provisioned — so a leftover URL in a developer's `.env`, or one copied into a hosted deployment, starts nothing; and both listeners report connection failures through [`connectionErrorText`](src/shared/lib/sync/connectionError.ts), which flattens the aggregate into `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:5432`.
  - **Never relayed:** reads; `/api/auth`, `/api/counter`, `/api/health` (Electron's boot probe), `/api/sync`, `/api/pos/counters/*`, backup/restore; bodies over 4 MB or of unknown size (Vercel's 4.5 MB cap, decided before sending); a released or unset-up PC (the local gate refuses as today); a request already carrying `x-abeer-relay`. `v_live` without `WRITE_API_URL` degrades to `local`, never `live`. Two new `check:counter-mode` variants assert all of it. Every relayed request carries `x-abeer-counter-machine`, `x-abeer-counter-number` and `x-abeer-app-version` so an office-side error can be traced to a till; the office ignores them today.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, `electron:compile`, eslint on every touched file, `pnpm precommit` (incl. the 5 `check:counter-mode` variants), `node --check` on the worker, `check-doc-sync`, plus the adversarial audit above. **Not run here — needs the trial till and the owner:** measure `[pos-timing]` under `local` vs `v_live` (and confirm first that the till is not already on `local` — if it is slow there, the WAN is not the cause); hosts-file block → local save, outbox, Offline badge; a host that accepts then hangs → refused, nothing written locally; release from the office → refused locally; open the bill and the POS stock grid right after save; a counter-2 till relaying → counter-1 series. **Before the pilot:** release the office first (a relayed write runs the office's code against the office's schema — "office ahead of till" is the only supported direction); the build machine's `.env` must carry the office's `AUTH_SECRET`; check the trial till for a stray `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/.env` that would keep its old per-machine secret and 401 every relayed write. **Follow-up before a second till:** an idempotency key the office checks, so the uncertain case can be retried instead of refused.
- **2026-09-06 — Every print matches its template, and there are exactly two print ways.** Owner brought a Counter 94 POS slip with no "Network Service" line while the thermal preview showed it. Root cause: `/api/pos/sale` shipped the raw template to the bridge, so the **A4** "Header text" switch governed the 80mm paper. Owner then asked for all print problems at once, and set the rules: (1) the thermal printer prints exactly the print template, silently or via the format-selection page; (2) editor thermal preview = receipt-page thermal view = paper; (3) never cross-format — A4 on the roll / thermal on an A4 sheet; (4) no printer selected in Printer Settings → fall back to the format-selection page; (5–6) on that page the user picks format **and printer** in the browser dialog, never a silent print; (7) 3-dot menus keep "Print (thermal)" first, "Print / PDF" second; (8) forms with Print / Thermal ticks decide per save, so "Default paper" is meaningless for them. And: **do not force-DRY across formats** — A4 HTML, thermal HTML, ESC/POS and the labels each keep their own renderer; only template data is shared.
  - **Silent prints are thermal-resolved now.** New `getThermalTemplate(kind)` in [`voucherTemplate.ts`](src/shared/lib/print/voucherTemplate.ts) (org merge + `resolveTemplateForFormat(…, "THERMAL_80MM")`); the sale response carries it, plus `receiptPrint` for the overpayment RV-… slip built by the new [`paymentSlip.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/paymentSlip.ts) — the same builder `/receipt/payment/[id]` renders from, so the counter's silent Rcv slip and the page are one document (it used to send no template at all).
  - **Bridge = preview, line for line** ([`thermal.js`](hardware-bridge/thermal.js)): `printHeader` / `printFooter` / `printMoneyMiddle` / `printReceiptsBlock` mirror `ThermalHeader` / `ThermalFooter` / `MoneyThermalMiddle` / the new receipts block of `DefaultThermalMiddle`. Doc title (`headerLine2`) now sits **below** the divider; brand comes from `headerLine1` only (the Printer-Settings `companyName` and every `COMPANY_DETAILS.brandName` fallback are gone from printing — `mergeTemplateWithOrg` always fills it); a blank footer prints nothing (no "Thank you · Visit again" fallback); signature and party balance obey their toggles; dates print as supplied; `Total` not `TOTAL`; `Customer:` / `Supplier:` row like the preview; **tender rows (Cash / UPI / Card) + balance due** print after the totals — owner chose to keep them, so they were **added to the thermal preview and the reprint** (`sample.receipts` from `settlementJson` tenders, party ledger excluded) rather than dropped from paper. No narration on invoice slips (the preview never printed it). RECEIPT / PAYMENT go through **`print/voucher`**; `print/payment`, the template-blind `print` command and `buildReceiptBuffer` / `buildPaymentBuffer` are **deleted** — the bridge has two document commands, `print/pos` and `print/voucher`. The printer test prints the Journal sample through the real path with the shop's own JOURNAL template. `fontFamily` / `fontSizePx` are browser-only (ESC/POS uses the head's font) — said so under the fields. Money slips default **party balance on** (`PARTY_BALANCE_KINDS`; the bootstrap seed flips rows still on the old both-off default, idempotent). **New offline parity tests: `pnpm test:bridge`** (`hardware-bridge/thermal.test.js`, `node --test`, builders run with logo/bank/MRP off and assert on the buffer text — 11 pass).
  - **Two print ways** ([`PrintToolbar.tsx`](src/shared/components/print/PrintToolbar.tsx)): `?mode=thermal` (a form's Thermal tick, 3-dot "Print (thermal)", every POS counter flow — `receiptHref`, `voucherPrintPageHref`, PosReceipt/Expenditure dialogs, sales register, till ledger) tries the bridge **once**; `config:get` first — blank LAN ip / COM port / Windows printer for the chosen type means no silent print at all — and any failure leaves the page open as the **format-selection page with nothing auto-printed**. That page prints only through the browser dialog. **Electron `printSilent` (`printer:print-silent`) is removed** — it went to the Windows default printer with no size, which is how an 80mm slip could land on an A4 sheet. PosScreen's bridge-down fallback opens the selection page on the 80mm view with `autoprint=0`. `resolvePaperFormat(template, paper, mode)`: `?paper=` → `?mode=thermal` → stored default **only for the repair family** (no ticks on their forms) → A4. The editor hides "Default paper" for every other kind and previews them on A4.
  - **Labels and cards join the template properly.** `labelSender()` / `labelFooter()` in [`mergeTemplateWithOrg.ts`](src/shared/lib/print/mergeTemplateWithOrg.ts) replace three hand-copied derivations; the 15 per-format field flags are passed as the template itself (`LabelFieldFlags` is structural); the parcel label's inert Show/hide rows are gone. **Two new kinds, `COUPON` and `GIFT_CARD`** (group `marketing`, 32 kinds total, `kind` is a free `String @id` → **no migration**; the bootstrap upsert and the list GET create the rows): [`CouponCard.tsx`](src/shared/components/print/CouponCard.tsx) and [`GiftCardCard.tsx`](src/shared/components/print/GiftCardCard.tsx) keep each card's own face and read logo / brand / small label / terms / foot (brand · phone · footer line) from the template; `/receipt/coupon/[id]` and `/receipt/gift-card/[id]` were hardcoded before. Editor: "Marketing" group, card preview standalone, irrelevant toggles hidden; gallery shows the single face.
  - **3-dot "Print (thermal)" prints like a POS sale — no tab.** Owner (2026-09-07): the menu was still opening the receipt page. New **`GET /api/print/thermal/[source]/[id]`** returns the bridge payload (`{ cmd: "print/voucher", payload }`) for any Day-Book voucher — money slips via `buildPaymentSlipPrint`, everything else via `buildVoucherReceiptSample` + `getThermalTemplate`; [`silentThermalPrint.ts`](src/shared/lib/print/silentThermalPrint.ts) checks the bridge and `thermalPrinterConfigured()` (moved into [`hardwareBridge.ts`](src/shared/lib/bridge/hardwareBridge.ts)), fetches, sends, and returns false on any failure, in which case `DayBookActionsMenu` opens the format-selection page on the 80mm view instead. Same day: preview brand at 2× like the paper, bare amounts (no "Rs.") on the thermal preview, invoice number printed as a line under the barcode (the RETSOL head ignores HRI text), item names wrap on paper instead of being cut.
  - **Voucher forms' "Print after save" with Thermal ticked prints silently too** (owner, 2026-09-07 — the Sale Invoice form was still opening the page). All six forms (`GlVoucherPage`, `JournalVoucherPage`, `TradingVoucherPage`, `BranchTransferPage`, `ManufacturingJournalPage`, `PhysicalStockPage`) call `printVoucherAfterSave(source, id, { thermal })` in [`silentThermalPrint.ts`](src/shared/lib/print/silentThermalPrint.ts): Thermal ON → bridge, no tab; cannot print silently → the selection page on the 80mm view; Thermal OFF → the selection page. `voucherPrintPageHref` now has one caller (that helper).
  - **Every remaining thermal reprint is silent too** (owner asked for an audit, 2026-09-07): `printVoucherThermal(source, id, kind)` in `silentThermalPrint.ts` (silent → toast; else the selection page on 80mm) now backs the Day Book 3-dot menu, the POS Rcv and Exp dialogs, the POS sales register's sale / return / cash-movement reprint buttons, and the till-ledger rows (the API returns `print: { source, id, kind }` instead of a `printUrl`). **Still page-based on purpose:** the repair ticket (`/receipt/[id]`, opened from the ticket page and TicketReopenButton) and the shipping slip — they carry bespoke bodies the print API does not build; their stored default paper decides and the page fires the bridge once.
  - **Left alone on purpose:** `showQrCode` (dormant column, removal = a live migration for nothing); `cfg.companyName` stays in the bridge config for the QR welcome screen and the device label.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (needs `--max-old-space-size=8192`), `eslint` clean on every touched file, `pnpm test:bridge` 11/11, `check-doc-sync` clean. **Not run here:** the local demo Postgres was down, so the party-balance baseline and the two new template rows apply on the next `pnpm prisma db seed`, and `pnpm seed:personal:capture` is still to do; the receipt / settings pages are auth-gated so the browser click-through is unrun. **Needs a counter build** (`hardware-bridge/` and `electron/` changed) — then the owner should check: a POS sale with thermal on (sale slip + Rcv slip), a reprint from the invoice page, `/receipt/payment/[id]`, the printer test, and a coupon / gift card print.
- **2026-09-04 — The desktop app can back up and restore Supabase by itself, with no GitHub runner and no Dropbox.** Owner asked for local backup/restore now that the app is a desktop app. Until now neither operation happened on the machine: [`/api/settings/backup`](src/app/api/settings/backup/route.ts) only dispatches a GitHub Actions run, which dumps and uploads to Dropbox, and restore dispatches a second one. Since Phase 6 stopped shipping the 4 `DROPBOX_*` / 3 `GITHUB_*` keys to counters, `backupAvailability()` answered `canBackup: false` on every shop PC — **a desktop shop had no way to take a backup at all**.
  - **The tools were already on the machine.** `resources/pgsql/bin` ships pg_dump/pg_restore **17.6** for the local replica, and Supabase reports `server_version = 17.6` — so a dump taken on a counter is what the runner's `postgresql-client-17` produces. New [`electron/backupManager.ts`](electron/backupManager.ts) runs them, reusing `getEmbeddedBinPath()` (now exported from [`postgresManager.ts`](electron/postgresManager.ts)) rather than re-deriving the path.
  - **Flags copied from the workflows on purpose**, so a local dump and a Dropbox dump stay interchangeable: `--format=custom --no-owner --no-privileges --schema=public` for the dump, `--clean --if-exists --no-owner --no-privileges --schema=public --jobs=4` for the restore. `--schema=public` is the load-bearing one — Supabase's own schemas are superuser-owned and produce hundreds of fatal errors on restore as the `postgres` role.
  - **Supabase both ways, never the replica.** A replica dump would look like a real backup and restore a partial database. `DIRECT_URL` first and `DATABASE_URL` only as a stripped fallback: `pgbouncer=true` is a Prisma-only parameter, libpq rejects any URI parameter it does not know, and `pg_dump` then exits on "invalid URI query parameter" leaving a **0-byte file**. A dump that comes out empty is deleted rather than left on the Desktop for someone to restore from six months later.
  - **The password never reaches argv.** The URL is split into `PGHOST/PGPORT/PGUSER/PGPASSWORD/PGDATABASE` env vars — parsed by hand, splitting at the last `@`, because Supabase passwords routinely contain characters `new URL()` mangles (the same workaround [`dbUrl.ts`](src/shared/lib/backup/dbUrl.ts) documents). `--dbname` on the command line stays only as the fallback when the split fails.
  - **UI:** new [`LocalBackupCard.tsx`](src/modules/settings/components/LocalBackupCard.tsx) at the top of the existing Backup & Restore page, rendered only when `isElectronEnvironment()`. Native save/open dialogs, live pg_dump progress, the target project ref named on the destructive card, `RESTORE` typed plus a second confirm. The Dropbox cards and the web build are untouched — the amber banner now only claims **Dropbox** is unavailable, which stopped being the whole truth the moment this landed.
  - **Not permission-gated separately:** the only page that calls the IPC is gated on `settings:manage`, and both calls need a human to complete a file dialog on that PC. **One limitation, said out loud on the card:** the workflow follows a restore with `prisma db push`; there is no Prisma CLI in the packaged app, so a dump older than the running version should be restored from the office instead. `pg_dump.exe`/`pg_restore.exe` were added to `REQUIRED_BINARIES` in [`fetch-pg.ts`](scripts/fetch-pg.ts) so a future trim cannot silently remove them.

- **2026-09-05 — A released counter now reacts to the release itself, and admin Release finally reaches the cloud.** Owner's report: after an admin releases a counter — from the Vercel office or from the desktop app — the target counter keeps working; it only comes to its senses when some *other* counter's activity arrives, or when it tries to write something, or on the ten-minute safety cycle. Owner's own reading was that the counter-binding table has no realtime reaction of its own. That is right in effect, and the code says why. **(1) Nothing in the app was subscribed to the signal.** `/api/pos/counters/*` has announced the `settings` topic since 2026-09-03, so the message goes out correctly — but on a counter the only listener is the sync worker (`sync-engine/lib/signal.js`), which ignores topics and syncs everything. [`CounterReconciler`](src/shared/components/CounterReconciler.tsx) listened *only* to the worker's `sync:applied` reporting `PosCounter`, which put the most urgent question in the app ("do I still hold this counter?") at the end of the slowest path: wake the worker, scan 113 tables, commit, report. A worker that is stopped, wedged or whose socket is down never reports at all. It now has a third trigger — `onChangeSignal` filtered to `settings`, settled 600 ms — and *that* trigger asks the DB itself (`reconcileCounterSetup(true)` → `my-binding?authoritative=1`), because the signal arrives **ahead of** the worker's pull and the replica would still answer "bound". One row read per real event, no poll; offline the authoritative hop is skipped by `setupGate` itself and the replica answers. Mount and `sync:applied` stay on the replica, and `sync:applied` stays as the offline safety net. Electron only. **(2) Admin Release was writing to the wrong database.** [`PATCH /api/pos/counters/[id]`](src/app/api/pos/counters/[id]/route.ts) applied `releaseLock` inside its ordinary `prisma.$transaction` — which on a counter PC is the replica plus `_outbox`. On push, `reconcileCounterBinding` ([`push.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts)) sees the server still naming a device and **restores the binding**, so a release made from the desktop app never reached Supabase and came back on the next pull. The release now runs on `prismaAuthoritative` before the transaction, exactly as `release-mine` and `claim-number` already did and for the reason they already documented; the response echoes the DB's answer, not the replica's. **(3) The three binding routes announce themselves.** They write authoritatively, so they enter no outbox — and a counter's browser publishes nothing under `WRITE_MODE=local` — which meant bind and self-release were silent everywhere. `PATCH` (on release), `release-mine` and `claim-number` now call `publishChangeServer(["settings"])`, the same server-side publisher `/api/calls` uses. **(4) Sudden logout with no reason, half of it.** A revoked token (a sign-out elsewhere on the same login, or `revokeRoleSessions` after any role/position/permission edit) produced a bare `401 Unauthorized`, indistinguishable from "never signed in", and [`SessionWrapper`](src/shared/components/SessionWrapper.tsx) jumped straight to the login page mid-work. `auth.ts` now marks the session `revoked`, `requireAuth` answers `401 SESSION_REVOKED`, and the browser shows "Your access was updated by an admin — please sign in again" for 2.5 s before redirecting. The sign-out itself is unchanged; only the silence is gone. **(5) `readCounterSetup` no longer reads a locked file as "no counter".** It returned `null` on *any* failure, and the staged `isThisPcWithoutCounter()` gate (which stops every API write and every protected page) read that as a positive "this PC has no counter" — one busy read would have thrown a working till into the setup wizard. Absent and malformed stay a positive answer; an I/O failure is now silence, per this file's own "never refuse on silence" rule. No schema, seed or permission change. **Owner still to confirm on real counters:** a web release with the worker deliberately stopped (must sign out in seconds), a desktop-app release staying released across a push, and whether `cycle start (change signal)` appears in the counter's sync log right after a web release — the one part of the original report the code alone could not explain.
- **2026-09-04 — Two `User.role` leftovers cleaned out of the GL: who a voucher is credited to, and what the counter part of its number means.** Same root cause as the inbox fix (57e126ba): authority flows from **Position appointments** (`userPositions[].position.roleTemplates[].roleId`), not the legacy `User.role` override, which is `null` for every normally-created user. (1) `resolveSystemActorId` in [`onlineReceipt.ts`](src/shared/lib/payments/onlineReceipt.ts) picked the admin an auto-posted online RECEIPT is attributed to by matching `User.role = "R1"`, then fell back to `findFirst({ orderBy: { id: "asc" } })` — literally any user. On an org whose admin holds R1 by appointment that fallback always won, so the voucher's `createdById` was whoever was created first. Now goes through the shared `findUserIdsHoldingRoles(["R1"])` and takes the lowest id among real holders; the any-user fallback survives only for an org with no R1 at all, and now logs. (2) Found while reading it: `engineCreateVoucher` passed `spec.userId` into `nextRefFromSeries`'s `ReferenceContext` slot — a leftover from when the reference carried a **user** number (`"user"` is still an alias for the `"counter"` token in `partOrder`). So every GL voucher reference carried the *creator's row id* where the **counter number** belongs, and the same series read `30012` or `70013` depending on who saved it. `CreateGlVoucherSpec` / `PostReceiptVoucherArgs` now take an explicit `counterNumber`, wired from `shift.counter.counterNumber` in the four POS routes that already load the counter (sale surplus receipt, shift receipt, shift expenditure, order advance); office and online vouchers pass nothing and default to counter 1, as `assembleReference` documents. The two `invNextRef(tx, type, userId)` sites (voucher duplicate, POS quote/order) stopped passing a user id too. **Consequence to know:** the counter part of newly issued references changes on any install where this was silently reading a user id. Existing rows deliberately untouched — a posted voucher's number and creator are a record of what happened, and this database is due for a full clean.
- **2026-09-04 — The realtime sweep finished: every register, book and board follows the floor.** Owner asked whether any page was still left without live updates, with the Day Book as the reference behaviour (a voucher posted at another counter appears on its own). A route-by-route audit — 258 pages, walking each route's import graph rather than grepping page files — put the honest number at **79 live**; the earlier count flattered itself because a page whose product combobox was live, or which merely imported a file that *mentions* the hook in a comment (`appChannel.ts`, `localDb.ts`), counted as live. **Now 120.**
  - **Wired at the shared component, not per page**, so 40-odd screens came from ~16 files: [`InvVoucherList`](src/modules/inventory/components/InvVoucherList.tsx) carries 14 registers, [`GlVoucherList`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GlVoucherList.tsx) 5, [`BookPage`](src/modules/accountancy/components/BookPage.tsx) the cash/bank/loan books, [`GstSummaryView`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GstSummaryView.tsx) both GST supply reports. The Day Book's own line **moved into [`useDayBook`](src/modules/accountancy/hooks/useDayBook.ts)** — the data source subscribes on `ledger` and calls its own `syncNow`, so the Day Book AND the "All" register are live by construction and the page no longer wires anything. Also live: POS sales register, recycle bin, account-groups tree and detail, contacts, recurring tasks, coupons, gift cards, customer tiers, loyalty milestones, retention protection, ecommerce products, Usable Fund. Server-rendered pages (repairing hub, device location, work-flow inbox, ecommerce overview, loyalty hub) use `LiveRefresh`, which **moved `src/modules/home/` → [`src/shared/components/LiveRefresh.tsx`](src/shared/components/LiveRefresh.tsx)** now that it is not the home page's helper.
  - **Two things every one of these needed, and the reason it is not a one-line paste.** A live re-read must not blank what someone is reading, so each surface grew a `silent` path (`if (!silent) setLoading(true)`) while the Refresh button keeps its spinner. And `onClick={load}` hands React's MouseEvent to that new first parameter — the exact regression that made Refresh silently do nothing in twelve reports on 2026-08-22 — so `BookPage`, `GstSummaryView`, `ContactsWorkspace` and `usable-fund` were changed to `onClick={() => load()}` in the same edit.
  - **[`pos/counters`](src/app/(protected)/office/pos/counters/page.tsx) closes a parked TODO.** `POS_LIVE_POLL_MS`'s comment said "revisit when real counters run side by side and one terminal must notice another's shift without being told" — that is now `useRealtimeTopics(["ledger", "settings"])`, with the 90-minute poll left as the distant fallback. `usable-fund` likewise dropped a hand-rolled focus/visibility listener that refetched on every tab return whether or not anything had moved; the hook does both halves.
  - **Deliberately NOT wired, and why.** Settings (24), BI (21), Admin (6), Pricing-strategy (4) stay fetch-once by the owner's standing decision — BI especially, where each page is a heavy aggregate. **Every voucher detail/new screen (18)** stays fetch-once on purpose: someone is editing that voucher and a refresh under the cursor would throw away typed input. Same reasoning excludes the editors — opening-balance, GST classification, the storefront home-layout designer, ecommerce masters. The shop/portal (22) is customer-facing and server-rendered; receipts, shipping labels and slips print once. `office/customers/[id]` turned out to be a **pure redirect** with no UI at all. **No `topics.ts` change was needed** — every write path these screens care about already announced its topics.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 / 0 errors and `eslint` exit 0 across all 28 changed files. Not yet exercised against two live counters — the behaviour is the same hook the Day Book has used since 2026-08-19, but a real two-counter round trip on the registers has not been run.
- **2026-09-03 — Release convention written down: "do a release" is one fixed list, never a hand-made step.** Owner's rules: a release happens on **`main` only** (`dev` is where work lands, `main` is what gets tagged and installed on counters); every release bumps the version, ships crafted `RELEASE_NOTES.md`, tags, pushes the branch **and** the tag to **both** source remotes (`origin` = `devloperakramul/abeersuite-electron`, `production` = `akramuljakir/abeersuite-electron`), and publishes a GitHub release with installer, blockmap and `latest.yml` to **every** `GITHUB_UPDATE_REPO_N`. and afterwards `dev` is brought back level with `main`. Written as [docs/commands.md](docs/commands.md) § 0, with Journey A step 12, Journey B and the § 8 safety rules corrected to match. `pnpm electron:release` already does all of it except the `production` push — [`scripts/release-electron.ts`](scripts/release-electron.ts) pushes `origin` only, so that one line stays manual (`git push production HEAD --tags`) until the script is taught the second remote. No hand-tagging, no GitHub-UI uploads, and `RELEASE_ONLY_REPO=` is a retry after a partial failure, never the normal path.
- **2026-09-03 — Auto-update: made to actually install, with a visible prompt and three tiers; two dead-on-arrival faults found.** Owner asked to fix and test auto-update and to say how it should behave mid-work, on a schema change, and for a plain UI fix. What existed checked a feed every 30 minutes, downloaded silently, and installed only on quit — which on a tray-resident counter means never — and sent an `app:update-ready` IPC that nothing listened to. **Two faults made it dead regardless.** (1) counter.env points counters at `akramuljakir/abeersuite-electron`, and that repo answers **404** to the baked download PAT and to `GH_TOKEN_1` alike (probed this session) — so every check on every counter fails; today's main.log shows it. The only release with a `latest.yml` is v5.0.0 on `devloperakramul/abeersuite-electron`, the SOURCE repo, which the 2026-08-24 decision forbids as a feed. **Owner action:** create (or grant the two PATs on) the releases-only repo and point `GITHUB_DOWNLOAD_UPDATE_REPO` and `GITHUB_UPDATE_REPO_1` at it. (2) Since `build.publish` left package.json (2026-09-01), electron-builder no longer writes `resources/app-update.yml` — and electron-updater reads that file in its download path even when the feed is set in code, so a check would have succeeded and the download failed with ENOENT. Reproduced in the harness, fixed by writing `<userData>/update-config.yml` and pointing `updateConfigPath` at it.
  - **Policy, in [`electron/updateManager.ts`](electron/updateManager.ts)** (main.ts keeps only the IPC wiring). Tier `quiet` (default): a corner card "Restart now / Later"; installs by itself only at a safe moment — empty POS cart AND (window hidden, or on the landing page, or no keyboard/mouse input for 10 minutes via `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime`). Never forced. Tier `urgent`: `[urgent]` anywhere in RELEASE_NOTES.md (electron-builder copies the notes into latest.yml); blocking dialog with a 30-minute countdown, installs as soon as the cart is empty and hands are off for a minute, and at the deadline regardless. Tier `schema`: the counter's own sync worker printed `SCHEMA DRIFT` ([`syncManager.ts`](electron/syncManager.ts) now relays that line); billing is already paused by ReplicaReadyGate, so a check runs at once and the countdown is 5 minutes. `autoInstallOnAppQuit` stays on for clean quits. `quitAndInstall(true, true)` — silent, and relaunch, because there may be nobody there to start it.
  - **Renderer:** [`UpdatePrompt.tsx`](src/shared/components/UpdatePrompt.tsx) in the root layout, fed by `update:state` pushes; it also reports the POS cart's line count as the busy flag from the `posDraftStore` singleton, so no POS screen was touched. Tray gained "Check for updates".
  - **Release order for a schema change:** publish the counter build FIRST, then push the schema to Supabase. A replica ahead of the source shows only `extra` columns, which the guard tolerates; the reverse order halts every counter until the update lands.
  - **Found on the way:** under `next dev`, a page loaded from `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` (what the desktop shell uses) never hydrated in Electron and reloaded itself every ~45 s — Next 16 refuses the HMR socket from an origin other than `localhost`, and the dev client force-reloads. `next.config.ts` now lists `allowedDevOrigins: ["127.0.0.1"]`; `next start` (the packaged app) has no such check, so production was unaffected.
  - **Tested** against a local fake feed (`UPDATE_FEED_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8765/`, `UPDATE_DRY_RUN=1`, an Electron harness driving the compiled module): check → download → ready → install decision for the quiet tier; the busy flag holding an urgent install and releasing it; drift escalating to the schema tier and its countdown firing. The real installer run is proven only by a real packaged release, which needs the repo above. Test knobs (`UPDATE_IDLE_SECONDS`, `UPDATE_URGENT_IDLE_SECONDS`, `UPDATE_*_GRACE_MINUTES`) are documented in [docs/commands.md](docs/commands.md).
- **2026-09-03 — A released counter is refused on sync, and the replica can finally see the release.** Owner's story: a counter goes offline at 10:00, the office releases it at 11:00, the cashier keeps billing, the PC reconnects at 13:00 — everything it queued must be rejected and the PC must come back fresh; and a released PC still online must not be able to save a task or a voucher. Two gaps were found underneath the 2026-09-02 onboarding work. **(1) The worker could not see a release at all.** `PosCounter` has no `updatedAt`, so it is a `full` table, and `full` tables were skipped whenever count + a hash of their PRIMARY KEYS matched — a release changes `boundDeviceId`, not the keys, so `pos_counters` never re-pulled and the replica said "bound" forever; the same blind spot hid every in-place edit on all 30 `full` tables (a Department rename, a Position edit). [`reconcile.fingerprint()`](sync-engine/lib/reconcile.js) now hashes `to_jsonb(row)` for `full` tables (key-only stays for watermark tables, whose edits ride `updatedAt`); the source query returns its own column list in the same statement and the replica hashes only those columns, so a replica-only stale column cannot make the sides disagree — chosen over the schema guard's `extra` list because signal-woken cycles trust a recent verdict and never run it. Both pools pin `timezone=UTC` ([`db.js`](sync-engine/lib/db.js)). [`signal.js`](sync-engine/lib/signal.js) treats every re-`SUBSCRIBED` as one settled signal, so a PC coming back online runs a catch-up cycle at once instead of waiting out the interval. **(2) Nothing refused the queue.** There is no server between a counter and Supabase for pushes — [`flusher.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/flusher.ts) writes from the counter's own Next server with the DB credential — so the refusal lives there: before every non-empty push it asks Supabase itself (one row, fresh, through the new [`releaseGuard.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/releaseGuard.ts)) whether `COUNTER_MACHINE_ID` still holds the counter in `counter-setup.json`; a positive "no" dead-letters every pending row with `rejected: counter released by admin` (`rejectAllPending` in [`outbox.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/outbox.ts)) and pushes nothing. Authoritative there on purpose: at reconnect the replica still says "bound" until the worker pulls. The API side reads the replica, cached 30 s, no egress: `requireAuth()` ([`permissions.ts`](src/shared/lib/auth/permissions.ts)) returns `403 COUNTER_RELEASED` for every route on a counter PC the DB has positively released, except the six the login/setup flow needs (`my-binding`, counters list, `claim-number`, `release-mine`, `/api/counter/status`, `/api/sync/status`), which pass `allowReleased`. `my-binding`'s query moved into `resolveBinding()` in the guard so the route, the flusher and the gate share one answer and one "unreachable is not released" rule. `electron/main.ts` forwards `COUNTER_MACHINE_ID` and `COUNTER_USER_DATA` to the forked server. Unchanged: the reconciler (sync-applied on `PosCounter` → replica → sign out → `counter:clear-setup` → wipe) — it simply works now that the pull lands. No schema, seed or permission change. **Owner still to confirm on a real counter**: the 10:00/11:00/13:00 story end to end, and that a `full` table with real data reports "skipped" on the second cycle (no perpetual re-copy).
- **2026-09-03 — Pull cycles run four tables at a time, and screens follow the floor without F5.** Owner asked how to cut sync time and how to make every open screen update on its own. Sync time: an idle cycle was ~91 Supabase round-trips one after another on a single connection. Narrowing the cycle by topic was ruled out earlier (the worker's topic→model map had rotted and was deleted in `ee38af8f`), so the win comes from concurrency — [`sync-once.js`](sync-engine/sync-once.js) now runs each pass `CONCURRENCY` (4) tables wide, each table on its own pooled connection pair, children one depth at a time so a depth-2 child still filters on ids its parent has just written; `__SYNC_APPLIED__` prints once after every lane commits. `index.js` passes `--reason` and a `signal`/`queued` cycle trusts the last schema verdict in `_sync_status` when it is under 10 minutes old, instead of re-scanning `information_schema` on both sides per signal; startup, interval and retry cycles always compare. Realtime: writes with no browser behind them now announce after their DB work returns — the Razorpay and PayU callbacks, the Shiprocket and Shipway webhooks (the recurrences run is a browser POST and already rode the interceptor); [`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts) gained rules for `/api/accounts`, `/api/inbox/rooms`, `/api/pos/counters`, the POS coupon/gift-card lookups, `/api/retention`, `/api/bi/gst-purchase-planning`, the ecommerce config routes and the courier webhooks. On a counter reading its replica, [`useRealtimeTopics`](src/shared/hooks/useRealtimeTopics.ts) now ignores the early Supabase signal and waits for `sync:applied` (`setReadsGoLocal` in `appChannel.ts`, fed by `useCounterMode`), so a screen refetches once with fresh rows rather than once stale and once fresh. Money and floor pages went live under the same one-line pattern: POS catalogue (delta top-up in `usePosBilling`), shifts list and detail, stock summary, products (browse mode only), reorder, inventory details, ledgers tree and group page, chart of accounts, orders & estimates, ticket detail, ecommerce orders list and detail, customers; the server-rendered office home re-renders through [`LiveRefresh`](src/shared/components/LiveRefresh.tsx) (`router.refresh()`). Settings, admin, BI and pricing pages stay fetch-once by owner decision. `schemaCheck.ts` (zero callers) deleted; roadmap §4 rewritten to describe the built system. **Found while verifying, and fixed:** `child` tables were never reconciled ("exact for any parent that moved"), so a child row inserted without touching its parent's `updatedAt` was never pulled and nothing ever looked again — the real replica had 0 of 26 `LoyaltyLedger` and 0 of 24 `LoyaltyLot` rows with every cycle reporting "0 failed". Pass 3 now proves child tables too (same two-number fingerprint). Measured on the real replica from the office machine: idle cycle 13 s (old, sequential) → 3 s (new, four lanes); `verify-replica` exact after the child repair.
- **2026-09-03 — Outbox push fires at commit, from Postgres, not from a 1 s guess**. Owner asked why the flusher debounced its kick for a second and whether push and pull share a trigger. They do not: the flusher ([`flusher.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/flusher.ts)) is push only, and the pull worker (`sync-engine/`) is a separate loop woken by Realtime. The debounce was a guess at commit time — the kick fired from the Prisma layer after each query, before the enclosing transaction committed, and `_outbox` rows are invisible until commit; a slow commit meant an empty flush and a 60 s wait, a kick during a flush was dropped. Now the `_outbox_capture` trigger ends with `pg_notify('outbox_changed')`, which Postgres delivers only at commit and de-duplicates per transaction; the flusher holds one `LISTEN` connection (reconnects with backoff), a notification mid-flush queues exactly one more pass, and a full batch re-runs itself. `kick.ts` and the `kickOutboxFlush()` call in `prismaClient.ts` are deleted; the 60 s interval stays as the reconnect safety net. Two hygiene fixes from inspecting a live replica (outbox empty, 114/114 tables in step): the `NEVER_PUSH` list moved to [`neverPush.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/neverPush.ts) and [`gen-local-schema.ts`](scripts/gen-local-schema.ts) now attaches **no capture trigger** to those six registry tables (DROP only, so an existing replica sheds it on re-apply) — 108 triggers, not 114 — which ends the dead-lettered `User` rows the badge counted as "Sync error"; and `sync-engine/lib/status.js` granted to `smx_ro`, a role the provisioner never creates, logging `role "smx_ro" does not exist` twice per cycle — now `smx_app`.
- **2026-09-02 — Counter onboarding: DB binding is the truth, file is written last, a PC without a counter holds no data**. Owner rules: (R1) onboarding is **online only**; (R2) a PC without a counter carries **no business data** — the wipe is immediate and the outbox is irrelevant on a non-counter; (R3) the **DB binding is the truth**, `counter-setup.json` is a local shortcut written at the very end; (R4) **no reassignment** — a counter must be released before any PC can onboard on it, and binding happens in onboarding only; (R5) **no entry without a ready replica**; (R6) a returning PC verifies its counter row live when online, from the replica when offline. Flow: boot with no file → [`wipeReplica`](electron/replicaWipe.ts) (truncate every public table except `_provision`) → login (user lookup goes to Supabase because the replica is not ready) → [`/setup/counter`](src/app/setup/counter/page.tsx) reads the list authoritatively; a row the DB already binds to this PC is the **only** selectable row ("reserved for this PC", others "release … first"), else only free rows → [`claim-number`](src/app/api/pos/counters/[id]/claim-number/route.ts) binds in Supabase (no write when the row already says this PC) → the wizard starts the worker through the new `counter:start-sync` IPC and polls `/api/sync/status` until the replica reports a completed cycle with `ready` (progress line shared with `ReplicaReadyGate` via [`replicaProgress.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/replicaProgress.ts)) → **then** `saveCounterSetup` → app. A killed onboarding leaves a bound counter and no file: next boot wipes, the wizard resumes on the reserved row; no other PC sees that counter as free. Boot with a file: worker starts, splash waits for ready, then [`replicaBindsThisPc`](electron/replicaWipe.ts) checks the file's counter still carries this PC (the replica has just been pulled when online) — no → `resetCounter()` (file, worker, wipe); `waitForReplicaReady` **timeout** (never filled) → reset too; **schema drift** → keep data, the app shows its update screen. Login calls [`my-binding`](src/app/api/pos/counters/my-binding/route.ts) with `authoritative=1` when `navigator.onLine` (one row from Supabase), the replica otherwise; result is `valid | released | unreachable | not-set-up` — the `reassigned` self-heal is gone. Shift open ([`shifts/route.ts`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/route.ts)) no longer binds an unbound counter; it only checks the lock and stamps `idRangeStart`. [`CounterReconciler`](src/shared/components/CounterReconciler.tsx) stays event-driven on `sync:applied` listing `PosCounter`. **One id per app version, never cross-matched** (owner): the desktop app binds and checks with the Electron installation id only, the web version with the local bridge's id only; a counter bound under the other id is simply "in use by another PC" even on the same machine — release first, then onboard. The bridge→Electron migration (`legacyDeviceId` on claim, shift open, my-binding, [`identity.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/identity.ts)) is deleted. **The "login outside counter" permission gates both versions**: desktop = local file + counter row (live when online, replica offline), web = bridge id bound in the DB ([`isWebCounterBound`](src/shared/lib/counter/setupGate.ts)); a machine that is not a counter signs in only with `auth:login:outside-counter`, which on the desktop app is also who may onboard a fresh PC. **"Sync error (1)" after the first sign-out on a counter**: the sign-out event ([`auth.ts`](src/auth.ts)) and the permission-change revokers ([`revokeSessions.ts`](src/shared/lib/auth/revokeSessions.ts)) bumped `User.tokensValidFrom` through `prisma`, which on a counter writes the replica and queues the row — and `User` is in push's `NEVER_PUSH`, so it dead-lettered and the badge counted it forever. Both now write through `prismaAuthoritative` (best-effort, skipped offline). Earlier today's "drain before wipe" and auto-claim were superseded by these rules.
- **2026-09-01 — Two dead Realtime layers deleted; the app is back to one change channel**. Owner asked how Realtime drives sync and spotted that the layers overlapped. They did not overlap — two of them did nothing at all, and both had looked implemented for long enough to be described as working in this file.
  - **`abeersuite:sync-events` had no publisher.** [`realtimeSync.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/index.ts) subscribed a second Supabase channel from `useRealtimeTopics`, so it opened a socket in **every tab** — outside the `navigator.locks` election in [`tabLeader.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/tabLeader.ts) that exists to keep it to one per computer. Nothing ever sent on it: `broadcastTableMutation` had zero callers repo-wide, the `app:live-delta` window event it also listened for was never dispatched, and the replica worker had been on `app:changes` since the sync-engine absorption. Four open tabs cost 8 sockets where 1 was needed. Deleted, with its `SyncBroadcastEvent` payload type.
  - **The worker's topic→model map was computed and thrown away.** [`signal.js`](sync-engine/lib/signal.js) expanded each signal's topics into a model list through a hand-written `TOPIC_MODELS` and passed it to `onChange(models)`; `index.js` registers `signal.listen(cfg, () => runCycle("change signal"), log)`, which **discards the argument**, and spawns `sync-once.js` with no `--only`. Every signal has always synced all 113 tables. The map had drifted too — **60 of 113 registered tables appeared under no topic** — invisible because an incomplete list narrows silently, while an unknown *topic* falls back to syncing everything.
  - **Removed rather than wired up, on measurement.** A cycle where little moved is already near-free: a watermark table returns 0 rows from one indexed query, a child table whose parent did not move issues no query at all, and a `full` table is skipped after two aggregate numbers by the fingerprint guard in `sync-once.js`. Row counts settled the rest — **every `full` table is small**, largest `RolePermission` at 376, `AccountGroup` 34, `Work` 0 — so the `full`-strategy re-reads that looked expensive are not. Filtering would have shaved a fraction off an already-cheap cycle while adding a way for the child pass (which depends on parents having actually been pulled in the same cycle) to go quietly wrong.
  - **Also corrected**: `sync/index.ts`'s header claimed the outbox had been removed as an unimplemented stub — `outbox.ts`/`push.ts`/`flusher.ts` are live behind `/api/sync/*` and `instrumentation.ts` since 2026-08-28.
  - **Unchanged**: all 22 `useRealtimeTopics` callers, the `{ topics, at }` wire, the settle windows, the hint-not-truth contract, the timer fallback, and chat's per-room broadcasts. **Not done**: publishing written tables from the SERVER via a Prisma `$extends` (where Prisma actually knows what it wrote) is the honest version of per-table syncing if it is ever wanted — it would also cover writes with no browser behind them. Left as a proposal, not started. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean (needs `--max-old-space-size=8192`), `node --check` on all three engine files, and no remaining reference to any deleted symbol.

- **2026-09-02 — The DB decides who holds a counter, and a closed shift stays closed**. Releasing a counter is a **device swap, not a cash event**: the PC dies, the admin moves the counter to another machine, and the shift and drawer carry on. Four things stopped that from working. (1) The shift-open route's three reads were commented "Authoritative" and called `prisma` — the replica on a counter PC — so a replacement PC's own replica still showed the old lock and refused the open, which is the exact moment the swap has to work; they now use `prismaAuthoritative` ([`shifts/route.ts`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/route.ts)). (2) [`reconcileCounterSetup`](src/shared/lib/counter/setupGate.ts) asked the replica-backed counter list whether this PC still held its counter — circular, since that row is the one this machine stamps at shift open; it now asks the new [`/api/pos/counters/my-binding`](src/app/api/pos/counters/my-binding/route.ts), which reads authoritatively. Unreachable still means "trust the local file", so a blip never signs anyone out. (3) `release-mine` wrote the release into the replica, where the push then **undid** it — the PC believed it had let go while the DB never agreed; it is authoritative now, and fails loudly when Supabase is unreachable rather than writing a lie. (4) New nullable **`PosCounter.releasedAt`**: `boundDeviceId = null` alone cannot distinguish "released" from "never claimed", and this table carries no `updatedAt` for last-write-wins to arbitrate with, so a counter that was offline when it was released woke up, pushed its queued row and **silently took itself back**. [`reconcileCounterBinding`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) now refuses a bind older than the stamp; that PC must run the setup wizard and take a new counter number. Bind clears the stamp. Separately, a `pos_shifts` write is **dropped when the DB already says CLOSED** — blind LWW would either overwrite the admin's declared cash and variance with a second closing Contra (the same cash swept to the vault twice) or drop the local close silently — and [`shifts/current`](src/app/api/pos/shifts/current/route.ts) confirms the replica's OPEN answer against the DB by primary key, so a machine recovering from a force-close is offered a **fresh shift** rather than handed one the office has settled. Force-close remains rare and by the counter's own request; recovery is the counter's job. Known residual: drops/expenditures/receipts minted offline *after* a force-close still carry the old `shiftId` and land on the closed shift, visible for audit rather than moved automatically. Schema: one additive nullable column, mirrored in [`localDbProvisioner`](electron/localDbProvisioner.ts)'s column migrations and omitted from the personal snapshot (it is runtime state, not config). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, sync registry and local schema regenerated.
- **2026-09-01 — Thermal printer moved to IPC too; the hardware bridge now listens on nothing**. Phase 2 of the move below. [`thermal.js`](hardware-bridge/thermal.js)'s 9 Express routes became a `COMMANDS` table on the same `{ mod, cmd, payload, id? }` channel, so `qr` and `thermal` share one IPC pipe with independent readiness gates. **`express`, `cors` and `cors-pna.js` are gone** — deleted, and dropped from the packaged dependency lists in [`prepare-bridge-deps.ts`](scripts/prepare-bridge-deps.ts) and [`verify-package.ts`](scripts/verify-package.ts). Unlike QR, every thermal command is request/response: a failed receipt must reach the cashier. **New shared client** [`hardwareBridge.ts`](src/shared/lib/bridge/hardwareBridge.ts) — `bridgeSend` (fire-and-forget), `bridgeRequest` (throws), `bridgeOk` (never throws) — picks IPC or HTTP per call from `window.electronAPI`, and is now the **only** place either port number appears. `qrDisplay.ts` collapsed onto it (its duplicate transport logic deleted), and the 5 direct thermal callers were repointed: `PosScreen`, `PrintToolbar`, printer settings, [`identity.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/identity.ts), [`bridgeHealth.ts`](src/shared/lib/bridge/bridgeHealth.ts) (which now probes through the shared client; `BRIDGE_ORIGIN` is deleted). `isElectronEnvironment` moved to its own [`electronEnv.ts`](src/shared/lib/bridge/electronEnv.ts) because bridgeHealth probing *through* hardwareBridge while hardwareBridge needed the Electron check would otherwise be an import cycle; bridgeHealth re-exports it so no caller changed. `PrintToolbar`'s public `endpoint` prop keeps its leading slash (5 receipt pages set it) and is stripped in one place. **The `EADDRINUSE` hazard is gone**: a legacy combined-bridge can now be installed alongside a running counter without colliding on :9191. Verified: real `thermal.js` + `qr.js` on one real IPC channel — both announce ready independently, all 9 thermal commands answer (`printers` returned the actual RETSOL TP806), validation errors, unknown commands and a genuine `Network timeout` from the print path all propagate as errors; the shared client in a stubbed browser produces exactly the 9 pre-IPC thermal routes and keeps QR on :9192; with `electronAPI` present it is IPC-only with zero HTTP, print failures throw and `bridgeOk` swallows them. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src electron` and `check-doc-sync` all clean. **Owner still to confirm printing on a counter** — the printer cannot be driven from a dev box.
- **2026-09-01 — QR display client falls back to the standalone combined-bridge in a browser**. Follows the IPC move below. [`qrDisplay.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/qrDisplay.ts) now picks its transport per call from `window.electronAPI`: present → IPC, absent → HTTP on :9192 exactly as before Electron absorbed the bridge. **No env flag** — owner's call, and the right one: a `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` switch is inlined at build time, so it could not be flipped in the emergency it exists for, while a browser simply never has `electronAPI`. (The existing server-side `COUNTER_PC` in [`counterMode.ts`](src/shared/lib/prisma/counterMode.ts) is untouched and unrelated — it gates local-DB read routing and is not readable from client code anyway.) **The bundled bridge stays IPC-only — no port is reopened.** The fallback works because `localhost:9192` resolves to the BROWSER's own PC: an admin opening the app from their desk drives their own hardware through their own combined-bridge install, which is the design the bridge always had (every PC its own bridge, printer and display). It was never able to reach the counter's display from another machine, before or after the IPC move. Command names were already chosen to mirror the old HTTP paths, so the fallback is one `switch`: four special cases (`health`, `config:get`, `config:set`, `ports`) and `POST /${cmd}` for the rest. **Known gap:** a browser on the counter PC itself reaches nothing unless that PC also has the standalone bridge, since the bundled one has no port — and if Electron is not running there is no bundled bridge at all. Verified: the real module driven with a stubbed browser — all 12 commands produce exactly the 12 routes the pre-IPC bridge served (diffed against `git show` of the old route table), with `electronAPI` present it uses IPC and makes zero HTTP calls, and an error reply throws rather than resolving silently; `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean.
- **2026-09-01 — Customer display moved off HTTP onto Electron IPC**. [`qr.js`](hardware-bridge/qr.js) no longer runs an Express app on :9192; its 12 routes are now a `COMMANDS` table driven by `{ mod: "qr", cmd, payload, id? }` messages over the bridge child's IPC channel (`stdio` gained an `"ipc"` slot in [`hardwareBridgeManager.ts`](electron/hardwareBridgeManager.ts)). The port was inherited from the standalone `combined-bridge` era, when the app ran in a plain browser and HTTP was the only way to reach a local process; inside Electron it bought nothing and cost real exposure. **Split by contract**: the 8 POS screens are fire-and-forget (`sendQr`) because [`qrDisplay.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/qrDisplay.ts) promises never to surface a display failure to the cashier, so they need no reply; only the 4 settings-page calls (`health`, `config:get`, `config:set`, `ports`) use the correlated `requestQr`, which means a wedged display cannot grow the pending map during a sale. **No POS component changed** — every caller already went through the `qrDisplay` facade, so only `send()`'s body moved; the settings page swapped 4 `fetch`es for the new `qrRequest` helper. **Readiness handshake, found by testing**: qr.js needs 1–2s to load `sharp` + `serialport`, and Node drops an IPC message that arrives before a `message` listener exists — no listener, no queue, no error. The child now announces `{ ready: true }` and main buffers behind it, else a command sent during boot vanished and the caller just waited out its timeout. `relayPort` stays in the saved config (unused by QR, still read by thermal). **Thermal deliberately NOT moved** — it has 8+ direct `localhost:9191` call sites and returns errors the cashier must see; it migrates behind the `BRIDGE_ORIGIN` seam once QR has run on a real counter, and `curl localhost:9191/health` still works for phone support meanwhile. No schema change. Verified: the real qr.js driven over a real IPC channel — all 12 commands, error propagation, unknown-command rejection, wrong-`mod` messages ignored, fire-and-forget producing no reply, child alive throughout, and the zero-warm-up case that failed before the handshake; `tsc --noEmit` clean (needs `--max-old-space-size=8192`), `eslint` clean. **Owner still to confirm on a counter with the DQ11 attached** — the hardware cannot be driven from a dev box.
- **2026-09-01 — Hardware bridge relays locked to loopback and an origin allowlist**. [`qr.js`](hardware-bridge/qr.js) (:9192, DQ11 customer display) and [`thermal.js`](hardware-bridge/thermal.js) (:9191, ESC/POS printer) called `app.listen(RELAY_PORT)` with no host, so both bound `0.0.0.0` — the shop hardware was drivable from any PC on the LAN — and [`cors-pna.js`](hardware-bridge/cors-pna.js) used bare `cors()`, i.e. `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. With no auth on these endpoints, any website the cashier browsed could POST to `localhost:9192/cart` and paint the customer-facing display, or fire the printer. Both now bind `127.0.0.1` and answer CORS only for origins whose hostname is loopback or one of this machine's own IPv4 addresses (refreshed every 60s, so DHCP cannot lock the app out of its own hardware); a missing `Origin` — curl, health probes, Electron main — is still allowed, being same-machine by definition. **Nothing legitimate is lost**: every client hardcodes `http://localhost:919x` ([`bridgeHealth.ts`](src/shared/lib/bridge/bridgeHealth.ts), [`qrDisplay.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/qrDisplay.ts), `PosScreen`, `PrintToolbar`, both settings pages), so a browser on another PC already resolved that to its own machine and never reached the counter. The Next server's deliberate `0.0.0.0` bind on :786 is untouched. Also corrected a stale header comment in [`hardwareBridgeManager.ts`](electron/hardwareBridgeManager.ts) claiming the QR half had not moved into the bridge — `index.js` has been starting it for a while. No schema change. Verified: relay probed with five origins (none / loopback / own LAN IP allowed; `https://evil.example.com` and a foreign LAN IP refused), `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **2026-08-28 — Counter goes local-first, behind two independent dials. Five separate faults meant the offline path had never once worked end to end.** Owner decision: a counter should use its own PostgreSQL the way the web app uses Supabase, with replica pull and outbox push as a separate background concern. Rolling out to 2–3 mostly-online counters; long-offline and adversarial-conflict correctness is explicitly deferred.
  - **What was broken.** Each of these alone was sufficient. (1) `applyIdRanges` connected on the URL `main.ts` handed it, which is the `smx_app` role — no `CREATE ON SCHEMA`, no sequence ownership — so both its `CREATE TABLE "_id_ranges"` and its `ALTER SEQUENCE` failed; reproduced against a real cluster as *permission denied for schema public* and *must be owner of sequence*. (2) Those errors were swallowed, so `isOfflineRangeApplied()` read the missing table as "no confirmed range" and blocked every offline write on the machine, silently. (3) **8 of the 11 hand-written sequence names did not exist** — Prisma names a sequence after the TABLE, so only the `@@map`'d POS ones were real; `universal_vouchers_id_seq`, `voucher_details_id_seq` and `tickets_id_seq` are fiction, and the first `ALTER` would have aborted the batch anyway. (4) [`electron/outboxFlusher.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/flusher.ts) POSTed to `/api/sync/push` from the Electron main process, which holds no session cookie, so `requireAuth()` answered 401 every time — and its `catch` was empty, so nothing was ever logged. (5) Three models had outbox entries but were missing from the 36-model write allowlist so their writes threw, while **27 models were in the allowlist with no outbox entry** — products, customers, accounts, stock ledger, loyalty ledger — so those writes succeeded locally and never reached the cloud.
  - **Writes are captured by Postgres triggers, not app code.** One `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger per replicated table, generated into [`resources/local-schema.sql`](scripts/gen-local-schema.ts) from `prisma/sync-tables.json`, so the set cannot drift from the schema (a `--check` fails the build if triggers ≠ registry tables). It records `to_jsonb(NEW)` — the committed row, not `args.data` — plus `row_key` as jsonb, which carries compound primary keys with their types intact. The old interceptor missed `createMany`, `upsert`, nested writes and raw SQL entirely; the trigger cannot be bypassed by any of them. **Echo suppression is free**: the sync worker writes pulled rows under `SET LOCAL session_replication_role = replica`, and Postgres fires only REPLICA/ALWAYS triggers in that mode — so ordinary triggers do not re-queue what was just pulled down. Verified non-vacuously: 4 writes made in replica mode landed and produced **zero** outbox rows, while the same tables written normally were captured.
  - **`_outbox.id` order is a valid foreign-key order**, which is what lets the pusher replay without a topological sort: local FKs are enforced immediately, so when a child was written its parent was already visible — hence committed earlier, hence a lower id. That argument needs `ORDER BY id` (the previous `created_at` is transaction *start* time and can invert between overlapping transactions) and a run that **stops at the first failure**. Stopping is only safe because `_outbox` gained backoff and a dead letter — without one, a single poison row would block every later mutation forever, visible only as a pending count that never fell.
  - **Id ranges now cover all 79 autoincrement models, discovered from `pg_depend`** rather than a hand-kept list of 11. Under local-first the counter writes all 79, and an unreserved sequence mints from 1 — which does not crash, it makes the push upsert over an unrelated cloud row. The privileged half moved into `_apply_id_range()`, `SECURITY DEFINER` and owned by `postgres`, so the app role gets `EXECUTE` and not the `DROP`/`ALTER` that sequence ownership would imply. It also never moves a sequence backwards inside its own block, so re-applying after ids have been minted is a no-op instead of reissuing them (a sequence found *above* the block — the counter was renumbered lower — is pulled down into the new block; see the 2026-09-06 renumber entry under Recently shipped). **2026-09-06 — it also steps past rows already in the table.** The pull inserts rows *with* their id and Postgres does not advance a sequence for those, so a reinstalled counter that pulled its own earlier rows back down sat with tickets at 1920000000–1920000004 and the sequence at 1920000000 → `Unique constraint failed on (id)` at the next sale (counter 93). `_apply_id_range()` now restarts each sequence above the highest id present **inside this counter's block only** — the table max would have jumped it into counter 94's block (1930000010 was sitting there) and made the two overwrite each other in cloud. It runs after every pull cycle ([`sync-once.js`](sync-engine/sync-once.js) pass 5, `smx_sync` has EXECUTE) and at every boot ([`localDbProvisioner.ts`](electron/localDbProvisioner.ts)) when `_id_ranges` already holds a range, so an upgrade heals the machine on first start. The stale hand-written `OFFLINE_SEQUENCE_MAP` (wrong snake_case names) is deleted.
  - **Two dials.** `READ_MODE` (live|shadow|local) decides reads; new `WRITE_MODE` (live|local, and since 2026-09-07 `v_live` — see the 2026-09-07 entry) decides writes. **WRITE_MODE picks the base client, READ_MODE decides whether reads are redirected off it** — that order matters, because a `$transaction` is a write, so it opens on the WRITE_MODE client and every read inside it stays on the same database. Both default to `live`, so a counter setting neither is unchanged, and `WRITE_MODE=live` is the escape hatch: one env var reverts a misbehaving till without a rebuild. The offline voucher-type policy is now conditional on that fallback — under `local` it would refuse those types permanently rather than protect anything.
  - **`prismaLive` now means Supabase and only Supabase.** It previously redirected reads to the replica like everything else, which on the push path would have been live data loss: the last-write-wins check would have read the counter's own just-written row from the replica, concluded the cloud was newer, and dropped the mutation as superseded. `prismaRead` is deleted (zero consumers); `prismaAuthoritative` keeps 4 sites across the two counter-**admin** flows, where each read gates a decision to REFUSE.
  - **First-boot gate.** An empty replica does not error, it returns fewer rows — a product list renders blank, which reads as "those were deleted". [`waitForReplica.ts`](electron/waitForReplica.ts) holds the splash until `_sync_status.ready`, showing real `tables_synced`/`tables_total` progress; [`ReplicaReadyGate`](src/shared/components/ReplicaReadyGate.tsx) covers a replica that goes unready mid-session, and is conditional on `READ_MODE ≠ live` because not-ready is the ordinary state on a counter that reads live.
  - **Still open.** Row-ownership on `/api/sync/push` (any authenticated session can address any row in a syncable table). The scripted end-to-end harness — provision, first sync, exercise each write class, pull the network, restore, compare row-for-row with `verify-replica.js`.

- **2026-08-28 — Aligned Live and Synced top bar indicators across Web and Desktop**. [`CounterStatusBadge.tsx`](src/shared/components/CounterStatusBadge.tsx) permanently displays green **`Live`** (with Wifi icon) when online and connected directly to Supabase cloud, green **`Local`** (with RefreshCw icon) when reading from the local replica, and red **`Local`** (with WifiOff icon) when offline/unreachable. [`SyncStatusBadge.tsx`](src/shared/components/SyncStatusBadge.tsx) displays local outbox write sync state (**`Synced`**, **`Pending (N)`**, **`Sync error (N)`**), backed by [`/api/sync/status`](src/app/api/sync/status/route.ts), [`useOutboxStatus.ts`](src/shared/hooks/useOutboxStatus.ts), and [`getOutboxStatus`](src/shared/lib/sync/outbox.ts). Both badges are integrated into [`FinanceTopBar.tsx`](src/shared/components/FinanceTopBar.tsx) and POS [`ActiveShiftBar.tsx`](src/modules/pos/components/ActiveShiftBar.tsx).
- **2026-08-28 — Branch number identity & POS counter prefixing**. Added mandatory, unique positive integer `number` field (`> 0`) to `Branch` across Prisma schema (`org.prisma`), API validation (`/api/branches` POST & PATCH with 409 conflict guards), branch management UI (`/office/admin/branches`), and bootstrap/personal seeds (`bootstrap-seed.ts`, `personal.local.json`). Updated POS Counter creation UI (`/office/admin/counters`) so selecting a branch prefixes the counter number input with the branch's number (e.g. Branch `35` + Counter `12` displays prefix `35` with input `12` and saves as `3512` in DB and GL account naming).

- **2026-08-25 — Shop/office deployment separation: strip script landed (Phase 0–1 of the roadmap, Vercel setup still to do)**. One repo/branch, two intended Vercel projects — public (`shop.networkservice.in`, storefront + portal) and office (`office.networkservice.in`, full staff app) — told apart by `APP_MODE`. [`scripts/strip-public-build.mjs`](scripts/strip-public-build.mjs) is the first step of `pnpm build`; on `APP_MODE=public` it **deletes** office-only source from the checkout before `next build` runs — absence beats a runtime gate, since `src/proxy.ts` doesn't match `/api/*` at all. Default-deny API allowlist (`auth, portal, shop, upload-signature, legal`) plus whole-subtree strips (`(protected)`, `staff`, `receipt`, `shipping-label`, `shipping-slip`). Office build is a no-op. `next.config.ts`'s legacy `/office/*` redirects and `src/auth.ts`'s staff `Credentials` provider now also skip under `IS_PUBLIC`, so the public bundle carries no staff auth path.

  **Audit found `/receipt/invoice/[id]` unauthenticated** — a raw `VoucherDetail.id` in the URL rendered the full invoice (party name, GSTIN, phone, line items) with no session check, and `src/proxy.ts`'s matcher excluded `receipt` entirely. Fixed as its own thing, not just deployment-scoped: all six `src/app/receipt/*` pages now call the new [`requireStaffPage()`](src/shared/lib/auth/permissions.ts) (redirects to `/staff/login`), and `receipt` was removed from the proxy matcher exclusion (defense in depth alongside the page-level guard); `wantsStaffSignIn()` now also matches `/receipt`. Customers lost their old direct link to that staff page — replaced with [`/portal/orders/[id]/bill`](<src/app/(external)/portal/orders/[id]/bill/page.tsx>), which checks the signed-in portal identity owns the voucher's party account before rendering. Both routes share one render body, [`InvoiceReceipt.tsx`](src/shared/components/print/InvoiceReceipt.tsx), extracted verbatim from the old inline page — staff keeps full paper/kind/autoprint controls, the portal route is pinned to A4/`ONLINE_INVOICE`.

  **Incident, same day:** local `.env` had `APP_MODE="public"` live; running `pnpm build` in the real working tree triggered the strip for real, deleting ~650 tracked files on disk (matching the script's own warning comment). Recovered via `git restore`, which also reverted the legitimate same-day edits — redone from scratch. Two guardrails added so this can't recur silently: `.env` defaults back to `office` with a comment explaining why, and the strip script itself now refuses to run outside `process.env.VERCEL` unless `ALLOW_LOCAL_PUBLIC_STRIP=1` is explicitly set.

  **Landed alongside it on the desktop branch, merged 2026-08-26:** the proxy matcher now also covers `shipping-slip`/`shipping-label` (not just `receipt`), and `wantsStaffSignIn()` matches `/vouchers` (and then `/lab`, deleted 2026-09-27) too, so every staff surface redirects to `/staff/login` rather than the customer `/login`. [`scripts/check-guard-lint.ts`](scripts/check-guard-lint.ts) asserts that every office API route handler imports an auth guard, wired into `pnpm precommit` — so the "guard-lint for new API routes" add-on below is **done**. The `/staff/login` links were also removed from `ShopFooter.tsx` and `src/app/(external)/portal/page.tsx`.

  **Not yet done:** the actual second Vercel project, DNS, env vars, payment/courier webhook re-registration against the shop host, and the verification checklist (public 404s on office paths, robots/canonical checks). Full roadmap: `~/.claude/plans/magical-growing-swan.md`. Add-ons still open (not yet decided): least-privilege DB user for the public deployment, office IP lock.

- **2026-09-04 — Visual C++ runtime pre-install audit (installer + web setup)**. The embedded PostgreSQL needs the VC++ 2015-2022 x64 runtime; on a clean counter PC `initdb` died with `0xC0000135` (DLL not found), the app fell back to live mode, and counter onboarding could never start its local sync. Not bundled (~25 MB on every installer and update): [`build/installer.nsh`](build/installer.nsh) checks `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\VisualStudio\14.0\VC\Runtimes\x64` (Installed=1, ≥14.20) and otherwise downloads `vc_redist.x64.exe` from Microsoft (inetc) and runs it silently; a failed download or install aborts setup with the URL. [`AbeerSuiteDownloader.cs`](scripts/downloader/AbeerSuiteDownloader.cs) runs the same audit before launching the installer, so whichever runs first does the work once. `postgresManager.ts` now names the missing runtime when initdb exits with that code.

- **2026-08-25 — Lightweight Web Setup Downloader (`AbeerSuite-WebSetup.exe`, ~33 KB) built**.
  - **Standalone bootstrapper installer.** Built [`scripts/downloader/AbeerSuiteDownloader.cs`](scripts/downloader/AbeerSuiteDownloader.cs) and [`scripts/build-downloader.ts`](scripts/build-downloader.ts) (`pnpm build:downloader`).
  - **Size & Performance.** Compiles natively with .NET 4.8 `csc.exe` into a single standalone ~33 KB executable with zero external runtime dependencies on Windows 10/11.
  - **Universal Latest Release Download.** Automatically queries GitHub API `/repos/devloperakramul/abeersuite-networkservice-update/releases/latest` using the baked/supplied PAT token, fetches the latest `AbeerSuite-Setup-*.exe` release asset, cleanly follows the 302 S3 redirect (stripping the Authorization header to prevent S3 HTTP 400 rejection), streams the download with a live UI progress bar / speed counter, and auto-launches the installer. Also accepts `--version=X.Y.Z`, `--token=...`, and `/s` silent install flags.

- **2026-08-25 — Release v0.2.0 built, packaged, verified, and published to GitHub**.
  - **Full production build executed.** Standalone Next.js compiled (`COUNTER_BUILD=1 pnpm build`), dependencies hoisted, `resources/counter.env` generated (10 keys), and NSIS installer packaged (`dist-app/AbeerSuite-Setup-0.2.0.exe`, 204.3 MB).
  - **Packaging gate passed.** `verify-package.ts` ran against `dist-app/win-unpacked`: 0 symlink leaks, closure of 24 packages verified, resources size 468.4 MB (under 800 MB limit), 0 credential leaks across 7,791 files.
  - **Release published.** [`scripts/release-electron.ts`](scripts/release-electron.ts) published `v0.2.0` with `AbeerSuite-Setup-0.2.0.exe`, `AbeerSuite-Setup-0.2.0.exe.blockmap`, and `latest.yml` to GitHub repository `devloperakramul/abeersuite-networkservice-update`.

- **2026-08-25 — System Tray background mode & Windows autostart for instant app opening**.
  - **System Tray lifecycle added.** [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts) now initializes a `Tray` icon with tooltip ("AbeerSuite POS (Port <PORT>)") and context menu ("Open AbeerSuite", "Open in Browser", "Start with Windows" toggle, "Quit AbeerSuite").
  - **Close-to-tray & warm reopening.** Closing the window now hides it (`event.preventDefault(); win.hide()`) rather than killing the server. Double-clicking desktop shortcut or start menu triggers `second-instance`, which shows and focuses the existing window instantly with 0 cold-start delay. Full exit is cleanly triggered from Tray -> "Quit AbeerSuite".
  - **Windows startup autostart support (`--hidden`).** Supports `app.setLoginItemSettings()` to auto-boot with Windows in the background on counter PCs, with pre-warming of local server and database connections.
