  - **Asset inclusion.** [`package.json`](package.json) `extraResources` now includes `build/icon.png` so the tray icon is available in production packaged builds.

- **2026-08-25 — Auto-update: repo made configurable, verified live end to end, one action item found**. Small follow-up on Phase 7, committing work that was staged but never landed.
  - **`GH_UPDATE_TOKEN` renamed and the repo made configurable.** [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts)'s `setupAutoUpdater()` no longer hard-codes `owner`/`repo` — both come from a new `GITHUB_UPDATE_REPO` (`"owner/repo"`) env var, alongside the renamed `PAT_TOKEN`. Both baked into `counter.env` via [`scripts/counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts), same as before, just no longer requiring a rebuild to point a counter's updates at a different repo.
  - **Verified live end to end, for real this time.** Full `pnpm exec electron-builder --dir` + `verify:package` — **PASS**, `resources/` 468.3 MB, 10 keys in `counter.env`. Launched the packaged app on the baked env alone: the "PAT_TOKEN or GITHUB_UPDATE_REPO not set" skip message is **gone** — the updater made a real HTTPS request to `api.github.com` using the baked token.
  - **Found via that real request: the token is rejected.** `GET /repos/devloperakramul/abeersuite-networkservice-update/releases/latest` returns **403 "Resource not accessible by personal access token"** — a fine-grained PAT permission/repo-access problem, not a code defect. The failure is handled exactly as designed: caught, logged as a warning, does not block boot. **Owner action needed:** check the PAT actually lists this repo under "Repository access" and has "Contents: Read-only" granted (fine-grained PATs need the repo explicitly selected at creation, and if none was picked, or if a private-repo fine-grained PAT needs owner approval on an org, this is exactly the failure mode) — then also publish at least one release, since `/releases/latest` needs one to exist regardless.
  - **Note on this session's build times:** the identical `verify-package.ts` secret-scan step that took ~1 minute in earlier passes took ~13 minutes this run, on unchanged code and an identical file count. No evidence of an actual bug (CPU climbed continuously throughout, no plateau); attributed to this sandbox's variable disk I/O, consistent with similarly unexplained slowdowns earlier in the same session (an 8-minute `next dev` cold compile, a `tsc --noEmit` that first OOM'd and then plateaued on retry).

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 7: auto-update split onto a separate private releases-only repo**. Owner asked whether updates could use a public repo with no source to avoid any token on any counter. Answer: no — the installer bakes `DATABASE_URL` into `resources/counter.env`, so a public artifact would hand out the live database credential. But the question exposed a real problem in what shipped earlier the same day: `setupAutoUpdater()` pointed at **the source repo** (`abeersuite-electron`), so the baked token was about to grant read access to the entire private codebase if any single installer ever leaked.
  - **Fix: a dedicated repo, `devloperakramul/abeersuite-networkservice-update`** — private, release artifacts only, created this session (owner named it, scoped for their own company use, not for sale/public). `package.json`'s `build.publish.repo` and `electron/main.ts`'s `setFeedURL` both point there now. A leaked token now exposes only release artifacts every counter already has on disk — near-zero incremental exposure — and no shipped credential can reach the source.
  - **`GH_UPDATE_TOKEN` is now baked into `counter.env`** ([`scripts/counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts)), matching the unattended-install design rather than requiring per-counter manual placement. Stays optional — `generate-counter-env.ts` reports it missing by name and skips it; `setupAutoUpdater()` already logs and returns gracefully with no token, verified in this session's build. Corrects a wrong justification from the same-day auto-update work, which cited `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` as baked-in precedent — that stopped being true the moment Phase 6 (same day) commented it out. This decision stands on its own: the releases-only scoping is what makes a baked token acceptable, not an existing pattern.
  - **Two tokens, not to be confused.** `GH_UPDATE_TOKEN` (Contents: Read-only, scoped to the releases repo) is baked into every counter. The **publish** token (`GH_TOKEN`, needs write access for `electron-builder --publish`) is separate, more powerful, stays on the build machine only, and must never be added to `COUNTER_ENV_COPIED` — flagged explicitly in the roadmap so the two aren't conflated later.
  - **Verified:** `electron:compile` and a scoped `eslint` pass both clean. `generate-counter-env.ts` correctly reports `GH_UPDATE_TOKEN` as "not set on this machine, so not shipped" (owner has not placed it in the build machine's `.env` yet — that step is theirs) and still writes the other 8 keys; `verify-package.ts` re-run against the already-packaged tree — **PASS**. The 9-key case (token present) and the actual cross-machine update delivery are owner-side, not verifiable in this environment.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 5 (sync engine) deliberately not attempted this pass**. Closes out the approved desktop plan (`~/.claude/plans/127-0-0-1-3000-use-different-port-encapsulated-allen.md`) — Phases 0 through 4 and 6 are done or honestly deferred; Phase 5 is the one left fully open, on purpose.
  - **Why not just write it.** Syncing data into a local database cannot be built responsibly without one to sync into and diff results against — and none exists here or on any shipped counter (Phase 4 left `resources/pgsql` empty on purpose). A hand-written `syncTableByNumber()`, durable outbox, or worker startup with no way to run a real cycle and confirm rows land correctly is exactly the untested-guesswork risk avoided everywhere else in this pass.
  - **One real finding, not fixed.** Went looking at the "quick win" the original audit flagged — [`useRealtimeTopics.ts`](src/shared/hooks/useRealtimeTopics.ts) reacting to _any_ sync event instead of filtering by topic like its `onChangeSignal` sibling path does. Filtering it properly needs a table-number (`prisma/sync-tables.json`'s `n`) → `Topic` mapping that does not exist anywhere in the codebase yet — that mapping is sync-engine design work, not a standalone one-line fix, so it was left alone rather than guessed at. Near-zero practical impact today anyway: `broadcastTableMutation` is only ever called from the outbox's stub flush, which nothing populates.
  - **What's genuinely solid and doesn't need touching:** `realtimeSync.ts` (the Supabase broadcast channel itself), `schemaCheck.ts` (real, working, just needs wiring as a precondition once there's a schema to check), and the 113-table registry in `prisma/sync-tables.json` — all confirmed real in the Phase 0 audit and unchanged since.
  - **Sequencing for whoever picks this up:** Phase 5 needs Phase 4's local-database provisioning (binary download + `createdb` + `prisma db push`, also deferred) finished first — there is nothing to test a sync engine against otherwise.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 6: Dropbox/GitHub backup made dormant, zero code change**. Owner decision: keep it off for now, keep the ability to turn it back on for a specific release trivially. [`scripts/counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts)'s `COUNTER_ENV_COPIED` no longer includes the 4 `DROPBOX_*` / 3 `GITHUB_*` keys — commented out in place, not deleted, so re-enabling is a one-line uncomment. `resources/counter.env` now ships **8 keys, down from 15**. Nothing else changed: `backupAvailability()` already reported `canBackup`/`canRestore` from key presence alone, and every backup/restore route already rendered the counter-only 503 — that design (from the 0.1.0 hardening pass) was already the mechanism this needed. Verified: regenerated `resources/counter.env`, re-ran `verify:package` against the already-packaged tree from Phase 4 — **PASS**, 8 keys reported.

- **2026-08-25 — 0.3.0: popup fix verified in a real packaged build, version-stamped title bar, and an installer regression found and fixed the same day**. Owner asked to test the previous day's popup/multi-window fix through an actual `win-unpacked` build before trusting it on a counter, plus three smaller asks raised in passing.
  - **Popup fix confirmed live, not just in a harness.** Built `dist-app/win-unpacked` at 0.3.0, launched it, logged in, and clicked a voucher/report action — a real second window opened. The previous session's Node-harness verification of `setWindowOpenHandler`'s logic held up under the actual packaged app.
  - **Title bar shows the version — needed a second fix beyond the obvious one.** Setting `title: `AbeerSuite ${app.getVersion()}``on the`BrowserWindow`constructor was not enough: Electron syncs the window title to`document.title`on every navigation by default, and the splash page, boot-error page, and every real app page each carry their own`<title>`, silently overwriting it the instant any of them loaded. Fixed by adding `win.on("page-title-updated", (e) => e.preventDefault())`in`attachWindowOpenPolicy()` (`electron/main.ts`), which now applies to every window since that function already runs for the main window and every child.
  - **Installer switched from one-click to assisted, for a real percentage + status text.** Owner wanted the progress bar to show "% too and echo current status" rather than the old one-click indeterminate marquee. `package.json`'s `build.nsis` gained `oneClick: false`, `allowElevation: true`, `installerHeaderIcon`. Electron-builder's stock assisted NSIS install page provides this natively — no custom NSIS scripting, which could not have been visually verified in this environment anyway.
  - **A real regression, caught by the owner on the actual installer, not by any automated check.** `runAfterFinish: true` was added alongside the assisted-mode switch to keep the flow to one added click. In practice it auto-launched the app the instant NSIS finished — the worst possible moment on a fresh `Program Files` install, with Windows Defender's real-time scanner most likely still touching the freshly-written 468 MB / 7,790 files. First launch missed `serverManager.ts`'s readiness window and hit the boot-error page; a relaunch a short while later (Defender settled) succeeded in 10-15s. The existing boot-error page (from the 2026-08-24 Phase 1 work) did its job — a real, actionable error, not a silent blank window — but a new counter's first-ever impression being an error page is a bad trade for one click. Fixed by setting `runAfterFinish: false` explicitly — electron-builder defaults it to `true` when unset (`options.runAfterFinish !== false`), so merely omitting the key would not have changed anything, confirmed by reading `CommonWindowsInstallerConfiguration.js` in `node_modules` before relying on it. _**Reversed on 2026-09-06** at the owner's request — setup now launches the app deliberately, and into the tray. See the Recently-shipped entry of that date for why it is safe this time; the short version is that the Defender exclusions added the same week remove the scan that caused this, not that the payload got smaller._
  - **Not yet visually inspected by anyone:** the assisted installer's actual wizard screens (welcome/progress/finish) — confirmed to build correctly (`oneClick=false perMachine=false` in the build log) and to no longer auto-launch, but no one has watched it run start to finish since the `runAfterFinish` fix.
  - **Explicitly not done this session:** publishing `AbeerSuite-Setup-0.3.0.exe` to `abeersuite-networkservice-update` (needed before a counter PC's auto-update can see it) — that touches the real update feed and needs its own go-ahead; the 403/PAT-permission issue from the prior session; the deferred public nsis-web distribution channel.

- **2026-08-25 — New-tab actions work again: the blanket popup deny is now an origin split (this is also multi-window)**. Owner report: "POS is supposed to open in a new tab on web, but after the conversion it is single window — icon click does nothing." That turned out not to be a boot failure at all, and not POS-specific.
  - **One line was breaking ~40 call sites.** [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts)'s `setWindowOpenHandler(() => ({ action: "deny" }))` made every `window.open(url, "_blank")` return `null` and do nothing. The app is multi-tab by design on the web, so this silently disabled: all **21** voucher-creation entries in `AllCreateActionsDialog.tsx`, the shared `ButtonBar.tsx`/`Sidebar.tsx` navigation, every receipt/print page (`QuickTicketButton`, `TicketReopenButton`, `ShippingSlipModal`, and `voucherPrintPageHref` in Branch Transfer / Physical Stock / Manufacturing Journal), the repairing auto-advance `openTabs` in `StatusNav`/`WorkActions`, and the WhatsApp/tracking links in coupons and gift cards. [`posHelp.ts`](src/modules/pos/lib/posHelp.ts) had already predicted the failure mode in a comment — "kiosk browsers, embedded webviews block popups" — Electron was exactly that case.
  - **Split by origin, not allow/deny wholesale.** Same-origin (`http://127.0.0.1:PORT`) targets now open as real `BrowserWindow`s with `overrideBrowserWindowOptions` carrying the _same_ hardening as the main window (preload, `contextIsolation`, `sandbox`, no `nodeIntegration`). Everything else goes to `shell.openExternal`, restricted to `http:`/`https:` so no arbitrary protocol handler reaches the OS shell. The original security concern behind the deny — a foreign origin getting a window with this preload attached — is still enforced; it just no longer takes our own pages down with it. `did-create-window` re-applies the policy to grandchildren.
  - **Multi-window came free, and it is parity rather than a new feature.** The owner wanted side-by-side report comparison. No special-case code exists for it: reports open like everything else. Child windows are ordinary windows — only the tray-backed main window keeps the hide-on-close behaviour, so a closed report window is not left invisible in memory with nothing able to restore it. Duplicate-submit risk is unchanged from the web app, which has always allowed multiple voucher tabs.
  - **`window:control` is sender-based now.** It resolved to `mainWindow` unconditionally, so a titlebar button in any secondary window would have minimised or closed the main one. Dormant today (grep finds no renderer caller) but it becomes a live bug the moment a custom titlebar ships; now uses `BrowserWindow.fromWebContents(event.sender)` like the printer handlers already did.
  - **Verified against real Electron, not just typechecked.** `pnpm electron:compile` and a scoped `eslint` pass both clean. The risky assumption — what the handler actually receives and whether `allow` inherits the overrides — was tested with a throwaway Electron harness: relative targets arrive **already resolved** to absolute; `allow` produces a child with `sandbox=true contextIsolation=true nodeIntegration=false`; `did-create-window` fires; children close for real; `https://wa.me/...` routes to `openExternal`; and `javascript:alert(1)` is neutralised to `about:blank` by Chromium _before_ the handler, then denied with no window created.
  - **Found in passing, not fixed:** auto-update is dead on every counter. `main.log` shows `403 "Resource not accessible by personal access token"` against `abeersuite-networkservice-update` — the fine-grained PAT needs **Contents: Read** granted on that specific repo, and a published release must exist.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 4 (detection half): local Postgres auto-detects for real, binary download and schema apply deliberately deferred**. Implements the achievable, safely-verifiable slice of Phase 4 from the approved plan. Also corrects a factual error from an earlier audit entry this same day.
  - **Correction:** the 2026-08-24 desktop-audit entry above claimed `refreshReplicaReadiness()` has zero callers. That was wrong — `GET /api/counter/status` calls it. It still leaves `replicaReady` permanently `false` today, but because it reads `_sync_status`, a table only the still-scaffold sync worker writes (Phase 5), not because nothing calls it.
  - **The gate is now a real per-machine setting, not a build-time constant.** [`electron/postgresManager.ts`](electron/postgresManager.ts)'s `COUNTER_PC !== "1"` hard gate is replaced by `isReplicaEnabled()` — reads `userData/replica.json`, defaults to enabled (matches the owner's "auto-detect, no prompt" decision), env override kept for dev/testing. No UI writes that file yet; it is the hook for a future "disable local replica" setting, not dead format.
  - **Detection is real now, not a port-listening guess.** Both the installed-on-5432 and embedded-on-54329 paths run an actual `SELECT 1` via `pg` before trusting a connection — `canConnect()`. `pg` was added to `prepare-electron-deps.ts`'s hoisted set (31 packages now, up from 17) since it is a genuine third runtime dependency of the Electron main process. Also fixed the check order: detecting an _existing_ installed PostgreSQL no longer requires the embedded binaries to be bundled at all — previously the whole function returned early on missing `postgres.exe` before ever checking port 5432, so "prefer an installed server" silently never fired.
  - **The missing wire: dynamic `COUNTER_PC` propagation.** Read-routing in `prismaClient.ts`/`counterMode.ts` gates on the `COUNTER_PC` env var, not on `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL`'s presence. `electron/main.ts` now sets `process.env.COUNTER_PC = "1"` from the actual detection result, before forking the Next server (which inherits it via `serverManager.ts`'s `env: {...process.env}`) — without this, a real local database being found would still never activate the read-routing that already exists.
  - **The choice is now visible, not just logged.** `counterStatus()` gained `replicaSource: "installed" | "embedded" | null`, mirrored in [`useCounterMode.ts`](src/shared/hooks/useCounterMode.ts)'s type and surfaced in the "Local copy" badge's tooltip in [`CounterStatusBadge.tsx`](src/shared/components/CounterStatusBadge.tsx).
  - **Deliberately deferred — needs real infrastructure to verify, not more code:** downloading the embedded PostgreSQL binary on first online run, and applying the schema (`createdb` + `prisma db push`) programmatically. Writing that blind, with no internet access to test a real download and no PostgreSQL instance in this environment to push a schema into and confirm, would be shipping untested guesswork as if it worked — the one thing the packaging-hardening pass earlier this same day specifically fixed the _last_ instance of. `resources/pgsql` stays empty; the fallback stays live mode.
  - **Verified live.** Full `pnpm electron:pack` rebuild, `verify:package` PASS, `resources/` 468.3 MB (vs Phase 3's 467.9 MB — the ~0.4 MB `pg` addition, as expected). Launched the packaged app on the baked `counter.env` alone: `main.log` shows the detection logic running and logging correctly — "No installed PostgreSQL found and no embedded binaries... staying in live mode" — proving `require("pg")` did not crash the main process, which was the real risk in this change. First launch attempt hit the Phase 1 boot-error page (`NextServer` didn't answer inside the 15s poll, likely system load from the sequential builds); a second launch immediately after succeeded cleanly, confirming it was transient and not a regression. Clean shutdown, `.env` override restored byte-identical.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 3 done (mostly): installer resources cut 67%, 1,436.0 MB -> 467.9 MB, measured**. Implements the size/speed half of Phase 3 from the approved plan; the `nsis-web` target is deferred (needs a hosting decision, below).
  - **The real duplication, found and fixed.** [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts) has exactly two runtime `require`s outside Node builtins: `dotenv` and `electron-updater`. Everything else in package.json's "dependencies" — Next, React, Prisma, Dexie — belongs to the web app, which already ships its own complete `resources/standalone/node_modules`. electron-builder's default packaging computes the closure of ALL of "dependencies" regardless, so the whole project's node_modules was being bundled a **second time** — that was the 646 MB `app.asar.unpacked/node_modules` from the Phase 0.5 audit. New [`scripts/prepare-electron-deps.ts`](scripts/prepare-electron-deps.ts) hoists just the two real deps (+ 15 transitive) into `dist-electron/node_modules`; `package.json`'s `files` now excludes the top-level `node_modules` entirely (`!node_modules/**/*`, `!**/node_modules/**/*`) with a targeted re-include (`dist-electron/node_modules/**/*`) so the exclusion doesn't also catch the one tree that's supposed to survive.
  - **The standalone tree lost its dead weight too.** New `denylist` predicate in [`scripts/lib/hoistNodeModules.ts`](scripts/lib/hoistNodeModules.ts) — factored out of `prepare-standalone.ts` so the two callers share the same tested pnpm-symlink walk rather than duplicating it — prunes `prisma` (the CLI), `typescript`, `playwright`/`playwright-core`, `fast-check`, and every `@babel/*` package: reachable through the pnpm store walk, never `require()`d by `server.js`. A `stripSuffixes` option also drops `.d.ts`/`.map` from what remains. Measured result: **172 packages hoisted before → 47 after** in the standalone tree.
  - **Guardrails so this can't regress silently.** [`scripts/verify-package.ts`](scripts/verify-package.ts) gained an 800 MB `resources/` size ceiling (new Check A4) — a duplicated node_modules payload creeping back in now fails the build instead of just growing quietly. `compression: "maximum"` and `electronLanguages: ["en-US"]` added to the electron-builder config.
  - **Verified live, not just measured.** Full `pnpm electron:pack` run: `verify:package` **PASS**, `resources/ is 467.9 MB` (the new check's own log line). Launched the packaged app on the baked `counter.env` alone (temporarily renamed the `%APPDATA%` override aside, same method as the Phase 0.5 baseline): boots, splash shows progress ("Starting database…" → "Starting server…" → "Almost there…"), lands on `/staff/login` (HTTP 200), the new `app:boot-progress` IPC and `main.log` file both worked exactly as designed with **no spurious errors** this run (the Phase 1 `ERR_ABORTED` cosmetic-log fix held), auto-updater correctly logged "GH_UPDATE_TOKEN not set — skipping" rather than failing, and shutdown left no orphan process. `.env` override restored byte-identical afterward.
  - **Not done — needs an answer, not code:** the `nsis-web` target requires a stable HTTPS `appPackageUrl` hosting the payload. GitHub Releases on this repo is the candidate (the `publish` block already points there for auto-update), but it needs the owner to confirm before wiring `win.target` to include it.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 2 done: counter machine identity moved off the bridge's random UUID onto Electron's own installation id**. Implements Phase 2 of the approved plan (`~/.claude/plans/127-0-0-1-3000-use-different-port-encapsulated-allen.md`) — bridge retirement stays step-by-step; only identity moved, printing and the QR display are untouched.
  - **New: [`src/shared/lib/counter/identity.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/identity.ts).** `resolveMachineIdentity()` returns `{ machineId, legacyDeviceId, deviceLabel }` — on Electron, `machineId` is the existing `COUNTER-<uuid>` installation id (`app:get-counter-id`, previously only consumed by `realtimeSync.ts`), and `legacyDeviceId` is read (never minted) from the bridge purely to spot a pre-existing binding. Off Electron, behaviour is unchanged: the bridge's id is minted on first use, same as before. `matchesThisMachine()` centralises the "does this bound id belong to me" check.
  - **All three real call sites now use it.** [`ShiftOpenDialog.tsx`](src/modules/pos/components/ShiftOpenDialog.tsx) (the picker lock + submit payload), [`useMyCounterExtension.ts`](src/modules/pos/hooks/useMyCounterExtension.ts) (the call-follow-up "which counter is this PC" lookup), and printer-settings' parked "Counter lock" card — which turned out to be **silently dead on every Electron machine already**: `checkRelay()` short-circuits before ever fetching the bridge's `/config`, so `config.deviceId` was permanently `null` there. Fixed for free by resolving identity independently of that fetch.
  - **Migration, not a hard cutover.** `POST /api/pos/shifts`'s `openSchema` gained `legacyDeviceId` (optional, alongside `deviceId`). The lock check now matches against **either** id; if a counter is bound to the legacy one but the request presents the canonical Electron id, it silently **rebinds** rather than refuses — a machine that already opened shifts under the old bridge UUID keeps working with no manual step. `epbxExtension` and the voucher-prefix logic in `voucherRef.ts` were not touched — the counter number was never the thing that needed fixing.
  - **Verification.** `pnpm electron:compile` clean, a scoped `eslint` pass over all five changed files clean. A full-project `tsc --noEmit` was attempted twice (default heap, then `--max-old-space-size=6144`) and both times the process grew past 3GB and either OOM'd or plateaued in GC-thrashing without finishing — an environment resource ceiling on this large a project, not a signal about the change itself. Falling back to the scoped lint pass plus manual line-by-line review of all five files as the verification record for this commit.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 1 done: cold-start splash, boot-failure page, and the wrong-app-in-the-window bug removed**. Implements the remaining part of Phase 1 from the approved plan (`~/.claude/plans/127-0-0-1-3000-use-different-port-encapsulated-allen.md`) — the port/LAN half already shipped separately the same day.
  - **The window is never blank now.** [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts) creates the `BrowserWindow` with `show:false` and a matching `backgroundColor`, loads a self-authored `data:` URL splash immediately, and only calls `.show()` on `ready-to-show` — so the OS never paints a default white frame while Postgres and the server are still starting. Boot progress ("Starting database…", "Starting server…") is pushed to the splash over a new IPC channel, `app:boot-progress`, added to `preload.ts` and `ElectronAPI` the same way `app:update-ready` already works.
  - **A real failure now looks like a failure.** `startNextServer()` in [`serverManager.ts`](electron/serverManager.ts) returns `{ url, ready }` instead of always returning a URL — the old code warned and returned success-shaped output on a 15-second readiness-poll timeout, which is exactly what produced the permanently-blank window. `loadApp()` now retries the real navigation up to 3 times before falling through to `showBootError()`, an error page naming the exact log file to check.
  - **A log file exists for the first time.** Session-scoped, truncated fresh on every launch (`%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/logs/main.log`) — `console.log/warn/error` are wrapped once at the top of `main.ts` to tee into it. Exists specifically so the new error page has something concrete to point at; not an audit trail, so it does not grow unbounded on a counter that runs for weeks.
  - **The wrong-app-in-the-window bug is gone.** Deleted the "reuse an existing listener" branch in `startNextServer()` — it used to adopt _any_ process already on the port, including a stray `next dev` left running on the same machine, and load that foreign app into the POS window. Single-instance locking already prevents two copies of this app racing each other; a genuinely stuck port (an orphaned process from an unclean exit) now correctly fails the readiness poll and shows the error page instead of silently succeeding.
  - **Verified live, not just typechecked.** Ran `pnpm electron:dev` end to end: the boot-progress messages appeared in order, and the window's title changed from the splash to the real login page title — confirmed via the OS window title, since Turbopack's cold compile of this app's `/staff/login` route took **30–40 seconds per request** in this environment (a `proxy.ts` step alone took ~14–20s), an order of magnitude past both the old 15s readiness poll and the original retry backoff. One real bug surfaced by that slow compile and fixed: the splash's own in-flight `loadURL` gets aborted (`ERR_ABORTED`) the instant `loadApp()` supersedes it with the real page — a benign race, but it was logging as `console.error`, which would have been a false alarm in the new log file on every single launch. Now silently ignored, kept for any other failure code.

- **2026-08-24 — Per-counter port + LAN access, and GitHub Releases auto-update**. Owner direction: LAN exposure is acceptable now (it enables browsing a counter from another PC), and each counter's port should come from its EPBX extension so counters on the same shop LAN never collide.
  - **Port scheme.** [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts) `resolvePort()` — `3000 + COUNTER_EPBX_EXT` when that's a 1-2 digit value (extension "04" -> port 3004), else 3000 unchanged. `COUNTER_EPBX_EXT` is a per-machine value, so it is **not** added to the shared `counter.env` allowlist — it goes in the existing `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/.env` override file, documented in `.env.example`. Dev keeps `PORT=3000` via `package.json` and is unaffected (`process.env.PORT` still wins first).
  - **LAN bind — deliberately reopens the earlier fix.** [`serverManager.ts`](electron/serverManager.ts) now sets `HOSTNAME: "0.0.0.0"`, reversing the `127.0.0.1`-only change from the 2026-08-24 distribution-hardening pass that was written specifically to stop the POS being reachable by anyone on the shop wifi. Staff login is still required at the app layer, but network-layer exposure is back — direct API hits from anywhere on the wifi are possible again. Owner-approved trade for being able to open a counter from another PC's browser by its dedicated port.
  - **Auto-update wired to GitHub Releases.** `package.json` gained a `publish` block (`provider: "github"`, owner `devloperakramul`, repo `abeersuite-electron`, `private: true`) so `electron-builder` generates real `latest.yml`/`app-update.yml` — fixing the stale-metadata defect found in the Phase 0.5 baseline. The repo is **private**, so update _checks_ need their own credential: `setupAutoUpdater()` in `main.ts` now calls `autoUpdater.setFeedURL()` with a `GH_UPDATE_TOKEN` (a fine-grained PAT scoped to `Contents: Read-only` on this one repo) read the same per-machine way as `COUNTER_EPBX_EXT`. No token, no update checks — the app just keeps running what it has; this was chosen over baking a shared token into every installer's `counter.env`, consistent with the existing "counter gets what a counter uses, nothing else" allowlist discipline.
  - **Still open:** `app:update-ready` has no renderer listener — an update downloads and installs silently on quit with no visible prompt. Not touched in this change; still tracked in the roadmap's capability table.

- **2026-08-24 — Phase 0.5 baseline build and smoke test (green, with two real defects found)**. Built `pnpm electron:dist` off the current `dev` tree and exercised the packaged app from `dist-app/win-unpacked` before changing anything, so later phases have something to be compared against.
  - **What passed.** Build green, `verify:package` **PASS** (7,789 app files scanned, 34 secret values checked, 15 allowlisted keys in `resources/counter.env`). The packaged app boots, serves `/staff/login` **HTTP 200**, and listens on **`127.0.0.1:3000` only** — the LAN-exposure fix holds in the shipped artifact. Shutdown is clean: no orphan process, port released.
  - **The unattended path is real, and was tested properly.** The first launch was invalid as a test — this machine has a hand-placed `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/.env` (2,906 bytes) which by design **overrides** the baked `counter.env`, so it proved nothing about a shipped counter. Backed it up, renamed it aside, relaunched: the app boots on `resources/counter.env` **alone**, serves login 200, and **generates `auth-secret.json` (66 bytes) on that run** — confirming the per-machine `AUTH_SECRET` path fires only when nothing else supplies one. File restored byte-identical afterwards. Ports 9191 and 5432 were both free during the test, so this was also an honest proxy for "no bridge, no local Postgres".
  - **Defect — `latest.yml` is stale and auto-update cannot work.** `build.publish` is **`null`** and `package.json` has **no `repository` field**, so electron-builder generates no update metadata at all. The `latest.yml` sitting in `dist-app/` is a leftover from an older run (releaseDate `2026-08-23T22:33`) recording `size: 365357460` and a sha512 for an exe that no longer exists — the freshly built one is **433,016,925 bytes**. `setupAutoUpdater()` runs on every launch against a feed that does not exist, and anyone uploading that stale `latest.yml` beside the new installer would ship an update electron-updater refuses on hash mismatch. Fold into Phase 3 with the `nsis-web`/`differentialPackage` work, since all of it needs the same `publish` block.
  - **Unexplained — the installer grew 68 MB (+18.6%) on an identical payload.** `resources/` measures **1,436.0 MB across 58,429 files**, byte-for-byte the same as the pre-build measurement (standalone 781.6 MB / 27,058 files; `app.asar.unpacked` 646.4 MB / 31,368 files), yet the exe went 365 MB → 433 MB. Compression is `normal` in both runs. Not chased further: Phase 3 deletes the 646 MB `asarUnpack` payload outright and re-measures, which either removes the question or makes it obvious.
  - **Not covered by this test, still needs a real counter PC:** window rendering (verified only that a window exists and the HTTP layer answers), actual staff login, thermal printing and the QR display (no bridge installed here), and install time (the `.exe` was never run — the unpacked build was launched directly).

- **2026-08-24 — Desktop app re-planned properly (audit + approved phase plan)**. The Electron conversion was a trial with no stated goal. An audit of what actually ships found the docs describing a three-tier offline-first app that **does not exist**, so the roadmap and this section are now corrected. Approved plan: `~/.claude/plans/127-0-0-1-3000-use-different-port-encapsulated-allen.md`; the rewritten status table lives in [`docs/electron-offline-sync-roadmap.md`](docs/electron-offline-sync-roadmap.md).
  - **What v0.1.0 honestly is:** a hardened kiosk shell that boots the standalone Next server on loopback, plus thermal printing and the customer QR display through an **external bridge service on `:9191`**. It reads and writes **live Supabase**, exactly like the web app. The packaging layer is genuinely solid; the product layer was never planned.
  - **There is no local database on any counter, twice over.** `resources/pgsql` is **empty (0 files)**, so the `extraResources` entry produces nothing — and `COUNTER_PC` is not in `COUNTER_ENV_COPIED` ([`scripts/counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts)), so `ensurePostgresRunning()` returns `null` at the first line of its body on every shipped machine. The live/local routing in `prismaClient.ts` is real and well-built, but `localUrl` is never set, so `prisma` _is_ `prismaLive` with zero overhead. Nothing was broken by this; it simply was not the feature the docs claimed.
  - **The sync engine is a scaffold, not an implementation.** `syncTableByNumber()` writes an in-memory watermark and returns `rowsSynced: 0` **without querying anything**; `flushOutboxQueue()` broadcasts an event but **never writes to Supabase**, and its queue is a plain array lost on restart, so enqueued mutations are silently discarded. `schemaCheck.ts` is real, working, and has **zero callers**. Exactly one file outside the folder touches any of it — `useRealtimeTopics.ts`, for the realtime subscription. `realtimeSync.ts` is the one file in there that genuinely works.
  - **`refreshReplicaReadiness()` has zero callers**, so `replicaReady` is permanently `false` and local reads could only ever have happened through the outage-demotion path.
  - **Three defects worth naming.** `serverManager.ts` adopts **any** listener on port 3000 as its own — a running `next dev` gets loaded into the POS window. There is **no splash and no loading state**, and the window is created even when the 15-second readiness poll **times out**, which is the blank-white-window symptom. And the installer ships `node_modules` **twice**: `asarUnpack` extracts 646 MB of loose files at install time that nothing at runtime ever loads (the server is forked from `resources/standalone`), on top of a 781 MB standalone carrying the `prisma` CLI, `typescript` and `playwright`.
  - **Owner decisions that set the direction.** Bridge retirement is **step by step** — counter identity moves into Electron first, printing and QR keep using `:9191` and are retired one at a time later, so the `serialport` native-module risk is not bundled into the first change. PostgreSQL is **auto-detected at first run with no installer prompt** (the unattended install is preserved) but the app must **display which server it chose**. Releases ship **both** a small `nsis-web` stub and a full offline `.exe`. Local database readiness is **lazy** — prepared in the background on first connectivity, never blocking a cashier.
  - **Counter identity, corrected.** The counter number is **not** random: `PosCounter.epbxExtension` is admin-edited and already supplies the voucher prefix, and it is not changing. What moves is only the _machine binding_ — currently a UUID minted by the **renderer** and stored in the bridge's config — onto Electron's own installation id, with the PC remembering its counter id and extension so staff see "Ext 04" instead of a UUID. Machines already bound to a bridge deviceId must **rebind rather than be refused**.
  - **Phase order:** docs honest → **build and install on a clean second machine first** (baseline, surfaces unknown errors before five phases of changes) → port + cold-start UX → counter identity → installer size → local Postgres → real sync engine → Dropbox dormant. Throughout: the web app stays the default path and every desktop capability degrades to today’s behaviour when absent.
  - **Still open:** the ~60 credentials in the two already-built 0.1.0 installers need rotating regardless.

- **2026-08-24 — Unattended counter install (credentials ship on purpose, in one allowlisted file)**. Owner decision: **this variant is internal only — company-owned counters, staff-operated, never sold and never handed to a customer.** That changes what "no secrets in the installer" is protecting against, so installation is now **100% unattended**: no `.env` to place, no secret to generate, no step for whoever sets up the PC.
  - **Why the earlier "ship the Supabase URL + publishable key" idea could not achieve it.** Measured, not assumed: `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is **inlined into the client bundle at build time**, so `supabase.co` and the publishable JWT already appear in the 0.1.0 chunks — shipping them "deliberately" is a no-op. And they cannot serve the data path: `@supabase/supabase-js` is imported in exactly **two** files, both realtime-only ([`supabaseClient.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/supabaseClient.ts), [`serverPublish.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/serverPublish.ts), which swallows its own errors by design). Every read and write goes through **Prisma over `DATABASE_URL`**, which carries the database password. So `DATABASE_URL` was the only thing standing between the operator step and an unattended install — nothing else would have removed it.
  - **The contract is a reviewed allowlist, not "whatever is in `.env`".** [`scripts/counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts) is the single source both sides import; [`scripts/generate-counter-env.ts`](scripts/generate-counter-env.ts) writes `resources/counter.env` during `electron:build` and electron-builder ships it as `extraResources`. **15 keys, 1.5 KB** — `DATABASE_URL`, the three `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*`, `INTERNAL_STAFF_EMAIL_DOMAINS`, `APP_MODE`/`READ_MODE`, the four `DROPBOX_*`, the three `GITHUB_*`, and `AUTH_TRUST_HOST` — against the **~60 keys / 11.6 KB the 0.1.0 build shipped by accident**. Deliberately excluded: `DIRECT_URL` (only seeds and maintenance scripts read it), `SUPER_ADMIN_PASSWORD` (a full-authority login in plaintext; it stays a per-machine recovery step), and payments / Cloudinary / Firebase / Meta WA / Resend / Sentry — no counter surface uses any of them, and a credential cannot be un-shipped once installed. The file is **generated, never committed** (`.gitignore` needs it spelled out: `.env*` does **not** match `counter.env`, because that pattern requires the basename to start with `.env`).
  - **`AUTH_TRUST_HOST` and `COMPANY_DOMAIN` are not in the build machine's `.env`.** A pure copy-from-`.env` generator would therefore have produced a build with no `AUTH_TRUST_HOST` — which next-auth v5 requires on a host it cannot verify, and the counter serves itself on `127.0.0.1:3000`, not Vercel. Hence the split: `COUNTER_ENV_COPIED` (from `.env`, falling back to `process.env` so CI can inject) vs `COUNTER_ENV_CONSTANTS` (written, identical on every counter).
  - **`AUTH_SECRET` is generated per machine, not baked** (superseded for `WRITE_MODE=v_live` builds on 2026-09-07 — see that entry; every other build still generates its own). First run writes `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/auth-secret.json` (`randomBytes(32)`), beside the installation-ID file so an upgrade does not sign everybody out. Strictly better than a shipped constant at zero operational cost: each counter runs its **own** Next server and the window loads `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, so session cookies never cross machines and there is nothing to share — a secret read off one till forges nothing on another. It also deletes the last operator step **and** the `AUTH_SECRET=your-auth-secret-here` hole, instead of documenting it harder.
  - **The hand-placed `.env` became an override rather than a requirement.** `main.ts` loads `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/.env` **first** and the baked `resources/counter.env` **second** — dotenv never overwrites an already-set variable, so ordering alone makes the per-machine file win, for free. Point one counter at a test database without a rebuild; delete the file to go back. [`.env.example`](.env.example) is rewritten as that optional override and warns that a key set blank here **shadows** the good baked value rather than falling back to it. The `REQUIRED_ENV` preflight stays for exactly that case.
  - **The packaging gate was inverted, and is stronger for it.** `verify-package.ts`'s old rule — _no credentials anywhere_ — would now fail every build (`.env` is in `SCANNABLE_EXTENSIONS`, so `counter.env` gets value-scanned). New check **B2**: the file exists, holds every key a counter cannot start without, and holds **nothing outside the allowlist**; `resources/counter.env` is excluded from the value scan (**B3**) so B3 keeps catching those same values leaking into any _other_ file. An allowlist, not a denylist of known-bad names, because the 0.1.0 failure was a key nobody had thought about — adding a service to `.env` must not silently add it to every counter. Both directions proven: absent file → FATAL, injected `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` → FATAL naming the key. Only key _names_ are ever printed. The duplicated `parseEnv` is gone; both scripts share `parseEnvFile`.
  - **A latent bug the parser fix caught before it shipped.** dotenv strips an inline `# comment` from an **unquoted** value but keeps everything inside quotes — and the generator quotes every value (so a `#` in `DATABASE_URL`'s query string cannot truncate it). `.env` really does write `DROPBOX_FOLDER=name        # empty = app-folder root`, so the naive parse baked the English sentence into the folder name: **74 chars instead of 19**, on every counter, and backup listing would have pointed at a folder that does not exist with nothing in the logs naming why. `parseEnvFile` now follows dotenv's own rule. Same fix makes B3 honest — searching the package for a secret with a comment glued to it would never match, and the gate would have reported PASS over a real leak.
  - **Backup/restore now works on a counter — and is no longer reachable by every cashier.** Both need shipped keys because the dump is made and applied by a **GitHub Actions runner** (the only place `pg_dump`/`pg_restore` exist); the app only dispatches the run and lists Dropbox for the restore picker. Hardened first: the Dropbox app is **App-folder scoped** and the PAT is limited to **one repo**. But all three routes were gated by `requireAuth()`, which is **not a permission check** — it only rejects unauthenticated users and portal customers. Shipping a working GitHub token to every till would have made `POST /api/settings/restore` with `{"confirm":"RESTORE","path":"…"}` a **full production database rollback available to any cashier from devtools**, with the settings panel never opening. All three now use `requirePermission(PERMISSIONS.SETTINGS_MANAGE)` — which is what the nav entry already required, so this only makes the server agree with the UI. `settings:manage` already exists in `permissions.ts` and `codeOwnedCatalog.ts`: **no migration, no seed run.**
  - **"Not configured here" is a state, not a crash.** The generator names any key it skipped, so a build can legitimately ship without the Dropbox/GitHub half. New [`availability.ts`](src/shared/lib/backup/availability.ts) answers that from key presence alone (no network call); the list route returns it alongside the backups it already fetches — one request, not two — and `BackupRestorePanel` disables what cannot work and names the missing **keys**. Previously that absence surfaced as `DROPBOX_APP_KEY is not set.` in a red toast: a developer's error message shown to a staff member who cannot act on a key name.
  - **Still open:** the ~60 credentials from the two already-built 0.1.0 installers must be **rotated** regardless — internal-only changes who may hold them, not the fact that a plaintext copy left this machine. Recommended, owner's call, no code: give counters a **dedicated Postgres role** instead of `postgres`, granted only the app's tables — same unattended install, swap the string and rebuild.

- **2026-08-24 — Desktop distribution hardening (installer made shippable)**. The 0.1.0 Windows installer built, but an audit of the _packaged_ artifact found one hard launch blocker, one credential leak, and one network-exposure problem. All three are fixed; the fixes are all in the build/shell layer, no app code changed. Approved plan: `~/.claude/plans/lest-fix-step-by-fluttering-catmull.md`.
  - **Launch blocker — `standalone/node_modules` never reached the package.** `next build --standalone` writes `.next/standalone/node_modules/*` as **absolute symlinks into the pnpm store**, and electron-builder 26 does not dereference symlinks when copying `extraResources` — so the installed `resources/standalone/` had no `node_modules` at all, `server.js`'s `require('next')` failed, the Next server never booted, and the window showed `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`. Two independent causes, and fixing only the first is what made this look done when it wasn't:
    1. **The symlinks.** [`scripts/prepare-standalone.ts`](scripts/prepare-standalone.ts) now runs a `hoistStandaloneDeps()` BFS that `realpathSync`es each symlink and copies the **real** package into a flat `standalone/node_modules/<name>/`. The subtlety that makes or breaks it: pnpm keeps a package's dependencies as **siblings** of the real package (`.pnpm/pg@8.20.0/node_modules/{pg, pg-pool → …}`), not nested inside it, so the dep list of a resolved package is the `node_modules` folder that _contains_ it — `containingNodeModules()`. Without that the walk finds 5 packages; with it, 76. A `visited` set terminates on the store's cyclic graph.
    2. **electron-builder drops it anyway.** `app-builder-lib/out/util/filter.js` rejects any entry whose path relative to an `extraResources` root is exactly `node_modules` — **hard-coded, whatever filter patterns you pass** (`createFilter` is what `copyFiles` uses for extraResources, `fileMatcher.js:289`). The source tree was complete and correct; the copy silently left it behind. Fix: copy `.next/standalone/node_modules` as its **own** `extraResources` entry, where no child of the root trips that rule.
       `NODE_PATH` could never have fixed either problem and the dead block in `serverManager.ts` is gone: once `next` loads, _its_ requires resolve relative to `next`'s location.
  - **The build now fails loudly instead of shipping a blank window.** `prepare-standalone.ts` asserts the standalone folder on disk is complete (`next`, `react`, `react-dom`, plus `@next/env` and `styled-jsx` — reachable only through next's own dep list, so they are the canaries proving the transitive walk ran). That was not enough on its own, because everything downstream succeeded regardless: the new [`scripts/verify-package.ts`](scripts/verify-package.ts) gate (`pnpm verify:package`, wired into `electron:pack` / `electron:dist`) checks the **packaged** tree — `server.js` present, `resources/standalone/node_modules` present, each required package real and not a symlink — so a regression in a hard-coded electron-builder rule cannot reach a shop PC again.
  - **Secrets no longer ship.** The installer was carrying `resources/standalone/.env` — **11.6 KB, ~60 keys in plaintext, outside the asar**, readable by any customer. Three independent guards now: the `extraResources` filters exclude `.env` / `.env.*`; `prepare-standalone.ts` unlinks any `.env*` from the standalone output after the copy pass; and `verify-package.ts` reads the developer's own `.env`, drops `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` / known non-secret keys / short values, then greps the packaged tree for those literal **values** — reporting only key _names_, never values. It also flags any `.env*` on disk or inside an asar (parsed from the asar header, so a 646 MB archive is checked cheaply). **Superseded the same day** — see the unattended-install entry above: a reviewed 15-key `resources/counter.env` now ships on purpose, and the gate asserts _that exact list and nothing else_ instead of "no credentials". The `.env*` sweep and the value scan both survive unchanged; only `resources/counter.env` is exempt from the value scan.
  - _(Renamed since: the template is `.env.example`; `.env.counter.example` was retired in `ac33dd33`. The links below point at the current file.)_
  - **Env contract — operator drops one file per machine** (**superseded the same day**: that file is now an optional per-machine _override_, and a build with no file at all starts fine — see the unattended-install entry above). No bundled fallback: [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts) loads `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/.env` only, and if it is absent shows `dialog.showErrorBox` naming the exact path and [`.env.example`](.env.example), then `app.exit(1)`. Failing visibly beats booting against whatever environment happens to be present. The forked Next server inherits `process.env`, so loading it once in the main process configures the whole app — still true, and it is why baking one file covers the server half too. **The folder is `abeersuite-electron`, not `AbeerSuite`** — `app.getPath("userData")` is built from `app.getName()`, which reads package.json `"name"`, not `build.productName`. Trust the path in the dialog over any path written in prose. (Renaming it via `app.setName()` was considered and rejected: it would move userData, orphaning the `.env` already placed on the counter and regenerating `counter-installation-id.json`.)
  - **v1 ships in live mode; the replica stays dormant.** [`electron/postgresManager.ts`](electron/postgresManager.ts) returns `null` unless `COUNTER_PC=1` **and** the bundled binaries exist (`resources/pgsql` is still empty), and no longer invents `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL` on the not-found path. That last bit is a correctness fix, not tidiness: `reportLiveDown()` demotes to `LOCAL` on a transient Supabase error and `shouldReadLocal()` then returns `true` _regardless of `replicaReady`_, so a bogus `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL` would turn a brief blip into a hard failure against a database that does not exist. Absent, it collapses cleanly to single-client live behaviour.
  - **Stopped listening on the shop LAN.** `serverManager.ts` bound the Next server to `0.0.0.0`, publishing the POS at `http://<counter-ip>:3000` to anyone on the wifi, unauthenticated at the network layer. Now `127.0.0.1` — only this Electron window needs to reach it.
  - **Ship-quality basics.** Single-instance lock (`requestSingleInstanceLock` + `second-instance` focus) so a second launch cannot start a rival server/Postgres against the same port and pgdata; renderer moved to `sandbox: true` (the preload only uses `contextBridge`/`ipcRenderer`/`process.platform`, all of which a sandboxed preload still provides) plus `setWindowOpenHandler` denying popups; `artifactName: "${productName}-Setup-${version}.${ext}"` so the artifact filename matches what `latest.yml` points at (the updater was pointing at dashes while the file had spaces); and a committed `build/icon.png` (`pnpm icon:build` → [`scripts/make-icon.js`](scripts/make-icon.js), a Chromium-canvas rasteriser — `public/logo.svg` is a 3.4:1 wordmark, unusable as a square icon). `.gitignore` had `/build` from a Next/CRA leftover, which silently hid the icon and would have made any other build machine fall back to the default Electron icon — now `/build/*` + `!/build/icon.png`.
  - **Removed `sync:trigger`.** The IPC handler returned `{success:true}` without syncing anything and had **zero callers** in `src/`; dropped from `main.ts`, `preload.ts` and the `ElectronAPI` type rather than kept as a stub the UI could believe.
  - **Verified end to end on 2026-08-24.** A full `electron-builder` run produced `AbeerSuite-Setup-0.1.0.exe` (365 MB — the installer grew ~110 MB because `standalone/` is now self-contained at 250 MB) with `latest.yml` pointing at that exact filename, `build/icon.png` rasterised to `icon.ico`, `resources/standalone/node_modules` byte-for-byte matching the source listing (55 top-level entries, 76 packages, zero symlinks), no `.env*` anywhere on disk or inside the asar, and `pnpm verify:package` passing. One gate refinement fell out of it: `NEXTAUTH_URL` / `AUTH_URL` joined the non-secret list — `http://localhost:3000` carries no credentials and next-auth bakes the callback origin into its server chunks, so the value scan flagged it. They are exempted **by name**, not by a "looks like a URL" rule, because `DATABASE_URL` and `DIRECT_URL` also end in `_URL` and do carry a password. **Correction (same day):** the "zero symlinks" in that check covered `standalone/node_modules` only — a second tree of them shipped unnoticed, see the next bullet.
  - **A second symlink tree, in a directory nobody was walking — and why the _packaged_ app looked fine.** With the fixes above installed, the app still answered `Internal Server Error` on every page. `standalone/node_modules` is not the only place `next build` puts dependencies: packages it externalises for the server also go to `.next/standalone/.next/node_modules/<pkg>-<hash>/` (`@prisma/client-403e33a7f81c97d6`, `pg-…`, two `import-in-the-middle-…`, `require-in-the-middle-…`), and under pnpm those are absolute symlinks into the store as well — in a folder `hoistStandaloneDeps()` never walked. The bug hid because **electron-builder ships the links as links while the NSIS installer dereferences them on install**: the `--dir` package was broken while the installed tree looked fine, which is the opposite of where you would look. Materialising them is not sufficient either — `@prisma/client` declares exactly one dependency, `@prisma/client-runtime-utils`, and pnpm keeps it as a **sibling** in the store, so the copied package's first require threw `Cannot find module` out of `instrumentation.js` at server start. `dereferenceServerExternals()` now replaces each link with a real directory **and returns the store folder that contained it**, seeding the hoist walk so those siblings land in the flat `standalone/node_modules` where Node's upward resolution finds them from the hashed folder. Confirmed by hand first: copying that one 225 KB package into the installed app turned `/staff/login` from 500 into 200.
  - **The gate that passed a broken package, fixed.** `verify:package` reported PASS on that tree, and failed twice over: it only ever lstat'd six named packages inside `standalone/node_modules`, and its walker **skipped symlinks entirely** — a `Dirent` does not stat its target, so a symlinked _directory_ is neither `isDirectory()` nor `isFile()`. Both holes are now closed by checks that do not depend on guessing the next directory Next invents. **A2** fails on _any_ symlink anywhere in the package, printing each link's raw target. **A3** walks the dependency closure of the externalised packages, resolving every declared name the way Node does — up the `node_modules` chain from the requiring package's own directory. Run against the old 0.1.0 package they report the five links plus four unresolvable dependencies, three of which hand-inspection had missed (`import-in-the-middle` also needs `acorn`, `acorn-import-attributes`, `cjs-module-lexer`). A3 is deliberately rooted at `.next/node_modules` rather than the whole flat tree: Next traces per-file, so a hoisted package can legitimately arrive without a declared dependency no reachable code path requires (`next` declares `postcss`, needed only at build time) — and a gate that cries wolf gets bypassed. `prepare-standalone.ts` carries the same whole-tree symlink sweep on the build side, so the same fault is named minutes earlier.
  - **Staff login refused every address, and nothing said why.** With the server finally booting, `admin@networkservice.in` was rejected as "Invalid email or password" whatever was typed. The password was never examined: [`isStaffEmailDomainAllowed()`](src/shared/lib/auth/staffEmailAllowlist.ts) fails **closed** when neither `INTERNAL_STAFF_EMAIL_DOMAINS` nor `COMPANY_DOMAIN` is set, and [`authorizeStaff.ts`](src/shared/lib/auth/authorizeStaff.ts) checks it _before_ the `user.findUnique` and the bcrypt compare, while [`staff/login/page.tsx`](src/app/staff/login/page.tsx) maps every `signIn` error to that one string. Cause of the cause: `.env.example` never listed the key, so the counter's `.env` was copied faithfully and came out incomplete — and `.gitignore`'s blanket `.env*` meant that template did not exist in a fresh clone at all, though `main.ts` names it to operators. Three fixes, all aimed at making a repeat self-explaining: the template is now **tracked** (`!.env.example`), lists `INTERNAL_STAFF_EMAIL_DOMAINS` as required, documents `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`SUPER_ADMIN_PASSWORD` as the database-free way back in, and was scrubbed of the **real production Supabase credential it had been holding** behind a comment claiming its values were examples; `main.ts` runs a `REQUIRED_ENV` preflight after loading the file and refuses to start with a dialog naming each missing key **and the symptom it causes**, counting a copied-verbatim placeholder as missing (an `AUTH_SECRET` published in this repo yet signing real session cookies is worse than an absent one); and the allowlist logs one line, once per process, when it is empty.
  - **Still open before wide rollout:** every credential in the two already-built installers on disk must be **rotated** (they were shipped in plaintext); ~~the counter's own `.env` carries `AUTH_SECRET=your-auth-secret-here`~~ — **resolved the same day**: `AUTH_SECRET` is generated per machine on first run, so the placeholder printed in this repository can no longer be signing real session cookies. On the one counter already set up, **delete** that line from `%APPDATA%/abeersuite-electron/.env` rather than replacing it — the override loads first, so a placeholder left there still shadows the generated key, and the preflight still refuses to start on it. Remaining: the update feed repo `devloperakramul/abeersuite-electron` is **private**, so electron-updater 404s until it is public or a token is configured; no code-signing certificate yet, so SmartScreen will warn; and the **clean-profile smoke test** (fresh Windows profile / VM, no repo, no pnpm store, no PostgreSQL — launch, login, thermal print, counter-ID persistence across restart) is the gate that actually proves the build, since every blocker so far came from the packaged environment differing from the dev tree.

- **2026-08-23 — Electron Desktop App, Zero-Egress Local-First Reads, Realtime Delta Sync, Live Reports & Offline Policy Engine**. Converted the application into an Electron-powered desktop experience for shop counters with embedded PostgreSQL replication and complete offline resilience:
  - **Electron Shell & Direct Hardware Integration**: Configured `electron/main.ts` with `/staff/login` startup route, persistent unique counter installation UUID (`AppData/counter-installation-id.json`), direct window controls, silent thermal receipt printing via `window.electronAPI.printSilent()`, and background `autoUpdater` release checks.
  - **Zero-Egress Local-First Reads**: Configured `prismaClient.ts` to automatically execute all read operations (`findMany`, `findUnique`, `count`, etc.) directly on Local PostgreSQL (`localhost:5432/smx`), dropping Supabase read bandwidth consumption to 0 KB and achieving sub-2ms query responses. **Superseded for the shipped installer (2026-08-24):** v1 ships in live mode — the desktop shell only sets `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL` behind a `COUNTER_PC=1` gate once real PostgreSQL binaries are bundled, so this path stays dormant on a stock install.
  - **Event-Driven Selective Delta Sync & Live Report Updates**: Realtime broadcast channel (`abeersuite:sync-events`) emitting `{ n, ts, originId, action, key }` on mutations, triggering targeted delta synchronization for specific table numbers via `prisma/sync-tables.json` (113 models). Connected `useRealtimeTopics` to delta sync broadcasts and local mutation events (`app:live-delta`) so open accountancy reports (Day Book, Trial Balance, Stock Register, Outstandings) live-update in real time without manual refresh or page flicker.
  - **2-Digit Counter Prefix on Voucher References**: Injected the 2-digit counter code / extension from `/office/admin/counters` (e.g. `01`, `02`) into `voucherRef.ts` and `invNextRef()` so all generated POS sales are prefixed with the counter index (`01/POS/26-27/0001`), guaranteeing human-readable, collision-free references across parallel offline counters.
  - **Safe Offline Voucher Engine & Master Data Controls**: Permitted branch-owned retail transactions (`POS`, `RECEIPT`, `POS_ESTIMATE`, `POS_ORDER`, `PosShift`, `StockMovement`) to commit locally and queue in `_sync_outbox` during offline periods, with strict append-only financial guards and a 100% prohibition on offline deletions.
  - **Offline Policies Settings UI**: Built `/office/settings/offline-policies` (`OfflinePoliciesForm.tsx`) with 7 comprehensive categories (~35 granular toggles) covering POS, GL vouchers, trading, inventory, quotations, tickets, and dedicated create vs edit toggles for Products, Accounts, and Taxonomy.

- **2026-08-22 — Offline-first counter PC: local Postgres replica (Phase 0–1 landed, dormant)**. A shop counter gets its own PostgreSQL replica of Supabase plus a local copy of this app at `localhost:3000`. **The driver is egress cost, not outages** — Supabase bills per row read out, so a report opened fifty times is billed fifty times, while a replica pulls each changed row **once** and serves it locally forever. Offline capability is the side benefit. **Acceptance is a measured before/after egress comparison**, not "does it work offline". Approved plan: `~/.claude/plans/after-all-we-are-twinkling-lamport.md`.

  **The mechanism** is one file. [`prismaClient.ts`](src/shared/lib/prisma/prismaClient.ts) now builds `prismaLive` (Supabase) and, only when `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL` is set, a replica client whose extension refuses every non-read op ([`OfflineWriteBlockedError`](src/shared/lib/prisma/offlineError.ts)) — belt to the `SELECT`-only Postgres role's braces. `prisma` becomes a Proxy that picks a client **at property access** and hands back its delegate **raw**. That rawness is the whole design: the obvious per-operation wrapper silently breaks the array form of `$transaction`, which needs genuine `PrismaPromise`s from one client instance — used at [`ledgers/route.ts:191`](src/app/api/accountancy/ledgers/route.ts) (a plain _read_ report) and five other sites, with `$transaction` appearing ~105 times overall. **Consequence, and it is the one that shapes Phase 4:** mode is per-client, not per-operation, so under `READ_MODE=local` a write also reaches the replica and is refused _even when online_. "Reads local, writes live" therefore cannot come from that switch. SUPERSEDED 2026-08-28: `prismaRead` was never adopted (zero consumers) and is deleted; the split is now WRITE_MODE picking the client and READ_MODE redirecting reads off it, so "reads local, writes live" is expressible directly.

  [`counterMode.ts`](src/shared/lib/prisma/counterMode.ts) holds the state, deliberately Prisma-free (prismaClient imports it, so the live probe is injected). Demotion is **failure-driven, not polled** — the request that pays the timeout is the one that reports it, and we only poll while demoted, so healthy operation costs nothing. `replicaReady` gates local reads **independently of `READ_MODE`**: a half-seeded replica does not error, it returns fewer rows, so a report would render successfully with records silently missing — reading as "those were deleted" rather than "not ready yet". Surfacing is one branch in [`handleApiError.ts`](src/shared/lib/api/handleApiError.ts) → `503 { error: "offline_read_only" }`, which covers all 335 routes because every one already funnels through it, plus toasts in [`http.ts`](src/shared/lib/http.ts) and [`SessionWrapper.tsx`](src/shared/components/SessionWrapper.tsx) (bare `axios` does not inherit the `http` instance's interceptors). [`CounterStatusBadge`](src/shared/components/CounterStatusBadge.tsx) renders **nothing** in the healthy case — a permanent green light is chrome people stop seeing within a day, which is when it stops working as a warning.

  **Registry.** [`gen-sync-registry.ts`](scripts/gen-sync-registry.ts) parses `prisma/schema/*.prisma` (Prisma 7's `prisma-client` generator ships no DMMF and `@prisma/internals` isn't a dep) into `prisma/sync-tables.json`: 113 models → 47 `watermark`, 35 `child`, 9 `append`, 22 `full`. Table numbers are **append-only** — derived from sorted position they would renumber on every new model and silently re-point every stored watermark. Two classifier bugs the tooling caught in itself: a **nullable** cascade FK is not a child (`TicketLog.ticketId` is optional because a log can hang off a Person/Company with no ticket — the CRM Reconnect timeline — so child-syncing it would have pulled only ticket-attached notes and never the customer-attached ones), and a **two-parent** join table can't be replaced on one parent's movement. Both now fall through to a safe strategy; `overrides` in the JSON is hand-maintained and survives regeneration.

  **Schema (needs `pnpm prisma db push`).** Five models mutate but had no watermark and no cascade parent, so an edit was invisible to every sync strategy: `Device` ([tickets](prisma/schema/tickets.prisma) — had _no_ timestamp at all), `PosShift` + `PosHandover` ([pos](prisma/schema/pos.prisma) — the close/accept mutations land long after `openedAt`/`initiatedAt`), `CouponRedemption` ([coupons](prisma/schema/coupons.prisma) — a reversal frees a usage slot the counter would keep refusing), `PersonCompany` ([base](prisma/schema/base.prisma) — an unlink would never reach the replica). Each gained `updatedAt DateTime @default(now()) @updatedAt` **and `@@index([updatedAt])`** — without the index, "anything since <ts>?" scans the whole table to answer "none", which is the answer almost every cycle. Both bootstrap seeds now refuse to run on a counter ([`assertNotCounter.ts`](prisma/seeds/lib/assertNotCounter.ts)): the replica's ids must stay byte-identical to Supabase's, and a seed would mint colliding autoincrement ids, silently attaching sessions and ledger lines to the wrong records.

  **Guardrails.** [`check-counter-mode.ts`](scripts/check-counter-mode.ts) — 14 assertions, no database needed, in `precommit` and CI. The load-bearing one: with `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL` unset, `prisma` **is** `prismaLive`, same object, no Proxy — production provably unchanged. (Note: delegate _methods_ can't be identity-compared; Prisma builds a fresh bound function per access, so `c.user.findMany !== c.user.findMany` even on a plain client. The delegate object is the real proof.) `COUNTER_BUILD=1` in [`next.config.ts`](next.config.ts) flips `output: "standalone"`, `images.unoptimized` (with the optimizer on, a Cloudinary `<Image>` makes the _counter's own_ Node server fetch Cloudinary — offline that hangs and holds a connection per image), and skips the Sentry source-map upload so the counter build needs no network. **Nothing changes on Vercel: every new var is counter-only and every branch's unset case is today's behaviour.** Phase 2 (sync worker, `D:\AbeerSuite\tools\counter-sync`), Phase 3 (self-seeding via `pg_dump` from the counter — Postgres ships its own client binaries, so no Dropbox round-trip), Phase 4 (shadow week, then per-route migration), Phase 6 (offline writes: append-only for money, counter-prefixed series + reserved id blocks) still to come.

- **2026-08-22 — Realtime extended to every accountancy report, and to the counter's sync worker.** The 2026-08-19 foundation deliberately stopped at Day Book pending consumption numbers; the local replica changes that calculus, because a signal now triggers a re-read that will increasingly come from local Postgres rather than Supabase. **14 reports wired** (balance-sheet, cash-flow, fund-flow, gst/hsn-summary, gst/liability, ledger-outstandings, optional-vouchers, payables, pending-vouchers, profit-loss, ratio-analysis, receivables, statistics, trial-balance) on topic `["ledger"]` alone — every money-moving path in [`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts)'s RULES emits it, including inventory vouchers and POS sales which add `stock` on top. Six report routes are placeholders or menus with no data fetch and one (`gst/classification`) is a server component, so nothing to wire there. **Day Book uses `syncNow` (a delta); these reports have no delta** — they recompute wholesale — so each `load` gained a `silent` flag and the realtime path calls `load(true)`: a refresh must not blank a report someone is reading. `optional-vouchers` and `pending-vouchers` refresh the view the user is actually on (page, search, filter, and for pending-vouchers only the visible tab), relying on `useRealtimeTopics` always invoking the latest closure. **A regression the new CI typecheck caught immediately:** twelve of these pages pass `load` straight to `onClick={load}`, so React handed its MouseEvent to the new `silent` parameter — making every manual Refresh silent, a button that appears to do nothing. All twelve now use `onClick={() => load()}`. ESLint did not flag it; `tsc` did.

  **Worker side** ([`lib/signal.js`](D:/AbeerSuite/tools/counter-sync/lib/signal.js)): the counter's sync worker subscribes to the same `app:changes` channel, so a purchase posted on Vercel makes the replica sync in ~1s instead of waiting for a timer. Topic→model expansion lives in the worker, never on the wire — the publisher derives its topic from the API path and cannot know which tables a route touched, so putting table names in the payload would mean a hand-maintained path→tables map across 335 routes. An unknown topic syncs everything (over-syncing costs a cycle; under-syncing loses data), and `staleModels()` checks the hand-maintained map against the registry at startup. **The signal stays a hint, never truth** — it is published by a browser interceptor, so scripts, seeds and the GitHub-Actions restore emit nothing; correctness remains with the watermarks and the reconcile pass. Verified live: four signals in quick succession produced one sync carrying the union of their topics (23 models), the 750 ms settle window collapsing what is really one user action.

- **2026-08-19 — App-wide live updates over Supabase Realtime (foundation shipped, Day Book wired)**. One change channel per tab ([`appChannel.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/appChannel.ts)) carrying only `{ topics, at }` — coarse buckets (`ledger`/`stock`/`tickets`/`parties`/`catalogue`/`tasks`/`settings`, see [`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts)) plus a timestamp, never a row. Publishing is automatic: `attachChangePublisher` rides the axios **response** interceptor on both the shared `http` instance and bare global `axios`, so every successful non-GET call to a listed `/api/...` prefix announces itself and no call site has to remember anything. Listening is [`useRealtimeTopics`](src/shared/hooks/useRealtimeTopics.ts) — the sibling of `usePolling`: silent until something moves, nothing runs while the tab is hidden (signals are remembered, flushed on return), one read per burst. **Day Book listens on `ledger`** and now has no interval at all — a voucher posted at another counter appears on its own. A signal calls the new **`syncNow`** (delta via `?since`, folded through the existing `applyDelta`), **not** `reload`: a live update must not throw away the pages someone scrolled through, so the loaded run and the scroll position survive; `reload` stays the **Refresh** button's meaning. Two bugs were found and fixed by testing rather than reading: (1) publishing **raced the channel join** — the publishing tab is normally the one with no listener, so its first publish created the channel and `send()` was dropped before `SUBSCRIBED`; `ensureChannel` now resolves a promise on join and every publish awaits it; (2) `loadMore` carried `loadingMore` in its `useCallback` deps, so its identity changed after every page and the Day Book's `IntersectionObserver` rebuilt — `io.observe()` fires immediately for an already-intersecting element, pulling a redundant extra page. Guarded by a ref now. **Deliberately measuring before scaling:** we run Realtime direct for now and watch consumption; the other registers (Vouchers Register, Account Ledger Explorer, Customer Transactions) and the ticket boards stay unwired until we have numbers. If the plan's connection/message limits bite, the fallback is a **VM relay** — one subscriber (or the VM notified directly at create/edit/delete) fanning the same `{ topics, at }` payload out over our own socket. Because the payload and the listener contract are transport-agnostic, that swap touches `appChannel.ts` only; no screen changes. Also the seam the planned **desktop app** will ride. **Known gap:** writes with no browser behind them (payment webhooks, cron, GitHub-Actions restore) publish nothing and surface on the next manual refresh. **Verified with two live sessions** (2026-08-19): a ₹1 and a ₹2 RECEIPT posted in one tab appeared unaided in the other — 100 → 101 rows, exactly one request carrying `since=`, no follow-up `cursor=` fetch, pages and scroll preserved; plus burst-of-5 → 1 read, wrong-topic → 0 reads, hidden-tab → 0 reads then 1 on return. Both test vouchers hard-deleted afterwards (snapshotted to AuditLog).

**2026-08-19 (same day) — one connection per computer, not per tab.** The first cut gated the socket on `document.hidden`, reasoning a background tab shouldn't pay a heartbeat. Measured against a real multi-tab session it did nothing: [`GlobalInboxListener`](src/modules/message/components/GlobalInboxListener.tsx) (chat) is mounted globally and subscribes to every room, in every tab, permanently and unconditionally — by design, so a message still notifies you on a backgrounded tab — and a heartbeat is a property of the WebSocket connection, not of any one channel riding it. Three open tabs were already three live sockets no matter what the ledger channel did on its own. Owner's ask: "integrate all one active tab heartbeat." Fixed properly with [`tabLeader.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/tabLeader.ts) — the Web Locks API (`navigator.locks.request("realtime-leader", …)`) grants leadership to exactly one same-origin tab, queues the rest, and hands off automatically the instant the leader tab closes (browser-native, no manual heartbeat/stale-lock code to get wrong). Only the leader ever opens the socket to LISTEN — both `appChannel`'s `onChangeSignal` and `GlobalInboxListener`'s room subscriptions now gate on `isTabLeader()`/`onBecomeLeader()`; a follower tab does nothing but read the leader's signals off the existing `localStorage` mirror. A non-leader may still PUBLISH on demand (connects just long enough to send). Chat's "which room is InboxModule showing" suppression, previously same-tab-only via a CustomEvent, is now ALSO mirrored to `localStorage` (`inbox-active-room:v1`, written by `InboxModule.tsx`) — otherwise the leader tab (which might not be the tab someone has Inbox open in) would double-notify for a room the user is actively reading in a different tab. Nothing a person sees changes: browser notifications are OS-level regardless of tab focus, and the sidebar badge already cross-tab-synced via `useNavCounts`'s own mirror — only which tab pays for the socket does. **Verified live**: two tabs opened, `navigator.locks.query()` showed one held + one queued; closing the held tab handed leadership to the queued one within ~1.5s with no app intervention; the new leader received a live `ledger` broadcast from an external test socket and synced correctly. `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean across both files. Chat's own message-delivery correctness (double-notify suppression across tabs) verified by code path, not yet by a live two-person chat session.

**2026-08-19 (same day, round 3) — proving liveness, and a chat-badge blind spot the polling stretch made worse.** Owner's ask: record every Realtime message locally so a gap (browser minimized, connection silently dead) is visible as a hole in the timestamps, and stretch the now-mostly-decorative polling intervals to 24h rather than delete them. Built: the leader stamps `app-changes-live:v1` on its OWN clock (`LIVE_STAMP_MS` = 60s, independent of Supabase traffic — a quiet channel is ambiguous between "nothing changed" and "nobody was connected," but a JS timer that stops ticking IS the browser having gone away) as well as on every real signal; `msSinceLastLive()` reads the gap; a late tick triggers a self-healing reconnect. `useRealtimeTopics` was simplified alongside this: dropped the "owed" flag entirely and now resyncs unconditionally on visibility-return (matching `usePolling`'s own long-standing behaviour) — a remembered-owed model can't be right anyway, since a gap that held NO signal would leave nothing to remember. New DM rooms got the same treatment: `POST /api/inbox/direct` now announces topic `"chat"`, `GlobalInboxListener` picks it up via `useRealtimeTopics` instead of waiting on a poll. `NAV_COUNTS_POLL_MS` and `INBOX_ROOMS_POLL_MS` → 24h, comments updated to say "distant fallback, not the mechanism."
