Testing the stretch surfaced a REAL bug, not a new one — the owner spotted it live: the sidebar's Inbox badge (`useNavCounts`) showed a stale count against the Inbox page's own accurate total. Root cause: `hasNavCountsChangedSince` — the cheap "did anything happen" check behind `/api/nav/counts`'s `?since=` shortcut — only ever checked `Ticket.updatedAt` and `UserSurfaceSeen.seenAt`. It had **never** looked at chat messages, so the server would answer `{unchanged: true}` forever regardless of real chat activity, and a client's stale cached number could never self-correct. That blind spot always existed; stretching `NAV_COUNTS_POLL_MS` to 24h made it far worse by accident, because the route was reusing THAT SAME constant as "how long do we trust a since-check before forcing a full recompute" — a coupling that made sense when the constant meant "the normal tick," and stopped making sense the moment it came to mean "a day-long insurance policy." Fixed both halves: `hasNavCountsChangedSince` now also checks `ChatMessage.createdAt` in any room the user belongs to; the trust window got its own constant, [`NAV_COUNTS_SINCE_TRUST_MS`](src/shared/lib/constants/polling.ts) = 3 minutes, decoupled from the poll interval. `InboxModule.loadRooms()` also now fires `nav-counts:changed` after every load, so opening Inbox pulls the sidebar to whatever it just found, not just whatever the delta check happens to agree with. **Verified**: with a since-cursor 49 minutes old and no code fix, the server said `unchanged: true` despite real unread messages existing (reproduced); with the fix, the same shape of request correctly returns a full recompute (`wasUnchanged: false`) reflecting live chat activity. `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean.

**Also resolved tonight, not a code issue:** the dev server intermittently produced a stuck Day Book spinner with zero API calls, reproducing across several clean `.next` wipes — traced to Turbopack itself degrading over this session's ~4+ hour continuous run (`Fast Refresh done in 23213ms`, `filesystem cache database compaction in 20.2s`), not a defect in any of tonight's changes; a genuinely fresh dev server process resolved it immediately every time. Worth knowing for future long sessions: if a page seems to hang with no network activity and no console error, suspect the dev server's own health before the code.

**2026-08-19 (same day, round 4) — a stuck active-room claim was silently eating one room's notifications.** Owner noticed live: DM notifications kept updating the sidebar, group notifications didn't — while working on a completely different page. Traced to [`inbox-active-room:v1`](src/modules/message/components/InboxModule.tsx) — the "InboxModule is showing this room, skip notifying" mirror added in round 2 — stuck pointing at the Organisation Group from an earlier test session that never unmounted cleanly. It was write-once with no expiry, so nothing was ever going to clear it; `GlobalInboxListener` had been treating that group as permanently "already being looked at," in every tab, since the moment it got stuck. The DM/group asymmetry was coincidental — whichever room happened to be open when the mirror last got stuck is the one that goes silent, not a room-type distinction. Fixed by making the claim expire: `InboxModule` now re-stamps the mirror every 60s while a room stays open (`ACTIVE_ROOM_STAMP_MS`), and `GlobalInboxListener` only honors a claim younger than `ACTIVE_ROOM_TTL_MS` (150s) — the same proof-of-life pattern as `appChannel.ts`'s `LIVE_STAMP_MS` from round 3, applied to a second place a claim can outlive its writer. **Verified live**, and the verification itself confirmed the root cause: a real full-page navigation away from `/office/message/inbox` did NOT clear the mirror (React's unmount cleanup does not reliably run across a hard navigation) — proving this can genuinely happen, not just in theory. Then: seeded a 200s-old stale claim on the group room → an external test broadcast to that room correctly fired notify + `inbox:unread` (claim ignored, expired). Re-armed a FRESH claim on the same room → an identical broadcast was correctly suppressed (no regression on the legitimate case). `tsc --noEmit` + eslint clean.

- **2026-06-30 — Voucher print: per-kind Print + Thermal toggles (uniform, office vouchers)**. The 6 office voucher forms (`GlVoucherPage`, `TradingVoucherPage`, `JournalVoucherPage`, `ManufacturingJournalPage`, `PhysicalStockPage`, `BranchTransferPage` — **POS/online excluded**) now share two soft toggles in the footer, each with a **per-kind default in one central place**: [`voucherTemplateDefaults.ts`](src/shared/lib/print/voucherTemplateDefaults.ts) holds `PRINT_AFTER_SAVE_DEFAULT_BY_KIND` (print after save?) + `THERMAL_DEFAULT_BY_KIND` (thermal-auto vs browser?), read via `printAfterSaveDefault()`/`thermalDefault()`. The footer toggle is one shared [`FooterToggle`](src/modules/accountancy/components/FooterToggle.tsx) (replaced the old print-only `PrintAfterSaveToggle`); both action bars (`VoucherActionBar` + `VoucherInlineActions`) render it, and **Thermal only shows while Print is on**. On save the form opens `voucherPrintPageHref(source, id, { thermal })` → **Thermal ON** = `/receipt/voucher/<src>/<id>?mode=thermal` (the generic page renders the 80mm slip + mounts `ThermalAutoPrint`, which tries the local relay then **falls back to auto browser print**, like POS); **Thermal OFF** = `?autoprint=0` (traditional browser page — paper comes from the kind's template set on the voucher-templates page, plus an A4/80mm/72mm format selector in `ReceiptButtons`, which gained an `autoPrint` opt-out + picker). The register's existing 3-dot Thermal/PDF menu (`receiptHref` → `/receipt/payment` relay etc.) is untouched. **Bridge endpoint `/print/voucher` — BUILT (separate repo `D:\AbeerSuite\tools\combined-bridge`, branch `main`, uncommitted; needs `restart.bat`).** Thermal-ON POSTs `{ kind, title, sample, template }` to the new `/print/voucher` route on the `thermal.js` bridge (localhost:9191); the added `buildVoucherBuffer()` renders the **same `ThermalReceiptSample` + per-kind `VoucherTemplateDefaults` the browser slip uses** (mirrors `<ThermalReceiptPreview>` — header/party/items table/discount-ladder OR subtotal-tax-total/terms/signature/footer), so thermal == browser for every kind. If the relay is offline `ThermalAutoPrint` still falls back to **auto browser print of the 80mm slip**. `buildVoucherBuffer` is **generic over sample+template**, so a NEW voucher kind needs NO bridge change — only a new _primitive_ or a structural redesign would. **Distribution (counter PCs, non-dev) = a double-click installer** `combined-bridge-setup.exe` (Inno Setup; source `combined-bridge.iss` in the bridge repo, compiled with ISCC at `C:\Program Files (x86)\Inno Setup 6\ISCC.exe`). It installs to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CombinedBridge` (per-user, **no admin/UAC**), runs `npm install`, registers the ONLOGON autostart task (new no-pause `register-autostart.bat` → `start-hidden.vbs`), and starts the bridge — automating the old unzip→npm i→autostart steps. Shipped from **Settings → Printer Settings** as `/combined-bridge-setup.exe` (primary button); the code-only `/combined-bridge.zip` (rebuilt from current source — no `.git`, no `node_modules`) stays as the advanced manual path (unzip → `npm install` → `setup-autostart.bat`). Both static files live in the web app's `public/`. **Two installers** (built by the separate `bridge-installer` repo): `combined-bridge-setup.exe` (**online**, ~2 MB — `bootstrap.ps1` fetches a local Node + `npm install` at install) and `combined-bridge-setup-offline.exe` (**offline**, ~11 MB — bundles portable Node + `node_modules`, no internet). The bridge runtime uses a **local `node\node.exe`** if present (start scripts + autostart), so **Node is no longer a counter prerequisite**. **Packaging = `D:\AbeerSuite\tools\bridge-installer`** (`build.ps1` + `combined-bridge.iss`): input = `combined-bridge` source, output = the `public/` files. **Release (dev):** commit the bridge → `bridge-installer\build.ps1 -PublicDir <webapp>\public` (auto-versions, builds both installers + zip + manifest, publishes) → commit web app + deploy. Counters never run `build.ps1`. **Updating a counter:** re-run the installer, or double-click **`update.bat`** in the install folder (pulls latest from `https://office.networkservice.in`, `npm install`, restart). Runbook: `bridge-installer/COUNTER-SETUP.md`. **Version check:** the bridge reports its `version` on `/health` (from `package.json`, now **1.1.0** for `buildVoucherBuffer`); Printer Settings fetches the latest from the static 3-line manifest `public/combined-bridge.version.json` and shows an **"update available"** banner with the installer link when running ≠ latest (bridge-not-running already shows the download). Version is **auto-derived** by `build.ps1` from the bridge git commit (`<base>+g<hash>`) and stamped into the installers + the shipped `package.json` (`/health`) + the manifest — **no manual bump**. **Counter lock (server-authoritative, redesigned 2026-07-06):** each PC mints a stable `deviceId` in the **bridge config** (via the generic `/config` — no bridge code change); the lock itself now lives **server-side** on `PosCounter` (`boundDeviceId`/`boundDeviceLabel`/`boundAt`). `ShiftOpenDialog` sends the `deviceId` on shift open → the **first** open **binds** the counter to that PC, and a later open from a _different_ PC is refused (`POST /api/pos/shifts`); the picker forces/disables from the server binding (both counter GET scopes return it). **Release** it centrally from **Admin → POS Counters** (per-counter Unlock → `PATCH /api/pos/counters/[id] { releaseLock: true }`) or self-serve from **Settings → Thermal Printer → Forget** (→ `POST /api/pos/counters/release-mine { deviceId }`). Replaces the old bridge-only `counterId`/`counterName` lock so an admin can forget a lock without visiting the counter PC. All three bridge static files (`.exe`, `.zip`, `.version.json`) are whitelisted in `src/proxy.ts`. The bridge static files (online `.exe`, offline `.exe`, `.zip`, `.version.json`) are whitelisted in `src/proxy.ts` and committed to `public/`. Gate: `tsc --noEmit` clean. _Verification gap (dev, accepted): forms are auth-gated + the dev DB has no saved voucher to render, so the click-through is unrun. Paper sizes (A5 etc.) + template specifics are deferred to the voucher-templates page per owner._

- **2026-06-29 — Per-kind voucher permissions (Phase A + B)**. Every hand-created voucher kind is now independently grantable (grant _Sale_ without _Purchase_). `voucherMatrix.ts` is the single source: all kinds, grouped by **economic nature** — `gl` (GL leg only), `trading` (GL + stock), `inventory` (stock only), `optional` (no real transaction) — each with `create/post/cancel/delete` ids. GL keeps `finance:voucher:*`; inventory adds `inventory:voucher:<action>:<kind>` (~56 perms, seeded from the matrix, R1 auto-granted). **UI** (Phase A): Departments pool shows one **Vouchers** section with the four groups as flex chips; Role page renders one eligibility matrix per group. **Enforcement** (Phase B): inventory voucher routes now gate per-kind — `create`/`post` via `userCanVoucherKind` (matrix kinds need the per-kind key OR the `inventory:edit` fallback; POS/online channel variants pass through untouched), `cancel`/`delete` via `userCanVoucher` (additive — the generic `inventory:voucher:cancel/delete` that gated them before still passes, so no one loses access). **Known follow-up:** the `[id]/status` toggle (OPTIONAL docs only) still uses the generic `inventory:voucher:cancel` gate, not per-kind. **Not yet verified at runtime** — the dev DB was unreachable when this landed; verify allow/deny paths + POS once it's back.

- **2026-05-29 — Backup & Restore → Dropbox, fire-and-forget** (on `dev`, PR pending). `pg_dump`/`pg_restore` run in GitHub Actions; the dump is stored in / read from **Dropbox** instead of Supabase Storage; the UI only triggers the run and no longer polls (close the page anytime). Backup lands in Dropbox; restore picks from a Dropbox list. New `src/shared/lib/backup/dropbox.ts` + `/api/settings/restore/list`; removed `/backup/status`, `/restore/upload-url`, `/restore/status`, `backupStorage.ts`. **Pending before prod:** one-time Dropbox scoped-app setup (`DROPBOX_APP_KEY/SECRET/REFRESH_TOKEN`) in GitHub secrets + Vercel env, then merge `dev → main`.

## 🆕 Recently shipped (last ~10)

- **2026-10-01 — POS: edit the customer from the counter.** Owner: *"make the party editable from pos so the user can correct spelling address etc"*. A pencil by the name on the POS panel opens [`PosEditCustomerDialog`](src/modules/pos/components/PosEditCustomerDialog.tsx). **Details** (name, phone, email, GSTIN, address) is open to every cashier, same as the office party PUT; it saves through `/api/person/[id]` or `/api/companies/[id]` plus the primary phone/email rows, patches the panel in place and keeps the bill. **Advanced** reuses the office's own cards: credit limit + customer group behind `customers:edit`, portal password reset behind `users:edit` (persons only); the APIs check the same ticks, and a save there re-reads the POS snapshot. Walk-in gets no pencil. **Bug fixed on the way:** renaming a person or company never renamed its ledger `Account.name`, so the Day Book kept the old spelling — both PUTs now carry the name to the account in the same transaction.
- **2026-10-01 — Spend tiers: an overall rank plus a medal per period.** Owner: *"different time frame can say different thing … these are like medal of a army officer"*. The customer badge used to be the title from the tightest bracket held, so one ₹20k bill 4 months ago read as "Platinum". Now [`resolveTierTitles`](src/shared/lib/pricing/resolveTierTitles.ts) scores **Σ the % of every title held** against **maxScore = Σ each period's top enabled level %**, both read live from the settings (delete or switch off a level and the top score drops with it). Rank = share of maxScore: Platinum ≥ 75%, Gold ≥ 50%, Silver ≥ 25%, else Bronze. A `currentSaleOnly` period (Fresh Customer Only) is left out of both sides. `badgeLevelName` is now that rank, so the bill's "Level Discount (…)" label and the shop follow it; `badgePeriodId` is gone. The POS panel shows only the medals, one chip per period (owner dropped the `Rank · score/max` badge) (newest bracket first, spend and days on hover); the old Titles held list became a one-line Tier discount row. Badge only — the discount maths (titles add up, then the cap) is unchanged.
- **2026-09-30 — Group pricing: each customer group has its own price type per product group (POS only).** Owner: *"some customer are actually bulk/whole sale customer so we need to take less profit"*, *"for some customer like govt supply … we my bill on mrp, or barcode price"*, and *"whole sale price are product group related, suppose a cctv technician may get wholesale price on his relevant group not on paper"*. New page **Pricing Strategy → Group pricing** ([`GroupPricingManager.tsx`](src/modules/pricing-strategy/components/GroupPricingManager.tsx), `GET /api/pricing-strategy/group-pricing`, `PUT …/[id]`). For each customer group you add rows of **product group → price type**: **MRP**, **Print price** (the barcode tag), **Profit sharing X%** or **Revenue discount X%**. There is an optional **All items** row, plus three ticks: **Tier discount**, **Coupons**, **Loyalty**. The schema adds `CustomerGroupPriceRule` (a `child` of `CustomerGroup` in the sync registry), three `allow*` booleans on `CustomerGroup`, and `CustomerGroup.updatedAt`. Every save writes the group row, so the counters' `full` pull sees its content move and re-pulls the rules with it. Migration: `20260930130000_customer_group_pricing`. **How it bills:**
  - **Rule lookup:** a rule on a parent product group covers its whole sub-tree and the nearest group wins ([`groupPricing.ts`](src/modules/pricing-strategy/lib/groupPricing.ts) `expandGroupRules`). The expanded map rides the POS account snapshot as `groupPricing`.
  - **MRP / Print price:** these only set the line's **starting price** at add-to-cart. A customer change re-prices lines the POS priced itself (`CartLine.autoPrice`); a typed price and hydrated edit or converted lines never move.
  - **Profit sharing / Revenue discount:** this is the **silent rebate**, owner: *"on effective price after all discounts. only if has profit left"*. It is worked out **last**:
    - Tier (per line), coupon, on-top coupon, loyalty and manual minus charges are spread over the unprotected lines by value.
    - The profit left over cost gets its share. Revenue discount is capped at the profit left, so the price never goes below cost.
    - Discount-protected items and lines with no cost are left alone.
  - **How the rebate posts:** it is taken out of the line's **rate before GST**, owner: *"nothing extra to mention in print or display just price difference silently"*.
    - No receipt row, no display row, no separate ledger. The sale just posts lower.
    - The printed "Counter discount" ladder absorbs it.
    - It also counts in the loyalty earn base's `combinedDiscount`.
  - **Client and server agreement:** `usePosBilling` (preview, `groupRebate: !isEdit`, so a new sale only) and `/api/pos/sale` run the same `groupRebatesExcl` on the same inputs, so the total collected is the total posted. Orders, estimates and edits stay rebate-free; their starting prices still follow MRP / Print price.
  - **Benefit ticks:** [`groupPricingServer.ts`](src/modules/pricing-strategy/lib/groupPricingServer.ts) `loadPartyPricing` is now the one read for "may this party get tier / coupons / loyalty". The account snapshot, `/api/pos/sale`, `coupon-validate` and `coupon-offers` all use it; before, each re-read `noDiscount` by itself. The customer's own **No discount** flag still wins: no benefits and no rebate, though MRP / Print price still apply. An **inactive** group is switched off.
  - **Not included:** the online invoice and shop checkout, which the owner parked (*"for now skip online invoice continue with pos"*: an online order is paid at checkout at the shop price, so repricing it at invoice time would leave a balance), and the office Sales invoice.
  - **Settings → Customer Groups retired into it** (same day; owner: *"now we can marge retire the page in setting"*):
    - Group pricing is now the one home of the group master. It has the add box, rename (`useFieldSave`; an emptied name puts the old one back, which closes the ui-ux-patterns D item), the Active switch and Delete, all over the existing `/api/settings/customer-groups` routes, which the Profile tab picker still reads.
    - The Settings page, its nav link and `CustomerGroupsManager` are deleted.
    - Access went to **R1 only** (owner chose (b): pricing is a business call). The old `ui:sidebar:settings:customer-groups` grants to R2, DEP-OP and DEP-SALES were dropped from the personal snapshot. Staff still pick a group on the Profile tab.
  - **Pending:**
    - Migration applied and browser-verified on the local copy on 2026-09-30. Live gets it through the `d_office` deploy, after the counter release (§3).
    - On live, tick **Group pricing** for R1 on the Role page, or use `seed:sync-permissions --grant ui:sidebar:pricing-strategy:group-pricing --roles R1`, then sign in again. The page is locked by that tick.

- **2026-09-28 — Convert and merge leave nothing behind: the old party row is deleted, the Trash is gone.** Owner: *"we need to remove totally because important transaction record and history is kept so no need Trash"*, then for merge: *"the looser related connections should be rewrite using winner relation and delete the looser"*. The history was never on the party row — it is on the **Account** (`UniversalVoucher.partyAccountId`, `VoucherGlLine`, `Ticket.accountId`, `Device.accountId`), which a conversion keeps (it only flips `Account.personId` ↔ `companyId`) and a merge dissolves into the winner's. So [`convertParty.ts`](src/modules/customer/lib/convertParty.ts) now does the same thing in both tools: one `movePartyRefs` re-points everything that names the party ROW — deals, help tickets, CRM notes (`TicketLog`) and the bills' stored `VoucherDetail.partyType/partyId` — at the surviving row, phones/emails/links move as before, and the old row is **deleted inside the same transaction**. Help tickets and CRM notes were left on the dead row by the old converter (a gap); the three ticket tables are `strategy: child`, so their parent `Ticket.updatedAt` is touched too or a counter would show the moved ticket under a party it no longer has until the hourly reconcile. **Removed:** the 8 tombstone columns (`convertedTo*`/`convertedAt`, `mergedInto*`/`mergedAt` on Person and Company), the Trash section + `/api/customer-tools/trash` routes + `hardDeleteTombstone`/`listTombstones`, the `Moved → movedTo` 404 hop in `/api/person/[id]` and `/api/companies/[id]` (and its follower on the all-customers page), the reactivate guard, the tombstone filters in party lists / Reconnect candidates / Vyapar scripts, the converted banners on both profile views, and the forwarding walk in [`partyLinkServer.ts`](src/shared/lib/parties/partyLinkServer.ts) — a stored party id now always names a live row, so `resolvePartyLinks` costs one query only when the caller wants the Account id. **Migration** (folded into the unapplied `20260928100000_storefront_ads`): a recursive walk resolves every existing tombstone — merge and convert, any chain — to the record live today, re-points bills / tickets / notes / contacts / links, deletes the tombstones, then drops the columns; sync registry + `local-schema.sql` regenerated. Counters take the build before the `d_office` deploy (§3). **Realtime:** `/api/customer-tools` now signals `parties`, `ledger` and `tickets` ([`topics.ts`](src/shared/lib/realtime/topics.ts)) since a convert/merge touches all three; the deletes reach the replicas through `noteDeleted` → reconcile as every office delete does. Supersedes the same-day "refused hard-delete says why" fix, which is moot with the Trash gone. Also closes the 2026-09-07 "ghost tombstone on the counter" leftover — there are no tombstones to ghost.
- **2026-09-28 — Storefront ads: E-Commerce → Ads fills the shop's empty spaces from one ad inventory.** Owner, on a catalog group with one product and two empty boxes: *"if grid is blank … it leave empty space"* → *"we will have adv inventory, adv may be static or dynamically created from products"*, *"find scope to place adv, create place holder adv, maintain industry standard"*. **Inventory** ([`ads.ts`](src/shared/lib/shop/ads.ts), stored whole on `OrgDefaults.storefrontAdsJson` — migration `20260928100000_storefront_ads`, so counters take the release before the `d_office` deploy): each ad is **static** (uploaded artwork + link, made for one size) or **product** (drawn from the live catalogue — photo, name, price, saving, button — to fit any size; the product is fixed, or picked on every visit by rule: best deal, from a category, under a price), with sizes, an optional "only in this category's rows", dates, priority and a switch. **Six sizes, IAB names where one exists:** Grid tile (one product card), Grid wide (two cards), Billboard 970×250, Medium rectangle 300×250, Half page 300×600, Mobile banner 320×100. **Nine spots** (`AD_SPOT_DEFS`): catalog top (billboard; mobile banner on a phone), every group's row gap (two empty boxes take one wide, else tiles — worked out for the 3-column and 2-column grids separately and CSS-switched, so a phone's single column never gets one), a billboard after every third group, sidebar rectangle + half page, cart summary, product page (between the tabs and "More like this"), wishlist gap. **`AdPicker`** walks a page once in reading order: on + complete + inside dates + size fits + category matches; higher priority first, static before product at equal priority, ties rotate on a per-visit seed **made on the server** so hydration matches; an ad never shows twice on a page, a product ad never repeats a product already on it (the product page loads four rows past its rail for that), at most six ads a page. Every unit carries a "Sponsored" chip and a `rel="sponsored"` link; house ads only, nothing sticky or pop-up. **Placeholders:** `?adPreview=1` on the shop, for a signed-in staff session only, draws a dashed "Ad space" unit in every unfilled spot (the six SVGs in `public/ads/`); the inventory starts with one **switched-off sample per size** using the same artwork. The office page ([`ads/page.tsx`](src/app/(protected)/office/ecommerce/ads/page.tsx)) lists the inventory, edits the selected ad, previews it **through the same `AdUnit` the shop uses** at every size it may fill, and "Where ads show" opens the spot map with a live count per spot. Rides the E-Commerce module link like Customize Home — no new permission key. Dropped from the plan on the way: the per-spot "fall back to product ads" switch (priority + static-first does the same with no knob) and the sticky sidebar (the filter panel can be taller than the screen). No click counting yet. `/api/shop/ads` (public, 5-min private cache) feeds the client-rendered wishlist and cart. Not seen in the browser until the migration reaches the DB the dev server points at.
- **2026-09-28 — A cash Payment to a party prints the Bank Deposit Slip, with the party's bank details and the note count.** Owner: *"payment voucher should print currency denom and party bank details like contra do for us, if its cash to party payment we will deposit to party account directly"*. **Rule** (one place, [`partyDepositSlip.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/partyDepositSlip.ts)): a PAYMENT whose money side is Cash-in-Hand only, whose particulars are ONE ledger of a person, company or staff profile, and whose payee has an A/C No on file (`canDepositToPartyBank` in [`partyBank.ts`](src/shared/lib/parties/partyBank.ts)). It prints on the **shared `BANK_DEPOSIT` template** (owner's pick — one slip design with the Contra): new `DepositSlipInfo.paidTo` adds a "Paid to" row (A4 face + counterfoil, 80mm preview, bridge `printDepositMiddle`) and the bank block is the **party's own** — never a fallback to our bank, which Contra does; depositor's PAN stays ours; the slip total is the cash side. **Form** ([`GlVoucherPage`](src/modules/accountancy/components/GlVoucherPage.tsx)): the Contra "Denomination details" tick now also appears on such a Payment, auto-ticked; one counting panel under the open bills, "short / more than payment" line, and Save blocked until the count equals the payment (untick → blank pen-fill slip). The count rides the existing `UniversalVoucher.denomJson` (create route now keeps it for PAYMENT too) — **no schema change**. Payee with no A/C No → normal payment voucher plus an amber "No bank details saved…" line (owner's pick). `/api/accountancy/gl-accounts` carries a `partyBank` flag on person/company/staff ledgers for that. **Two print builders:** `buildPaymentSlipPrint` returns null for a party deposit, so the thermal API falls through to `buildGlSample` and `/receipt/payment/[id]` redirects to `/receipt/voucher/gl/[id]`. Edit dirty-check now includes the count (a count-only edit used to read "No changes"). **Follow-up (owner, same day):** *"the party amount from denom if ticked, otherwise like normal … if bill wise is so long and denom also yes make sure overflow not hide"*. While ticked the payee row's amount is LOCKED to the count (net of any other row), like a Contra cash row; ticking with nothing counted keeps an amount already there (e.g. from Settle this bill) as the target. The count does **not** trim bill ticks as it climbs (the typed amount does, keystroke by keystroke — counting note by note would cut ticks made first down to the first note); they are fitted at Save by new `settleFromPicks({ maxOwn })`, which caps the voucher's own share (last bill in fill order left short, ticked credits still spent) — done there, not in the form, because the form cannot tell a bill from a credit. **Then (same day):** the typed-amount trim had the same blind spot — the form's `trimPicksTo` summed bills AND credits, so ₹11,000 of bills + a ₹1,000 advance (net ₹10,000) was cut the moment ₹10,000 was typed. It is gone; [`OpenBillsPanel`](src/modules/accountancy/components/OpenBillsPanel.tsx) trims its own ticks via new `fitPicksToAmount` in [`billLinkPlan.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/billLinkPlan.ts): only bills − credits must fit, only bills give way, last in `autoLinkOrder` first (was: last ticked first), an emptied amount clears all, an amount the ticks set is left alone; `trimToAmount={false}` for a counted payment. The side column stays sticky but its height is capped to the measured body height (ResizeObserver) and it scrolls on its own, so a long open-bills list plus the count never runs off the bottom — Contra's several cash panels too. Bank payments, expense-only and multi-party payments print as before. The bridge's "Paid to" / "A/C Name" rows reach counters with the next release; until then the 80mm slip prints without them.
- **2026-09-28 — An untouched tab no longer signs you out of the one you are working in.** Owner: *"if any tab is active other tab should respect it"*. Every window shares one sign-in but each ran its own 45-minute idle clock, so a tab left alone signed the person out of the tab in use. [`ActivityPulse`](src/shared/components/ActivityPulse.tsx) now shares input over `BroadcastChannel "abeer-activity"` (at most every 15 s); the other windows move their idle clock up and clear the "Still there?" flag, so the idle sign-out needs **every** window untouched. Only the window with the input pulses `/api/hr/activity`, so duty time is not counted twice. Same commit: [`markDeliberateSignOut(notice)`](src/shared/lib/auth/signOutTo.ts) carries its reason (idle, revoked, counter released) and the other windows show it for 2.5 s instead of jumping to the login page in silence (a regression from d93e8712, found by review). Browser only, no server change; tsc + ESLint clean, not driven in the browser.
- **2026-09-28 — A revoked session no longer loops a counter through the login page, and Sign out stays signed out.** Owner, on Counter 94 (`v_live`): *"Your access was updated by an admin"* kept coming back; Sign out took several clicks, then the till **signed itself back in**; a second Sign out finally reached the PIN screen. **Cause — two judges of one session.** `User.tokensValidFrom` (the "end every login" stamp: a sign-out anywhere, a role / position / permission edit, a password change) is written straight to Supabase. The office checks relayed saves against Supabase; the till checks its own pages against the **replica**, which learns the stamp only when the worker next pulls `User`. In that gap every save came back `401 SESSION_REVOKED`, the browser went to `/staff/login`, the till's proxy still saw a good session and sent it back to the inbox — round and round. The same gap brought the cookie back: `updateAge: 0` re-issues it on every request, so a page request already in flight when Sign out cleared it put a fresh one back, and the stale replica accepted it. And each Sign out of the already-dead session stamped "now" again, ending any fresh login made elsewhere. **Fix:** (1) [`writeRelay`](src/shared/lib/counter/writeRelay.ts) pulls `User` into the replica (bounded 3 s) **before** handing a `SESSION_REVOKED` back, so the login page agrees with the office; the pull moved to [`replicaPull.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/replicaPull.ts), one listener per server process on `globalThis` (the proxy and `/api/auth` get separate module copies), and a listener still connecting is waited for inside the same budget. (2) [`SessionWrapper`](src/shared/components/SessionWrapper.tsx) clears the till's own cookie (`signOut`, capped 4 s) after the 2.5 s notice, so the loop ends even with the worker stopped. (3) The [`auth.ts`](src/auth.ts) sign-out event stamps only a login that is still valid (conditional `updateMany`, the mirror of the jwt check), so signing out of a dead session touches no other device; on a counter it then pulls `User`, so a cookie that sneaks back is refused by the till too. (4) [`signOutTo`](src/shared/lib/auth/signOutTo.ts) tells other windows the sign-out was on purpose (`BroadcastChannel "abeer-sign-out"`), so they no longer say *"signed out after 6 hours with nothing happening"*. (5) One sentence, `SESSION_REVOKED_MESSAGE` in [`constants/session.ts`](src/shared/lib/constants/session.ts), for the API answer and the notice: *"You were signed out — your access changed, or you signed out on another device."* — the old "by an admin" was wrong whenever the owner had signed out elsewhere. No schema, seed or permission change. tsc + ESLint clean; reviewed adversarially twice. **Not proven on a real till** — the dev server does not relay, so the loop itself cannot be reproduced here. Ships with the next counter release; the API wording reaches the office with the next `d_office` deploy. **Left open, found on the way:** the three inventory screens that use the `http` axios instance (not bare `axios`) get no sign-out on `SESSION_REVOKED`, only the error toast; revocation still compares timestamps from two machines' clocks, so a till whose clock runs behind the office could be refused right after signing in.
- **2026-09-28 — Customize Home: the fallback sections can be unlocked and duplicated.** Owner, looking at E-Commerce → Customize Home with every section a fallback: *"how to change/update fallback"*. Since 926aa892c a fallback (priority -1) was locked outright — Priority, Duplicate and the switch included — so once all ten sections were fallbacks the whole home page was frozen. Now: **Unlock** sits next to **Full size** in the Live preview header (never on a row, so a stray click cannot flip it), asks first, and opens **every fallback at once** — owner: *"not per section, its for all at once"* — until **Lock** or Save layout. It needs the new `ecommerce:fallback:unlock` ("Can unlock fallback" beside the Customize Home tick on the Role page). Typing -1 into Priority also needs the fallbacks unlocked, since otherwise the row would drop into the locked group with no way back. **Duplicate** works on every fallback without a key: the copy stays in the same Section No. as an ordinary section (priority from 0, the fallback's ignored dates dropped) and starts **switched off**, so copying alone never changes the storefront. Page-level gate only, like the rest of RBAC for now — `PUT /api/ecommerce/home-layout` does not check the key. Live needs R1's grant: a full seed's R1 top-up, `pnpm seed:sync-permissions --grant ecommerce:fallback:unlock --roles R1 --no-revoke --apply`, or the tick on the Role page.
- **2026-09-28 — Desktop reads that needed a key the PC lacked: courier prices, Payment Methods, the super admin's Permissions.** Owner, after the Cloudflare fix: *"find similar problem"*. The rule behind all three: under `v_live` a GET is never relayed (`relayPolicy.ts`), so any read that needs a server key runs on the PC and fails quietly there. **Change courier** on an E-Commerce order (`GET /api/ecommerce/orders/[id]/courier`) dropped Shiprocket and Shipway from the list; the owner added `COUNTER_ENV_COURIER_KEYS` (`SHIPROCKET_EMAIL`, `SHIPROCKET_PASSWORD`, `SHIPWAY_USERNAME`, `SHIPWAY_LICENSE`, `SHIPWAY_TOKEN`, `SHIPWAY_API_BASE`) to the `office` variant in [`counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts) (cac59e52). Booking, labels, manifests and tracking are writes and were already fine. **Admin → Users → Permissions** did not mark the env super admin as full access; `COUNTER_ENV_SUPER_ADMIN_KEYS` ships `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL` alone, which grants no sign-in because `checkSuperAdminEnv` also needs `SUPER_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, still withheld. **Settings → Payment Methods** showed "Keys missing" for every gateway; payment secrets stay off the PC, and [`credentialStatus.ts`](src/shared/lib/payments/credentialStatus.ts) now sets `checkedOnOffice` when `COUNTER_MACHINE_ID` is present, so the page says *"Checked on office website"* (6a25ae3b). Owner's call: the office desktop app is private to the business, so these keys may ship; **remove the courier and super-admin keys before any public desktop release.** Reaches desktops with the next release.
- **2026-09-28 — Backup & Restore → "Back up now" (Dropbox) works from the desktop app, backing up live.** Owner: *"backup button in app should backup the live data"*, then *"use same way for drop box fix"*. `/api/settings/backup` is `NEVER_RELAY`, so it runs on the PC, which has no `DIRECT_URL`; it sent the live `DATABASE_URL` to the GitHub runner with Prisma's `?pgbouncer=true` still on, and `pg_dump` refused it — the app said "started", Dropbox got nothing. New `libpqUrl()` in [`dbUrl.ts`](src/shared/lib/backup/dbUrl.ts) drops every non-libpq parameter, the same way "Back up to this PC" (`stripPrismaParams`, [`backupManager.ts`](electron/backupManager.ts)) always has; both backup routes (`settings/backup`, `cron/backup`) now use `DIRECT_URL`, else the cleaned `DATABASE_URL`. The two copies are twins by hand — the Electron build cannot import `src/`. Found on the way and fixed in both: a password containing `?` was cut at that `?`; the query is now looked for only after the last `@`. Still the transaction pooler (:6543) on the desktop, as the PC backup is — owner's choice of "same way"; if a dump is ever cut off as the database grows, the session pooler (:5432) is the next step.
- **2026-09-28 — The office desktop build ships the Cloudflare keys, so staff set their email handle and forward address in the app.** Owner, on 1.8.3's Me → Security: *"user should be capable to set/update email handle and forward email"*. The form already existed; the card said *"Mail forwarding is set from the office website."* because `GET /api/users/[id]/forward-email` is a read, runs on the PC under `v_live`, and `counter.env` withheld Cloudflare. New `COUNTER_ENV_CLOUDFLARE_KEYS` in [`counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts) (`CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID`), `office` variant only — same pattern as Cloudinary. Cost, accepted: an office installer can change the domain's mail routing. Reaches desktops with the next release.

- **2026-09-27 — The office desktop build ships the Cloudinary keys, so photo upload works in the app.** Owner, on 1.8.3's E-Commerce → Products → Photos: *"Upload failed — Cloudinary not configured"*, then *"we can save cloudinary credential in app … do the simple"*. Cause: `GET /api/upload-signature` is a read, so `v_live` never relays it — it runs on the PC's own server, and `counter.env` withheld Cloudinary on purpose. New `COUNTER_ENV_CLOUDINARY_KEYS` in [`counterEnv.ts`](scripts/counterEnv.ts) (`NEXT_PUBLIC_CLOUDINARY_CLOUD_NAME`, `CLOUDINARY_API_KEY`, `CLOUDINARY_API_SECRET`) is copied for the `office` variant only, like the backup keys; `counter` keeps going without. Chosen over relaying the signature GET to the office. Cost, accepted: every office install holds the Cloudinary secret in plaintext. Reaches desktops with the next release; product photos (Add, From URL) and staff avatars were all affected.

- **2026-09-27 — SL Search: find a sale or a ticket from the SL no alone.** Owner: *"customer dont have any record of buying from us and also forget name/mobile/email … check using SL no, and also SL no in ticket"*, then *"add a sl search in office/workspace in sidebar, a modal dialog"*. Sidebar → Workspace → **SL Search** opens [`SlSearchDialog`](src/shared/components/SlSearchDialog.tsx), mounted once in the office layout so it works from any office page; staff type or scan the SL no and press Enter. There is still **no serial column**: a bill carries the SL no only in its **line note** (the POS forces it for products with *Must fill line note*), so [`GET /api/search-slno`](src/app/api/search-slno/route.ts) scans `VoucherInventoryLine.description` on every POSTED voucher — the POS `"<sku> — <name> · "` prefix is stripped first, so a SKU or product-name word never counts as a hit — and returns each bill with its type, number, date + "N days ago" (the warranty question), the party frozen on the bill, product and note, with **Open** → `editFormUrl`. Tickets: repair and help-desk tickets whose warranty ref, service-centre ref, accessories, device model / problem, title, notes or help subject mention it, each saying which field matched; `accessInfo` (PIN / pattern) is never searched. Search needs 4+ characters (`SL_SEARCH_MIN_CHARS`, [slSearch.ts](src/shared/lib/search/slSearch.ts)) and runs by itself 0.4 s after typing pauses, or at once on Enter — a USB scanner's last key — after which the SL no stays selected so the next scan replaces it (not after the pause-search: a slow typist's next key would wipe the number); only the newest answer paints, and a repeat within a minute is answered from memory. Same day, owner: *"include ticket also, right click open in new tab, invoice/ticket and also party/customer open"* → both groups show after every search (an empty Tickets group says so, rather than vanishing); the bill no, ticket # and party / customer name are links (party → `partyProfileHref`: a bill's live party via its ledger Account, a help ticket's via `resolvePartyLinks`); right-click on any of them gives the shared **Open in new window** menu. [`LinkContextMenu`](src/shared/components/LinkContextMenu.tsx) now corrects its own position inside a transformed ancestor — a centred dialog made `position: fixed` land off the pointer. Then *"it should include challan, grn, purchase all if has see purchase right"* / *"show suppliers exist"*: challan, GRN and purchase were already searched (every POSTED type); the **supplier side** — every `PER_KIND` whose party is Sundry Creditors (purchase, purchase return / order, GRN, rejection out) — now comes back only to a role holding **Show suppliers** (`customers:view:creditors`), filtered in the query. Challan is customer-side and shows to everyone. Then *"mount it to pos invoice below lead in right side bar"* → an **SL Search** button under **Lead** on the POS action rail (`POS_RAIL_ACTIONS`, every POS screen — invoice, estimate, order); `PosScreenShell` mounts the same dialog with `linksInNewTab`, so on the till every link opens a new tab and the bill in progress is never left. `NavItem` is now a link-or-action union so a Workspace entry can open a dialog. Visibility is the usual Workspace tick — **`ui:sidebar:workspace:sl-search`**: admins see it at once, every other role needs it ticked on the Role page (no seed). **Retired:** the hidden tickets-only `(to-do)/core/search-slno` page, its `/api/search-slno/[deviceId]` and the whole `(to-do)` route group (and its counter-strip entry). **Dropped:** a separate SL No box on repair tickets (`Device.serialNo`) — built, then deleted on the owner's call (*"delete it, and add the hint"*): the search already reads a ticket's problem / notes / model, so the box would only have cost a migration and a counter-first release. Instead the Problem box on intake and Edit Ticket now says *"include the SL no if the unit has one"*.
- **2026-09-27 — `/lab` and the welcome page deleted.** Owner: *"delete welcome and lab"*. Gone: the `/lab` Front/Back-office prototype (`src/app/(protected)/lab`, `src/modules/lab` — 17 files, ~3,100 lines, including the Universal Customer Finder prototype), `/office/welcome`, and `shared/lib/auth/freeRoutes.ts`. Nothing linked to either page: the "no position → welcome" redirect had already left the office layout, so this settles cleanup-audit **E4** as *dropping it was intended* — staff with no position land on the normal office screens. `/lab` is also out of the proxy's staff-sign-in list, `robots.ts` and the storefront reserved slugs. **Left alone:** `/api/quick-notes` — the lab was its only caller, but it sits on a table, so removing it is its own decision. To see the old prototype: `git show cfe711ff:src/modules/lab/lib/buildCandidates.ts`.
- **2026-09-26 — The Employee Portal: one page for a person's whole working life, at `/office/me`.** Owner: *"need proper user/employee portal with all details that an employee need … the existing /office/social/users/[id] becoming portal so the path name should be corrected to avoid confusion. again social also available from user portal … keep them as united so user can see his leave application, remaining leave, attendance/activity, targets, penalty, i card, payslip etc. all things in good to find arrangement and social also somewhere but it should not distract user from official career information."* Until today the person's own information sat in two places — **My Info** (`/office/hr/me`: attendance, leave, target, payslips) and the social-first profile page (wall, friends, then Edit Profile, Security, Personal & Bank, Salary Slips, ID Card at the tail) — and nothing anywhere showed them their salary structure, their money ledger, their job details, or a penalty before the slip arrived. Now [`/office/me`](src/app/(protected)/office/me/page.tsx) renders [`EmployeePortal`](src/modules/hr/components/portal/EmployeePortal.tsx): six groups across the top, career first — **Overview** (today, the month, extra time, **penalties so far**, leave left, money owed or advance held; pending applications; last payslip with Print; the target card; quick links), **Work** (attendance grid + working rules · activity breakup · **Penalties & extras**), **Leave** (balance per kind, every application, **Withdraw** on a pending one, this month's holidays), **Pay** (payslips · salary structure · money ledger · target), **Me** (job as HR keeps it, read-only · personal & bank · ID card · profile · security), and **Social** last (wall · friends · activity timeline · about), each loaded only when opened. The open group and view live in `?tab=&view=` so a link can land on the right place. **Penalties are new data**: [`GET /api/hr/me`](src/app/api/hr/me/route.ts) now also returns `effect` — the month's netting from `loadHoursBreakup` run through payroll's own [`payRuleLines`](src/modules/hr/lib/payRulesApply.ts) against the salary revision in force, so the estimate and the eventual slip are the same arithmetic; with no structure or no earning it says why instead of showing zero. **Paths corrected**: a colleague's profile is [`/office/user/[id]`](src/app/(protected)/office/user/[id]/page.tsx) (your own id there redirects to the portal), `/office/hr/me` redirects to `/office/me` and its sidebar link reads **My Portal** on the same `ui:sidebar:hr:me` tick, `/office/me` and `/office/user` are the free routes in place of `/office/social`, and every link in the app (`socialActor`, Org pages, Admin → Users, customers, the welcome page, the Sidebar avatar) was rewritten. **DRY**: the 1,400-line profile page is split into [`src/modules/social/components/`](src/modules/social/components/profileTypes.ts) (header, sidebar, wall, about, activity, edit profile, security, friends, org position), the wall and activity timeline now fetch for themselves when opened rather than on every visit, `StaffMoneyTab` takes `canPay` so the portal hides *Pay salary / advance* from a person without `hr:payroll:run`, and the old `MyHrPage` is gone — its tiles live in [`shared/components/StatCard.tsx`](src/shared/components/StatCard.tsx). No schema, no migration, no seed, no new permission; the owner's 2026-09-20 rule stands — no new read gates on the HR APIs.
- **2026-09-26 — A staff salary ledger cannot be deleted from the chart of accounts.** Owner, on the Job tab's *Their salary ledger* line: *"what happen if some one delete the salary ledger ?"*, then *"ok block delete"*. The ledger delete route already refused a ledger with posted entries, so the hole was an **empty** one — a new joiner's before their first month posts. Deleting it blanked `StaffProfile.ledgerAccountId` (SET NULL) and the profile made a fresh one on its next save, which was fine; but a **draft payroll run** generated before the delete keeps the old id on `PayrollRunLine.ledgerAccountId` (a plain number, no FK), and posting it wrote the credit to a ledger that no longer existed — `VoucherGlLine.accountId` has no FK either, and the engine skips the balance delta for an account it cannot find rather than refusing. Salary & Wages went up, the person's owed amount landed nowhere, the trial balance went out by that net, and nothing said so. Now `DELETE /api/accountancy/ledgers/[id]` refuses any ledger a `StaffProfile` points at, naming the person ([route.ts](src/app/api/accountancy/ledgers/[id]/route.ts)); `GET /api/accountancy/account-groups?withLedgers=1` carries `isStaffLedger` (one small read of `staff_profiles`, not a join across the chart) and the Ledgers page tags the row **Staff** and locks its Delete beside Customer / Company. **Not done (offered, not taken):** the engine refusing any GL line whose ledger does not exist, and a live check for GL lines already pointing at a deleted ledger. No schema, seed or permission change.
- **2026-09-27 — Leave requests can be deleted.** Owner: *"let delete"* on HR → Leave. New `DELETE /api/hr/leave/[id]` (`deleteLeaveRequest` in leaveServer.ts): HR (`hr:leave:approve`) any request, the requester only their own pending one — the same line as Cancel. An approved one takes its LEAVE days back off the register in the same transaction, as Cancel does. Bin icon on each row, confirm first.
- **2026-09-26 — One home for each staff detail.** Owner: *"audit for similar information … so those are not disagree"*, then *"do all four fixes"*. (1) `PUT /api/users/[id]` takes every field optional and writes only what is sent; HR's role/manager save (`StaffRoleFields`), the Access tab's login and password saves, and Edit User send only their own fields — before, HR's save re-sent a loaded copy of name/email/title/phone and could undo a Profile edit or a Security rename. (2) Job title is the HR seat's: the Profile tab lost Job Title, `PUT` no longer writes `User.title`, and the ID card and profile header use `jobTitleOf` (seat first, an old `User.title` only for someone with no seat). (3) Staff phone and emergency phone share `staffPhoneSchema` (10-digit Indian mobile; spaces, dashes, +91 / 0 stripped) and the `STAFF_PHONE_HINT` placeholder — the Profile box said `+880`. (4) The sign-in email is renamed only on the person's own **Security** tab (Company email box beside the forward, `/api/users/[id]/forward-email` with `handle` — Cloudflare rule moves first, then the login) or by an admin on HR → Access; `PUT` refuses `email` from a non-admin, and Admin → Users' Edit User shows it read-only. The Profile tab's Email box is gone. Self password change now asks for the current password. Security tab lays its cards side by side, Counter PIN first. HR & People lost its **My Portal** link — the sidebar name/photo already opens `/office/me`; `/office/hr/me` still redirects there for old grants and bookmarks.
- **2026-09-26 — Staff Directory loses Delete login.** Owner: *"remove the delete login button"*. It deleted the whole person (login, seats, HR profile, salary history) while sitting in the Login card as if it only stopped sign-in. The Access tab now only edits the login; deleting a person is done on Admin → Users alone. The `selfId` / `canDeleteUsers` / `onDeleted` props went with it. Same day, owner: *"need forward gmail input box"* → the Login card gains a **Forward to** row (Gmail box + Save + status), hidden when the address is not a company one; it and the profile Forward Email card share `useForwardEmail`. Then *"remove the forward to from the modal"* and *"i dont like the ui modal, make it on the page"*: the Access tab has no popup any more — three open sections, **Login details** (name + sign-in email, Give company email), **Mail forward** and **Password** (set a new one, signs them out everywhere), all on the same `/api/users/[id]` routes. **Role** and **Reports to** moved to the Job tab (`StaffRoleFields`). The shared Add/Edit User dialog lost its Forward to box; a new login still forwards to the office default.
- **2026-09-25 — Admin → Users and the Staff Directory share add / edit / delete; the shield button works again.** Owner: *"both page doing same thing"*, then *"keep but remove HR profile link, and make sure edit/delete/add user sync if i do from any page"*. Both pages keep their list. They now use one `UserForm` for Add and Edit (the HR Access tab gets a **Login** card with **Edit login** / **Delete login**), one delete helper ([deleteUserLogin.ts](src/shared/lib/users/deleteUserLogin.ts)) and the same `/api/users` routes. Each write announces `settings`, and both pages listen to it. If the person open in the directory is deleted elsewhere, the pane goes back to the list. Admin → Users loses its HR-profile button and keeps the **shield**, the emergency per-person permission box. The shield was broken three ways:
  - **Save.** It called a `PUT` that did not exist. It now saves every change in one transaction through a new batch [`PUT /api/users/[id]/permissions`](src/app/api/users/[id]/permissions/route.ts), then signs the person out once. The single-row POST / `[permissionId]` DELETE had no callers and are gone.
  - **Seat roles.** It counted only `User.role`, not the roles that come with a person's seats. It now reads both, like sign-in does. Both paths go through the same `applyPermissionOverrides` in [crudLadder.ts](src/shared/lib/auth/crudLadder.ts).
  - **DENY shown as a grant.** It showed a DENY as "+Grant". Fixed.

  Guards: `admin:bypass` is never listed, and nobody can grant what they do not hold. Server tightening:
  - A password reset or role change by someone else now really signs that person out (`PUT /api/users/[id]` and the password PATCH).
  - `DELETE /api/users/[id]` needs `users:delete` itself, where the edit rung used to be enough, and refuses deleting your own login.
  - **Add staff** shows on `users:create`, the rule the server enforces, not `hr:staff:edit`.
- **2026-09-25 — Staff can reset a forgotten password, and set where their staff mail goes.** Staff addresses (`name@networkservice.in`) have no mailbox — Cloudflare Email Routing forwards them. New **My Profile → Security → Forward Email** card writes that forward from the app ([cloudflareRouting.ts](src/shared/lib/email/cloudflareRouting.ts), [`/api/users/[id]/forward-email`](src/app/api/users/[id]/forward-email/route.ts)): the person or `users:edit` (admin sees the Security tab too, minus Counter PIN), Cloudflare is the only record (no DB column), a new inbox gets Cloudflare's own verify mail first. Needs `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` / `_ACCOUNT_ID` / `_ZONE_ID` on the office; elsewhere the card says so. New public [/staff/forgot-password](src/app/staff/forgot-password/page.tsx) + [`/api/staff/password-reset`](src/app/api/staff/password-reset/route.ts): 6-digit code to the staff address on the portal's signed-cookie OTP (own cookie), same answer whether or not the account exists, throttled; a reset bumps `tokensValidFrom`. Link on staff sign-in. Also a **Counter PIN** card on the same tab (set / remove on this or all counters; still asks for the password — replacing that with an email code is next). Add User now takes only the name part and fixes `@networkservice.in` (`COMPANY_DETAILS.staffMailDomain`); nothing is created in Cloudflare — the zone's catch-all takes a new address's mail until a forward is set, and the card says where. A Gmail added once and never verified is removed and re-added on Save, which is how Cloudflare re-sends its verify mail. **2026-09-26:** Add / Edit User carries a **Forward to** box (prefilled `networkaby@gmail.com` = `COMPANY_DETAILS.staffMailDefaultForward`, or the current forward on edit); `POST /api/users` and `PUT /api/users/[id]` take an optional `forwardTo` and run `syncStaffForwarding` after the save — create if Cloudflare has no rule, edit if it has, and a renamed address hands its rule over. A Cloudflare refusal comes back as `forwardingError` and never undoes the login. Logins on another domain get a **Give company email** link (switches to `name@`, forwards to the old address). Password box is `autoComplete="new-password"` — Chrome was filling the admin's own password into Edit User. **2026-09-27:** the reset route was hardened and allowlisted in `check-guard-lint.ts` (it is public by design). Wrong codes are now counted on the server too, 5 per 15 min per address keyed on the cookie's address, because the tries count in the cookie can be replayed from 0 by a script (later the same day moved into `checkOtpCode`, shared with the portal sign-in — see the In flight entry); the code mail goes out with `after()` so a real address answers as fast as a made-up one; and a code is mailed only to a staff-domain User not marked as left (same checks as sign-in). _Rakesh's first save was refused by Cloudflare at the rule step — reason not yet read._

Older history lives in `git log`. This section is trimmed; don't grow it past ~10 entries.

- **2026-09-25 — A shift can be deleted once nothing is on it, whichever shift it is.** Owner, on an open Shift #21 whose vouchers he had already deleted: *"shift should be available to delete if there is no transaction/voucher in the shift."* The detail page hid Delete on **till movement ≠ ₹0** — a whole-account figure (till balance now − opening float) that any deleted *older* voucher on that cash account moves; here a ₹1 voucher from #19 had carried through #20 into #21's opening, so deleting it left #21 at −₹1 with nothing on it. The server never had that check, and its own guard counted only RECEIPT/PAYMENT vouchers — POS sales were invisible to it. One guard now, [`shiftDeleteBlockers`](src/modules/pos/lib/shiftManage.ts), run by the delete route and returned by `GET /api/pos/shifts/[id]` as `deleteBlockers` for the page: **any** live voucher with `sourceShiftId` = the shift (not deleted, not cancelled, the shift's own opening/closing contra and variance journal excluded — the delete cancels those) blocks, naming up to five voucher numbers; so do cash drops. **The cashier handover is part of the delete, not a blocker** (owner: *"the cashier handover, other shift closing, opening voucher are part of delete mechanism"*): the `PosHandover` row the shift gave or received is removed and the other shift's `handoverToShiftId` cleared, in the same transaction that cancels the shift's own contras and variance journal — the giving shift stays CLOSED with its own close vouchers, it just no longer points at a successor. An AWAITING_HANDOVER shift now shows Delete too. Dropped for delete: till movement, and the "only the latest shift on a counter" rule — deleting an older shift cancels its sweep, which moves the later shifts' till figures; the owner accepts that. Reopen keeps the latest-shift rule. No schema, seed or permission change.

- **2026-09-25 — Every child window closes the moment any window lands on sign-in.** Owner, with a signed-out main window and a POS tab still showing Shift #19 and Hand over beside it: *"how logout keep open some window"*. Nothing in the app ever closed a second window: "New POS", "Office" and every receipt open one with `window.open`, and the Electron side only hardened it. Sign-out is decided per window — each watches its own session and hears of a sign-out elsewhere only through next-auth's BroadcastChannel, which fires solely when `signOut()` is called (an Electron probe confirmed the broadcast does reach a `noopener` child). A cookie gone cold after 6 idle hours, or a token an admin revoked, sends the main window to sign-in through `goLogin()` with no broadcast; a POS tab beside it, visible and idle with an empty cart, never re-checks and keeps the stale screen until its next API call. [`main.ts`](electron/main.ts) now keeps a `childWindows` set (filled in `did-create-window`) and `closeChildWindowsOnLogin` watches every app window's `did-navigate` / `did-navigate-in-page`: a URL under `/staff/login` closes every child, and a child reaching it also brings the main window up from the tray so nobody is left with an empty desktop. Every route to sign-in, not only a button press — the owner's call. Verified with two Electron probes of the same wiring (main to login closes both children; a child to login with main hidden closes both and shows main); `electron:compile` and ESLint clean. _Not yet run in the packaged app — the owner's smoke test._
- **2026-09-25 — The lock screen's two till cards are one card, and "next duty" is the headline.** Owner, looking at the screen: *"along with cash we can show other payment method also; we can merge both card; next day opening little big (important)."* [`DutyCard`] and [`CounterShiftCard`] are gone; [`TillCard`](src/app/staff/login/TillCard.tsx) draws one `.duty` with three blocks in the order they matter to the man standing there — **Next duty** as a lit hero panel (day in 20px, time in 40px, the closure as an amber tag), **Today** (worked of duty with the bar, then a tile strip: verdict, overtime, away, late — only what happened; the month's netting was dropped on the owner's word, *"drop the month line, keep the card shorter"* — it belongs on the payslip), and **the drawer**, which leads with the figure the next person has to verify: on a deliberate handover the cash the cashier **counted**, books' expectation beside it; on a plain sign-out — *"in case the user can't return from break or any emergency"* — the books' expected cash, then every payment method the counter takes, two to a row, ₹0 rows kept but dimmed. The two data rules are unchanged: the day is still read once from `sessionStorage`, the drawer still fetched silently from `/api/counter/shift`, and the card renders nothing when neither is there. `buildShiftTenderBreakdown` gained `includeZero` — the lock screen asks for it, the close report and the receipt do not, so a quiet evening now says "UPI ₹0" instead of hiding the method. Browser-verified off a counter shell with a stashed day and a mocked shift response; the counter branch of the API is unchanged.
- **2026-09-24 — Cashier handover is now "the whole drawer to whoever signs in next", checked on the lock screen and accepted by signing in.** Owner, on the old Hand-to-cashier tab (a carry-over float box and a "no eligible cashiers" dead end): *"we have drop vault option separately, here we are transferring the whole"* — then *"A counter and clicked handover it logged out, and the lock screen show waiting handover with expected cash ### and card ###, so next user see on lock screen"*, and on a plain sign-out *"B can see on screen before login and after login the details, still he accepting its B's responsibility."* **The flow:** A counts denominations on the close-shift screen and clicks *Hand over & sign out* — no float, nobody named, nothing to the vault; the shift goes `AWAITING_HANDOVER` with a `PENDING` [PosHandover](prisma/schema/pos.prisma) carrying A's count and `denominationsJson`. A plain sign-out leaves the shift `OPEN`. **Either way the lock screen** ([TillCard](src/app/staff/login/TillCard.tsx) (then `CounterShiftCard`) off the unauthenticated, `thisCounterId()`-fenced [/api/counter/shift](src/app/api/counter/shift/route.ts)) shows whose drawer it is, expected cash and every method collected; while it or the duty card is up, the showcase steps aside and the cards sit level with the sign-in card in one row (`.stage:has(.duty)`). **The next person to sign in** — the sign-in is their confirmation — gets the information-only [ShiftTakeoverDialog](src/modules/pos/components/ShiftTakeoverDialog.tsx) (A's denominations when counted, the same four drawer cards as the close dialog, now shared in [ShiftSummaryCards](src/modules/pos/components/ShiftSummaryCards.tsx) off one server builder [shiftSummary.ts](src/modules/pos/lib/shiftSummary.ts)): **Accept** or **Reject & sign out**. A busy counter picked in the open-shift dialog offers *Review & take over* instead of the old "in use" dead end. **A coming back first** carries on as if nothing happened — the handover is quietly cancelled ([currentShift.ts](src/modules/pos/lib/currentShift.ts)). **The books, on accept** ([takeover route](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/takeover/route.ts), one transaction, the claim is the status write so two accepts cannot both win): A's variance from A's count (none when nobody counted — the drawer passes at the books' figure and is B's from then on), the day's safe drops swept to the vault (the old accept left them stranded on the till), A's shift CLOSED, B's OPEN with the whole drawer as opening cash (the old accept recorded B's float wrong), the handover row ACCEPTED — written then, as the audit trail, when A never asked for one. **Fixed in passing:** a second handover after a cancel hit the unique `fromShiftId` (now an upsert); B could end up with two open shifts. **Retired:** `handovers/[id]/accept`, `shifts/pending-handover`, `shifts/handover-candidates`, `HandoverAcceptDialog`; `countedByBPaise` / `carryOverPaise` stay for old rows only. **Schema:** migration `20260924120000_whole_drawer_handover` (`toUserId` + `declaredByAPaise` nullable, `denominationsJson` added) — **counters take the build before the `d_office` deploy.** **Not done:** a handover slip print; the manager's force-close still only offers OPEN shifts (less needed now — anyone allowed can take over).

- **2026-09-21 — The old Account Group detail page is gone; every group drill-in lands on Group Report.** Owner, with the two screens side by side: *"use new page, rewiring"*. The old `masters/groups/[id]` page showed a group as a flat list of names and one closing balance; Group Report shows the same group with **opening / debit / credit / closing** for a period, and it gets that from the one trial-balance read that already feeds the screen — so the figures agree with Trial Balance by construction. What blocked the switch was that Group Report kept its selection in React state, so nothing could link *into* a group. The selection now lives in the **URL** (`?group=<id>&from=&to=`, `asOf` accepted as the closing date for a Balance Sheet drill), which makes the screen linkable and gives back/forward for free. One builder, [`groupReportUrl`](src/modules/accountancy/lib/groupReportUrl.ts), owns the URL; all five call sites went through it — Balance Sheet, P&L, Trial Balance, the shared [`DrillRow`](src/modules/accountancy/components/DrillRow.tsx) `kind: "group"` target, and the group badge on the ledger explorer. The old route stays as a **redirect** so a bookmark still works, and its now-unused API `groups/[id]/detail` is deleted. **The Stock-in-Hand special case did not survive, and should not have:** the old page had to bounce that group to the stock summary because its API knew nothing about stock — the trial-balance read synthesises **Closing Stock (live)**, so Group Report shows it like any other group (verified on screen at 859,189.42 Dr). Masters → Groups, the list editor, is untouched. _Verified in the browser on :3000: all five drill-ins land with the right group and period, in-page selection and Esc rewrite the URL, the old URL forwards; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 on an 8 GB heap, eslint clean._

- **2026-09-21 — The department pool stops being a save-time gate; a ticked box is now always saved.** Owner, on the Role page refusing Group Report with *"Not saved — outside the department pool: ui:sidebar:accountancy:group-report"*: *"department page is basket of module, nothing else … role are grouped in module, module are grouped in department"* and *"the concept of basket is limited to display in role page only, which module are available to tick in role page. not a security gate."* Live data was healthy — `DEP-OP` held 26 Accountancy links, the `ui:sidebar:accountancy:group-report` `Permission` row existed, and `expandSidebarModules()` does expand the module to all 27 current links — so the drop came from an office build predating the link, but the gate that produced it was the real problem. [`PUT /api/roles/[id]/permissions`](src/app/api/roles/[id]/permissions/route.ts) **no longer re-filters the submitted ids against the department pool**: it persists what the editor sent. That deleted the whole hole-punching apparatus the gate needed — the `isUnpooled` carve-outs (Works / POS / Workspace / `POOL_EXEMPT_PERMISSION_IDS`) and the four *unlock* sets (module-level sidebar, per-link granular CRUD, per-link extra toggles, module extra toggles) — along with the route's `getDepartmentPool` / `SIDEBAR_MODULES` / `roleMatrixConfig` imports. The response's `dropped` now carries one bucket, `unseeded` (no `Permission` row, and none in code for `ensureCatalogPermissions()` to mint), and the Role page's second toast branch went with it. **Unchanged on the owner's word:** taking a module off a department still cascade-strips those grants from the department's roles and revokes their sessions — a pool change acts on grants there and nowhere else; and a page added to an already-ticked module still appears on the Role page silently without disturbing the department chip. No schema change. Typecheck clean; the route compiles and still guards (401 unauthenticated). _Verification gap: the authenticated Role-editor click-through is unrun — it needs a signed-in session on the office app._
- **2026-09-20 — A page added to a module a department already has is offered on the Role page at once; the department chip stays ticked.** Owner: *"as soon as we add/edit permission or add new page the department become unticked, so we cant tick that page in role page"* — and the Departments save answered *Unknown permission(s): ui:sidebar:settings:services*. The owner's rule, restated: **a department is a UI filter for the Role page, not a gate** — it says which modules are relevant to the roles homed there, and each page is then ticked per role. Two faults. **(1)** The pool is stored one row per link, and the Departments chip was ticked only when *every* link of the module was stored — a new link is never stored, so the chip went off. Yet the chip only ever toggles a whole module, so "any link stored" already meant "the department has the module". [`getDepartmentPool()`](src/shared/lib/auth/departmentPool.ts) now **expands a pooled module to every current link from code** (`expandSidebarModules`), and the chip reads `.some(...)`; the Role page and role API already gated per module with the same `.some`, so all three agree. Vouchers and loose permissions pass through untouched. **(2)** The link's `Permission` row existed in no database until `sync-permissions` or a full seed ran, so the pool save was rejected and the role save silently dropped the tick (`dropped.unseeded`). The catalog moved to [`permissionCatalog.ts`](src/shared/lib/auth/permissionCatalog.ts) in `src/shared` (`codeOwnedCatalog.ts` re-exports it, so both seeds and `check-doc-sync` are unchanged in behaviour), and new [`ensureCatalogPermissions()`](src/shared/lib/auth/ensurePermissions.ts) creates any code-known row the DB lacks — called first by the Departments pool PUT and the Role permissions PUT. Never deletes; the prune stays with the seeds. **Deliberately left alone:** the role API still strips grants outside the pool when a role changes department — owner said department is only a filter, but removing that is a separate decision — and nothing grants a new page to any role automatically. No schema change, no migration. **Same day, the reverse direction is a clean removal** (owner: *"is permission also removed for those"* → *"yes clean removal"*). Unticking a module on Departments already stripped the module's `ui:sidebar:*` grants from the department's roles, but left behind the action permissions those links had unlocked (create/edit/delete, per-link extra toggles, module toggles) and did not revoke sessions, so holders kept the module until their next login. The pool PUT now compares module-expanded sets, strips the dependents too via [`idsUnlockedByLinks`](src/modules/admin/lib/roleMatrixConfig.tsx) — the same maps the Role save uses to *allow* them, so allow and strip cannot drift — keeps any id a still-pooled link also unlocks, and calls `revokeRoleSessions` for every role in the department when anything was stripped. The toast says "holders signed out".

- **2026-09-18 — The counter's sign-in screen now offers a face to tap and six digits to type, instead of a full email address and a password.** Owner: *"problem is typing long username and password is time killing"* — the slowest minute of the shop's day, at the worst moment of it. Staying signed in across a reboot was never the gap: `hasStaffSession()` in [main.ts](electron/main.ts) has always walked straight into the app when the cookie is there. This is the sign-in that genuinely has to happen — after a real sign-out, a shift handover, an expired session. **The shell now paints a grid of the people who have signed in on that PC**, each a card with their profile photo and their position under the same greeting the form wears; tap yours, type a 6-digit PIN, you are in. **Per counter, and that IS the security model.** `UserCounterPin` ([pos.prisma](prisma/schema/pos.prisma)) holds one row per person per counter, never a column on `User` — every till keeps a replica of that table, so a PIN that worked everywhere would turn one stolen counter PC into a key to every counter. A PIN set on counter 2 is refused on counter 5 until they sit there and set another. **Six digits, not four** — the hash rides down to every replica, and 10,000 possibilities fall in minutes off a stolen machine. **It is not a credential on the website at all**: `authorizeStaffPin` ([authorizeStaff.ts](src/shared/lib/auth/authorizeStaff.ts)) refuses unless `COUNTER_MACHINE_ID` *and* `counter-setup.json` both answer — the env var the Next child gets from the Electron shell, which `releaseGuard` and `writeRelay` already trust, and which Vercel never sets. The counter id is deliberately **not** taken from the request: a counter id the client chooses is a counter id an attacker chooses. **The same session either way** — `buildStaffAuthUser` was extracted from the password path and is shared, so six digits can never buy a different set of permissions, and the two cannot drift. **A PIN never replaces the password.** Two clocks on the row: `lastPasswordAt` forces the real password after **10 days**, `lastLoginAt` after **3 idle days**, whichever bites first; typing the password resets both and the PIN survives. The counter stamps them itself (the one thing it is `localAuthority` for), because the till has to judge this with the line down. **"Wrong PIN" and "password time" stay distinguishable** — [`/api/counter-pin/status`](src/app/api/counter-pin/status/route.ts) exists only for that, since a correct PIN refused in silence reads as a broken till; it answers nothing but none/ready/password-due, only on a counter, and an unknown address looks identical to one with no PIN. **The whole sign-in is one sequence now, on both screens.** Owner, after three rounds of patching: *"i broke the original sequence you created, re-create not just patch"*. Four states — **faces → keypad → password → choosePin** — with the same names in `resources/shell/login.html` and in [staff/login/page.tsx](src/app/staff/login/page.tsx), and **every state has a way out**. Before this the photographs existed in exactly ONE situation, a cold boot with no session cookie: every sign-out, expiry, counter release, proxy bounce and refused PIN dropped a cashier onto a bare email box on a PC that had a photograph of them on disk. The page now reads the same roster the shell does — the preload was attached there all along and simply never used. **Three one-way doors closed**, all from one link doing two jobs: a face with no PIN, "Someone else" where nobody had a PIN, and a keypad tapped before the server was ready (which froze with six digits buffered and said nothing; it now holds them and goes the moment the server answers). **The stale-PIN check had never once run** — the shell is a `file://` page, so its relative `fetch("/api/counter-pin/status")` resolved to `file:///api/…` and died in a silent catch; it goes through main now. **A roster used to hide the boot status entirely**, the bar and tips living inside the password form the faces card hides; the status sits under both cards. **And on a v_live till, setting a PIN could never have worked**: both counter-pin routes are non-GET, so they relayed to the office, where `COUNTER_MACHINE_ID` is unset and the route correctly refused its own counter — they are on `NEVER_RELAY` now, asserted in `check-counter-mode.ts`. Nothing is lost by staying local: the write already goes to the office through `prismaAuthoritative`, and the status read stays on the replica, which is what makes a PIN work offline. Setting a PIN is still a step on the sign-in card, before the app opens, with Skip — the password is in hand there, so no new door had to be opened to ask for it. **The card names every seat a person holds, with its scope.** Owner: *"name, position, branch — for executive, name, executive (or what applicable), executive council"*. So the line under a name is no longer one role label: it is each `Position` they hold, senior first, with **the branch it sits in** beneath it — and for a seat with no branch, the words this business already uses for org-wide, **Executive Council** (the heading the Positions page and the Org Chart both group those seats under). Rakesh Biswas reads *Branch Manager / Head Office* and *Executive / Executive Council*, because holding two seats is exactly the thing a face on a till has to answer for. The scope rule was already written inside [admin/positions/page.tsx](src/app/(protected)/office/admin/positions/page.tsx) and is now one shared function, [positionScope.ts](src/shared/lib/org/positionScope.ts), used by both — the admin page keeps its fuller "Head Office / Managment", the 151px counter card takes the branch alone. The seats ride in the session (two short strings each) so the roster can be written at sign-in, and **`User.title` still wins outright** where anybody has typed one in. **The photograph travels as a data URL, and it follows the web.** Owner, on the finished screen: *"where is the photo and position"* — initials and a blank line, on a till whose cached photographs were sitting on disk the whole time. Two faults. The roster handed the page a `file://` path: the shell is a `file://` document and could load one, but the app's sign-in page is served over http, and Chromium refuses a `file://` subresource there — so the shell would have shown faces and the page never could. Both get a `data:` URL now, typed by sniffing the first bytes so every photograph already cached keeps working, with the face cache capped at 1MB since it is carried as base64. And the position came from `User.title`, which is **null for every person in the real books**; the designation people actually hold is their role, which `authorizeStaff` already resolves to a label — "Role-Admin", "Counter Operator". Title first, role label behind it. **The faces are a cache, never an authority.** `counter-roster.json` + `faces/` in userData, written by `/staff/login` on the way past and capped at 12; a hand-edited roster buys nobody a permission, and "Edit list → ×" forgets a face on that PC and touches nothing on the server.
