A person with no PIN still shows — their card fills the email in and asks for the password, because a card that quietly disappeared would be the most confusing thing on that screen. **Honest weakness, written into the code**: the clocks live with the counter, so someone with file access to the PC could wind one back. They already hold the machine and its data; the per-counter binding and the web refusal are what carry the weight. **Migration [20260918090000](prisma/migrations/20260918090000_counter_pin/migration.sql) — counter release ships and a till is verified on it BEFORE the Vercel deploy** (AGENTS.md §3), since `user_counter_pins` is a synced table and a server ahead of a replica halts every table on that till. Gate, PIN shapes and both clocks exercised directly; `pnpm precommit` and both tsconfigs clean. **Not yet verified on a real counter** — the shell is a `file://` page only the Electron window renders, so the packaged smoke test is the owner's.
- **2026-09-17 — Every voucher now names the branch it was raised at, and "All branches" can no longer post.** Owner, reading the Day Book: *"why some voucher dont have branch, voucher are create from some branch?"* — the shift-close Contra `CV-10792-26` and the variance Journal `UV-3725` both showed `—` while the Payments beside them said Head Office. **Two different faults wearing the same blank.** `UniversalVoucher.branchId` was made nullable in Phase 5 5D-12 on the reading that a money voucher can be "org-level", and [`POST /api/accountancy/vouchers`](src/app/api/accountancy/vouchers/route.ts) resolved the working branch inside a `try/catch` that, on the combined **"All branches"** sentinel, **saved null rather than refusing** — so org-level was never a state anybody chose, it was whatever the picker happened to be on. Worse, the two vouchers the POS raises at shift close passed **no branch at all** ([close route](src/app/api/pos/shifts/[id]/close/route.ts)) though `shift.branch` was already loaded three lines up — a till's own closing paperwork filing itself under no branch. **A blank branch is not neutral**: every branch-wise report filters on this column, so those vouchers are absent from all of them and absent silently — the totals just come out short. **The guard already existed and had zero callers.** [`requireWritableBranchId`](src/shared/lib/auth/activeBranch.ts) refuses the sentinel with `select_working_branch`, [`branchAccessResponse`](src/shared/lib/api/branchAccessResponse.ts) turns it into a 400, and [http.ts](src/shared/lib/http.ts) already had the toast — *"Switch from 'My branches' to a specific branch to post this transaction."* Wiring it up was most of the fix; it now also guards POS shift-open and POS order, which used `resolveActiveBranchId` and would have let the sentinel through as if it were a branch id. **The rest is enforced by the compiler, not by review.** `branchId` became a **required** argument on `engineCreateVoucher` / `enginePost` / `engineCreateSpine` / `postContra` / `postReceiptVoucher` / `repostParkedVoucherInTx`, and `enginePost`'s `opts = {}` default was dropped — which is what found the remaining holes: POS sale/return/lost, both ecommerce order routes, both gift-card routes, the manufacturing-charges leg and the trading post in [postVoucherInTx](src/modules/inventory/lib/postVoucherInTx.ts), whose comment had recorded the blank as intentional ("GL JE lines stay branch-less, exactly as the legacy bridge produced them"). A `?? ""` fallback in the POS return route — an empty string standing in for a branch id — went too. **No user, no picker → Head Office** (owner's decision): one [`systemBranchId`](src/shared/lib/org/systemBranch.ts) now owns that rule for the gift-card breakage sweep and the online-receipt path, and absorbed **three** separate hardcoded `const HQ_BRANCH_ID = "BR-HQ"` copies, one of which had no fallback at all. **History is backfilled** by [migration 20260917140000](prisma/migrations/20260917140000_voucher_branch_required/migration.sql) — counter's branch first (exact: a till belongs to one branch), then the shift's, then Head Office — so it reaches live and every replica on deploy. `pnpm backfill:voucher-branch` previews the same thing against live and lists the Head Office bucket in full, that being the only guessed step. **The column is NOT NULL, and getting there needed the sync guard fixed.** `schemaGuard.js` compared nullability as a bare string, so any difference halted a till's whole worker — and a replica can never receive a nullability change (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` is a no-op on an existing column). It is now DIRECTIONAL: replica-nullable against a NOT NULL source is reported, not fatal, because the replica only permits what the source never sends; the reverse still halts, as do type changes and missing columns. **The counter release must still go out before the deploy** (AGENTS.md §3) — a till on the old guard halts regardless. Backfill measured and applied on live 2026-09-17: 76 blanks, 10 exact from their counter, 66 all OPENING rows of 1 Apr 2026; 0 remain of 3,714. **Two more writers only the required column could expose**: `openingBills.ts`, which is what created those 66, and the ecommerce/online-receipt paths. Verified against the running app — "All branches" answers 400 `select_working_branch`, a named branch posts.
- **2026-09-15 — A half-arrived phone number is now read as half a number, not as a foreign caller.** Owner, on the call follow-up popup: *"why the last no is looks not correct?"* — a chip reading **`+91600204286`**. Count the digits after the country code and there are **nine**. The EPBX delivers an incoming CLI as an analogue signal **digit by digit**; if the handset is lifted before the last digit lands, the record keeps only what had arrived — owner's own diagnosis. The display was the smaller half of the fault: [`formatCallerNumber`](src/shared/lib/format/phone.ts) prefixed `+` to anything over ten digits, so a **part number was dressed up as a valid international one**. The real damage was in **`phoneMatchKey`**, which took the **last ten digits of anything**, checking nothing: this capture became the key **`1600204286`** — a number that exists nowhere — and because that key was not null it **passed the popup's only guard** (`c.phone !== null`) and nagged the counter about a caller nobody could ever ring back, while also putting junk into `@@index([phone])` where two unrelated broken captures could in principle collide. **The fix is one shape rule, read once.** New `readCallerNumber()` classifies a capture before anything downstream trusts it — `india` (reduces to a whole 10-digit subscriber number, however the PBX dressed it: bare, trunk `0`, `91`, `+91`), `international` (11–15 digits), `partial` (`91` + fewer than ten, or under ten — the cut-off cases), `none`. `phoneMatchKey` now answers **null** for a partial, exactly as it already did for an internal extension, so a part number can never become a match key; `isCallableNumber()` states the popup's law in its own words — **only a complete Indian or international number may appear there** — and the display shows `91600204286…`, visibly incomplete, with no inventing `+`. **And staff can complete it.** Owner: *"for 9 digit there 10 chances so nearest may be more then one… let us confirm the predicted number."* So the shortlist is offered only when **exactly one digit is missing** (`PARTIAL_CLI_GUESS_DIGITS` = 9 — ten possible numbers, a list short enough to choose from; shorter captures are rare and would list half the phone book), the Call Log shows **"N possible"** instead of Unknown, and a **"Who called?"** dialog lists each candidate with their full number. Picking one and confirming writes it into `Call.phone` through **`PATCH /api/calls/[id]` `confirmPhone`**, and the row becomes a **normal matched call** everywhere — customer named, full number shown, back in the counter popup (its rule is now "a complete number **or** a call that resolves to a customer", which still keeps the stale junk keys out, since they resolve to nobody). **And a wrong CLI can be set by hand.** Owner, on the next row down: *"38446 is a real call but its cli mistake. let put the correct number."* A wrong capture shares **nothing** with the real number, so no shortlist can ever reach it — only the person who took the call knows. That row now offers **"Set caller"** and the same dialog carries a **customer search** (`/api/person?lookup=`, the party pickers' own endpoint), offered beside the shortlist too, since a shortlist can be wrong and the person who answered wins. **The prefix guard was dropped to make this possible** — it was right for a cut-off number and impossible for a wrong one. What guards the write now: the captured number must be **unusable** (a whole, valid number can never be reassigned to someone else), and the answer must **belong to an existing customer** — an identity is confirmed, a number is never invented. The evidence survives either way, because `rawNumber` still holds `38446`, so a staff-supplied number is always visible as exactly that. **"Completed" means the number resolves to a real customer**, which only a human answer produces — the first cut tested "a part number with a key", and every pre-fix row has a junk key, so a stale `1600204286` announced itself completed and then displayed the junk. And the number on screen is now **the digits the prediction is made on** (`600204286…`, country code stripped like every other shape) — owner: *"it should be 9 digit to justify the reason of prediction"*. `confirmPhone: null` **undoes** a wrong pick, and only on a part number, so a genuine match can never be unmatched by accident. **`rawNumber` is never rewritten** — it stays the audit of what the EPBX actually sent, moves to the hover tooltip, and the amber badge becomes a quiet **"completed"**. **The call is not thrown away, though.** On the CRM Call Log the row is badged **"part number"**, and `GET /api/calls` offers a lead: one index-backed `startsWith` query over the stored-phone variants finds customers whose number **begins with the digits that did arrive**, and when **exactly one** matches, the name is shown in amber with a `?` — *ring back to confirm*. Nothing is attached automatically: a shortlist is a **question**, not an answer. Create-lead is hidden on a part number, since it would save a phone nobody can ring. **The country-code rule, owner's own** (2026-09-15, second pass): a number carrying `+91` is Indian, and after the country code the subscriber part may be 9, 10 or more digits — ten is whole, **more than ten keeps the FIRST ten** (a stray keypress, junk lands at the end of what was keyed), fewer than ten is a part number. Two gaps closed by it: `91` + more than ten used to be read as a foreign number and keyed on its **last** ten, sliding off the front; and a short capture kept its `91`, so `9160020` would have hunted for numbers starting `9160020` instead of `60020` — a different caller entirely. **At exactly ten digits the leading `91` is NOT a country code** — `9163456789` is a whole, valid mobile and stripping it would chop a real number in half — so the country-code reading applies at every other length. Where the truncation is genuinely ambiguous (`916345678` is either `91` + seven digits or a ten-digit mobile one short), **both** nine-digit readings are searched. Landlines are unaffected: the rule is about a number's **shape**, not about starting 6–9. Verified against the real captures: `91600204286` and the older malformed `091863809999` both read partial (prefixes `600204286` / `863809999`), the five-digit trunk CLI `38446` stays partial with no guess, and `9954403763` / `919954403763` / `+91 99540-12345` / `09954403763` / landline `03512256789` / an outgoing stray keypress / a Bangladesh number all key and display exactly as before. **Judged on the shape of what the PBX sent, never on whether `phone` is null** — a correction the owner's own screenshot forced: every call already in the log carries the junk key the old slice wrote, so a `!c.phone` test skipped the entire history the counter is looking at and the near-match appeared on nothing. A junk key matches no customer, so those rows are unmatched in fact whatever the column says; the guess keys off the resolved customer instead. The stale keys are left in place — harmless, since nothing matches them — rather than rewritten on live data. **No schema change, no migration.** _Still open: an 11-digit capture starting 6–9 is treated as international and keyed on its last ten — ambiguous by nature (60–98 are real country codes), and unchanged from before._
- **2026-09-13 — A call logged from the counter popup now stays logged on the counter.** Owner's report: on a real till, the call follow-up popup ([CallFollowUpPopup.tsx](src/modules/pos/components/CallFollowUpPopup.tsx)) came back for the same call after staff had saved a note / raised a deal / raised a help ticket — and stayed back for a long time. Two causes, one small and one structural. **Small (fixed 2026-09-12):** a save is two writes — the ticket/deal, then the `PATCH /api/calls/:id` that marks the call handled — and the first publishes the "tickets" realtime signal, so a refresh started while the second was still in flight and put the call straight back on screen. The popup now holds a just-logged id out of the queue until a refresh no longer returns it, and drops out-of-order refresh answers. **Structural (this entry):** a till runs `READ_MODE=local` + `WRITE_MODE=v_live` — the edit goes to Supabase, the popup reads the replica — and `Call` synced as **`append`** on `createdAt`, because it had no `updatedAt`; an edit to an existing call never moves `createdAt`, so the regular pull never brought the note down and only the slow row-hash reconcile eventually did. Same defect the 2026-09-03 entry fixed for `Device`/`PosShift`/`PosHandover`/`CouponRedemption`/`PersonCompany`, and the same cure: `Call` gained `updatedAt DateTime @default(now()) @updatedAt` + `@@index([updatedAt])` ([calls.prisma](prisma/schema/calls.prisma)); the generator now files it as `watermark` (table number 11 unchanged, so no stored watermark is orphaned — 51 watermark / 8 append). Prisma stamps `updatedAt` on `update` and `updateMany` alike, so note, ticket link and the by-number spam flag all move it; no API change. One-time effect on each replica: every `Call` row is re-pulled once, which also brings down whatever notes the tills are currently missing. `sync-tables.json`, `syncRegistry.generated.ts`, `resources/local-schema.sql` regenerated (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, so an older replica self-heals). **Requires `pnpm prisma db push` — owner's step. Per the counter rule, publish the counter build first.** Not verified on a till here; the check is: log one call as a note and one as a deal, both vanish on save and stay gone after a restart.
- **2026-09-11 — The software now knows the date it started holding our books, and refuses a voucher dated before it.** Owner's question: *which was the first FY in this software, and for which FY must we search the previous software or the old register?* Nothing could answer it. `BooksConfig` held only `restartMonth`/`restartDay` — **when** the year turns, never **when we began**; `VoucherSeries.fyStartYear` rolls forward and forgets; and `financialYearFrom` / `booksBeginningFrom` existed only on **`Company`**, the customer/supplier model, so they described a *party's* books, not ours — a gap [`opening-bills-preview.ts`](src/scripts/opening-bills-preview.ts) had already written down in a comment ("no org-level books-beginning date exists, so the conversion must be told one"). The only available answer was `MIN(date)` over the vouchers, which a single mistyped year silently rewrites. **One nullable `@db.Date` anchor** now sits on `BooksConfig` ([finance.prisma](prisma/schema/finance.prisma)): `booksBeginningFrom`. _It started as two, mirroring the `Company` pair, and the owner killed the second on sight — the page showed "Books restart on: April / 1" directly above "First financial year from: 01/04/2026" and they said the same thing twice._ They did: a stored `financialYearFrom` carries **no information** — its month and day ARE `restartMonth`/`restartDay`, and its year is whichever FY contains the books-beginning date, so the first FY here is `fyStartYearFor(restartMonth, restartDay, booksBeginningFrom)`, **derived and displayed, never stored beside the thing it is computed from**. That holds for a mid-year migration too, which was the case the second field was supposed to earn its keep on: books beginning 15 Nov 2023 still derives FY 2023-24. Null by default and **never auto-filled**: the Year Ending page *prefills* the 1st of the current FY from the new `fyStartDate()` in [voucherRef.ts](src/modules/accountancy/lib/voucherRef.ts) — the opening bookend to the existing `fyClosingDate()`, sharing `fyStartYearFor()` so the default can never drift from the restart day the same page sets — but the row stays null until someone presses Save. An install that has never been told its start date says so, instead of quietly claiming this year. The card sits under Books-restart on [`/office/settings/year-ending`](src/app/(protected)/office/settings/year-ending/page.tsx), reuses that card's one Save path, binds `TallyDateInput` (no `<input type="date">` anywhere in office settings), and prints from the **saved** row, not the draft: *"First financial year here: 2023-24. Before 1 Apr 2023 — look in the old books."* **The rule that follows**: a voucher may not be dated before the books begin. `assertVoucherDate()` in the new [booksPeriodServer.ts](src/shared/lib/accountancy/booksPeriodServer.ts) is called from exactly two places in [transactionEngine.ts](src/modules/accountancy/lib/transactionEngine.ts) — `engineCreateSpine` and the create-POSTED branch of `engineGlArm` — which between them cover all 13 voucher routes including POS, ecommerce and gift cards; the ATTACH branch is deliberately **not** guarded, or a draft saved legitimately before the date was set could never be posted. The two routes that can edit a spine's `date` afterwards ([accountancy](src/app/api/accountancy/vouchers/[id]/route.ts), [inventory](src/app/api/inventory/vouchers/[id]/route.ts)) check it again, since editing is otherwise the way round the guard. The boundary day itself is **allowed** — opening balances and opening bills are dated exactly at books-beginning. It **fails open** — a null anchor means no restriction, so every existing install behaves exactly as before — and it is **cached for 60s** on `globalThis`, copying `offlinePoliciesServer.ts` next door, because it runs on every voucher and a query per bill is felt at the till; the settings PATCH drops the cache so the admin sees it bite immediately. One `VoucherDateError → 422` branch in [handleApiError.ts](src/shared/lib/api/handleApiError.ts) covers the whole API surface, same trick the offline case already uses. Opening bills and audit-restore bypass the engine and are therefore exempt by construction. Nothing added to the sync push path on purpose: the guard fires on the counter's own replica, so a bad row never reaches `_outbox`, and a rejection in [`push.ts`](src/shared/lib/sync/push.ts) is a `PermanentPushError` that would dead-letter a legitimate sale. Seeds need no change — `personalSnapshot.ts` spreads the whole `booksConfig` row both ways. `resources/local-schema.sql` regenerated (counters self-add the column via `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`). **Our own value: 1 Apr 2023** — owner confirmed no voucher predates it, so FY 2023-24 is the first FY here and it is complete. **Requires `pnpm prisma db push`.**

- **2026-09-09 — Every party now carries its own bank account, with a scan-to-pay QR, on a new Bank tab.** Paying a supplier or refunding a customer meant hunting for an A/C number in a chat thread or a paper slip. The customer detail panel now has a **Bank** tab beside Profile, for **both Person and Company**, on all three screens that show that panel ([customers/all](src/app/(protected)/office/customers/all/page.tsx), `customers/person`, `customers/companies`) — one shared [`PartyBankTab`](src/modules/customer/components/PartyBankTab.tsx), so the three can never drift, the same arrangement as `PersonProfileTab`.
  - **Two blocks, because Indian payments are two rails.** NEFT/RTGS/IMPS wants beneficiary + bank + branch + A/C no + IFSC as text; UPI wants one id. A/C no and IFSC carry a copy button — those are the two values staff retype into net-banking and the two where a typo costs a reversal. The UPI id renders a live `upi://pay` QR beside the field via the existing [`QrSvg`](src/shared/components/print/QrSvg.tsx) — no second QR library, and no amount in the link, so one saved code covers every future payment.
  - **This is the party's bank, NOT ours.** Our shop's bank (`COMPANY_DETAILS.bank`) prints on the customer's invoice so they can pay us; this one is internal and never printed. A third, separate thing is `Account.bankJson` — the particulars of a bank *ledger* in the chart of accounts. Three neighbours worth keeping straight; each file says so at the top.
  - **One JSON column, `bankJson`, on `Person` and on `Company`** — `{ beneficiary, bankName, branch, accountNumber, ifsc, upiVpa }`, mirroring `Account.bankJson`'s naming so there is nothing new to learn. JSON because the next field a bank asks for should not cost a migration. Shape, parser and the UPI-link builder live in [shared/lib/parties/partyBank.ts](src/shared/lib/parties/partyBank.ts); the parser is total, so a malformed row yields blanks instead of taking down the customer screen.
  - **Saved through the party's existing PUT route**, not a new endpoint — `/api/person/[id]` and `/api/companies/[id]` gained one validated field each, sharing [`resolvePartyBank`](src/shared/lib/schemas/partyBankSchema.ts). IFSC and VPA are format-checked before the row is touched, so a typo returns a sentence rather than a quietly-stored bad account, and an all-blank form writes `DbNull` instead of six empty strings. The tab is readable to everyone and frozen without `customers:edit`, matching the Credit Limit card.
  - **The replica needed nothing by hand.** Person and Company sync whole-row on the `updatedAt` watermark, so `pnpm db:local-schema` picked the column up on its own.

- **2026-09-08 — Every voucher and every ticket now records the counter it was raised at, and the POS register stops showing the newest sale in the middle.** The report was a screenshot: an 08 Sept sale sitting below four 06 Sept ones. The cause was [`/api/pos/sales`](src/app/api/pos/sales/route.ts) ordering by `VoucherDetail.id` alone — the row's auto-number, which records when it was **inserted at the office**, not when it was rung up. A till that spends time offline pushes its backlog later, so the office stamps its **older** sales with **higher** numbers and they float to the top. `UniversalVoucher.date` is `@db.Date` — day only, no time — so it can group days but can never order within one; `sourceCreatedAt` (the faithful posting wall-clock the shift reconciliation already trusts) does the real work. Every register now orders `date desc → sourceCreatedAt desc nulls last → id desc`, with `id` only as a tie-break, which keeps the order a **total** one and so keeps the Day Book's cursor walk gap-free. Fixed in the POS sales, inventory-vouchers, day-book, accountancy-vouchers and journal-entries routes; the Orders/Estimates tab's client-side `.sort((a,b) => b.id - a.id)` is gone, because it was re-imposing the exact ordering being removed.
  - **`sourceCounterId Int?` is new on `UniversalVoucher` and on `Ticket`** — the same column, the same meaning, the same resolver, so one formatter renders both. On the spine it covers **every voucher kind at once**: since Phase 5 all kinds are rows in that one table, so there was nothing per-type to add. Null is a real answer, not a gap: it means the office raised it.
  - **It stores the counter's `id`, not its `counterNumber`, and no FK.** A counter can be **renumbered** (`/api/pos/counters`), and a stored number would silently re-point every historic row at whoever holds that number next; the id never moves. No foreign key because retiring a counter must not erase the provenance of the vouchers it raised, which an optional relation's `SetNull` would quietly do.
  - **Branch is never stored twice.** Counters are unique org-wide, so the counter identifies the branch. A voucher already carries `branchId` on its own row; a ticket has no branch column and reads it off the counter. Rendered `branch-counter` — e.g. `1-24` — by `formatSourceCounter()` in [shared/lib/format/sourceCounter.ts](src/shared/lib/format/sourceCounter.ts), with the spelled-out `Head Office · Counter 24` as the tooltip. Numbers, not names, because the register columns are already tight.
  - **The plumbing to fill it already existed and was being thrown away.** [`writeRelay.ts`](src/shared/lib/counter/writeRelay.ts) has always stamped `x-abeer-counter-number` on every relayed write, and `relayPolicy.ts` said in as many words that "the office ignores it today". Under `WRITE_MODE=v_live` the route code runs on the **office** machine, where `counter-setup.json` belongs to nobody — so reading the local file there would have stamped every relayed sale as "office", losing exactly the rows most worth attributing. New [`sourceCounterId()`](src/shared/lib/counter/sourceCounter.ts) resolves **relayed header → this PC's own setup → null**, and is called once inside `engineCreateSpine`/`engineGlArm`, the two places every voucher kind already passes through. Ten ticket-create sites call it directly.
  - **It takes the caller's `tx`.** Reading through the global client while a transaction is open takes a **second** pool connection and holds it until the first commits — under load that is how a pool starves. Every voucher is written inside a transaction, so that is the common case here. The counter→id map is cached for 5 minutes (under 100 rows, near-static) so the lookup is not paid per save.
  - **Trusting the header is deliberate and bounded.** It is client-supplied and forgeable, and that is acceptable *here* because it is **provenance, never permission** — nothing reads it to decide what anybody may do. An unrecognised number resolves to null rather than being stored, so a bad value can leave a row unattributed but can never attribute it to a counter that does not exist. Tightening it against the `x-abeer-counter-machine` id that travels alongside is available if wanted.
  - **"Only this counter"** is a checkbox in the POS register's shared filter bar, **on by default** — a cashier opening the register wants their own till, and another counter's bills among theirs is the confusion that started this. It covers Sales, Returns, Orders, Estimates and Cash In/Out (the Cash Book was already locked to the active shift's counter) and is disabled with no shift open, there being no "this counter" to mean. Filtered **server-side** via `?counterId=`: the list is paged 50 at a time, so a browser-side filter would sift only the current page and leave `total` and the paging wrong.
  - **The Day Book's client comparator had to move in step.** [`useDayBook.ts`](src/modules/accountancy/hooks/useDayBook.ts)'s `byAge` decides what lies beyond its last loaded row, so a comparator disagreeing with the server's `orderBy` would put the page boundary in the wrong place and silently drop rows. It now mirrors the three-part key exactly, nulls last included.
  - **Old rows stay null and read as "Office".** No backfill: past POS sales cannot be traced to a counter reliably — the voucher number glues the counter digits straight onto the padded serial with no separator ([voucherRef.ts](src/modules/accountancy/lib/voucherRef.ts)), so `POS-910789-26` is either counter 9 serial `10789` or counter 91 serial `0789`, and nothing in the string tells them apart. Guessing would attribute money to the wrong till.
  - _**Schema NOT applied.** `DIRECT_URL` points at the live Supabase pooler, so `prisma db push` was left for the owner — the change is two nullable columns plus indexes, additive and non-destructive, but it is production. Per the counter rule, publish the counter build before applying it. Gates run here: `tsc` clean, ESLint clean, `pnpm seed:check-docs` green. Not verified in a browser._

- **2026-09-07 — My Profile: the customer owns their photo, name, address and contacts; the profile in use is never a mystery.** Before this, `/portal/settings` had a buried "Active profile" dropdown and a change-password card, and nothing else — a customer could not see their own phone or email anywhere, let alone fix a wrong one. Now [/portal/profile](src/app/(external)/portal/profile/page.tsx) (old `/portal/settings` redirects) shows: **who you are** (photo, name, address — editable, optimistic with rollback; a rename also renames the personal Account so bills follow), **your profiles** (personal + every linked company as cards, the one in use marked, "Use this" on the rest), **mobiles & emails** (add / verify / make primary / remove, each row badged Primary · Verified · Login), and the password card. The same switch also lives in the portal header and on checkout as a **"Using: Rahul ▾" chip** ([PortalProfileSwitch.tsx](src/modules/external/portal/PortalProfileSwitch.tsx)) — orders, wallet, coupons and bills follow whichever profile it shows. **One photo per customer:** `Person.avatarUrl` (new) replaces `CustomerSocialProfile.avatarUrl` (dropped); Social shows the same picture and its Edit tab writes it through `PATCH /api/portal/me`. `/api/portal/social/upload-signature` moved to [/api/portal/upload-signature](src/app/api/portal/upload-signature/route.ts). **Verification rule (owner's):** `Phone.verifiedAt` / `Email.verifiedAt` (new, nullable). Staff-entered contacts are verified on entry — the shop met the customer — and every existing row is backfilled as verified; only a contact the customer types into the portal **without** an OTP is unverified. An unverified contact still lets POS find the party, but it **cannot be primary** (enforced in [contactWrite.ts](src/modules/customer/lib/contactWrite.ts), including first-row auto-primary and the after-delete promotion), **cannot sign in by OTP, and cannot set/reset a password** — [findPortalPerson.ts](src/shared/lib/auth/findPortalPerson.ts) now answers `found | unverified | none`, and the passwordless sign-in, `/passwordless-identity` and `/register` all refuse `unverified` with a message that says how to fix it (sign in another way, verify from My Profile). That is also the safety rule: without it a customer could add a stranger's number to their own profile and the stranger's OTP login would land inside it. Verify = the existing OTP send/verify + ticket, no second OTP system: `POST /api/portal/me/contacts` takes `{ value }` (unverified add) or `{ token }` (marks the matching own contact verified, or attaches it verified), and binds the password login to the new channel when that column was empty. Removing the login contact or the last verified contact is refused. New routes: [/api/portal/me](src/app/api/portal/me/route.ts) (GET/PATCH), `/api/portal/me/contacts` (POST), `/api/portal/me/contacts/[phone|email]/[id]` (PATCH primary / DELETE); loader in [portalProfile.ts](src/shared/lib/portal/portalProfile.ts). Browser state: `usePortalAccountStore` now holds the whole profile, loaded once from `/api/portal/me` (every mutation returns the fresh profile), with an optimistic `switchAccount`; `PortalAccountSwitcher.tsx` deleted. **Schema + data move:** `ensurePortalProfileColumns()` in [codeOwnedCatalog.ts](prisma/seeds/lib/codeOwnedCatalog.ts) (run by both seeds) adds the new columns itself, copies every social photo onto the Person, and backfills `verifiedAt = createdAt` — **run it (or `pnpm prisma db seed`) BEFORE `pnpm prisma db push` on a DB that still has `CustomerSocialProfile.avatarUrl`**, because the push drops that column. `sync-tables.json` / `local-schema.sql` regenerated; per the counter rule, publish the counter build first, then apply the schema to Supabase. **Skipped on purpose (owner, 2026-09-07):** company details stay read-only in the portal — "primary contact edits the company" needs a real `PersonCompany.isPrimary` flag (today the office's "primary contact" is just the first linked person) and is parked until wanted. Gates: tsc clean (one stale `.next/types` line for the deleted route, gone on next build), ESLint clean, `pnpm precommit` green. _Not run here: `db push` — `.env` points at the Supabase pooler, i.e. live. Not verified in a browser — the portal needs a real customer OTP sign-in._

- **2026-09-06 — Customer login completed: password accounts are now OTP-verified, and forgot-password exists**: `/api/portal/register` used to take a bare mobile/email + password with **nothing proving ownership** — and because it links to an existing customer by number, a stranger could type a customer's mobile, pick a password, and walk into their orders, invoices, wallet and repairs. It now requires the same verified token the passwordless sign-in uses ([register/route.ts](src/app/api/portal/register/route.ts)): identity comes only out of the OTP ticket (or a Google ID token), never the body. The route is also idempotent, so **create-account and reset-password are one route** — once the token proves who you are they are the same operation. New [PortalSetPasswordFlow.tsx](src/modules/external/portal/PortalSetPasswordFlow.tsx) (verify → set password → signed straight in on the still-valid ticket) backs both [/portal/register](src/app/(external)/portal/register/page.tsx) and the new [/portal/forgot-password](src/app/(external)/portal/forgot-password/page.tsx); a **Forgot password?** link now sits on the login page's password tab (there was no reset path at all before). Person-creation deduped: `onboardPerson` and the register route's near-identical transaction collapsed into shared `ensurePortalPerson` ([findPortalPerson.ts](src/shared/lib/auth/findPortalPerson.ts)) — Person + primary contact + cash-only Account, one definition; an existing customer is no longer renamed by a self-service form (staff own `Person.name`). Mobile OTP pinned to exactly 10 digits (`normalizeMobile10`): `normalizePortalPhone` stays loose at 10–15 for the DB's international rows, but a 12-digit entry used to be texted at `slice(-10)` while baking 12 digits into the ticket, so the customer verified fine and was then treated as a stranger. Env/doc drift closed: `WA_OTP_LANG`, `WA_OTP_BUTTON`, `FAST2SMS_ROUTE`, `FAST2SMS_OTP_TEMPLATE_ID` added to `.env.example`, and the Meta Cloud API walkthrough in [docs/portal-passwordless-setup.md](docs/portal-passwordless-setup.md) rewritten. Gates: tsc clean, targeted ESLint clean. _Live `.env` still has `WA_OTP_LANG`/`WA_OTP_BUTTON` unset — if the approved template is `en_US` and/or carries Meta's default "Copy code" button, **every WhatsApp send is being rejected and silently falling back to ~Rs 5 SMS**. Owner to confirm the template's language and button in WhatsApp Manager._

- **2026-09-06 — Uninstall now actually takes AbeerSuite off the PC, and the live database password no longer survives it.** The question was "does uninstall clean 100%". It did not, and the interesting part was not the disk space. **Three things were always left behind:** `%APPDATA%\abeersuite-electron` (~155 MB, untouched — `deleteAppDataOnUninstall` was never set, and electron-builder's `uninstaller.nsh` only wipes app data when that flag is defined); `%LOCALAPPDATA%\abeersuite-electron-updater` (~160 MB, one whole downloaded `installer.exe` — `counter-teardown.ps1` dropped the *Defender exclusion* for that path but never deleted the folder, and nothing else did either); and the auto-start value `electron.app.AbeerSuite` under `HKCU\...\CurrentVersion\Run`, which the **app** writes for itself (`setAutoStart` in [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts)) and which electron-builder therefore does not know exists. **The first of those is a credential leak, not clutter.** That folder holds `sync-config.json` with the **live Supabase pooler connection string, password in plain text**, `update-config.yml` with the GitHub release PAT, the NextAuth signing key, the signed-in cookies — and `pgdata`, a full local copy of the shop database (the sync log's own words: `replica READY - 116/116 tables`), with **no password on the local cluster**, so copying the folder and attaching it reads `Person`/`Phone`, every `UniversalVoucher` line, `Account`, `PosReceipt`, `LoyaltyLedger`, `AuditLog` and the rest. All of that stayed on a PC that was no longer ours.
  - **The whole cleanup lives in `customUnInstall` ([`build/installer.nsh`](build/installer.nsh)) rather than behind `deleteAppDataOnUninstall`, because that flag is all-or-nothing** and the owner wants the counter's **hardware settings kept**: the thermal printer, the customer display and the slideshow folder are physical facts about the shop that do not change when the app is reinstalled, and all three sit in one file. So `KEEP_ON_UNINSTALL` = `hardware-bridge.json` is copied to `$PLUGINSDIR`, the folder is `RMDir /r`'d, and the file is put back — one define to extend when more should survive. Keeping it alone is safe: `hardwareBridgeManager.ts` treats an absent config as a fresh machine and the settings page rewrites it, so nothing depends on the rest of the folder existing. **The VC++ runtime is deliberately never removed** — it is a shared Microsoft runtime other software may be using (owner's call, 2026-09-06).
  - **The load-bearing rule, and the thing not to break later: an UPDATE runs this same uninstaller.** `installUtil.nsh` hands the old uninstaller `/S /KEEP_APP_DATA --updated _?=<dir>` purely to clear the program folder before the new version is written into it. Deleting any of the three at that moment would wreck the version being installed — the replica re-downloaded over the shop's internet, the staged installer pulled out from under electron-updater, the firewall rule the new version wants taken away, and the counter's whole settings folder emptied — **including `autostart.json`, which since the same day is where the auto-start preference itself lives**. Every removal therefore sits behind `$UninstallIsUpdate`, read from the command line with `${GetOptions}` rather than through electron-builder's internal `${isUpdated}` — the same `--updated` flag, but the file stands on its own. _An earlier draft of this entry justified the guard by saying auto-start would be **lost for good**; the autostart re-assertion landing the same day (see the entry below) made that untrue — the entry now repairs itself on the next start. The guard stays, for the narrower reason that a counter rebooted before that next start would not come back up, and the preference file driving the repair sits in the folder being wiped._
  - **The startup entry is removed in two passes, and the second key is the one everybody forgets.** By **name** (`electron.app.${PRODUCT_FILENAME}`), which catches it even if its target has been repointed; and by **target**, enumerating `Run` for anything pointing inside `$INSTDIR`, so renaming the app later cannot quietly start leaving entries behind. Both passes also clear `Explorer\StartupApproved\Run` — the separate record Windows writes when somebody switches a startup item off in Task Manager, which if left behind would **pre-disable** auto-start were the app ever installed on that PC again. The enumeration restarts from index 0 after each delete, because removing a value shifts every index above it down by one and carrying on would step straight over the next entry. _This pass came from a draft written in a parallel session that was never committed; it is better than the single `DeleteRegValue` this change originally had, so it was taken rather than lost._ _(`${GetOptions}` unprefixed is correct inside an uninstaller here: `un.GetOptions` is `!define`d identically to `GetOptions` and both go through `CallArtificialFunction`.)_
  - **A latent bug found on the way, and it would have made this change worse rather than better.** `postgres.exe` runs out of `$INSTDIR\resources\pgsql\bin`. electron-builder's `CHECK_APP_RUNNING` only reaches it on its **PowerShell** branch, which matches every process whose path is under `$INSTDIR`; where PowerShell is missing or its execution policy is `Restricted` it falls back to `taskkill /IM AbeerSuite.exe` and nothing else — so a live `postgres.exe` held files open and `RMDir /r $INSTDIR` **already** left half the program folder behind, and would now have blocked the data wipe too. `customUnInstall` therefore stops it first, **on the update path as well**, with the same `pg_ctl stop -m fast` the app itself uses on quit ([`electron/postgresManager.ts`](electron/postgresManager.ts)), falling back to a **path-scoped** kill only if that fails — never a bare `taskkill /IM postgres.exe`, which would take down an unrelated PostgreSQL on the same PC.
  - **A silent uninstall no longer gives up.** It used to skip the firewall/Defender teardown entirely, on the correct reasoning that `runas` would raise a UAC prompt with nobody there to answer and `ExecShellWait` has no timeout. But a silent uninstall is normally **already elevated** — an all-users install records `"...\Uninstall AbeerSuite.exe" /allusers` — so it now runs `counter-teardown.ps1` **in place via `nsExec`**, which never shows a window and never prompts. When the rights are there the PC gets fully cleaned; when they are not, the script is written to report and still exit 0, so the attempt costs a logged line. _Deliberately **not** `UserInfo::GetAccountType`: that answers "is this user an administrator", which stays true for an admin running **without** elevation, so it would claim rights we do not have._
  - **`sayStep` had been unsafe in silent runs all along.** With no page on screen `$hwndparent` is 0, which turns both its `FindWindow` calls into **top-level** searches for any `#32770` — every dialog on the machine — so the `SendMessage` could have relabelled a control in an unrelated program. Nothing was ever seen to go wrong, but silent runs now do real work in the uninstaller, so it is guarded; `DetailPrint` still records the line either way.
  - _**Verified by compiling, not by uninstalling.** Both passes were built with electron-builder's own cached `makensis` against small harness scripts, and the preprocessor output was read back to confirm the expansions — the `--updated` guard falling on the right side, the `SetShellVarContext current`/`all` pairs balanced (an all-users uninstall runs with the context set to `all`, where `$APPDATA` means ProgramData and every path would otherwise be wrong), and `DeleteRegValue` naming exactly the `electron.app.AbeerSuite` value read out of the live registry. **The packaged run still needs the owner**, in this order: update an installed counter first and check `pgdata`, auto-start and the firewall rule all **survive**; then uninstall and check everything except `hardware-bridge.json` is gone and the VC++ runtime is not; then reinstall and confirm the printer, display and slideshow are already set._

- **2026-09-06 — Setup starts the app itself now, straight into the system tray.** Owner asked for it: when the installer finishes, the counter should already be running — Postgres up, server listening, sync going — with nothing on screen until somebody wants it. The finish page's tick box is ours now ([`build/installer.nsh`](build/installer.nsh), `customFinishPage`), and it launches through `--hidden`, the same argument Windows already uses to auto-start the app every morning (`isHiddenLaunch` in [`electron/main.ts`](electron/main.ts)) — so a counter's first-ever start behaves exactly like every one after it. The box reads **"Start AbeerSuite now (it waits in the system tray)"** rather than "Run AbeerSuite": ticked, Finish clicked, and no window appearing would otherwise read as a failed install. `nsis.runAfterFinish` went back to `true` in [`package.json`](package.json), but it no longer governs this page at all — defining `customFinishPage` makes `assistedInstaller.nsh` skip its own `StartApp` + `MUI_PAGE_FINISH` block — and is set only so the config does not contradict what happens.
  - **This deliberately reverses the 2026-08-25 `runAfterFinish: false` decision, and the reason it is safe is not the one you would guess.** That revert was made because auto-launching the instant NSIS finished ran into Defender still scanning the freshly-written payload, missed `serverManager.ts`'s readiness window, and made a new counter's first impression the boot-error page. **The payload is bigger today, not smaller** — 468 MB then, 629.2 MB now — so size is not the fix. The **Defender exclusions** added on 2026-09-06 (`build/counter-setup.ps1`, covering the program folder and the updater's staging folder) are: they remove the scan itself. Note the one hole that leaves — those exclusions need an administrator, and `ensureCounterSetup` skips them without complaint if the cashier declines the UAC prompt. A counter installed by somebody who clicked "No" is exactly the machine the 2026-08-25 regression was found on.
  - **A hidden start now breaks its own rule for one case.** `main.ts` used to suppress the boot-error page whenever the launch was hidden — fine for a 9 a.m. auto-start with a working install behind it. Straight after setup it is not: that first boot is the slow, risky one (`initdb`, then provisioning the replica), and a tray icon with no explanation is nothing anybody can act on. A failed boot now shows the window and the existing error page. Every ordinary hidden start is unchanged.
  - **Not proven on a real counter.** A fresh install on a clean PC — watched from the Finish button through to a working tray icon, and again with the UAC prompt declined — is owner work; nothing here can run a packaged installer.
  - **Auto-start stopped being a one-shot.** Owner then asked that the app also start with Windows. It already did — `setAutoStart(true)` on the app's **first packaged run**, guarded by an `autostart-initialized.json` marker in userData — and it now fires a minute after install rather than whenever somebody first opened the app, which is a real gain on its own. But it **only ever tried once**: after the Run entry went away (a cleanup tool, a Windows profile reset, a reinstall that kept the settings folder) it never came back and nothing noticed. The marker is replaced by a preference file, `autostart.json`, and `ensureAutoStartMatchesPreference()` re-asserts the entry on **every** start when the preference says on and Windows has none. **The tray tick box now records the choice** — without that, the next start would read a missing preference as "never answered", default to on, and undo what was just clicked.
    - **A switch-off in Task Manager → Startup apps is deliberately left alone** (owner's call). Windows keeps that veto in a separate key (`StartupApproved`) and leaves the Run entry itself intact, so `openAtLogin` stays true and the check returns early. `executableWillLaunchAtLogin` is the field that would report the veto, and nothing reads it on purpose.
    - **Counters already in the field are seeded from Windows, not from `true`.** The old one-shot never recorded what the tray tick box was set to, so the live setting is the only surviving record of it — seeding from a plain `true` would have forced auto-start back on for anyone who had deliberately turned it off, by way of the very check being added.
    - **Still open:** the electron-builder uninstaller does **not** remove the Run entry (checked its template), so uninstalling leaves Windows trying to start an exe that is gone. Three lines in `customUnInstall` would fix it; not done, not asked for.

- **2026-09-06 — A renumbered counter can no longer keep minting ids in another counter's lane.** An audit of creation-id uniqueness per counter: every autoincrement id a counter PC creates comes from a 10-million block derived from `counterNumber` (unique at the DB level), voucher numbers carry the counter number in the reference and come from a per-(kind, counter) series row, and the demo DB showed counters 91–94 each minting only inside its own block — so today's ids are clean. Two ways a **renumber** broke that: (1) `_apply_id_range()` never moved a sequence backwards, so a counter renumbered to a *lower* number kept its sequences in the old, higher block — it only logged "block exhausted", stamped `_id_ranges` with the new block anyway, and the PC went on minting in a lane the next counter given the old number would share; the push upserts by id, so the two would overwrite each other's rows in cloud. A position above the block is now dropped and the sequence restarts at the block start, still stepping past any id the block already holds; inside its own block it still never moves backwards, and a genuinely exhausted block still only warns. **Proved on a scratch cluster from the bundled `pgsql` binaries** (fresh apply, re-apply no-op, reinstall with rows pulled back, renumber up, renumber down onto an empty and a populated block, exhaustion, `_outbox` excluded): all pass on the new function, and the old one fails the renumber-down step exactly as diagnosed. (2) `PATCH /api/pos/counters/[id]` cleared `idRangeStart` on a renumber, but nothing reached the PC until its next shift open, and meanwhile the old number was free. A **bound counter now refuses a renumber (409)** unless the same request releases its device lock; the released PC is sent back to `/setup/counter`, which claims the new block and moves the sequences. PATCH also refuses number 1, as POST always did (the office's voucher-series slot). The unused 9-digit FY/counter/sequence formula (`shared/lib/id/numericId.ts`, and its twin in `uvNo.ts`) is deleted — ids are plain autoincrements in per-counter blocks — and the comments in `pos.prisma` and the counters API that still pointed at it are corrected. _Not changed: `voucherNo` has no unique index, and with the counter glued straight onto the number, counter 1 at voucher 10042 prints the same as counter 11 at 0042 once the number overflows the pad width. A separator in the series format or a unique index is the fix when the owner wants it._ _Not verified here: a real renumber on a bound counter PC end to end (release → setup → new block); the SQL half is proven, the API half is by reading._

- **2026-09-06 — The installed app is 629.2 MB instead of 913.0 MB, and a counter is never left staring at a blank screen mid-update.** The complaint was an `[urgent]` update taking the till down for minutes. `updateManager.ts` was not the problem — it already picks a safe moment. **What the install has to do was.** electron-builder's NSIS does not unpack into the program folder: `extractAppPackage.nsh` extracts the whole payload into `%TEMP%\7z-out` and then `CopyFiles` the tree into `$INSTDIR`, and `NsisTarget.js:29` hardcodes `USE_NSIS_BUILT_IN_COMPRESSOR = false`, so **there is no config off that path**. At 913 MB / 15,358 files every update wrote ~1.8 GB and Defender scanned all of it twice — so **every MB removed is worth two on a counter**, and the install needs ~2x the app size free. **Measured result: 913.0 → 629.2 MB installed, 15,358 → 14,700 files, `verify:package` PASS — and the installer itself 228.5 → 161.0 MB, so the update download shrank by a third too.** The ~284 MB cut is all payload the counter cannot reach, dragged in by `hoistNodeModules.ts` copying whole pnpm packages rather than what Next traced: **Prisma's per-engine WASM query compilers** (~112 MB — shipped **twice**, once hoisted and once in the externalised `@prisma/client-<hash>`, for four engines that are unreachable because `src/generated/prisma/internal/class.ts` imports `query_compiler_fast_bg.postgresql.mjs` **by name** at generate time); **`@next/swc-*`** (130 MB in one file, the largest thing in the installer — the Rust *build* compiler, reachable only through next's `optionalDependencies`, whose one server-side reference sits behind `experimental.useLightningcss`, which we never set); **`sharp`/`@img` inside standalone** (~14 MB, dead while `images.unoptimized` holds — now asserted against the built `required-server-files.json` rather than assumed; hardware-bridge keeps its own copy, which `qr.js` genuinely uses); `next/dist/docs` (421 files); and **15.5 MB of StackBuilder's wxWidgets DLLs and the ecpg precompiler in `pgsql/bin`** (every `icu*67.dll` stays — `icudt67.dll` is 27.7 MB of collation data `postgres.exe` links against). `RESOURCES_SIZE_CEILING_MB` drops 800 → 650 so none of it creeps back. _**The swc binary survived only because `verify-package.ts` listed it as a canary — and that file's own comment said so**: it was there to prove the transitive walk ran, not because anything needed it. Swapped for `@swc/helpers` — same walk depth, 200 KB. Safe because `checkDependencyClosure` already skips anything in `optionalDependencies`._ **Three latent bugs found on the way, none of them what I was looking for.** (1) **The Windows Firewall rule has never existed on any counter** — `netsh advfirewall add rule` needs admin, the install is per-user (`perMachine: false`), and the return value was never popped off the stack, so it failed silently every time and Windows asked the cashier itself. (2) **The installer's six `DetailPrint` lines were invisible to everybody, always** — `common.nsh` sets `ShowInstDetails nevershow` and `installSection.nsh` sets `SetDetailsPrint none` for every non-silent install. A new `sayStep` macro writes the progress page's status label directly, the way electron-builder does for its own one-click mode. (3) **`build/` was ignored wholesale**, negating only `icon.png` and `installer.nsh`, so the two new install-time `.ps1` files would have been dropped from every other machine's build — the installer would have compiled and then failed at install time looking for a file that was never committed. Firewall and **Defender exclusions** (program folder + updater staging — the biggest single lever, since it removes the second of the two scans) now go through one elevated `build/counter-setup.ps1`, which runs `-CheckOnly` unelevated first so **a routine update prompts for nothing**; `counter-teardown.ps1` removes both on uninstall. A free-space check in `preInit` replaces a halfway failure nobody could act on. **The blank screen** is now a 24 KB WinForms splash (`scripts/update-splash/`, sharing its csc plumbing with the downloader through the new `scripts/lib/csc.ts`), copied to `%TEMP%` and started detached because the Electron binary lives in the folder NSIS is replacing; it closes when the next version deletes its sentinel on boot **and** on a hard timeout, so it can never outlive a failed update. _**The install deliberately stays silent** — load-bearing, not a preference: `installSection.nsh:106-109` only auto-relaunches on the `${Silent}` path, and with `perMachine:false` a visible run stops on an "install for me / for all users" chooser and again on a Finish button, leaving a counter dead until somebody walks over and clicks._ **The SmartScreen prompt is the unsigned installer, and it stays for now** — no code change removes it, only a certificate does, and the **owner has decided not to spend on one at this stage** (2026-09-06). Do not re-propose it until they raise it. When they do, the options are Azure Trusted Signing (~$10/month, no hardware token) or an EV certificate that needs a USB token plugged into whichever machine runs the release; signing would also let electron-updater verify the update's signature via `publisherName`, which is worth having given the read-only PAT baked into `counter.env`. **Also changed to make the build run at all:** `win.icon` and `installerHeaderIcon` now point at a committed `build/icon.ico` instead of `icon.png`. electron-builder's PNG→ICO step is a WebAssembly tool that dies with `Fatal process out of memory` on this machine after `next build`'s 8 GB heap, and it re-ran on every build even though its own output was already cached — the committed file is **those exact bytes** (7 sizes, 16x16 to 256x256, generated by electron-builder itself), so the shipped icon is unchanged and the converter simply never runs. _Not verified here, and it needs the owner: the packaged `Setup.exe` on a real counter — install wall-clock before and after, the status lines reading clearly in a shop, and the free-space message._

- **2026-09-05 — `helper-roles.ts` deleted; the seed cleanup ends at 10 files, down from 41.** It was quarantined with a warning a day earlier, but `helper-inventory-masters` was then deleted on identical reasoning, and a file nobody may run is not a file worth keeping. Both failed the same way: **they described a different business using our ids.** `helper-roles` upserted `R2` as "Role-Service Engineer" (`DEP-REPAIR`) and `R3` as "Role-Counter / Cashier" (`DEP-DESK`) — we run `R2` **Branch Manager** (`DEP-OP`) and `R3` **Counter Operator** (`DEP-SALES`) — **and rewrote their grants on every run**, so one run would have renamed two live roles, re-homed them, and replaced the permissions real staff depend on. Ownership is now unambiguous: the `R1`/`R2`/`R3` rows and their department homes come from `bootstrap-seed.ts` (captured from live), `R1`'s grants from bootstrap, and `R2`/`R3` grants plus the 241 `DepartmentPermission` pool rows from the personal snapshot. `hard-clean`'s restore instructions named the deleted helper in three places and now say `pnpm prisma db seed`. **Remaining 10:** the seed core (`bootstrap-seed`, `personal-bootstrap`, `export-personal`), four cleaners (`hard-clean`, `purge-vouchers`, `purge-demo-contacts`, `purge-demo-products`), `recompute-balances`, `helper-brands`, and `apply-taxonomy` — the last applier, and the only one with work still ahead of it. An orphan sweep confirms every `lib/` file is still imported and every script is wired to a `pnpm` command. _`helper-brands` was checked too and **kept**: of the ~78 names it declares only "CP Plus" is not already live, so it is largely redundant — but brands carry no tax or structural fields and it upserts by name, so unlike the other two it cannot do damage, and it is the quickest way back after `hard-clean` wipes `InvBrand`._ _The snapshot had gone stale — 121 brands against 161 live, so a `hard-clean` + reseed would have silently lost 40 — and **the owner re-captured the same day** (`exportedAt` 2026-09-05T16:38Z): brands 121→**161**, groups 100→**102**. That capture also carries `backupConfig` for the first time (`weekdays:[0], hours:[2]` — a weekly Sunday 2 AM backup), which closes the loop on the earlier BackupConfig fix: the schedule is now real config that survives a reseed instead of a row only a deleted script could create._

- **2026-09-05 — Two more seeds deleted: `apply-voucher-series-counters` (the app self-heals it) and `helper-inventory-masters` (it described a different business).** `prisma/seeds/` is down to **11 files** from 41, and `apply-taxonomy` is the only applier left. **The voucher one** gave every `VoucherSeries` its counter-1 row; `seedVoucherSeries.ts` does the identical work — same source table, same counter 1, same `nextSeq`/`fyStartYear` carry-across, same never-touch-existing (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` vs `createMany({skipDuplicates})`). It runs from **three** places, and the third settles it: `GET /api/accountancy/voucher-series` calls it on **every request** (the route calls it an "always-run seed"), so the rows self-heal just by opening the Voucher Series page. Live state agreed: 24 series, 24 counter rows, all counter 1, and the script's INSERT would add **0**. **The inventory helper** was the more interesting find — not redundant but *wrong for this business*. Of the 77 names it declared, exactly **one** ("Photo Paper") exists in our tree: it seeds "Computer Mice", "Memory (RAM)", "Surveillance Cameras", "Desktop Systems" where we run "Mouse", "RAM", "HD Dome", "Desktop & CPU", and its categories (Laptops/Desktops/Printers/…) share **zero** names with ours (Retails Solution, Professional & Office, Small Business, Home Security, Industrial security system, IT Products — a merchandising axis, not a parts list). Running `pnpm seed:helper-inventory` would have created **~76 duplicate groups** beside our real 102, straight into the live tree with nothing to isolate them, and `@@unique([name])` would never have objected because the names differ. _I expected worse and was wrong, which is worth recording: `upsertGroupNode` does a full `update` including `gstRateId` and `hsnCode`, so I predicted rewritten tax classification — measured against live, only 2 rows matched, both merely **gained** an HSN code, and **0** GST rates would change._ Our real taxonomy was built by `apply-taxonomy` then curated by hand, so it is **config, not a generic master**: it lives in `personal.local.json` and comes back with `pnpm prisma db seed`. `hard-clean`'s restore instructions, which told you to re-run the deleted helper, now say so. Ownership in AGENTS.md §5 updated: brands → `helper-brands.ts`, everything else configured → `personal.local.json`. **Because categories and groups exist only in the snapshot and not in `bootstrap-seed.ts`, `personal-bootstrap` still cannot retire.**

- **2026-09-05 — `apply-permissions.ts` deleted: the catalog is the source of truth and both seeds already apply it in full.** Owner's call, and the numbers backed it. The script upserted a hand-kept `ADDITIONS` list and granted the non-`systemOnly` rows to R1, as a way to ship a permission without a full seed. But `bootstrap-seed.ts` (line ~865) and `applyCodeOwnedCatalog()` both loop the **entire** `PERMISSION_CATALOG` with the byte-identical upsert, so every row it could write was already covered. `ADDITIONS` had become a **third copy** of each permission — after `codeOwnedCatalog.ts` and `permissions.ts` — and unlike those two (which `check-doc-sync` holds together) nothing guarded it. Checked against the live DB before deleting: **all 12 `ADDITIONS` were already in the catalog, already in the database and already granted to R1 — running it was a complete no-op**, exactly the state its own header said should end in the list being cleared. _Also worth recording: an earlier pass of mine mis-measured the catalog at 93 by grepping literal ids. **The real catalog is 286** — most ids are generated at runtime by `voucherPermissionSeeds()` from `voucherMatrix.ts` and `sidebarPermissionSeeds()` from `MODULE_SECTIONS`, so any future audit must import `PERMISSION_CATALOG` rather than grep for it. On the true numbers the permission layer is spotless: catalog 286, DB 286, zero missing and zero orphaned in either direction._ **The one real behaviour change**, now written into AGENTS.md §5 and `docs/seeds.md`: shipping a permission means a full `pnpm prisma db seed`, and that also runs `personal-bootstrap` Step 3, the **R1 top-up** — it grants R1 every non-`systemOnly` catalog permission. R1 holds **177 of 286** today and does **not** hold `admin:bypass`, so the next reseed adds ~108 grants; a permission deliberately taken off R1 in the Role Matrix will come back. That is a decision to make before reseeding, not a surprise to discover after. (R2 holds 165, R3 holds 37.) The Role page's save toast, which told staff to run the now-deleted script, points at `pnpm prisma db seed`. `prisma/seeds/` is down to 13 files.

- **2026-09-05 — `bootstrap-seed.ts` now describes OUR business instead of a generic demo world; verified field-by-field against the live DB.** Owner's direction: this app is 100% for our company, a code-owned generic installer is a later job for the public build, and personal-bootstrap should retire soon — so bootstrap must stop describing an imaginary shop. **Checked against the live database, not the snapshot** — which mattered, because `data/personal.local.json` was captured 2026-08-27 and was nine days stale. **What was wrong: bootstrap seeded 9 Works and 43 TicketWorkers, and the live DB has ZERO** rows in `Work` / `WorkGroupWork` / `TicketWorker` / `WorkerTransition` with no status carrying a `startWorkerId` — the board runs on statuses alone, so every reseed was rebuilding an automation layer staff had abandoned. All four arrays plus `STATUS_STARTS` are gone (~90 lines); the 5 **WorkGroups** are real and stay. Their colours are now the real hex (`#268D8A` …) — they were Tailwind classes, and the seeder replaced a non-`#` colour with a **random hex on every run**, silently repainting the owner's groups. **Corrected to match live:** statuses 59→61 (`S65` Sent To Service, `S66` Rejected by Technician), status transitions 28→**48** (bootstrap had `S2→S3` / `S2→S9` which do not exist live; nothing steps out of Diagnosing), roles 1→3 (`R1` Role-Admin + the real `R2` Branch Manager/`DEP-OP` and `R3` Counter Operator/`DEP-SALES` — rows and homes only, grants stay snapshot config), departments 8→9 (`DEP-OP` "Managment", live spelling kept deliberately), branch number **1→9** (it prefixes voucher numbers — seeding 1 would renumber the shop's documents). **Couriers and payment, previously absent or generic, are now ours:** the 6 real delivery methods including Local delivery's per-neighbourhood pricing for 783384 Abhayapuri/Lengtisinga and 783380 Barpara, INDIA POST zone rates, Shiprocket/Shipway pickup pincode; and a new Section 17 seeding 4 gateways. **The gateway link is by ledger NAME, not id, and that is load-bearing** — live rows store `settlementAccountId` as a raw int inside `configJson`, and `personalSnapshot`'s `externalize` does not walk into Json blobs, so those ints were already non-portable; copying them as literals would have pointed a gateway at whatever ledger took that id on a fresh DB. The seeder resolves the name at run time and **skips a gateway whose ledger is missing rather than writing a dangling account**, because a gateway settling into the wrong ledger moves real money quietly. Required adding the `Payment Gateway` account group (previously refused as "real-config only") and its 4 ledgers. Every write stays create-only, so UI edits survive a reseed. **Proof: a read-only differ scored 42/42 against the live DB — pipelines, work groups, the four empty worker tables, all 9 departments, all 3 roles, branch number, all 6 couriers, all 4 gateways plus their settlement ledgers — and statuses and transitions diff to zero in both directions.** `tsc --noEmit` clean. _**Hazard found and quarantined:** `helper-roles.ts` upserts `R2` as "Role-Service Engineer" and `R3` as "Role-Counter / Cashier" in different departments **and rewrites their grants every run** — running it would rename two live roles and replace the permissions staff depend on. Removed from `pnpm seed:helper-all`, warning banner added, kept only as raw material for the public build._ _**Also flagged, not acted on:** the env in the main checkout points at a REMOTE host holding 3,543 party accounts while AGENTS.md says `CURRENT_DB=demo`. Only SELECTs were run. Confirm which database that is before anyone test-runs a full seed._

- **2026-09-05 — The backup schedule row is now guaranteed to exist; a fresh install could previously come up with backups silently off.** `/api/cron/backup-due` reads `BackupConfig` (row id=1) every hour to decide whether this hour is due, but **nothing created that row except a hand-run script** (`apply-backup-config.ts`) — it was in neither `bootstrap-seed.ts` nor `personalSnapshot.ts`. So a new client (`pnpm seed:generic`) or a reseed after a hard reset (`pnpm prisma db seed`) started with **no schedule at all, and no symptom**: the tick fired hourly, found nothing, and did nothing. That is the worst shape a bug can take — it says nothing about itself until the day somebody needs a restore. Breaks the AGENTS.md §5 rule that every config table is carried by the generic catalog AND the personal snapshot. **Fixed in three places, because the obvious two were not enough.** (1) `bootstrap-seed.ts` upserts the row (create-only, `update: {}`) so a new client is covered. (2) `personalSnapshot.ts` carries it in both `exportPersonal` and `importPersonal`, so your real schedule ports across a reseed — `lastRunAt` is deliberately **excluded**: it is runtime state, and restoring a stale stamp could make the "don't fire twice in one hour" guard suppress the first backup after a reseed. (3) **The catch the first two miss:** the personal path only overlays what the snapshot contains, and the snapshot has no `backupConfig` until the owner re-captures — worse, with no snapshot file at all `personal-bootstrap` returns early after step 1. So the row's *existence* went into `lib/codeOwnedCatalog.ts`, which runs in step 1, **before** the overlay and before that early return. The split is the point and is now documented: the **schedule** is the owner's config and rides the snapshot; the **row existing at all** is a code invariant with schema defaults (every weekday, hour 22 — daily 10 PM IST, safe rather than clever). Every write is create-only, so an admin's edit in Settings → Backup & Restore is never clobbered by a seed. `apply-backup-config.ts` is **deleted** — redundant on every path except the explicit `--overlay-only` opt-out — and `docs/backup-schedule-activation.md` step 1 now says `pnpm prisma db push && pnpm prisma db seed`. _Owner's step, not done here: `pnpm seed:personal:capture` to get your real schedule into the snapshot._

- **2026-09-05 — `prisma/seeds/` cut from 41 files to 15: spent one-shots and all demo data deleted.** The folder had become a graveyard. `docs/seeds.md` documented 21 files; the other 20 were `apply-*` / `migrate-*` patches written for a single DB fix, most carrying a header comment that said *"delete this file once applied — it is a scratchpad, not a record"*, which nobody ever did. **Deleted, each verified dead against the current schema first, not just against its own comment:** seven `migrate-*` scripts whose target no longer exists (`Position.roleTemplateId` is gone; `CustomerTierPeriod.windowDays` is gone; the scoped permission ids `accountancy:view:own` and `pos:shift:view:branch` appear **nowhere in the repo except inside the migration that retired them**); `apply-repair-ticket-series.ts` (no `pnpm` script, zero references — `ticketNo` is in `tickets.prisma` and `REPAIR_TICKET` is code-owned in `seedVoucherSeries.ts`); `apply-voucher-series-counter.ts`, one letter away from the live plural `…counters.ts` and a real trap; `rename-update-prices-permission.ts` (its R1 top-up is now Step 3 of `personal-bootstrap.ts`); `diagnose-shop-catalog.ts`; the six "Spent" appliers that `docs/commands.md` §7.5 had explicitly decided to keep — that decision is **reversed**, and §7.5 now explains why (all six only ever added AccountGroup / DeliveryMethod / pipeline rows that `bootstrap-seed.ts` already carries — checked line by line, e.g. `Credit Card`, `BNPL A/c` and `Wallets` are at bootstrap-seed 789/790/802). **All seven `dummy-*` seeds are gone too** (users, org-rich, customers, shop, vouchers, tasks, deals) plus their `data/demoCatalogSeed.ts` fixture and `apply-test-shop-products.ts` — owner's call, demo data can be written again when it is actually wanted. Impact was checked before deleting: every reference outside the deleted set was a **comment or a console hint**, never an import (the one real import, `dummy-shop` → `demoCatalogSeed`, went with it), so nothing broke; the hints in `bootstrap-seed`, `hard-clean` and both `purge-demo-*` now point at `pnpm seed:helper-all` or the CSV importers, and the Help page's *"run `pnpm seed:apply-help-pipeline`"* message now says `pnpm prisma db seed`. Also deleted: **`prisma/seeds/data/Export Items.xlsx`, 827 KB of binary committed to git that nothing read** — the two `scripts/` mentioning "Export Items" read a CSV off the Desktop. `package.json` lost 16 dead scripts and gained `seed:helper-all` (roles → brands → inventory) and `seed:apply-taxonomy` (which had existed with no script at all). **The rule now written into `docs/seeds.md` and `commands.md` §7.5: a script earns a place in `prisma/seeds/` only if it will be run more than once** — a one-time fix shipping with a schema change belongs in `bootstrap-seed.ts` plus a commit message, because a spent patch is not documentation and only ever raises "should I run this?". _**Found on the way:** `BackupConfig` was created **only** by `apply-backup-config.ts` — in neither `bootstrap-seed.ts` nor `personalSnapshot.ts`, so a fresh install or a post-reset reseed came up with **no backup schedule row** while `/api/cron/backup-due` reads that table hourly. Fixed in the next entry._

- **2026-09-04 — System inbox notifications now follow Position appointments, not the legacy `User.role` column.** `createSystemInboxNotifications` resolved recipients with `findMany({ where: { role: { in: roleIds } } })` — the manual override column **only**. Authority in this app flows from **Position appointments** (`userPositions[].position.roleTemplates[].roleId`), and a user created the normal way starts with `role = null`, so on a normally-seeded org every one of the 8 system notifications — new shop order, order paid, order confirmed, dispatched, courier booked, courier auto-booking failed, delivery failed/RTO, return posted — was addressed to **nobody**. It only ever worked for the bootstrap admin and for seeded dummy users, which is why it looked fine in dev. New shared helper [`findUserIdsHoldingRoles`](src/shared/lib/auth/roleHolders.ts) resolves holders live by the same union `authorizeStaffCredentials` builds at login — the Position path OR'd with the `role` match, mirroring [revokeSessions.ts](src/shared/lib/auth/revokeSessions.ts)'s `revokeRoleSessions`. The fan-out also stopped being a query per recipient: rooms are fetched in one `findMany` and the messages land in one `createMany` + one `updateMany`, instead of a `findUnique` + `create` + `update` per user. No schema change, no caller change — the 8 call sites still pass `roleIds: ["R1", "R2"]`. **Still open** (deliberately not widened here): target by **permission** rather than role id — "who should hear about a new shop order" is a duty, not a job title — and note `R2` is a *helper* seed role (`isSystem: false`), so on a generic client install only `R1` holders are addressed.
- **2026-09-04 — Offline Policies are real: one DB row, enforced in the replica write guard.** The settings page had 40 toggles that changed nothing. They saved to **localStorage**, which the server cannot see, so `getOfflinePolicies()` on the server always returned `DEFAULT_OFFLINE_POLICIES` — the single enforcement point (`UniversalVoucher.type` in `createLocal()`) was checking the hard-coded defaults, and `isMasterOperationAllowedOffline` had **zero callers**, so all 8 master-data toggles were decorative. **Now:** new singleton `OfflinePolicy` (id=1, one `policies` Json column — the shape is code-owned by `DEFAULT_OFFLINE_POLICIES`, so a new toggle needs no migration and an unknown key from a newer build is ignored rather than crashing an older counter), read through `loadOfflinePolicies()` ([offlinePoliciesServer.ts](src/shared/lib/accountancy/offlinePoliciesServer.ts)) with a 60 s cache — the guard runs on every write, so a query per write would put a round trip in front of every line of every bill. **Enforcement widened** ([offlinePolicyTargets.ts](src/shared/lib/accountancy/offlinePolicyTargets.ts)): master data (`InvProduct`/`InvProductCode`, `Account`/`AccountAlias`/`AccountGroup`, `InvBrand`/`InvCategory`/`InvUnit`/`InvProductGroup`, `Person`/`Company`) checks create and edit separately; ticket intake gates **create only**, resolved through a 5-minute pipeline-name→kind cache, so a repair already on the board still moves while offline; `TicketLog` follows `allowTimelineLog`. **Every path fails OPEN to the defaults** — a missing row, an unreachable DB or an unsynced replica must not become "nothing may be written", and the defaults already hold the conservative set. **A counter may never write the policy**: `OfflinePolicy` is refused by the guard and is on `NEVER_PUSH_MODELS` (no `_outbox_capture` trigger), so the machine being restrained cannot loosen its own restraints, offline, where nobody could review it. Registry 114→115 tables. **Requires `pnpm prisma db push`.**
- **2026-09-05 — A counter PC with no counter may only onboard; admin can no longer work on a released PC.** Owner spotted the gap: after a release the reconciler wipes the PC (`resetCounter` deletes `counter-setup.json`, stops the worker, `TRUNCATE … RESTART IDENTITY` puts every sequence back to **1 — the cloud's range**), and then `isThisPcReleased()` went silent ("nothing to be released from"), so `requireAuth` passed and an admin with `auth:login:outside-counter` could reach any office URL by typing it — the wizard redirect lived only in the login page's client code. Anything saved there took cloud-range ids in an empty replica with no worker to carry them up: **without a counter there is no id block, and nothing can beat that conflict.** New [`isThisPcWithoutCounter()`](src/shared/lib/counter/releaseGuard.ts) (counter PC + `COUNTER_USER_DATA` set + no file; one `existsSync`, no DB) is a hard stop in `requireAuth` (403 `COUNTER_RELEASED`, same six `allowReleased` routes — the wizard needs only counters list, `claim-number`, `/api/counter/status`, `/api/sync/status`) and, via `redirectIfPcWithoutCounter()` ([permissions.ts](src/shared/lib/auth/permissions.ts)), a redirect to `/setup/counter` from the three top-level (protected) layouts (`office`, `lab`, `vouchers`) and `requireStaffPage`. Kept separate from `isThisPcReleased()` on purpose: the flusher's "no file → nothing to refuse" stays as it was. Unchanged: release still means "unplug this PC" — the counter, its open shift and drawer are untouched, and the web version's "login outside counter" still means working with no counter.
- **2026-09-05 — Local-write counter is the web app; sale save on a counter fixed.** A plain SALE on a `WRITE_MODE=local` counter failed with `offline_read_only`. `syncVoucherLink()` ([voucherLinks.ts](src/modules/accountancy/lib/voucherLinks.ts)) treated a null target as "clear the link" and ran a blind `deleteMany` for every kind a voucher does not carry — and the replica guard refuses every delete, so a delete that would have matched nothing rolled the whole sale back. It now deletes only a link that exists. **Rule made explicit:** under `WRITE_MODE=local` the counter is the shop's web app, gated by roles alone — the guard's `OfflinePolicy` refusal moved inside the `localWriteIsFallback()` branch (a live-write counter cut off from Supabase still may not loosen its own restraints), and `OfflinePolicy` left `NEVER_PUSH_MODELS` so a policy saved on a local-write counter reaches Supabase like any other setting (`local-schema.sql` regenerated: 109 capture triggers). Offline Policies apply **only** to a live-write counter during an outage; the settings page says so, and says on a local-write counter that they do not restrict it.
