## 🧭 Design: Order-to-Cash (O2C) — agreed 2026-06-07

Full spec: [docs/srs/order-to-cash.md](docs/srs/order-to-cash.md). Summary waterfall:

```
RAW LEAD (staging) ─qualify→ CONTACT (LEAD, no ledger) ─signal→ DEAL (P6) ─convert→
  ORDER (P7 parent, reserves stock, NO GL) ─invoice→ SALE (GL + stock-out; Account created, →CUSTOMER)
    ├─ DELIVERY pipeline (carriers: Shiprocket/DTDC/own-boy/pickup; rate-shop→book→track)
    ├─ RECOVERY pipeline (post-invoice dues)   (TODO)
    └─ RETURN & REFUND pipeline                (TODO)
  → "happy" (delivered + settled)
```

**Locked decisions:** (1) one Order backbone, channels are adapters; (2) counter Sale = always
hand-to-hand, deferred = Order identical to phone order; (3) POS mode-first (Sale/Order/Estimate,
separate pages, frozen POS untouched); (4) parent Order + child pipelines on the shared engine;
(5) Order/Estimate GL-silent — only Sale + Recovery touch GL; (6) lazy ledgers (Account at first
txn); (7) advances ride the customer account, netted at invoice; (8) 3-tier lead funnel keeps the
hot `Person` table clean; (9) carriers pluggable, own-boy is a manual carrier, don't double-
integrate what Shiprocket aggregates. **Build pending** — see Next-up item 0.

## 🧭 Design: Workflow engine — the Worker model (agreed 2026-06-13)

The whole ticket/CRM/order workflow engine reduces to one small, repeatable shape. A **Pipeline** is an ordered set of **Workers**; an entity riding the shared `Pipeline`/`TicketStatus` spine (Ticket / Task / Deal / Order) sits on one Worker at a time and moves along configured edges.

**A Worker = state + one executor + routing + retry.** Nothing more — the earlier "6 powers" framing was a mirage.

- **State** — where the entity sits (the Worker is the sub-status, renamed `TicketSubStatus → TicketWorker` on 2026-06-13).
- **Executor — exactly one, a person _or_ a machine** ("one worker, one thing"):
  - **Human Work** — permission-gated (`work:<id>`); surfaces the entity in the **Work Inbox** (`/office/work-flow-inbox`) for whoever holds the permission. For activities that _can't_ be automated — packaging, physical QC, handing the device over, an in-person call. The person does it and records **Success / Unsuccess**.
  - **Action (automated)** — the system performs it: send email, create a deal/order/sale, spawn a child entity. For activities that _can_ be automated. Dispatched through an `ACTION_REGISTRY` (same shape as `childEntities.ts`).
  - The human-vs-action choice lives on the **Work** (W1–W8): each Work is _either_ a human activity _or_ an automated action ("different action for different work"). A Worker just _places_ one Work at one point in a pipeline.
- **Routing** — every Worker exits the same way: `Success → <worker>` / `Unsuccess → <worker>`. **The engine never branches on executor type** — a human clicking the outcome and an action reporting its result feed the identical routes.
- **Retry** — for _waiting_ executors (a human follow-up loop, or an action polling an external result): the front-loaded `retrySchedule` re-arms attempts and drops the entity into the Inbox **Waiting** tab until due.

**Composition is by chaining, not flags.** "Auto-email, then a human confirms the reply" = two Workers in sequence: `[Action: Email] → success → [Human: Confirm reply]`. The pipeline is the composition; each Worker stays atomic.

**Spawn was never a special power — it's the first Action.** "Create a child ticket/deal/order on another pipeline" (the polymorphic spawn shipped 2026-06-13) is just `action(spawn, <pipeline>)`. The old powers list (route / retry / triggerMap / spawn / action) collapses to: _one Work (human or action) + routing + retry._

**How an Action reports its outcome** (the machine equivalent of the human button):

- **Sync action** (email sent, customer row created) → returns success/fail immediately → routes now.
- **Async action** (waiting on an external result — a courier status, an email reply, a spawned child terminating) → the Worker parks as **waiting**; the outcome arrives later via callback/signal and _then_ fires the route. This is exactly the spawn's existing report-back: a child Worker carrying a `parentSignal` ("success"/"fail") resolves the parent. **Async actions = `parentSignal`, generalized.**

**When is a Work "done"?** When its executor produces an outcome — a human work when the person records Success/Unsuccess; a sync action when the handler returns; an async action when its signal/callback arrives (or it exhausts its retry schedule). Until then the Worker is _in progress_, or _waiting_ if snoozed.

**Context — what a worker collects and hands on.** A completion isn't just an outcome; it can also produce **data**. Each Work carries a small, configurable **field schema** (kinds: `text · number · money · date · entity-ref`) — and the data runs a four-step lifecycle:

- **Collect** — a human work renders those fields as a **mini-form in the completion dialog** (replacing reliance on the free note); an action **produces** them (email → message-id, payment → ref).
- **Carry** — the values ride **`Ticket.context`** (the JSON bag already on the ticket), keyed, readable by downstream workers on the same ticket.
- **Pass** — a spawn **maps parent context → the child** entity (any pipeline), so a spawned _Tracking_ entity is born already knowing the `trackingId`. (The old "configurable spawn context" idea is _only_ this step.)
- **Render** — an activity/title **template** turns the fields into human text — _"Letter posted to President · India Post · #EX123IN"_, not _"mail sent"_ — so anyone reads "what and where".

Typed fields are **mandatory where a downstream worker can't function without an input** (Issue-Charger needs `neededPart`; Track-Letter needs `trackingId`); the free-text note stays as the unstructured catch-all. Kept to a small field-kind set, not a form-builder. So a Work = **executor** (who/what does it) + **context schema** (what data it collects/carries/passes/renders) — see Next-up / task list.

**The money/workflow boundary (hard constraint).** The workflow is **disposable and fully data-configurable**; the **accounting core is ACID and protected**. Actions are the bridge — but a money action **stages or opens a flow prefilled; it never silently posts a GL entry.** So actions sit on a spectrum: fully-auto (email, spawn) → one-click (create deal) → **staged-with-human-confirm (sale/order/voucher)**. Integrations (dial/email/WhatsApp) start as safe stubs (`tel:`/`mailto:` + a logged activity) behind provider config, real providers wired later.

**Status.** Shipped (2026-06-13): polymorphic child-entity spawn + `TicketWorker` rename. Pending: the `ACTION_REGISTRY` + `Work.actionType` (+ optional per-Worker config) — first slice = **Dial** on W1 Tele Calling (additive, no DB reset). Folds into the configurable-spawn work (Next-up item 6) and memory `workflow-access-work-layer-redesign`.

## 🧭 Design: Permissions — three-axis model (agreed 2026-07-03)

The RBAC rethink. Old model was one tangled grid — `module × action × scope` (e.g. `accountancy:edit:branch`) — which mixed three unrelated questions into a single permission row. The new model splits them into **three independent axes**, all **sourced from the position** (the appointment), never stapled to the person. A person = a bundle of positions; every effective value is a **union over their positions**.

**Axis 1 — UI (which surfaces you get).** Give each position a customised UI; hide the irrelevant. Driven by `ui:sidebar:*` grants, generated from the constants file (WORKSPACE_NAV + MODULE_SECTIONS + ADMIN_NAV + SETTINGS_NAV). A nav grant = "you get this page, may open it, and may load its data." Coarse on/off per nav item, full depth. **Built** (sidebar authoritative + constants-driven). Note (2026-07-13): reads no longer gate on `requireModuleAccess` — GET routes + page views are plain `requireAuth`; nav grants drive _visibility_, not API read access.

**Axis 2 — Action (what you can do inside).** The finer matrix, once you are on a surface. Two _sub-flavours_ that default in **opposite directions**:

- **Read / content-visibility** → default **OPEN**, restrict _down_. A nav grant implies you may read everything on the surface; a per-kind `view`/`open` un-tick _hides_ a kind. (Answer to "has Day Book, can he see Payment?" → **yes by default**.) Stored additively (nav auto-implies view on all kinds; un-tick removes) so we never run two opposite mechanics. **Built (2026-07-13):** every GET API + page/layout/nav view is default-open (`requireAuth`) — the hidden capability gates were swept out. Remaining: the per-kind `view`/`open` un-tick to hide a _specific_ kind on a shared surface.
- **Write / act** → default **DENIED**, grant _up_. Seeing a tool never implies mutating with it. Per-kind voucher `create/post/cancel/delete`, and tool actions like a future `pricing:modify`. (Answer to "has pricing-strategy, can he modify price?" → **no by default** — a separate grant.) **Building** (per-kind voucher matrix exists; View/Open columns added; enforcement on the write side + report filtering pending).

**Axis 3 — Radius (whose records, across which branches).** A property of the **position's posting**, not the person, and not a permission suffix:

- **Own branch** — baseline, no grant needed; it's just the branch of your position.
- **Multi-branch** — holding positions in several branches → the **union** of those branches (`branchIds`, resolved at login).
- **All-branch (executive)** — one position carries an `access:all-branches` grant that lifts the branch fence entirely.

Data queries filter by the resolved branch set (`where.branchId in branchIds`), skipped when the all-branch grant is present. This **replaces the `:own / :branch / :org` suffix** — the old grid's scope dimension collapses to just "my branches vs all branches", carried by postings + one grant, so the `scope` enum's finer gradations (`OWN/TEAM/DEPARTMENT/REGION`) are no longer needed as a radius system.

**Migration stance.** The old `module × action × scope` permissions (the `accountancy:create:own` etc. grid) have **no distinct job left** — Axis 1 replaced `view`, the per-kind matrix replaced `create/edit/delete`, postings replaced the scope suffix. They survive **only as `newCheck OR oldCapability` fallbacks** so nothing breaks mid-migration. **Do not build new roles on them.** Retire them (seeds + `OR` fallbacks) in the cleanup pass once Axis-2 write enforcement + Axis-3 branch filtering are wired. See memories `sidebar-visibility-permissions`, `develop-new-then-cleanup`.

## 🧭 Design: Product search — tiers, rescues and confidence (built 2026-09-10)

**One matcher, six callers.** `searchProducts()` in [productSearch.ts](src/shared/lib/inventory/productSearch.ts) is the only product search in the app; the scoring underneath it is [productCodes.ts](src/shared/lib/inventory/productCodes.ts). Before this, four components had each grown their own ranking for the same job and they disagreed: the till had tiers, `ProductScanInput` had exact-SKU-then-prefix, `InvProductCombobox` and `InvProductPicker` had a plain substring test.

**The ladder.** A query is split into whitespace tokens. Each token scores the best tier it can reach against the product's searchable text — name, SKU, brand, string attributes, every scannable code:

| Tier | Token matched | Score |
|---|---|---|
| Word start | at a word boundary | 400 |
| Substring | anywhere in the text | 300 |
| Prefix | starts a product word | 200 |
| Inside | inside a word | 100 |
| Typo | Levenshtein ≤ 1 | 10 |
| Missing | not found | 0 — product dropped |

The product's score is the **worst** token's tier (every token must match), plus **100** when the whole query appears as one contiguous run. A tier-10 score means *nothing actually matched* — the query was merely one edit away — so those rows count as guesses, not hits.

**Run-together rescue.** A token that scores 0 is retried by splitting it at every position; **both halves must begin a word**, and neither may be fuzzy. This is what makes `pen32`, `a4paper`, `papera4` and `camcp` work. Splitting only at letter↔digit boundaries was tried first and fails on `papera4` (`papera` + `4`). The word-boundary rule was learned the hard way in the browser: allowing a half to match mid-word made `paper` split into `pa` + `per`, found inside "com**pa**tible" and "o**per**ates", and pulled a **CCTV camera** into a storefront search for A4 paper. Splitting is already a guess about what was meant — letting a half be a guess too matches almost anything.

**Fuzzy has a floor.** A token under 4 characters gets no typo tier at all. One edit out of two characters is a 50% error rate: `a4` was reaching the bare word "a" in a product description. This is why `abc` no longer returns "TV Remote **ACC**" as a plain row.

**Three passes, and when the later ones fire.** `fallback: true` is the **till's setting only**. Pass 2 drops the every-token rule and widens the typo budget to 2 edits on tokens of 5+ characters (`hddseaget` → the Seagate disk); pass 3 ranks by letter-pair overlap. They run **only when pass 1 found nothing genuine** — padding a search that worked is worse than a short list, because `seagate` finding its two hard disks must not bury them under ten "Sata cable" guesses. Filler must still score, so nothing arbitrary is ever shown: `xyzq` yields one row, not twelve.

**Confidence is a property of the row, not of the pass.** A row is a guess when no word of the query really matched it — the fallback rows *and* a typo-tier hit from pass 1. The POS dropdown reads normally for hits and amber for guesses, two levels, no captions. **Enter never adds a guess row on its own**: an unknown barcode also ends in Enter, and a wrong item on a bill is worse than no item, so the cashier must aim at the row first.

**Scanning is exact, never ranked.** `findByExactCode()` short-circuits Enter wherever a scanner is used — the voucher item row, `ProductScanInput`. Ranking already puts an exact SKU/barcode first; this makes it a guarantee, because "sorts first" is not good enough when the cost of being wrong is the wrong item on a purchase bill.

**A repeated item merges.** Picking or scanning a product already on a trading voucher adds this row's quantity to that line and blanks the row, with a toast naming the line. Owner's call over bump-unless-rate-differs; the toast is what keeps a two-rates-one-item purchase bill recoverable by hand.

**The web gets the matcher, not the machinery.** `ShopCatalogClient` builds its own searchable text from `displayName` + `description` + `category` and calls `scoreSearchText` — a **leaf import**, never the inventory barrel, which would pull Dexie into a public bundle. No fuzzy, no guesses (on a storefront those read as broken), no local catalogue and no polling: the replica may not hold the full list and polling it costs egress. It fixes a real bug — the old test was one literal substring, so a shopper had to type a contiguous fragment of the title: against the live catalogue **"copier paper a4" matched 4 products where 28 are relevant, and "a4paper" matched none at all**.

**Cost.** Every picker reads the shared in-memory catalogue via [useProductCatalog](src/shared/hooks/useProductCatalog.ts) — one array per tab, IndexedDB first, then a server delta, kept live by the `catalogue` realtime topic. `InvProductPicker` used to refetch the whole catalogue on every dialog open; it no longer does. Dropdowns that open on focus are capped at 50 rows with a "type more to narrow down" footer — uncapped, `InvProductCombobox` mounted one `<li>` per catalogue row on every focus, the same shape as the ledger dropdown that used to block the main thread for 1.8s.

**Two shapes, one matcher.** A dropdown wants ranked and capped rows, so it calls `searchProducts()`. A table filter wants neither — it keeps its own sort (age, margin, stock) and shows everything that matches — so it calls `productMatchesSearch()`, the same scoring as a yes/no. Rows that are not product-shaped (a report row with extra searchable columns, a list of group names, a public storefront product) pass a `text` extractor. The old substring `productMatchesQuery` is **deleted**: every product search in the app, dropdown and table alike, is now one function, so `papera4` and multi-word queries behave the same wherever they are typed.

## 🧭 Design: Shop category/collection/product routing — Plan 1 (proposed 2026-08-05, NOT yet decided)

Reported as "shop category and office product category not matching". Investigation found this is largely **by design, not a bug**: the storefront already runs a deliberate vocabulary bridge — office `InvProductGroup` root name → customer-facing **"Category"** (chips/filter/nav; per-product `InvProduct.shopCategory` free-text can override it) — while office `InvCategory.name` → customer-facing **"Collection"**, computed into `ShopProductPublic.collection` but currently **rendered nowhere** (grepped every `.tsx`, zero usages — dead field). Neither Category nor Collection has its own page today: Category is a same-page anchor-scrollable section (`#cat-<name>`) + `?category=` query filter on `/shop` and `/` (see [groupCategory.ts](src/shared/lib/shop/groupCategory.ts), [productPublic.ts](src/shared/lib/shop/productPublic.ts)); Collection isn't surfaced at all.

**Plan 1 (one candidate, not yet approved/executed):** give Category, Collection, and Product each a real flat root-level URL — flatter path depth ranks better for SEO than nesting under `/shop`:

- Collection → `/kids-collection` (one per `InvCategory` row)
- Category → `/laptops` (one per `InvProductGroup` root row)
- Product → `/sample-product-hard-disk-drive-500gb-seagate` (per `InvProduct.slug`), replacing the current nested `/shop/product/[handle]`

Sketch discussed:

1. **Schema** — add `slug` to `InvProductGroup` (unique, only meaningful on root groups since only roots surface as a shop category). Reuse `InvCategory.slug` as-is — it already exists ([inventory.prisma:65](prisma/schema/inventory.prisma:65), unique) but has never been read or written by any app code; this would be its first real use.
2. **Admin UI** — add a slug field (auto-fill from name, editable) to the Group master; wire up the already-existing but unused Category slug field in office UI.
3. **Routing** — one catch-all `src/app/(external)/[slug]/page.tsx` resolving in order: product slug → category(group-root) slug → collection(InvCategory) slug → 404. Existing static routes (`/shop`, `/portal`, `/login`, `/cart`, `/checkout`, `/compare`, `/wishlist`, legal pages) always win over the catch-all in Next.js's own routing, but a **reserved-word guard** is still needed when a slug is saved/edited so nobody can pick a slug equal to a real static route.
4. `/shop` (search + all-products browse with filters) stays as-is — still useful as the "see everything" page.
5. Redirect old `/shop/product/[handle]` → the new root product URL.
6. Point the home-page category tiles (currently anchor-scroll) and `/shop` category pills (currently `?category=` query filter) at the real new `/[category-slug]` URLs instead.

**Status update (2026-08-05, later the same day):** Phase 0 + the first slice of Phase 1 are **DONE** — see the "Recently shipped" entry. Office **Category IS the customer-facing "Collection"** — the cross-cutting merchandising axis beside Group's navigation tree (`ShopProductPublic.collection`). It also keys `InvAttributeMaster`, `LoyaltyEarnRule`, `CouponProductRule` and the BRAND → GROUP → CATEGORY → ORG pricing chain, so its rows may be edited freely but must not be re-keyed or restructured casually. _(An earlier draft of this entry said Category was "retired from the storefront / office-only" — that was wrong: being load-bearing office-side does not stop it being customer-facing, and the two-axis Group+Collection model was the agreed design all along.)_. The **Group tree is now the customer-facing taxonomy, un-flattened**. Still **not** built from Plan 1: flat root-level URLs, the `ShopSlug` registry, dedicated category/brand/collection pages, the `ShopCollection` many-to-many model, `InvProductRelated` cross-sell, and per-group facets.

## 🧭 Reference: Vyapar backup comparison (analysed 2026-09-11)

The owner's old Vyapar data (`D:/AbeerSuite/vyapar_closing_24.07.2026/*.vyb`, a zip holding one SQLite file, Apr-2023 → 23-Jul-2026: 13,926 sales, 2,843 receipts, 353 sale returns) was profiled table by table and compared with our engine. Keep this as the reference for "how does Vyapar do it" questions and for the migration import.

**How Vyapar is built (single entry).** One `kb_transactions` row per document with a `txn_type` code; the sale row carries `txn_cash_amount` + `txn_balance_amount` side by side (415 bills were part cash, part credit at the till). `kb_lineitems` stores the NET unit price and tax separately, line total = the gross rupee the customer sees. `txn_payment_mapping` = which cash/bank account the money hit. `kb_txn_links` = receipt→bill settlement with an amount per link; an unlinked receipt IS the advance (297 of them), and a receipt's `txn_discount_amount` counts toward settlement (used 74×). Credit-note returns are linked to the sale (165), cash-refund returns never are (188). There is NO ledger: trial balance / P&L / balance sheet are computed at report time from per-type sign rules in the app, and balances are cached columns (`txn_current_balance` per bill, `kb_names.amount` per party, opening + mappings per bank) that the app nudges on every save. A small journal + chart-of-accounts mapping was bolted on in Jan-2025 for GST input / TDS / manual journals only (17 entries here).

**Mistakes found in their data (do NOT reproduce on import).** 304 sales where items ≠ bill by ±₹0.47 with round-off stored as 0 (older versions rounded silently); line totals with float noise (1050.0018); one sale with a blank invoice number; party `amount` drifted to 0 on parties with open bills; invoice number = prefix + sequence with no separator (`SALE/26-27/786` + `1414` prints as `SALE/26-27/7861414`). Their bill↔receipt link math was correct on every sale.

**Our equivalents.** `kb_transactions` → `UniversalVoucher` + `VoucherDetail`; `kb_lineitems` → `VoucherInventoryLine`; `txn_payment_mapping` → role=TENDER `VoucherGlLine`; `kb_txn_links` → `VoucherAllocation`; their bank/cash adjustments and loans → CONTRA/PAYMENT/JOURNAL vouchers. What they have no table for at all is our BODY/TAX/DISCOUNT/ROUNDOFF GL legs — the reason our books cannot drift.

**What we are missing vs Vyapar (verified in code on 2026-09-11, not from docs).**

| Vyapar has | Our position today |
| --- | --- |
| Part cash, part credit on one bill at the till | **Have it** (2026-09-16) — the counter takes part-paid and fully-unpaid bills; the unpaid part rides the customer's ledger and the effective credit limit, `max(manual, auto)`, is the only gate |
| Discount / write-off on a receipt (short payment forgiven, used 74×) | Missing; bill stays open unless a journal is raised |
| Credit note auto-linked to the sale it came from | Missing; POS return in CREDIT mode parks on AR with no allocation, manual link needed |
| Cheque tracking with status (received / deposited / bounced) | Missing |
| Payment-gateway link + QR printed on the bill, EDC card machine | Gateway model exists; no QR on the invoice, no EDC |
| E-invoice IRN generate / cancel, e-way bill | Missing |
| TCS, TDS, reverse charge, ITC-applicable flag, cess per unit | Missing (CGST/SGST/IGST split + place of supply only) |
| Batch number, manufacturing date, expiry per line | Missing |
| Serial number per unit | Missing; product codes only, not per-unit serials |
| Wholesale price with minimum qty; per-party item rate | Covered differently: customer tiers + barcode price bands |
| Fixed-asset purchase / sale document | Missing |
| Loan accounts with EMI schedule | Journals + a ledger; no dedicated screen |
| Custom fields on bills and items | Missing |
| SMS / WhatsApp message template on save | Missing |
| Recycle bin with one-click restore | Hard delete + AuditLog snapshot (restore exists only via the audit route) |
| Multi-currency with exchange rate on a bill | Missing |
| Multi-firm in one database | Branches instead |
| Terms & conditions per document type | Print template |
| Service reminders per party + item | Missing |
| Payment terms (net 15 / net 30) on a bill | Have credit period days per customer / voucher |
| Dozens of ready-made reports | Fewer report screens, every figure traceable to GL |

First-week pain for a shop moving off Vyapar: the first three rows plus batch/expiry. The GST block matters only once e-invoicing becomes mandatory for this turnover.

**Our numbering has the same ambiguity.** `voucherRef.ts` joins counter and padded sequence with no separator, so counter 1 + seq 10001 = counter 11 + seq 0001 once a counter passes 9,999 bills in a FY (Vyapar's single till did 4,570/yr). Low risk today; fix by putting a separator between the counter and number parts before any counter gets near that.

---

## 🧠 Key decisions

| Area                  | Choice                                          | Why                                                                                           |
| --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Framework             | Next.js 16 App Router                           | Server + client co-location; Turbopack for dev speed.                                         |
| ORM                   | Prisma split schema                             | One datasource, modular schema files.                                                         |
| Auth                  | NextAuth v5 JWT, two providers                  | Staff and portal credentials never crossover; `CustomerPortalCredential` is a separate table. |
| State                 | Zustand + localStorage for client carts         | No server round-trip until checkout.                                                          |
| Money                 | Integer paise everywhere                        | Avoid float drift; format with `moneyInr.ts`.                                                 |
| Accounting model      | `AccountType → AccountGroup → Ledger`           | Tally mental model in code, normalised in DB.                                                 |
| Suspense ledger       | Auto-route opening-balance mismatches           | Trial balance always balances during legacy imports.                                          |
| Inventory unification | Single `InvProduct` for shop + warehouse + POS  | No sync layer between catalog and stock.                                                      |
| POS tills             | Counter-based, not user-based                   | Any cashier can work any counter; variance lands on the counter account.                      |
| Pipelines             | Generic engine reused for Tickets and CRM Leads | Don't dual-write; leads are device-less tickets.                                              |

---

## 💡 Ideas (parking lot)

- **Cloudinary orphaned-image cleanup.** No image is ever deleted from Cloudinary when replaced or when its parent record is deleted — only `secure_url` strings are stored (`InvProduct.imageUrl` + `galleryJson`, `InvCategory.imageUrl`, `InvProductGroup.imageUrl`, `InvBrand.imageUrl`, `User.avatarUrl`, `CustomerSocialProfile.avatarUrl`/`coverUrl`); no `public_id` is persisted anywhere and there's no server-side Cloudinary SDK/Admin API wired yet (uploads go straight browser→Cloudinary via a signed-upload flow, `src/shared/lib/cloudinary.ts`). **Decided approach:** not per-route delete-on-replace/delete-on-parent-delete wiring (rejected — touches too many routes for the current simplification phase). Instead, a standalone reconciliation script `scripts/cleanup-cloudinary.ts` (pattern like `apply-permissions.ts`) that lists all Cloudinary assets under the `avatars/` and `shop/products/` folders via the Admin API, cross-references against every DB field above, and reports/deletes whatever's orphaned (in Cloudinary, not referenced in DB). Default dry-run (print only); `--delete` flag to actually destroy. Run manually/ad hoc, no cron. Not yet built.
- Pipeline-specific dashboards per pipeline (Repair / Sales / etc.).
- B2B Company portal — companies see all linked devices/tickets.
- Despatch courier integration — auto-generate tracking label from "Pending Despatch" worker.
- Position model — named slots in the org chart (needed at 50+ users).
- Per-branch reorder inputs on `InvProductBranch` — the Reorder page (2026-07-23) computes usage per branch, but `lowStockThreshold` / `leadTimeDays` / `orderPackQty` / `minOrderQty` are still global on `InvProduct`.
- Supplier-specific pack/MOQ — `orderPackQty` / `minOrderQty` (added 2026-08-12) live on the product, so a product bought from two suppliers on different terms can only carry one. Move them onto a supplier↔product link when multi-supplier procurement is built.
- Assemble/bundle products (assembled PC, CCTV set kits) — sellable bundles of group products; rides BOM + Manufacturing like repacking, needs its own product/pricing surface (noted 2026-07-23).
- Customer Quality flag block-scope **settings toggle** — today the no-discount flag is a hard-coded all-block (tier + coupons + loyalty); the future refinement is an admin setting choosing which benefits the flag blocks. (Feature itself shipped 2026-07-23.)
- GSTIN auto-fill from public GST lookup API when typing a 15-char GSTIN.
- POS exchange as one atomic transaction (currently credit note → new sale).
- Personal till accountability via a user dimension on variance JEs (currently counter-only).

## ⚠️ Known gotchas

- **Changing a column to `NOT NULL` still needs the counter release to go out FIRST.** `sync-engine/lib/schemaGuard.js` compares each column's nullability, and until 2026-09-17 *any* difference halted that till's entire sync worker (exit 4) — which made nullability effectively unchangeable, because a replica receives schema only through `resources/local-schema.sql`, whose `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` is a no-op on a column that already exists. The guard is now **directional**: a replica that is nullable while the source is `NOT NULL` merely permits something the source never sends, so it is reported (`looser`) and does not halt; the reverse (source nullable, replica `NOT NULL`, which would drop rows) still halts, as do type changes and missing columns. **But a till running the OLD guard still halts**, so the ordering rule in AGENTS.md §3 stands: ship the counter build, then deploy the migration. `UniversalVoucher.branchId` was the first column through this path.
- **`UniversalVoucher.deletedAt` / `deletedById` / `deleteReason` are vestigial.** Soft-delete was removed 2026-08-01 (Next-up item 6) but the columns stayed; nothing writes them, every delete route hard-deletes, yet `ledgerStatusFilterUv()` and `billLink.ts` still filter on `deletedAt`. Don't describe the app as having soft delete; either wire them back through the hard-delete routes (recycle bin) or drop them with the filter — owner's call, not decided (2026-09-11).
- **`pnpm electron:pack` does NOT compile the NSIS script.** It runs `electron-builder --dir`, which stops at `win-unpacked` — so `verify:package` can pass green while `build/installer.nsh` has a fatal syntax error. Only `electron:dist` / `electron:release` compile it. To check the installer script alone without redoing `next build`: `pnpm exec electron-builder --prepackaged dist-app/win-unpacked`. _(Found 2026-09-06 the hard way: a rewritten installer.nsh passed the gate twice and only failed when actually compiled.)_
- **In `build/installer.nsh`, never use `${__FILEDIR__}`.** electron-builder feeds the whole script to makensis on **stdin**, so it resolves to app-builder-lib's own templates directory and any `File` command using it dies with "no files found" — in the **uninstaller** pass first, since that is built first. Use `${BUILD_RESOURCES_DIR}` (electron-builder supplies it to both passes); `${PROJECT_DIR}` is also available.
- **Run electron-builder through `pnpm`, never `npx`.** Under `npx` it misdetects the package manager as npm and shells out to `npm list -a`, which never returns on a pnpm store — the build sits with flat CPU and no file writes, looking like a hang rather than a misconfiguration.
- **Server restart required after `prisma generate`.** Turbopack caches Prisma client chunks.
- **Middleware is `src/proxy.ts`, NOT `middleware.ts`.** Next.js 16 rename.
- **Split Prisma schema** — `prisma/schema/*.prisma`, configured in `prisma.config.ts`.
- **NextAuth v5 beta** — cast `session.user` for `id`/`role`/`roles`/`permissions`. Never use `getToken()`.
- **Scalar list filtering** — `String[]` uses `{ has: "R3" }` (Postgres only).
- **Worker `allowedRoles`** — R1 always bypasses, by design.
- **Opaque IDs** — S1–S10 statuses, SS1–SS37 workers, R1–R4 roles, P1–P4 priorities, `P-REPAIR` / `P-SALES` pipelines.
- **`@@unique` with nullable fields** — Postgres treats NULL as distinct; `skipDuplicates` is unreliable. Use `findFirst` + `create`.
- **Razorpay reads only `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` + `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET`** ([paymentGateways.ts:55-56](src/modules/pos/lib/paymentGateways.ts)). _This line used to say `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` must be set or "webhooks reject silently" — that key appears nowhere in `src/`, and there is no Razorpay webhook route, so it sent people hunting for a setting that does not exist (corrected 2026-09-06)._
- **Portal and staff auth never crossover.** Don't reuse staff cookies for portal.
- **Shop prices in paise.** Use `moneyInr.ts` to format. Never divide by 100 inline.
- **Schema added via `db push`, no migration**: GST/HSN columns, naming-convention columns. Migrate before any prod deploy.
- **Legacy `src/modules/tally/`** co-exists with `src/modules/accountancy/`. Rename was 2026-05-07; folder removal is pending separate work.
- **POS operator access = `ui:sidebar:workspace:pos`, NOT `ui:sidebar:pos:*`.** The office `pos` module (`ui:sidebar:pos:*` = Shift Reports / Counters, manager-facing) is a _separate_ grant from the operator's Workspace → POS link. POS **write** routes gate on `requirePosVoucher` (the `pos:voucher:create:<kind>` tick), never `requireModuleAccess("pos")` — else a pure counter operator 403s on checkout even with an open shift. (Fixed 2026-07-13.)
- **Reads are default-open; don't re-add read gates.** Since 2026-07-13, GET APIs + page/layout/nav views take only `requireAuth`. A restriction must be a role-page tick (sidebar visibility or a write permission) or branch radius — nothing else. New read-gating is a _deliberate, parked_ follow-up, not something to reintroduce route-by-route.
- **Date-field vocabulary in reports: "Opening" = start of day, "Closing" = end of day.** In any `from`↔`to` range, `from` is pinned to start-of-day and `to` to end-of-day (`startOfDayUtc`/`endOfDayUtc` in [period.ts](src/modules/accountancy/lib/period.ts)) — so `from` = opening of that day, `to` = closing of that day, both inclusive. Report **"As at"** fields (Balance Sheet) are **closing** balances inclusive of that date's vouchers (`parseAsOf` also uses `endOfDayUtc`).
- **Ledger opening balance / product opening stock is universal — no date field at all (decided 2026-07-28).** `Account.openingBalance(Type)` and `InvProduct.openingStock/openingRate` are always effective from the start of the books, whether the row was imported from another system or created fresh — no per-account/per-product cutover date, ever. The `openingBalanceDate`/`openingStockDate` **columns still exist** (nullable, unused) because every read path already treats null as "always applies" (`aggregateLedger.ts`, `valuation.ts`, `costBasis.ts`, `balance.ts`) — so this was a pure UI/API removal, not a schema change. The date input was removed from every entry point (ledger/product opening-balance forms, the Add-Ledger/Add-Product quick-create dialogs, the customer/product CSV importers, the merge-duplicate-party flow) and every write API now hardcodes the date column to `null`. **Do not re-add a per-row opening date field** — if staged/staggered migration cutover is ever needed again, that's a new, deliberately-scoped feature, not a revival of this one. Leftover non-null values were cleared by a one-off script that has since been deleted (`prisma/seeds/migrate-clear-opening-dates.ts`, in git history) — a null date is the correct state, so nothing needs re-running.
- **Departments are intentionally cosmetic for now (decided 2026-06-23).** They structure the org chart and derive `user.departmentIds`, but nothing user-facing keys off them. The home dashboard ([office/page.tsx](<src/app/(protected)/office/page.tsx>)) was switched from department-routed briefings to a **common, permission-gated** dashboard — every user sees the same page and each briefing card shows by permission (admin:bypass sees all), reusing the same `DeptBriefings.tsx` components. **Department-scoped data visibility is also deferred**: the `*:department` permission keys (`tickets/customers/assignments:*:department`) and the `department` tier in [ticketScope.ts](src/modules/repairing/lib/ticketScope.ts) are real code but **granted to no role**, so the filter never fires. All of this is by design, not a bug — do **not** wire dept-scoped silos or dept-routed UI until the department feature is built. Branch is the enforced scope (GL / POS / vault).
